# Rumoca Compiler — Full Static Review & OpenModelica Competitive Analysis

**Date:** 2026-07-22
**Snapshot:** HEAD `928348f` on branch `msl-trace-parity-50`, with 46 locally-modified files (a separate agent was editing the working tree during the review; findings were verified against on-disk content at read time, so a few line numbers may have drifted).
**Scope:** Static review only — no code was modified. Dimensions: Modelica Language Specification (MLS 3.7) compliance, project-spec (`spec/`) compliance, efficiency, tensor-nativeness, correctness, numerics, testing, tooling, plus a research-backed competitive analysis against OpenModelica (old backend and the array-preserving new backend), MARCO, ModelingToolkit/Dyad, and Dymola.

## Method

- **16 area review agents** covered the pipeline end-to-end (parse/AST, resolve/instantiate, typecheck, flatten/connections, DAE/events, structural analysis, solve lowering, runtime evaluators, integrators, codegen/exec backends) plus six cross-cutting audits (tensor-nativeness, efficiency, project-spec conformance, core/API, tests/MSL validation, tooling).
- **3 research agents** gathered primary-source facts (papers, coverage dashboards, release notes, GitHub issues) on OpenModelica's new backend, the competitive landscape, and published scaling benchmarks. All facts in §8 carry sources (Appendix C).
- **Adversarial verification:** every non-info finding was handed to an independent skeptic agent whose job was to *refute* it — re-reading the cited code, checking the MLS/spec citation, and grepping the rest of the pipeline for handling elsewhere. Only findings that survived are integrated below. Verification ran in two passes (first pass on Fable 5, continuation on Opus 4.8 at high effort after a session-limit interruption).
- **Completeness critic:** after the area reviews, a critic agent hunted for blind spots and spawned 4 follow-up investigations (synchronous/clocked semantics, nonlinear/event solver numerics, state machines, systematic balance failures).
- **Volume:** ~300 agents total, ~11M tokens, ~3,600 tool calls.

## Remediation status — 2026-07-23

The findings below remain the record of the reviewed snapshot. On the current
branch, the six critical silent-wrongness channels in §4 and the immediate
correctness items in §9 have received their P0 remediation:

1. Generic Solve-IR expression lowering now rejects surviving `der()` calls
   with their source span.
2. Structural state demotion now rejects any surviving derivative reference
   whose target is no longer in the state partition.
3. Elimination now rejects removal or substitution touching a cheapened
   structured family whose placeholder interiors have not been materialized.
4. `delay()` and `terminal()` now lower to explicit DAE/Solve metadata and
   runtime-managed parameter slots. Terminal state is activated only for the
   final event; transport delay uses accepted-solution history with continuous
   interpolation, discrete right limits, variable query-time delay, bounded
   retention, and solver-visible delayed-discontinuity roots.
5. `Connections.rooted(a)` is now evaluated branch-relatively as
   `depth(a) < depth(b)` for `Connections.branch(a, b)` in the selected VCG
   forest.
6. Stream operators are eliminated during Flat connection expansion, the
   earliest stage that owns resolved connection sets and stream/flow
   attributes. Two-port `inStream()` keeps its exact peer form; larger sets use
   a nominal-scaled, regularized positive-flow weighted mean; and
   `actualStream()` lowers to its flow-direction `if` expression. DAE, Solve,
   and evaluator boundaries now reject any leaked stream operator instead of
   treating it as an identity.
7. Runtime, compile-time, interpreter, and Cranelift paths now agree on
   three-way `sign()` and IEEE division semantics; shared Modelica helpers live
   in `rumoca-core`.
8. Parser lowering now preserves skipped output positions and their source
   provenance, applies declaration dimensions before type dimensions, and
   validates, decodes, and re-escapes the MLS string escape set.
9. DAE-level C template helpers now return render errors for missing or
   non-iterable RHS equations instead of emitting a plausible `0.0`.
10. Connection equality and flow generation now reject incompatible resolved
    array sizes instead of silently truncating to the smaller or first size.

These remediations are covered by focused regressions. Full unit suites for 14
affected crates pass (3,129 tests total), as does a workspace-wide all-targets
`cargo check` excluding the separately built Python and WASM bindings. The lint
and architecture gates pass, including parser provenance, Rust file-size, and
spec-size budgets. The tiered-model suite passes (151 tests).

The first P1 numerics/runtime tranche is also complete on the current branch:

1. Periodic stop events are scheduled on demand with compact schedule state;
   the former 200,000-tick materialization cap and its silent truncation are
   gone, including for coincident schedules.
2. The default BDF simulation driver checks `max_wall_seconds` throughout its
   step and event loop and reports a typed timeout.
3. Interactive BDF root handling reuses the existing model, runtime, problem,
   and solver allocation instead of rebuilding and leaking a complete problem
   at every root.
4. Solver event right limits now derive from the configured event tolerance
   with a one-ULP fallback, so they no longer grow as
   `1e-6 * (1 + abs(time))`; generated FMI 2 code uses `nextafter`.
5. Algebraic projection performs finite, residual-certified causal seeding
   before block Newton. This retains IEEE Modelica division semantics while
   recovering dependency-ordered singular direct-chain guesses.
6. Solve lowering evaluates numeric `nominal` attributes into solver-slot
   metadata. Initialization and algebraic Newton systems use row/column
   scaling derived from `max(nominal, abs(start))`, while BDF and RK45 receive
   per-variable absolute tolerances from the same policy. Invalid or absent
   nominal metadata safely uses the Modelica default of one.
7. Initialization now lowers `homotopy(actual, simplified)` to the exact
   `simplified + lambda * (actual - simplified)` blend and runs a
   backend-neutral adaptive lambda continuation from zero to one. Rejected
   steps restore the previous unknown vector and halve the continuation step;
   both stateful and zero-state Diffsol/RK45 initialization paths finish on the
   actual system. Coupled algebraic causal seeding is now limited to
   non-finite residual blocks so it cannot overwrite a finite damped-Newton
   iterate near an expression-domain boundary.
8. Event restart retains the cheap Picard/Gauss-Seidel path, but when that
   path reaches its iteration limit it now escalates to a backend-neutral
   scaled coupled Newton solve over algebraic `y` slots and Real-valued event
   `p`/`y` targets. Discrete-valued modes and relation-memory overrides remain
   frozen during each Newton solve, runtime assignments are refreshed for
   every residual probe, trial steps are line-searched, and both the dense
   unknown vector and caller-owned runtime state are left unchanged on
   terminal failure.
9. RK45 accepted steps retain their seven DOPRI5 stages and construct the
   standard free quartic continuous extension only when a root crossing needs
   localization. A safeguarded secant/bisection solve evaluates root
   conditions on that interpolant without any derivative re-integration,
   stops on residual or time tolerance, selects the earliest crossing rather
   than the first root index, and clusters simultaneous crossings by their
   localization tolerances.

Regressions cover post-cap periodic ticks, coincident schedules, BDF timeout,
interactive-session horizon extension and root reuse, bounded right limits at
large absolute time, projection after IEEE division, numeric nominal lowering,
mixed-magnitude Newton scaling, per-variable BDF/RK45 tolerances, exact
homotopy blends at lambda zero/one-half/one, rejected continuation-step
rollback, and runtime completion on the actual initialization system. The
event regressions include an algebraic/discrete-Real system whose alternating
projection cycles between two points while the coupled Newton fallback
converges to its simultaneous solution. RK45 regressions prove the dense
extension at intermediate and endpoint states and exercise two crossings
whose earlier event has the later root index. The combined unit suites for
`rumoca-solver`, `rumoca-eval-solve`, `rumoca-solver-diffsol`, and
`rumoca-solver-rk45` pass (304 tests).

This completes the P1 numerics/runtime items enumerated by this review.

The next Solve-lowering/runtime efficiency tranche also resolves both
mass-matrix findings in Appendix A. Solve schema 20 replaces the quadratic
`Vec<Vec<f64>>` artifact with compact identity, diagonal, and sparse-COO
variants. Both Solve-lowering entry points now emit a payload-free identity,
and Diffsol prepares the selected form once with each `OdeModel` instead of
inside derivative guesses. Identity and diagonal operations remain allocation
free apart from returned solution vectors; sparse application is O(nnz), and
general solves reuse an LU factorization instead of storing an explicit
inverse. Regressions cover compact serde, a state count of `usize::MAX` without
mass allocation, diagonal solve/application, sparse solve/application, and
invalid payloads. The five directly affected unit suites pass (1,074 tests),
all MLIR integration targets compile, and lint plus 176 architecture/policy
checks pass.

The subsequent P0/P1 review tranche closes three latent correctness gaps found
while validating those remediations. Runtime-managed delay/terminal slots are
excluded from translation-time constant folding. Direct initialization
assignments to discrete P-storage are partitioned as initialization updates at
Solve lowering rather than being projected as continuous unknowns. Solve schema
21 also makes initialization projection storage explicit: blocks contain Y/P
`ScalarSlot` unknowns, so nonlinear `fixed=false` parameters participate in
the same scaled Newton and homotopy continuation as solver-Y unknowns. The
Solve shape contract rejects malformed rectangular, duplicate, or out-of-range
projection inventories before runtime construction.

The transport-delay review additionally replaced source-time event scheduling
with root surfaces over the actual delayed query coordinate
`time - delayTime(time)`. Discrete source discontinuities remain retained while
reachable by `delayMax`, including parity across history pruning, so a variable
delay can cross them in either direction. Accepted zero roots are suppressed
until the query leaves that side of the discontinuity. Both stateful and
zero-state paths use the combined model/delay root inventory, and their
evaluation scratch is reused. A backend regression proves that RK45 processes a
variable-delay discontinuity at the query-time root rather than the obsolete
source-time estimate.

The P3 tensor-native tranche is now implemented through the portions of the
pipeline that can preserve compact ownership without changing Modelica
semantics. Whole-array, slice, and regular connection equations acquire
authoritative structured-family owners during Flat lowering. Direct
family-to-`Map`/`AffineStencil` lowering handles compact aggregate templates
without first materializing every scalar row, and the reference evaluator now
executes those nodes as native affine loops. Shipped WGSL and MLIR Solve
templates consume the same native-family partition; their scalar fallback
contains only genuinely unsupported rows and therefore cannot accidentally
force scalarization of otherwise-native families. Variable layout keys use
interned `VarName` identity, mass matrices use compact identity/diagonal/sparse
forms, and dummy-derivative aliasing now runs in the structural DAE-to-DAE phase
rather than as a mathematical rewrite inside Solve lowering. Structural
matching records compact translated-family descriptors only when corner
incidence proves the numeric layout, while scalar structural rewrites drop
affected family metadata before matching can consume stale descriptors.

The MSL pipeline now records a stable, body-weighted tensor-preservation KPI,
including compact-domain points, derived scalar-view rows, native Solve node
counts, scalarized rows, and reason-coded fallbacks. This is intentionally a
ratchet rather than an assertion of complete O(1) compilation. Component-array
instantiation and the scalar compatibility graph used by matching/BLT remain
linear in domain cardinality, and the general direct-residual API still exposes
scalar rows for irregular or unsupported bodies. Those are the remaining P3
scaling limits; the branch does not claim they are solved.

The evaluator consolidation requested by §9 is complete at each source IR.
AST integer, Real, and Boolean syntax dispatch is owned by one scalar
interpreter parameterized by phase lookup/function/diagnostic policy; its
function statement control flow is likewise shared. Flat scalar entry points
are typed adapters over the single `Value` interpreter, while shape inference
remains a separate non-value analysis. Solve evaluation remains separate
because it interprets Solve IR, but shares compiler-owned builtin semantics
with the source evaluators. Runtime operators `delay()` and `terminal()` and
stream operators now have their MLS semantics rather than only fail-fast
coverage.

The post-remediation full-MSL run restores and exceeds the supported-surface
ratchet without lowering its baseline: 546 of 566 models compile and 533 are
balanced; 276 produce initialization systems and 224 simulate. Trace comparison
completed for 218 models. Of all 566 models, 197 (34.81%) meet the acceptable
trace threshold and 172 (30.39%) avoid severe deviation; within the compared
cohort, 150 of 218 (68.81%) are high-parity, 47 are near, and 21 deviate. The
run reports 415 severe channels out of 1,337 bad channels and preserves 99
native tensor bodies out of 1,922 measured bodies. The runtime cohort's median
wall-time ratio remains about 37× OpenModelica, so performance parity is still
open even though the compile and quality gates pass.

Dynamic validation against `/home/jgoppert/git/modelica_models` loads 345 source
documents from 348 `.mo` files. Rumoca compiles `Tests.All` (2,762 raw
equations), runs zero-time SO2 and SE2 Lie-group simulations, emits the CUBS2
and RDD2 GALEC production targets, and builds both CUBS2 and RDD2 FMI 3 shared
libraries. The same pinned OpenModelica environment passes `Tests/run.mos`
(`Tests.All`: 2,837 equations/variables and a successful simulation to 1.0) and
`Tests/run-loglinear.mos` (RDD2 log controller plus 55-equation
`Tests.LogLinearTests`, simulated to 0.02).

That external corpus exposed one upstream compiler defect: Flat's structured
reference attachment visited materialized equations but not compact family
templates, so an FMI 3 export of CUBS2 failed when `vehicle.R` retained its
spelling but not its `DefId`. The fix now canonicalizes exact variable
references in both regular and initial structured templates at the Flat output
boundary. A focused regression proves the declaration identity is attached
before DAE lowering.

The final diff-focused review also removed evaluation behavior from
`rumoca-ir-ast` (compact connection-family evaluation now has one owner in
`rumoca-eval-ast`), made malformed compact-family row inventories fail with a
spanned structural contract error instead of silently skipping runtime
protection, and decomposed every branch-touched Rust source that exceeded the
accepted 2,000-line hard limit. The exact working-tree snapshot passes
`cargo xtask repo review-scan --base origin/main --head <snapshot>
--fail-on-forbidden`, `git diff --check`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.

**Verdict tally: 173 findings CONFIRMED** (6 critical, 32 high, 98 medium, 36 low, 1 info), **13 REFUTED** (dropped; listed in Appendix B.1 for the record), **1 uncertain**, **12 reported but never verified** (over the per-area verification cap; listed in B.3). Confirmed findings by category: correctness 49, efficiency 35, MLS compliance 31, project-spec 20, tensor 21, architecture 11, testing 6. Every confirmed finding in Appendix A carries file/line evidence and a concrete remediation.

**Known original-review limitations:** the initial review was static and the
tree was concurrently edited. The remediation was subsequently exercised by
focused crate suites, the full MSL gate, and the external Rumoca/OpenModelica
corpus above. The fourth gap investigation (systematic
Fluid/Batteries/Magnetic balance-count failures) remains an **open question**;
it deserves dedicated dynamic triage because `compile.dae` unbalanced errors
still gate part of the remaining 20-model compile gap.

## 1. Executive summary

Rumoca is an unusually disciplined, spec-governed Modelica compiler whose **architecture is roughly one generation ahead of classic scalarizing compilers — and currently one generation behind its own SPEC_0032 tensor-native target.** The IR story (canonical serializable Appendix-B DAE, versioned Solve IR with first-class tensor nodes and tensor-preserving forward/reverse AD, 27 codegen targets including a genuinely tensor-native WGSL path and a best-in-class eFMI/GALEC exporter) has no equivalent in OpenModelica. The engineering discipline (431 CI-enforced MLS contracts, ~60 architecture-hardening boundary tests, full-MSL OMC trace-parity gate with ratcheted baselines on every PR) exceeds what any open-source Modelica tool practices, including OpenModelica itself.

The gap between that architecture and shipping reality is concentrated in four places:

1. **Silent-wrong-results channels (the most dangerous class).** Six confirmed critical findings share one failure shape: the compiler accepts a construct it cannot handle and produces wrong numbers instead of an error — `der()` in general expression position lowers to constant `0.0`; demoted states leave live `der(x)` references that compile to `0.0`; dropped "cheapened" tensor families leave `der(x[i]) = 0.0` placeholder rows behind as real equations; `delay()` evaluates as identity; `rooted()` ignores spanning-tree proximity; N≥3 stream sets never get the MLS 15.2 mixing formula. A compiler competing on *trust* cannot afford silent wrongness; every one of these must become either a correct implementation or a hard, spanned error.
2. **The scalar equation vector is still the authoritative owner at every phase boundary.** Structured families exist and are genuinely engineered (compact domains, stride proofs, corner-cell incidence synthesis), but they are an *overlay* on materialized scalar rows: instantiation expands component arrays per element, whole-array/slice/connection equations never receive families, the entire structural phase (matching, BLT, tearing, index reduction) runs on the scalarized bipartite graph with string-keyed rows, and the reference interpreter clones one program per index tuple. `Real x[1000]; der(x) = -x;` currently compiles *worse* than the equivalent for-loop. Compile cost is Θ(N) best case.
3. **Runtime numerics lack the robustness layer that separates research solvers from CVODE/IDA-class production stacks.** No nominal-based scaling anywhere in the Newton/projection paths, homotopy machinery that is never actually swept, Picard (not Newton) coupling between discrete and continuous unknowns at events, a fixed time-epsilon event right-limit, silent truncation of periodic events at 200k ticks, and no wall-clock budget on the default BDF path. This maps directly onto the dominant MSL failure buckets: `sim.structural.algebraic` (80), `sim.solver` (54), `sim.init` (32), `sim.event` (31).
4. **MLS coverage is deep where it was driven by MSL parity work (ch. 7 modifications, ch. 8 events/when/reinit, ch. 9 flow/potential) and thin or wrong at the edges:** ch. 5 lookup deviations (imports resolved lexically, encapsulated scopes fall through to global), ch. 15 streams (mixing formula unimplemented), ch. 16 clocks approximated on a float grid with drifting activation predicates, ch. 17 state machines validated but never elaborated — while the contract matrix over-reports these as "Implemented."

**Bottom line:** measured against the same 566 MSL example models, rumoca simulates ~37% where OpenModelica's old backend simulates ~92%. But OpenModelica's *new* backend — its answer to the tensor-native future — simulates only 42.6% of MSL and verifies 29.2%, remains explicitly experimental, and is funded to close that gap through mid-2027. Rumoca's window is real: it is already ahead of the NB in IR architecture, export breadth, AD, tooling, and validation honesty. The path to winning is (P0) close the silent-wrongness holes, (P1) add the numerics robustness layer, (P2) fix the cohort-blocking MLS gaps, (P3) finish making the family — not the scalar row — the authoritative owner. Details in §9.

## 2. Scorecard

| Area | Confirmed | One-line verdict |
|---|---:|---|
| Parse & AST | 12 | Grammar coverage is excellent and literate (full Appendix A); conversion layer has 3 silent-data-loss bugs; AST purity and allocation discipline need work. |
| Resolve & instantiate | 10 | Strong ch. 7 modification machinery and inner/outer; real ch. 5 lookup deviations; instantiation scalarizes component arrays and leans on string identity against SPEC_0001. |
| Typecheck | 7 | Strong dimension-inference engine; headline equation-compatibility check no-ops on most real equations; algorithms/bindings unchecked in the production path. |
| Flatten & connections | 11 | Flow/potential core is trustworthy; streams (N≥3), `rooted()`, nonempty `equalityConstraint`, expandable connectors are broken/missing; engine built on string tokenization. |
| DAE, events & init | 12 | One of the strongest parts — canonical, positively-validated Appendix B artifact; `delay()`/`terminal()` silently wrong; origin-string-driven semantics. |
| Structural analysis | 11 | Deterministic and conservatively gated, with novel corner-cell incidence; but scalar at heart, heuristic state selection whose failure mode is silent (der→0), quadratic elimination. |
| Solve lowering | 11 | Best-in-class IR contract with tensor nodes + AD; but lowers scalar-first then re-coalesces (prohibited by SPEC_0032 §2), `der()`→0.0 hole, string-keyed layout, 92k-line crate with duplication. |
| Runtime evaluators | 11 | Prepared register-machine interpreter with real AD is strong; `sign(0)=1`, guarded division diverges from IEEE and from compile-time folding; 5 drifting evaluators; Map/AffineStencil scalarized at prepare time. |
| Integrators & sim | 10 | Genuinely close to production for index-1 DAEs (analytic sparse Jacobians, correct pre()/event semantics); missing scaling/budgets; 200k-tick truncation; per-event leak. |
| Codegen & exec | 11 | 27 targets, honest FMI 2/3, exceptional eFMI/GALEC; only WGSL is tensor-native — MLIR/C/cranelift scalarize and unroll; silent 0.0 fallbacks in DAE-level templates. |
| Tensor audit | 11 | "O(N) rows with O(1) bodies + compact metadata" — not O(1) compilation; family-drop hazard is a correctness risk; structural phase is where structure dies. |
| Efficiency audit | 11 | Runtime hot loops near-allocation-free; compile middle-end quadratic-plus (elimination, 7× DAE clones, triple scalarize, O(n²) dup removal). |
| Project-spec audit | 9 | Constitution largely obeyed *and mechanically enforced* — rare and valuable; violations cluster at eval-solve→solver upward dependency, dummy-derivative rewrite inside phase-solve, and the legacy string-scalar layer. |
| Core, API & bindings | 11 | Real incremental+parallel compile driver, enforced contract harness, thin bindings; string-rendered scalar identity is the foundational debt; codec panics on malformed wire data. |
| Tests & MSL validation | 7 | Honest, reproducible, ratcheted full-MSL OMC-parity apparatus (beyond any open-source peer); sim-correct rate ~37%; semantic/assertion/fuzz layers thin; trace metric has a near-zero-channel defect. |
| Tooling (LSP/fmt/lint) | 9 | LSP is a genuine competitive differentiator; UTF-16 panic on non-ASCII lines and config bypass must be fixed; linter is a placeholder. |
| Gap: clocked (MLS 16) | 3 | Clocks reduced to independent f64 (period, phase) pairs; activation predicate re-derived from `time` with 1e-9 tolerance vs the 1e-12 scheduler grid; pre-history committed at every event boundary — explains the worst trace cohort. |
| Gap: nonlinear/event numerics | 3 | A real damped line-searched block Newton exists (critic partially refuted); the actual holes are unscaled numerics, dead homotopy, and Picard discrete↔continuous coupling. |
| Gap: state machines (MLS 17) | 3 | Validation-only; no elaboration/lowering; contract matrix over-reports as Implemented. |
| Gap: balance-count failures | 0 | Investigation inconclusive — **open question** (needs dynamic triage of the Fluid/Batteries/Magnetic unbalanced cohort). |

## 3. What rumoca already does better than OpenModelica

These are verified strengths, not aspirations — each was confirmed by reviewers reading the code (details in the per-area strengths lists in Appendix A context and in §8's sourced comparison):

- **A stable, versioned, serializable canonical DAE artifact** (MLS Appendix B form, schema-gated, positively validated by `appendix_b_validation.rs`). OpenModelica has no equivalent externally-stable DAE contract; this is rumoca's platform play (CasADi/JAX/SymPy/Julia export all hang off it).
- **First-class tensor compute IR with machine-checkable stride proofs** (`Map`, `AffineStencil`, `MatMul`, `LinSolve` with sparsity annotations, surviving serde round-trips) plus **tensor-preserving forward AD and reverse VJP** differentially tested against scalar references. OMC's new backend keeps for-equations but has no comparable kernel IR or AD story at this layer.
- **Export breadth no Modelica tool matches from one template contract:** FMI 2 ME+CS and FMI 3 ME (+honestly-gated CS), CasADi SX/MX, JAX, SymPy, SymForce, Julia/MTK, ONNX, embedded C, Rust, CUDA, WGSL, MLIR, cranelift JIT, and a **mature eFMI/GALEC exporter** (real checksum webs, Beta-1 trap enforcement, roundtrip-tested parser) — OpenModelica offers nothing comparable for eFMI, and its FMI 3 export is still experimental.
- **Enforced MLS compliance tracking:** 431 SPEC_0022 contracts as data, 531 accept/reject test cases, CI coverage guards that fail when the registry and tests diverge. OpenModelica has no contract-level compliance harness.
- **Validation honesty:** every PR runs the full 566-model MSL corpus against OMC reference traces with per-channel normalized-L1 comparison, deviation-shape taxonomy, state-selection parity, and ratcheted no-regression baselines. This is beyond OMC's own nightly practice (which tests coverage, not PR-gated parity ratchets).
- **Modern developer experience:** a feature-complete LSP (diagnostics, completion with MSL index, hover with flattened-DAE preview, rename, code lens with balance status, in-editor simulation) with measured latency engineering, WASM-reusable handlers, an incremental blake3-fingerprinted parallel compile session, and a conservative, comment-safe formatter. OpenModelica's LSP is minimal; OMEdit is a heavyweight IDE.
- **Memory-safe, deterministic, single-binary Rust toolchain** with deterministic collections enforced by spec (SPEC_0021) — reproducible BLT/state selection across runs, which OMC does not guarantee.
- **Runtime speed where it works:** on trace-passing models, runtime medians favor rumoca 1.49× (system time) over OMC's generated C (45.7× wall including OMC's gcc step) — the interpreter is competitive *without* a C compile step.

---

## 4. The six critical findings

Full evidence and remediation for each is in Appendix A; this is the executive view. All six were adversarially confirmed.

1. **`der()` silently lowered to constant 0.0 in general expression position** (`rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/scalar_ops.rs:273`). Any `der()` reaching root conditions, discrete rows, observations, or initial residuals becomes `0.0` — `when der(x) > 0` yields a permanently-false root. MLS Appendix B's variable vector explicitly includes ẋ. **Fix:** make `Der` a hard error in generic lowering (as `Pre` already is) and add a proper lowering that references the derivative slot.
2. **Demoted-state `der()` references silently compile to 0.0** (`rumoca-phase-structural/src/dae_prepare/state_row_reduction.rs:402`). Heuristic demotion can orphan `der(x)` references; combined with (1), the model silently integrates a different system. **Fix:** post-demotion scan for surviving `der(v)` of non-states → spanned `StructuralError` or re-promotion.
3. **Dropped cheapened families leave `der = 0.0` placeholder rows live** (`rumoca-phase-structural/src/eliminate/mod.rs:487`). With the production default (`materialize_structured_families=false`), interior family rows are placeholders; elimination drops the family metadata on any touching substitution, leaving fake bodies as real equations — a boundary substitution into a method-of-lines corner can zero all interior derivatives. **Fix:** re-materialize interiors before any family drop, or hard-error; add a post-elimination validation pass.
4. **`delay()` evaluated as identity — transport delay unimplemented** (`rumoca-eval-dae/src/eval/builtin_runtime.rs:88`). Compiles, simulates, wrong physics, no diagnostic. **Fix:** reject at ToDae with an ED-class error until a real history-buffer implementation exists (same policy for `terminal()`, which is hardwired false — a confirmed high).
5. **`rooted()`/`Connections.rooted` ignores spanning-tree proximity** (`rumoca-phase-flatten/src/vcg.rs:592`). No spanning tree is built; both endpoints of a mid-chain branch report `rooted=true` — wrong kinematics for reversed MultiBody attachments. **Fix:** build the actual BFS spanning tree (parent+depth) and evaluate `rooted(a)` per enclosing `branch(a,b)` as `depth(a) < depth(b)`.
6. **Stream connectors: `inStream` mixing formula (N≥3) and `actualStream` unimplemented** (`rumoca-phase-flatten/src/connections/equation_generation.rs:386`). Only 2-connector sets are rewritten; larger sets silently pass through — this invalidates Fluid/Media numerically. **Fix:** implement MLS 15.2's max(-ṁ,ε)-weighted mixing formula and the `actualStream` if-expression at connection expansion; hard-error until then.

The unifying remediation principle: **rumoca already has the right error-policy spec (SPEC_0008 fail-fast, no plausible-value recovery) — these are the places the code predates or evades it.** Converting silent wrongness into loud errors is cheap, immediately raises trust, and turns invisible bugs into countable triage buckets that the existing MSL harness can burn down.

---

## 5. Tensor-nativeness: SPEC_0032 aspiration vs. reality

**Per-stage verdicts from the audit** (tracing `Real x[1000]; der(x) = -x;` and a MultiBody-style model):

| Stage | Verdict | Where structure dies |
|---|---|---|
| Parse/AST | preserved | Symbolic `shape_expr`, ranges, comprehensions all structural. |
| Instantiate | partial | Scalar arrays stay whole; **arrays of components expand per element** with full clones + per-element modifier resolution. |
| Flatten | partial | For-equation families genuinely compact (corner-cell default); but **families are created only for for-equations** — whole-array, slice, and connection equations never get one; connects over arrays fully scalarize. |
| DAE | partial | 1:1 preservation, O(N) family remap; discrete (f_z/f_m) and condition (f_c) partitions carry no families at all. |
| Structural | **scalarizes** | `scalarize_equations` expands everything; matching/BLT/tearing/index reduction run on the scalar bipartite graph with rendered-string row keys. Corner-cell incidence synthesis is the one tensor-aware island. |
| Solve lowering | partial | Regular families become one `Map`/`AffineStencil` — but by lowering every scalar row first and re-coalescing (the exact "scalar-row reassembly" SPEC_0032 §2 prohibits); non-regular families pay O(N²); implicit-row and Runtime-profile paths re-scalarize everything (JVP artifacts, dense O(n_x²) identity mass matrix). |
| Runtime interpreter | scalarizes | `Map`/`AffineStencil` cloned into one program per index tuple at prepare time; MatMul/LinSolve have real native kernels. |
| Codegen | split | **WGSL is truly tensor-native** (one parametric kernel per family — O(1) code size); MLIR/C/FMI templates scalarize and unroll (the linalg renderers exist but are never called by shipped templates); CUDA launches a single GPU thread. |

**Honest characterization:** O(N) rows with O(1) bodies plus compact metadata that lets Solve re-derive kernels — not O(1)-in-N compilation. The ironic headline: **the idiomatic whole-array equation compiles strictly worse than the equivalent for-loop**, because only for-loops get families.

**How to fix it (this is P3 of the roadmap, and the strategic core):**
1. Synthesize `StructuredEquationFamily` for whole-array equations, slices, and uniform connection-generated array equations at flatten/DAE time (SPEC_0032 §1 already mandates this; the audit calls it "the single highest-leverage tensor change").
2. Keep vectorized connects symbolic — one connection with a `StructuredIndexDomain` binder instead of per-element `InstanceConnection`s, with per-element expansion as the derived view.
3. Make the structural phase family-aware: matching/index reduction over compact domains (the corner-cell incidence synthesis already proves the approach; extend it from incidence to the matching/BLT decisions themselves). This is precisely where OMC's PAC paper and the ACM TOMS array-aware-matching results (see §8) show one-to-two-orders-of-magnitude build-time wins — and rumoca can do it with *proof-carrying* domains rather than OMC's heuristic reconstruction.
4. Lower families directly to tensor nodes without materializing scalar rows first, and give the interpreter a native loop kernel for `Map`/`AffineStencil` (execute the program once per domain with remapped strides, not N cloned programs).
5. Migrate scalar identity from rendered strings (`"x[1,2]"`, `parse_scalar_name`, 65+ call sites) to `(DefId | VarNameId, flat-offset)` keys. This is simultaneously the SPEC_0001/0007 conformance fix and the enabler for O(1) layouts.
6. Replace the dense identity mass matrix with `Identity{n} | Diagonal | Sparse` variants; stop eager JVP materialization in the Runtime profile.
7. Add a **tensor-preservation KPI to the MSL gate** (kernel inventory counts are already reported by `tensor_report.rs`) and a ScalableTestSuite-style compile-time-vs-N benchmark to CI so scalarization regressions become visible.

---

## 6. Efficiency: where compile time goes

Runtime hot loops are near-allocation-free (prepared programs, `_into` APIs, scratch reuse) and competitive. The compile-time middle-end is the weak half — the audit's estimate for a 10k-equation MSL-class model ranks the dominators:

1. **`eliminate_trivial` substitution application** — O(n_eq × n_subs × |expr|): every equation linearly re-walks the full cumulative substitution list; the fixpoint re-applies cumulative substitutions to the whole DAE each pass; each application deep-clones the entire DAE as a read-only derivative source. **Fix:** index substitutions by target `VarNameId`, one-walk rewrite with memoized chain closure (union-find for pure aliases), compose new substitutions against the applied set instead of re-applying, borrow the derivative source.
2. **The sim-lowering funnel deep-clones the DAE ≥7 times** and runs full scalarization on three separate copies. **Fix:** thread borrows/`Arc`s; scalarize once behind a shared view.
3. **`remove_duplicate_continuous_equations`** renders every equation to a `String` and does linear `Vec::contains` — O(n²) string compares. **Fix:** reuse the existing `expression_semantic_fingerprint` bucketing (already the standard elsewhere in the codebase).
4. Parser-side: every token owns a `String` file path; TryFrom conversion deep-clones whole compositions per nesting level; the rewrite transformer clones every `Arc` node unconditionally. **Fix:** `SourceId` in `Location`, `Cow`/`Arc::make_mut` patterns.
5. The 45 s phase-budget `sim.timeout` failures (EngineV6, FullRobot, Spice3 adder — 20 models) are mostly *Solve-phase* timeouts, i.e., this same middle-end, not the integrator.

There is no compile-time benchmark harness; the per-pass timing instrumentation already exists (`structural_trace!`, eliminate profilers), so wiring a scaling benchmark (e.g., ScalableTestSuite subset) into CI is low-effort and should accompany the fixes.

---

## 7. Other cross-cutting results

**MLS compliance shape.** Deep and spec-annotated where MSL parity forced it: ch. 7 (modifications/redeclare — including selective model extension, which OMC gained only recently), ch. 8 (when/elsewhen/reinit/event actions — with genuinely correct edge-vector and reinit-inactive-branch semantics), ch. 9 flow/potential, Appendix B partitioning. Thin or wrong at: ch. 5 (imports resolved lexically instead of top-level; encapsulated scopes fall through to the whole global scope; inherited members shadowed by enclosing scopes), ch. 6/12 (equation compatibility check no-ops on Binary/Unary/If/function-call sides; production path skips algorithms, component-class equations, and bindings; no function-call argument typing here), ch. 15 (streams — critical above), ch. 16 (clocks approximated: independent f64 (period, phase) per derived clock, no rational base-clock lattice, activation predicate re-derived from `time` at 1e-9 vs the scheduler's 1e-12 grid, clocked `previous()` history committed at every event boundary — fully explains the worst-cohort traces), ch. 17 (state machines: excellent graph validation, zero elaboration — and the contract matrix reports the chapter "Implemented," which is a matrix-honesty problem since `ContractStatus::Partial/NotImplemented` already exist). Parser-level silent data loss: skipped output slots `(x, , z)`, dual-subscript dimension order `Real[3] x[2]` → `[3,2]` instead of `[2,3]`, undecoded string escapes.

**Numerics robustness (from the gap investigation — it partially *refuted* the critic).** A real damped, line-searched block Newton with analytic AD Jacobians and LU→SVD fallback exists (`project_algebraics_with_plan`), and the event-restart path genuinely re-solves the algebraic loop at the new mode. The actual holes: (a) **no nominal/start-based scaling** of residuals, tolerances, or guesses anywhere; (b) **homotopy is dead code** — `homotopy()` lowers to a binary init/actual switch, no λ sweep exists on any path; (c) discrete↔continuous coupling at events is **Picard/Gauss-Seidel, not coupled Newton** — the `sim.event` divergence mechanism; (d) fixed `1e-6·(1+|t|)` event right-limit bridged by forward-Euler extrapolation (a tolerance-independent error floor); (e) rk45 localizes roots by 48 blind bisections instead of using DOPRI5's free dense output; (f) `max_wall_seconds` ignored on the default BDF path; (g) periodic events materialized for the whole horizon and silently truncated at 200,000 ticks; (h) interactive BDF session `Box::leak`s a full ODE problem per root event. These map one-to-one onto the `sim.init`/`sim.solver`/`sim.event`/`sim.structural.algebraic` triage buckets — **this is the single biggest lever on the MSL sim rate.**

**Project-spec conformance.** The constitution is largely obeyed and mechanically enforced (architecture-hardening tests, deny-level complexity lints, single-source helpers verified). Confirmed violations worth fixing: `rumoca-eval-solve` (Tier 3) depends **upward** on `rumoca-solver` (Tier 5) and hosts a full sim driver — exactly the bundled-solver drift SPEC_0031 warns about; **dummy-derivative elimination lives inside `phase-solve`** and mutates DAE math after finalization (SPEC_0007 placement violation with real consequences: the solver integrates a different DAE than the "externally stable" artifact other consumers see); connection equality generation **silently caps mismatched connector array sizes** (SPEC_0008-prohibited recovery); `phase-solve`'s `LowerError` is uncoded/span-less; two ACCEPTED specs share the ID `SPEC_0032`; SPEC_0008 sanctions `panic!` for invariants while CI bans it.

**Validation reality.** Baseline: parse 566 → flatten 565 → DAE 545 → Solve 446 → sim-ok 207 (36.6%); OMC old backend simulates 519/566 of the same set; of 202 trace-compared models 93.1% are acceptable (70.8% high-agreement). Blind spots: the ModelicaTest semantic gate runs exactly **one** model; the assertion-corpus gate simulates with `--stop-time 0.0` (no assertion ever checked over a trajectory); no fuzz/property tests anywhere in 53 crates; the event-heavy cohort (Friction, CoupledClutches, Backlash) is excluded by the 45 s watchdog, masking event-semantics evidence; the trace metric's 1e-12 normalization floor misclassifies near-zero reference channels, polluting the headline bad/severe numbers; cross-backend MSL suites validate against rumoca's own simulator, not OMC. The current working-tree snapshot (sim 167/414, 11.1% bad channels) trails the promoted baseline — consistent with mid-refactor churn from the concurrent branch work.

**Tooling.** The LSP is a real differentiator but has two blockers before demoing on real MSL files: helpers byte-slice lines at UTF-16 column offsets and **panic on any non-ASCII character** (°, Ω, umlauts are everywhere in MSL) — despite `rumoca-lsp-position` existing to prevent exactly this; and the editor path hardcodes default fmt/lint options, bypassing the SPEC_0018 config hierarchy the CLI honors. Hover runs an uncached strict compile to DAE under the session write lock. The linter's missing-documentation rule inverts Modelica's description-string placement (checks the line *before* the declaration) and would flag nearly all correctly-documented code. No formatter idempotence test exists.

---

## 8. OpenModelica and the field: where rumoca stands

All facts sourced; URLs in Appendix C.

**OpenModelica old backend (the incumbent):** MSL 4.0.0 coverage 503/514 simulate (97.9%), 479/514 verify (93.2%) on the 2026-07-21 nightly. Mature, but scalarizing: documented compile-time pain on large/array models (superlinear translation growth — 330 s vs Dymola's 15 s at N=1280 on the ThermalFluid benchmark; trac tickets and forum threads spanning years; "code generation OK up to ~200 grid components" on ScalableTestGrids), and its per-solver C runtime carries decades of accumulated event-code paths.

**OpenModelica new backend (the challenger rumoca must beat to the future):** architecture is Pseudo-Array Causalization (Modelica 2023) — map array elements to scalar indices, run *scalar* matching/sorting, then reconstruct array equations and for-loops. Published wins: 1–2 orders of magnitude build time, 2–3× runtime, 3.2M-equation models. But as of mid-2026 it is explicitly experimental and not default: **MSL 4.0.0 simulation 219/514 (42.6%), verification 150/514 (29.2%)**; ~30% success on the whole library testsuite (Casella, Feb 2026 keynote); 80 open NB-labeled issues including "NB is less efficient than the OB on large models without arrays" (Jul 2026), missing dedicated linear-system handling, Jacobian failures on the power-grid libraries it was funded for, and clocked-model failures. Dynamic index reduction and partial-array index reduction are open issues. Funded (RTE contract) through June 2027.

**Assessment vs rumoca:** rumoca's tensor approach is architecturally *stronger* than PAC — compact domains with machine-checkable affine stride proofs carried in the IR, versus PAC's internally-scalarized matching graph with post-hoc reconstruction; and rumoca has tensor-preserving AD, a kernel inventory report, and a GPU path the NB lacks entirely. But PAC has something rumoca does not: **the structural phase itself is array-aware in production.** Rumoca's structural phase is where its structure currently dies (§5). Meanwhile rumoca's *correctness* position (37% sim) sits below the NB (42.6%) today, though rumoca's trace-parity honesty is stricter than OMC's verification criteria and rumoca's failures are concentrated in fixable numerics plumbing (§7) rather than missing symbolic machinery.

**MARCO:** validates the whole tensor-native thesis (14.8M equations compiled in 10 s, MLIR/LLVM direct, array-aware matching proven NP-complete with an effective polynomial heuristic in ACM TOMS 2023) — but it is a v0.0.1 prototype using OMC as its frontend, with no library/package support and no event handling. It is a proof of concept, not a competitor for users. Rumoca should mine its matching literature, not fear its adoption.

**ModelingToolkit/JuliaSim/Dyad:** commercially energetic but has *left the Modelica language* — Dyad is a new source-available language with LLM-assisted migration as the Modelica story; MTK's own docs acknowledge array scalarization friction; memory-bound around 25k equations in published comparisons; tens of seconds of JIT latency even on small models. Rumoca's "keep Modelica as the frontend, export to JAX/CasADi/Julia" positioning is the direct counter, and it is a real differentiator against both Dyad (language switch) and OMC (no such export surface).

**Dymola:** the retention drivers are diagnostics (per-system nonlinear breakdowns, initialization debugging), robust initialization, vendor libraries, and FMI maturity. Two of those four (diagnostics quality, initialization robustness) are exactly rumoca's P1 items — and rumoca's structured, spanned, coded diagnostics foundation is *better* placed than OMC's to close that gap.

### How rumoca wins

The window: **OpenModelica cannot yet offer both array-preservation and library robustness in one backend, and is publicly ~18 months from trying.** MARCO won't productize; Julia left the language; Dymola is closed and expensive. The winning position is being the first tool where users don't choose between the two:

1. **Trust first (P0+P1):** convert every silent-wrongness channel into a loud error, then burn down the numerics buckets with scaling/homotopy/coupled-event-Newton. Target: sim-ok from 37% → 60%+ on the fixable buckets (init 32 + event 31 + solver 54 + a large share of structural.algebraic 80 are plumbing, not missing theory), then chase the OMC old backend's 90%+ as the NB never has. The existing ratcheted parity harness makes every step verifiable — *keep publishing it*; a public per-library coverage dashboard (mirroring libraries.openmodelica.org) turns rumoca's validation honesty into a marketing asset.
2. **Win the array race with proofs, not heuristics (P3):** finish family-as-owner through the structural phase and demonstrate O(1)-in-N compile on ScalableTestSuite classics (CascadedFirstOrder, OneDHeatTransfer, TransmissionLine) against both OMC backends. Rumoca's stride-proof IR + WGSL/MLIR kernels + tensor AD is a *better* endpoint than PAC; the missing piece is the middle of its own pipeline.
3. **Own the export/embedding surface OMC structurally cannot:** stable DAE artifact + CasADi/JAX/Julia/SymPy + FMI 2/3 + eFMI/GALEC + WASM-in-browser + LSP. This is the "Modelica as a universal algebraic frontend" identity (arXiv:2606.14998) — no competitor combines it, and it monetizes even before full MSL parity (optimization, ML-in-the-loop, embedded/SIL users care about the DAE contract, not Fluid).
4. **Match Dymola where it's paid:** initialization diagnostics. Rumoca already names the worst residual row and target variable on projection failure; extending that to Dymola-grade nonlinear-system breakdowns (which iteration variables, which equations, what to fix) on top of coded/spanned errors is a tractable, high-perceived-value feature OMC has never shipped well.

**Suggested headline KPIs to steer by:** MSL sim-ok % (566-model fixed denominator) vs both OMC backends; trace-verified % at OMC's own tolerance criteria; ScalableTestSuite compile-time exponent vs N (target: flat where NB/PAC is flat, and flat where NB is *not* — non-regular families); tensor kernel inventory coverage % of MSL equations; time-to-first-diagnostic in the LSP on MSL.

## 9. Remediation roadmap

Ordered by leverage; each item cites the finding(s) in Appendix A that specify file-level fixes.

Status audited against the current branch tree on 2026-07-24:

- [x] **Resolved** — the complete roadmap outcome is implemented and covered by
  focused regression evidence.
- [ ] **Partial** — useful work landed, but at least one stated outcome remains
  open.
- [ ] **Open** — the roadmap outcome has not been implemented.

**P0 — Stop silent wrongness (days-to-weeks each, immediate trust payoff).**

- [x] **Resolved:** derivative-slot lowering is explicit, and a surviving
  general-position `der()` is a spanned Solve-lowering error.
- [x] **Resolved:** structural state demotion validates that no live derivative
  reference targets a variable removed from the state partition.
- [x] **Resolved:** elimination rejects a rewrite or removal that would leave
  authoritative cheapened-family placeholder rows live without their compact
  owner.
- [x] **Resolved:** `delay()` and `terminal()` have explicit DAE/Solve metadata
  and runtime semantics, including accepted-history interpolation, bounded
  retention, variable-delay discontinuity roots, and final-event terminal
  activation.
- [x] **Resolved:** `Connections.rooted()` is evaluated from branch-relative
  depth in the selected connection graph.
- [x] **Resolved:** Flat connection expansion implements two-port and general
  MLS stream mixing plus flow-directed `actualStream()`; later IR boundaries
  reject leaked stream operators.
- [x] **Resolved:** runtime, compile-time, interpreter, and Cranelift paths
  share three-way `sign()` and IEEE division behavior through compiler-owned
  builtin helpers.
- [x] **Resolved:** builtin semantic ownership lives in `rumoca-core`; one
  policy-driven AST scalar interpreter owns integer, Real, and Boolean syntax
  dispatch for typecheck, instantiation, and structural shape evaluation, with
  shared function-statement control flow. Flat phase scalar entry points are
  thin typed adapters over its single `Value` interpreter, including scoped
  lookup, symbolic array shape, enum aliases, and named function arguments.
- [x] **Resolved:** parser lowering preserves skipped output slots and
  provenance, declaration-before-type dimension order, and validated decoded
  string escapes.
- [x] **Resolved:** DAE-level templates return render errors instead of
  fabricating `0.0` for missing or non-iterable equation forms.
- [x] **Resolved:** connection equality and flow generation reject incompatible
  resolved array cardinalities instead of truncating them.

**P1 — Numerics robustness = MSL sim rate (the biggest single lever).**

- [x] **Resolved:** numeric `nominal` and `start` metadata scale initialization
  and algebraic Newton rows/columns, causal seeding, and per-variable BDF/RK45
  absolute tolerances.
- [x] **Resolved:** initialization lowers the exact homotopy blend and runs
  adaptive, rollback-safe lambda continuation through the actual system.
- [x] **Resolved:** event restart escalates a failed Picard/Gauss-Seidel pass to
  a scaled, line-searched coupled Newton solve without corrupting caller state
  on failure.
- [x] **Resolved:** event boundary probes derive from solver tolerance with an
  adjacent-representable fallback rather than a time-growing fixed epsilon.
- [x] **Resolved:** RK45 root localization uses the DOPRI5 quartic dense
  extension, selects the earliest crossing, and clusters simultaneous roots.
- [x] **Resolved:** the default BDF driver enforces `max_wall_seconds` throughout
  stepping and event processing and reports a typed timeout.
- [x] **Resolved:** periodic events are scheduled on demand with no 200,000-tick
  truncation and with coincident schedules merged.
- [x] **Resolved:** interactive BDF root handling reuses its model, runtime,
  problem, and solver allocation instead of leaking a new ODE problem.
- [x] **Resolved:** the post-remediation full-MSL gate was rerun without lowering
  the baseline: 546/566 compile, 224 simulate, and 197/566 meet the acceptable
  trace threshold.

**P2 — Cohort-blocking MLS gaps.**

- [ ] **Partial:** clocked same-tick dependency propagation has backend
  regressions; Solve schema 22 now associates clocked `previous()` history
  with its owning schedule and commits it only on that clock's tick, while the
  lowered activation predicate and scheduler share the same dimensionless
  tolerance formula. Exact rational base-clock lattices remain open.
- [x] **Resolved:** import paths begin at the top-level scope, encapsulated
  lookup exposes only local/imported and predefined names, and effective
  inherited-member entries participate before enclosing-scope lookup without
  arbitrarily resolving ambiguous multiple inheritance.
- [x] **Resolved:** production instanced typechecking covers compound
  expression/operator types, builtin and user-function arguments, algorithms,
  per-instance bindings, reachable component-class/base equations, and
  variability/causality validation.
- [x] **Resolved by explicit rejection:** identically declared expandable
  connectors continue through normal connection expansion, while any declared
  member union that would require MLS 9.1.3 augmentation now fails with spanned
  `EF020` before connection-set construction instead of connecting only the
  intersection or silently dropping the connection.
- [x] **Resolved by explicit rejection and honest status:** every MLS chapter
  17 operator remains rejected with `ER073`; `SM-001`/`SM-008` are now
  `NotImplemented`, and the syntactically validated `SM-002`..`SM-007`
  contracts are `Partial` rather than claiming executable state-machine
  support.
- [ ] **Open:** dynamic root-cause triage of the remaining
  Fluid/Batteries/Magnetic balance-failure cohort.

**P3 — Tensor-native completion (the strategic differentiator).**

- [x] **Resolved:** whole-array, slice, and regular connection equations acquire
  authoritative compact structured-family owners during Flat lowering.
- [ ] **Partial:** regular connection equations preserve a compact family, but
  component-array instantiation and its compatibility connection view remain
  linear in domain cardinality.
- [ ] **Partial:** structural matching carries translated-family descriptors
  when corner incidence proves the layout and invalidates them on scalar

- [x] **Resolved:** regular families lower directly to compact

  native affine loops rather than cloning one program per tuple.
- [ ] **Partial:** solver layout identity uses interned `VarName` keys, but the
  complete `(DefId, flat-offset)` migration and removal of rendered-name

- [x] **Resolved:** Solve schema 20 uses compact identity, diagonal, and
  sparse-COO mass matrices; Diffsol prepares the selected representation once.
- [x] **Resolved:** shipped MLIR and WGSL Solve templates consume the native
  family partition and reserve scalar fallback for unsupported rows.
- [x] **Resolved:** the MSL gate reports a stable, body-weighted
  tensor-preservation KPI with reason-coded scalar fallbacks.
- [x] **Resolved:** a compiler-linked ScalableTestSuite-style benchmark measures
  fresh-session, full-pipeline compile time for whole-array and cascaded

# Appendix A — Confirmed findings (173, adversarially verified)

_Each finding survived an independent refutation attempt. Severities reflect verifier corrections where applied. Line numbers are as-read from a concurrently-edited tree; locate by content if drifted._

### A.parse — Parsing & AST (12 confirmed)

#### [HIGH] Skipped output slots in output-expression-list silently dropped

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/expressions.rs:949` — MLS 11.2.1.5 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** MLS allows empty slots in output lists to skip function outputs, e.g. `(x, , z) := f(...)`. The OutputExpressionList conversion pushes only present expressions and records nothing for empty slots, so the AST for `(x, , z)` is identical to `(x, z)`. Positional correspondence between LHS targets and function outputs is unrecoverable by any later phase, producing misbound outputs or spurious arity errors on valid Modelica. The same loss occurs in convert_output_primary for tuple equations `(a, , c) = f(...)`.
- **Evidence:** if let Some(opt) = &ast.output_expression_list_opt { v.push(opt.expression.clone()); }
for expr in &ast.output_expression_list_list {
    if let Some(opt) = &expr.output_expression_list_opt0 {
        v.push(opt.expression.clone());
    }
} // empty slots produce no placeholder — (x, , z) == (x, z) in the AST
- **Verifier note:** Grammar (modelica.par:1076) allows empty slots per MLS; conversion (expressions.rs:949-957) pushes only present exprs, so (x,,z)==(x,z), positions lost. Same in convert_output_primary tuple path. Downstream in lower/statements.rs relies on this: line 1396 exact-length check yields spurious arity error, line 1432 zips positionally, and the len==1 branch (1373) silently misbinds (, y) to output[0]. flatten .flatten() (ast_lower.rs:256) never reconstructs positions. Real MLS-conformance/correctness defect, not handled elsewhere. High is appropriate.
- **Remediation:** Push Expression::Empty { span } placeholders for absent slots so Statement::FunctionCall.outputs and Expression::Tuple preserve arity/positions; teach downstream output binding to treat Empty as 'skip'.

#### [HIGH] Type-level and declaration-level array dimensions concatenated in wrong order

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/elements.rs:568` — MLS 4.4.2, 10.1 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** MLS 4.4.2/10.1 defines `T[k] x[n]` as equivalent to `T x[n, k]`: dimensions written after the component name come first, type-level dimensions are appended. process_single_component seeds shape/shape_expr with ctx.type_level_shape (the subscripts after the type specifier) and then appends declaration-level subscripts after them, yielding [k, n] instead of [n, k]. `Real[3] x[2]` becomes a 3x2 array instead of 2x3, producing wrong shapes (and wrong simulation results or spurious shape errors) for this valid form. Note the alias path (type_dims in phase-instantiate dims.rs:156) gets the order right, making the two paths inconsistent.
- **Evidence:** // ctx.type_level_shape/-expr copied first (lines 544-545), then:
if let Some(decl_opt) = &c.declaration.declaration_opt {
    for subscript in &decl_opt.array_subscripts.subscripts {
        value.shape_expr.push(subscript.clone());
        if let Some(dim) = try_extract_dimension(subscript) { value.shape.push(dim); }
    }
}
- **Verifier note:** Re-read elements.rs: process_single_component seeds value.shape/shape_expr from ctx.type_level_shape (class-name subscripts, extract_type_level_shape reads ComponentClauseOpt.array_subscripts) then appends declaration-level (component-name) subscripts, yielding [k,n]. MLS 10.1 requires component-name dims first, class-name last: Real[3] x[2] == Real x[2,3] (shape [2,3]), but code yields [3,2]. Confirmed via alias path dims.rs:156-157 which correctly appends type_dims after component dims, making the parser path inconsistent. No downstream reordering; comp.shape/shape_expr flow into instantiate dims directly. Bug only triggers on dual-subscript form (uncommon but valid) -> wrong shapes/results. High is defensible.
- **Remediation:** Build shape/shape_expr as declaration subscripts first, then append type-level subscripts, matching MLS `T[k] x[n]` == `T x[n, k]`; add a parser test for `Real[3] x[2]`.

#### [MEDIUM] Chained exponentiation parsed then silently truncated to first exponent

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/expressions.rs:1262` — MLS A.2.7, 3.4 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** modelica.par extends the MLS factor rule to a repetition (`factor : primary { ('^'|'.^') primary }`), but the Factor conversion only reads factor_list[0]. Input `2^3^2` — which MLS forbids (factor allows at most one exponent; parenthesization is required) — is accepted and silently becomes `2^3`, dropping `^2` with no diagnostic. The tool neither rejects the illegal input (as MLS requires) nor evaluates what the user wrote.
- **Evidence:** let rhs = ast.factor_list[0].primary.clone();
Ok(rumoca_ir_ast::Expression::Binary { op, span: binary_span(&ast.primary, &rhs), lhs: Arc::new(ast.primary.clone()), rhs: Arc::new(rhs) })
// factor_list[1..] discarded; grammar rule: factor : primary { ( '^' | '.^' ) primary }
- **Verifier correction:** Bug is real but only reachable via MLS-illegal input (chained `^`), which conforming Modelica never contains; downgrade high->medium since no valid model is miscompiled.
- **Verifier note:** modelica.par:936 uses repetition `primary { ('^'|'.^') primary }` while its own MLS comment (934) shows the optional `[ ... ]` form. Generated parser yields Vec factor_list; expressions.rs:1252-1269 reads only factor_list[0], discarding factor_list[1..]. Grep shows factor_list used in only these 3 non-generated sites — no length check anywhere. So `2^3^2` is accepted and silently becomes `2^3` with no diagnostic. Claim confirmed. Severity reduced: only illegal input triggers it, so no conforming model produces wrong results, though silent truncation of illegal input (vs. MLS-required rejection) is a genuine defect.
- **Remediation:** Either restore the MLS grammar shape `primary [ ('^'|'.^') primary ]`, or keep the repetition and emit a parse error when factor_list.len() > 1 (non-associative operator diagnostic).

#### [MEDIUM] String escape sequences never decoded (LEX-013)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/components.rs:11` — MLS 2.4.4 · SPEC_0022 LEX-013 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** String token conversion strips only the surrounding quotes; S-ESCAPE sequences (\" \\ \n \t \r \a \b \f \v \?) are left as raw two-character source text, and no later phase unescapes them (workspace grep finds unescape only in XML/OMC tooling). A Modelica string "a\nb" is therefore a 4-character value in the IR instead of 3 with a newline, corrupting String constants, assert messages, and any String comparison/concatenation semantics. Additionally the lexer regex `\\[\s\S]` accepts any escape including \<newline> line continuation, a superset of the MLS S-ESCAPE set.
- **Evidence:** let mut tok: rumoca_core::Token = ast.string.clone().into();
// remove quotes from string text (with bounds check for malformed input)
if tok.text.len() >= 2 {
    tok.text = std::sync::Arc::from(&tok.text[1..tok.text.len() - 1]);
} // no escape decoding anywhere downstream
- **Verifier note:** Re-read components.rs: String->Token conversion only strips outer quotes (text[1..len-1]), no escape decoding. Lexer regex at modelica_parser.rs:196 is `"([^"\\]|\\[\s\S])*"` — accepts any post-backslash char incl. newline continuation, superset of MLS S-ESCAPE. Grepped crates/ for unescape/decode-escape across parse/core/instantiate/codegen: only hits are OMC/XML tooling (omc_session.rs, xml_escape), never the source-string->IR path. SPEC_0022 line 344 explicitly requires LEX-013 (§2.4.4 escape sequences). So "a\nb" yields a 4-char IR value not 3. Genuine, unhandled MLS violation; medium fits (bounded to strings using escapes).
- **Remediation:** Decode S-ESCAPE sequences here (rejecting escapes outside the MLS set), and re-escape in Display/to_modelica; tighten the STRING lexer regex to the MLS escape set.

#### [MEDIUM] Equation/statement descriptions and annotations discarded at parse

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/equations.rs:116` — MLS 18.1, A.2.6 · SPEC_0007 Stage 1 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** The grammar parses `some_equation : some_equation_option description` and `statement : statement_option description`, but TryFrom<&SomeEquation>/<&Statement> ignore ast.description entirely, and the Equation/Statement enums in rumoca-ir-ast have no description or annotation fields. Every `connect(a,b) annotation(Line(...))` in MSL loses its annotation, and equation description strings vanish. SPEC_0007 designates the AST as the IR for formatters/doc generators ("needs syntax + spans"), so this is unrecoverable data loss for the AST's own declared consumers, and blocks ANN-013-style annotation processing on equations.
- **Evidence:** fn try_from(ast: &modelica_grammar_trait::SomeEquation) -> ... {
    match &ast.some_equation_option { ... } // ast.description never read
}
// nodes.rs Equation enum: Simple { lhs, rhs } — no description/annotation/span fields
- **Verifier note:** Verified all three claims. Grammar: `SomeEquation` (trait line 5034) and `Statement` (5069) each carry `pub description: Description`, and `Description` holds `description_string` plus `description_opt` = `AnnotationClause` (3305-3317). TryFrom<&SomeEquation> (equations.rs 116-166) and TryFrom<&Statement> (210-310) never read `ast.description`. IR enums `Equation` (nodes.rs 779) and `Statement` (1389) have no description/annotation/span fields. Class specifiers DO preserve description_string, so equations/statements are the outlier. SPEC_0007 Stage 1 designates AST as "concrete syntax with comments and spans" for formatters/doc generators, so this is a real contract gap. Not handled elsewhere; connect annotations vanish. Bounded (no simulation impact) -> medium holds.
- **Remediation:** Add description: Vec<Token> and annotation: Vec<Expression> (plus a span) to Equation and Statement variants or wrap them in a spanned struct; populate from ast.description.

#### [MEDIUM] Conversion pipeline deep-clones whole subtrees at every nesting level; rewrite transformer clones every Arc node

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/definitions.rs:608` — SPEC_0021 · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** Because parol actions receive &refs, every TryFrom level clones its children wholesale: Composition::try_from clones the ElementList maps, convert_standard_class_specifier clones the entire composition (extends, imports, classes, equations, components), and StoredDefinition conversion clones each class again — so a package nested d levels deep is deep-copied O(d) times, on top of the copy retained inside the generated grammar-trait tree. Separately, ir-ast's ExpressionTransformer::walk_expression does Arc::new(self.transform_expression((*arc).clone())) unconditionally, discarding Arc sharing and reallocating every node on every rewrite pass even when unchanged. Both are compile-time scaling costs for MSL-sized libraries.
- **Evidence:** let class_def = rumoca_ir_ast::ClassDef { ..., extends: spec.composition.extends.clone(), imports: spec.composition.imports.clone(), classes: spec.composition.classes.clone(), equations: spec.composition.equations.clone(), ... components: spec.composition.components.clone(), ... };
// rewrite.rs:35: start: Arc::new(self.transform_expression((*start).clone())),
- **Verifier note:** Both evidence quotes verified verbatim. definitions.rs convert_standard_class_specifier (lines 600-639) clones the whole composition (extends/imports/classes/equations/components) per level; extends variant (line 667) does the same plus a Vec clone. rewrite.rs walk_expression (lines 35-119) does Arc::new(self.transform_expression((*x).clone())) unconditionally, dropping Arc sharing and reallocating every node each pass regardless of change. Characterization of O(d) repeated deep-copies for nested packages is plausible given bottom-up TryFrom cloning already-converted child classes. Not mitigated elsewhere. Purely compile-time cost, no simulation impact — medium is defensible (borderline low).
- **Remediation:** Take ownership out of grammar-trait nodes (std::mem::take on nt_type payloads) or convert via consuming From; in ExpressionTransformer use Arc::try_unwrap/Arc::make_mut and return the original Arc when a child is unchanged.

#### [MEDIUM] Every token owns a String copy of the source file path

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-core/src/ir_primitives.rs:299` — SPEC_0021 · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** Location.file_name is a String, and ParserToken::try_from allocates it per token via to_string_lossy().to_string() (rumoca-phase-parse/src/lib.rs:194). Every Token clone — and the conversion pipeline clones tokens pervasively — reallocates the full path string. For MSL-scale parsing (millions of tokens) this is a dominant, avoidable allocation stream and memory multiplier; it also makes Location comparisons/Span derivation string-based instead of using the existing interned SourceId.
- **Evidence:** pub struct Location { pub start_line: u32, ..., pub file_name: String }
// lib.rs:194: file_name: value.location.file_name.to_string_lossy().to_string(), — executed once per token
- **Verifier note:** Verified: ir_primitives.rs:299 Location.file_name is String; Location derives Clone/Eq. parse/lib.rs:194 allocates it per token via to_string_lossy().to_string(). Token embeds Location and is retained pervasively in the AST (nodes.rs: many Token/Vec<Token> fields) and cloned throughout the pipeline (~138 clone sites), so each clone reallocates the full path. An interned SourceId (u64, from_source_name) and SourceMap already exist, making the recommendation feasible. Real, avoidable allocation stream; no correctness impact, so medium (bounded efficiency) is correct.
- **Remediation:** Store SourceId (or Arc<str> interned once per file) in Location instead of String; derive display names through the SourceMap already present on ClassTree.

#### [MEDIUM] Duplicate class definitions silently overwritten (components are checked, classes are not)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/definitions.rs:195` — MLS 4.4 · SPEC_0022 DECL-001 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** DECL-001 requires element names to be unique in a class. Duplicate component declarations get a precise parse-time error, but duplicate class names do not: merge_classes and the public-section branch use IndexMap::insert/extend, process_class_definition and process_replaceable_element insert without checking def.classes, and StoredDefinition::try_from inserts top-level classes unchecked. `package P model A ... end A; model A ... end A; end P;` compiles with the second A silently replacing the first — an accepted-invalid model that can silently change simulation structure.
- **Evidence:** fn merge_classes(target: &mut IndexMap<String, ClassDef>, source: ..., set_protected: bool) {
    for (name, mut class) in source { ... target.insert(name, class); } // no duplicate check
}
// contrast merge_components: returns Err("Duplicate declaration of '{}'")
- **Verifier note:** Re-read definitions.rs and elements.rs. merge_classes (204), public-branch classes.extend (1059), StoredDefinition insert (326), process_class_definition (482), and process_replaceable_element (655) all insert classes with no duplicate check, while merge_components (176) and check_duplicate_component (222) reject duplicate components. Resolve-phase check_duplicate_names (semantic_checks/mod.rs:376) only iterates components and can't detect class-class dupes since the IndexMap already deduped; duplicate_definition() is dead code. DECL-001/MLS §4.4 treats classes as elements needing unique names, so this is a genuine accept-invalid gap. Bounded (malformed input only) → medium.
- **Remediation:** Mirror the component duplicate check for classes at all four insertion sites (top-level, element list, public/protected merge, replaceable), reporting both source lines.

#### [MEDIUM] Equation/Statement nodes carry no spans; FieldAccess drops the field token

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-ir-ast/src/nodes.rs:779` — SPEC_0007 Stage 1 · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** SPEC_0007 Stage 1 states "Every node carries a source Span", but the Equation and Statement enums have no span field — get_location() falls back to the first token of a sub-expression (a When equation points at its condition; Equation::Empty returns None), so diagnostics cannot highlight full equation extents or keywords. Expression::FieldAccess stores field: String, discarding the identifier token and its span, and ArrayIndex/ArrayComprehension spans reuse only the base/body span, excluding subscripts and for-indices, degrading diagnostic precision for exactly the constructs where subscript errors occur.
- **Evidence:** pub enum Equation { #[default] Empty, Simple { lhs: Expression, rhs: Expression }, ... } // no span/location field on any variant
FieldAccess { base: Arc<Expression>, field: String, span: Span } // field token lost
// expressions.rs:1086: span: base.span() for ArrayIndex (subscripts excluded)
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified every claim. nodes.rs:779 Equation and :1389 Statement enums have NO span field; get_location() (816-830, 1444) falls back to sub-expr tokens, returns None for Empty, and When points at cond. FieldAccess (961) stores field: String; parser (expressions.rs:1096) does field.ident.text.to_string(), so the ident's own location is not recoverable (only merged into overall span at :1094). ArrayIndex span=base.span() (expressions.rs:1087) excludes subscripts; ArrayComprehension span=base_expr.span() (:62) excludes for-indices. SPEC_0007 Stage 1 (spec line 60) explicitly states "Every node carries a source Span" — genuinely unmet. Impact is diagnostic-precision only (no correctness/MLS/sim impact), so medium (real but bounded) is correct.
- **Remediation:** Add a Span to Equation/Statement (wrap variants in a spanned struct), store the field ident as Token, and merge subscript/index spans into ArrayIndex/comprehension spans.

#### [LOW] Semantic material fabricated in parse phase: default start values, lossy shape cache, 'shape' modification hijack

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/elements.rs:112` — SPEC_0007 Stage 1 · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** SPEC_0007 Stage 1 requires the AST to represent source text, not semantics, yet the parser injects a default start expression (0.0/0/false) chosen by the syntactic type name (aliases get Empty, so `Real x;` and `MyReal x;` produce different ASTs for identical semantics), maintains Component.shape as a numeric cache that is rank-lossy for mixed dims (`Real x[2,n]` yields shape=[2], rank 1) — a hazard phase-instantiate explicitly documents it must not trust (dims.rs: "Do not trust parser/early fallback dimensions") — and process_named_arg special-cases any modification literally named `shape`, swallowing user record-field modifications named shape without storing them.
- **Evidence:** fn default_start_value(type_name: &str, ...) -> Expression { match type_name { "Real" => ...Terminal{text:"0.0"...} ... } }
...
"shape" => { value.shape = extract_shape(rhs)?; } // modification dropped from modifications map
- **Verifier correction:** All three facts hold, but downstream mitigations reduce impact below medium: shape rank-loss is compensated by shape_expr (dims.rs:128 explicitly distrusts comp.shape and prefers shape_expr); the alias start inconsistency is reconciled during instantiation (MLS 4.8 defaults); and the 'shape' modifier hijack is latent — no .mo fixture uses `shape=` and no builtin/record path exercises it. These are genuine SPEC_0007 Stage-1 contract deviations but produce no demonstrated wrong simulation output, so low rather than medium.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read elements.rs: default_start_value (L112-144) keys defaults on the literal type_name, aliases hit the Empty arm (L142), called at L539 with type_name.to_string(). shape built only from integer/Boolean subscripts via try_extract_dimension (L69-91,571-573), so [2,n]->[2]; shape_expr retains both. process_named_arg L1046-1048 intercepts "shape" and drops it from modifications. SPEC_0007 Stage 1 forbids semantics in AST. dims.rs:128 confirms the distrust comment. Facts all verified; impact is bounded/latent, hence severity lowered.
- **Remediation:** Remove parser-side start defaulting (apply MLS 4.8 defaults during instantiation), make shape purely derived (or drop it in favor of shape_expr + a resolved-dims table), and stop interpreting 'shape' as a builtin attribute name.

#### [LOW] Error recovery drops all non-class content and matches keywords case-insensitively

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/recovery.rs:207` — MLS 2.3 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** The fallback recovery path (used whenever the main parse fails) produces only a class-header skeleton: components, equations, imports and modifications are all discarded, so IDE features degrade to an outline on any single syntax error; multi-error reporting relies on up-to-32 full reparses with inserted semicolons. The recovery lexer also matches keywords with eq_ignore_ascii_case, violating case sensitivity (LEX-004): a legal component declaration `Model m;` is misrecovered as a class definition named m, corrupting the best-effort tree editors see.
- **Evidence:** fn token_text_eq(token: &LexToken, expected: &str) -> bool {
    token.kind == LexTokenKind::Ident && token.token.text.eq_ignore_ascii_case(expected)
}
// RecoveredClassBuilder::finish builds ClassDef { ..ClassDef::default() } — no components/equations preserved
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified recovery.rs: token_text_eq (L207) uses eq_ignore_ascii_case; parse_class_keyword/modifier loop (L428,472) use to_ascii_lowercase — genuinely case-insensitive, violating LEX-004 (SPEC_0022 L335, MLS §2.3). finish() (L251) uses ..ClassDef::default(), preserving only header + nested classes; components/equations/imports dropped. Traced `Model m;`: parse_class_header maps `Model`→keyword model, name m, no short-terminator, pushes spurious class m. MAX_SEMICOLON_RECOVERY_PASSES=32 confirmed in lib.rs. All claims accurate. Impact confined to error-recovery/IDE-outline path (valid code parses normally), so low severity is correct.
- **Remediation:** Make recovery keyword matching case-sensitive, and extend skeleton recovery to retain statement-level tokens (or re-parse unaffected class bodies individually) so a single error does not erase whole-file content.

#### [LOW] Bare-name element modifications dropped as Empty

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/expressions.rs:829` — MLS 18.2 · SPEC_0022 ANN-002 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** An element-modification consisting of only a name (legal, though deprecated per ANN-002, and common in annotations such as Dialog(group)) is converted to Expression::Empty, losing the name entirely. The code comments this as a deliberate stopgap pending a dedicated AST node, but it means annotation payloads and modification lists silently lose entries rather than preserving or diagnosing them.
- **Evidence:** // An element modification without a modification (a bare name,
// MLS §18.2) is dropped here: representing it as a ComponentReference
// corrupts modification consumers ... Preserving it needs a
// dedicated AST node (see ANN-002 blocker note).
None => Ok(empty_expr(target.span)),
- **Verifier note:** Re-read convert_element_modification (expressions.rs ~768-830). It builds `target` from the name, but when element_modification_opt is None it returns empty_expr(target.span), discarding the name entirely. The inline comment confirms this is a deliberate stopgap and that preservation needs a dedicated AST node (ANN-002). No other code path recovers the bare name. Behavior matches the claim. Impact is bounded to bare-name modifications, largely annotation/GUI payloads that don't affect simulation numerics, so low severity is correct.
