- **Remediation:** Add the dedicated bare-modification AST node (name + span, no value) and emit the ANN-002 deprecation warning instead of dropping.

### A.resolve — Name resolution & instantiation (10 confirmed)

#### [HIGH] Import paths resolved in lexical scope instead of top-level scope

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-resolve/src/extends.rs:303` — MLS 13.2.2 · SPEC_0022 PKG-005 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** MLS §13.2.2 states import lookup 'deviates from the normal lexical lookup by starting the lexical lookup of the first part of the name at the top-level'. resolve_import starts at the importing class's scope for all non-leading-dot imports, walking the full scope chain (including local members and enclosing classes). If any enclosing class declares a name matching the first segment of an import path (e.g. a nested package named like a top-level library), the import silently binds to the wrong target; it also accepts relative import paths that conforming tools reject.
- **Evidence:** let resolve_scope = if import.is_global_scope() {
    ScopeId(0) // Global scope
} else {
    scope
};
...
let mut current_def_id = self.scope_tree.lookup_excluding(scope, &first_path, None)?;
- **Verifier note:** Re-read extends.rs: for non-leading-dot imports resolve_scope=scope (importing class scope). resolve_import_path calls lookup_excluding(scope, first_path, None); lookup_excluding (scope.rs) walks the full lexical chain (members, imports, enclosing parents) up to global — so the first import segment is resolved lexically, not top-level-first. is_global_scope is true only for leading-dot imports, so the common form never forces global start. No other resolver does a top-level-first import pass. This deviates from MLS §13.2.1.1 (import names fully qualified, first id from top-level): it over-accepts relative imports and can mis-bind when an enclosing scope member shadows the first segment. Wrong binding requires a name collision (bounded), but the deviation is real and unhandled — high per MLS-violation rubric.
- **Remediation:** In resolve_import_path, always start the first-segment lookup at ScopeId::GLOBAL (restricted to top-level classes, not built“in variables), regardless of is_global_scope(); keep the leading-dot form as pure global lookup per MLS 5.3.3.
- **Resolution (2026-07-24):** Resolved. `resolve_import_path` now performs an
  exact local lookup in `ScopeId::GLOBAL` for every first segment, independent
  of the importing lexical scope. Regressions cover both an enclosing-name
  collision (which must still select the top-level package) and a formerly
  accepted relative import (which must now be rejected).

#### [HIGH] Encapsulated scopes fall through to the entire global scope

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-ir-ast/src/scope.rs:160` — MLS 5.3.1 · SPEC_0022 INST-034; SPEC_0002 lookup semantics · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** MLS §5.3.1 stops lookup at an encapsulated class except for predefined types/functions/operators. next_lookup_scope instead redirects encapsulated scopes to ScopeId::GLOBAL, where ALL top-level user classes are registered as members, so an encapsulated class can reference any top-level package (e.g. 'Modelica.Blocks.X') without an import. This accepts invalid models and makes the 'encapsulated' keyword semantically inert for top-level names; the existing test only covers non-top-level enclosing names.
- **Evidence:** fn next_lookup_scope(&self, scope_id: ScopeId, scope: &Scope) -> Option<ScopeId> {
    if scope.is_encapsulated() && scope_id != ScopeId::GLOBAL {
        Some(ScopeId::GLOBAL)
    } else {
        scope.parent
    }
}
- **Verifier note:** Re-read scope.rs: next_lookup_scope (l.160-166) redirects any encapsulated scope to ScopeId::GLOBAL, and lookup (l.79-102) returns any GLOBAL member with no builtin filter. registration.rs l.24 registers ALL top-level user classes as GLOBAL members; register_builtins (lib.rs l.364-382) also puts builtins there. So an encapsulated class sees top-level packages (e.g. Modelica.*) by simple name without import. This violates MLS §13.1/§5.3.1 (encapsulated sees only predefined types/functions/operators + imports) and the project's own SPEC_0002 ("must be found locally or via imports"). is_builtin (l.387) + builtin_count exist, so the recommended fix is feasible and confirms builtins were meant to be the only fall-through. Permissive (accepts invalid models) so not critical; MLS violation → high.
- **Remediation:** When hopping from an encapsulated scope to global, restrict the member search to builtin DefIds (Resolver::is_builtin already gives an O(1) range check; pass the builtin bound into ScopeTree or filter in the resolver's lookup wrapper).
- **Resolution (2026-07-24):** Resolved. `ScopeTree` now stores predefined
  members separately from arbitrary global members. At an encapsulated
  boundary lookup checks local members and imports, then only this predefined
  set and stops. Tests cover rejection of an unimported top-level package,
  acceptance through an explicit import, and continued visibility of
  predefined `Real`.

#### [HIGH] Conflicting duplicate inherited components silently merged when type_def_id matches

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-instantiate/src/inheritance.rs:1302` — MLS 7.1 · SPEC_0022 INST-006, DECL-001 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** MLS §7.1 requires duplicate elements from multiple extends to be identical, otherwise it is an error. components_are_compatible returns true whenever the two components' type_def_id matches — without comparing bindings, variability, causality, dimensions, or modifications. Since all Real-typed components share the builtin Real type_def_id, 'model M extends B1; extends B2;' where B1 declares 'Real x = 1' and B2 declares 'parameter Real x = 2' is silently accepted with B1's declaration kept, dropping B2's binding and variability instead of erroring. The model compiles with arbitrary (first-extends-wins) semantics.
- **Evidence:** // Check type compatibility via type_def_id (handles import aliases)
if let (Some(existing_type), Some(incoming_type)) = (existing.type_def_id, incoming.type_def_id)
    && existing_type == incoming_type
{
    return true;
}  // no comparison of binding/variability/causality/dims
- **Verifier note:** inheritance.rs:1301-1306 returns true whenever both components share type_def_id, before any variability/causality/binding check. resolve/contents.rs:126-130 sets type_def_id for every component, and resolve/lib.rs:364-382 registers builtin Real as a single DefId. So all Real components share one type_def_id; two extends declaring `Real x=1` vs `parameter Real x=2` hit the fast path and are deemed compatible, keeping first-extends-wins and dropping B2's variability/binding. The fallback string path (1312-1314) checks only type_name+variability+causality (not bindings) and is never reached anyway. This silently accepts an MLS §7.1-illegal model with arbitrary semantics. High is apt: manifests only on already-invalid input (missed diagnostic + arbitrary pick), not on valid models.
- **Remediation:** Make the type_def_id fast path require additionally variability_eq, causality_eq, equal shape/shape_expr, equal bindings and modifications (the same fields the string-comparison path partially checks); only the same source def_id (true diamond) may skip the deep comparison.
- **Remediation status (2026-07-24): resolved on the current branch.** Only identical source `DefId`s retain the diamond fast path. Distinct declarations now compare resolved/rendered type, variability, causality, connection prefix, inner/outer/final/replaceable/redeclare prefixes, binding and start semantics, shapes, conditions, annotations, source and effective modifications, constraints, final/each metadata, structural status, and descriptions without comparing source spans. Focused tests pin conflicting bindings and variability for a shared `Real` type and preserve the true-diamond case.

#### [HIGH] String-keyed semantic identity pervasive after resolution (SPEC_0001 violation)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-instantiate/src/lib.rs:271` — MLS 5.6 · SPEC_0001 Semantic Identity Keys; SPEC_0002 PROHIBITED list · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** SPEC_0001 prohibits hashing rendered names for semantic identity after resolution, yet instantiate is built on it: known_int_params is FxHashMap<String,i64> keyed by rendered flat paths ('cellData.nRC'); overlay.outer_prefix_to_inner / inner_outer_to_parent_inner map rendered flat path to rendered flat path; resolve_inner_outer_type_name and resolve_type_name_in_source_scope re-derive type identity by joining scope prefixes and probing tree.name_map (a String→DefId map that resolve exports past the phase boundary at lib.rs:499, exactly what SPEC_0001's exception forbids); find_class_in_tree, is_type_subtype, and redeclare constraint resolution all navigate by qualified-name strings.
- **Evidence:** known_int_params: rustc_hash::FxHashMap<String, i64>,
...
self.known_int_params.insert(format!("{scope_prefix}.{k}"), *v);
...
(lib.rs:1189) target_map.insert(outer_path, inner_path); // flat-string → flat-string
(resolve lib.rs:499) tree.name_map = self.name_to_def.clone(); // String-keyed map escapes resolve
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified every cited construct. name_map is AstIndexMap<String,DefId> (ir-ast/lib.rs:111), exported by resolve at lib.rs:499 and used downstream by instantiate — a raw-String-keyed name→DefId map that escapes the resolution phase, exactly what SPEC_0001's exception forbids ("structured keys, not raw string keys"; "must not escape the resolution phase"). known_int_params is FxHashMap<String,i64> keyed by format!("{scope_prefix}.{k}") (lib.rs:271/407). Inner/outer overlay maps key to_flat_string→to_flat_string (lib.rs:1190,1337). resolve_type_name_in_source_scope rebuilds identity via format!("{prefix}.{type_name}") + name_map probe (1254-1268) — precisely SPEC_0002's PROHIBITED "rebuild semantic identity by formatting names." Genuine hard-rule spec violation, pervasive. No wrong sim results demonstrated (keeps it below critical), but pervasive Hard-rule breach warrants high.
- **Remediation:** Carry DefIds through instantiation: key known_int_params by (instance path as structured QualifiedName or instance id), key inner/outer redirection by instance identity, and replace name_map probing in resolve_inner_outer_type_name with the already-resolved comp.type_def_id (falling back to a scope-tree query, not string prefix joining).

#### [MEDIUM] Subtype/extends caches and visited sets keyed by short class name

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-instantiate/src/inheritance.rs:980` — MLS 7.3.2 · SPEC_0001 (rendered names are not identity) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** class_extends_cached keys its SubtypeCache by the class's SHORT name ('class.name.text'), not the qualified name or DefId. MSL reuses short names heavily (BaseProperties, ThermodynamicState, PartialMedium variants), so within one is_type_subtype query whose recursion touches two distinct classes sharing a short name, the first result poisons the second, yielding wrong subtype verdicts (wrongly rejected or wrongly accepted redeclarations). Similarly, is_type_subtype_cached's 'subtype == supertype' raw string equality can equate different relative spellings, and find_member_type_in_class's visited set uses short names, which can prematurely terminate BFS and fail to find a member in a valid model.
- **Evidence:** let class_name = class.name.text.to_string();
...
let cache_key = (class_name.clone(), base_name.to_string());
if let Some(&result) = cache.get(&cache_key) { return result; }
(type_lookup.rs:132) let bc_name = bc.name.text.as_ref(); if visited.contains(&bc_name) { continue; }
- **Verifier note:** Re-read inheritance.rs and type_lookup.rs. SubtypeCache (line 54) is IndexMap<(String,String),bool>; class_extends_cached (l.974/980) keys by class.name.text — a Token holding the declared leaf/short name — plus base_name. Recursion fixes base_name and varies only the short name, so two distinct classes sharing a short name in one query's extends DAG collide and the first verdict poisons the second. find_member_type_in_class BFS visited set (type_lookup.rs:132) uses bc.name.text likewise, prematurely skipping a same-named distinct base. ClassDef.def_id (nodes.rs:412) is available, so the DefId-key fix is sound. External callers use fresh-cache wrappers, so damage is within-query; manifestation needs diamond/duplicate-leaf-name inheritance with divergent answers — real but bounded. Medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Key SubtypeCache by (DefId, DefId) — both endpoints have DefIds available via class.def_id and target_base_def_id; use DefId sets for BFS visited tracking; delete the raw 'subtype == supertype' equality in favor of type_names_match/DefId comparison.

#### [MEDIUM] Nominal-plus-sibling subtyping instead of MLS structural subtype for redeclarations

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-instantiate/src/inheritance.rs:646` — MLS 6.4, 7.3.2 · SPEC_0022 TYPE-001, INST-044 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** MLS §6.4 defines subtyping structurally (interface containment); §7.3.2 requires a redeclared type to be a subtype of the constraining type. is_type_subtype_cached accepts (a) exact/nominal extends chains and (b) 'sibling types' sharing a common base gated by members_plug_compatible. This both under-approximates (a genuinely plug-compatible type that neither extends the constraint nor shares a base is rejected — valid models fail) and over-approximates in the sibling direction relative to the constraining-type containment rule (a sibling passing the member-wise check need not contain every element of the constraining type if plug_compat's comparator is looser than §6.5 for protected/attribute details). The code comment itself frames siblinghood as an MSL workaround.
- **Evidence:** // Check for sibling types: both extend the same base class.
// This supports replaceable component redeclarations where both
// types share a common base (e.g., CellRCStack and CellStack both
// extend BaseCellStack). ...
types_share_common_base(tree, subtype_class, supertype_class, cache)
    && crate::plug_compat::members_plug_compatible(tree, subtype_class, supertype_class)
- **Verifier note:** Re-read inheritance.rs: is_type_subtype_cached accepts nominal extends OR (types_share_common_base && members_plug_compatible), and is the hard gate at lines 409/506 that emits redeclare_constraint_violation. plug_compat.rs:80 members_plug_compatible deliberately skips component-type comparison and protected/attribute details, only checking public-member causality/connection/condition/inner/outer — genuinely looser than §6.4/§6.5 containment (over-approx). Nominal+sibling gating rejects a pure structural subtype lacking a shared base (under-approx). Spec TYPE-001/INST-044 require MLS structural subtyping; not handled elsewhere. Both deviations real; medium (bounded to redeclaration edge cases) stands.
- **Remediation:** Drop the shared-base gate and implement §6.4 directly: subtype holds iff every public element of the constraining type exists in the candidate with a compatible interface (members_plug_compatible is most of this already — promote it to the primary check against the CONSTRAINING type, keeping the nominal chain as a fast path).

#### [MEDIUM] Unevaluable conditional-component conditions silently keep the component

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-instantiate/src/lib.rs:1117` — MLS 4.4.5 · SPEC_0022 INST-016, INST-053 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** MLS §4.4.5/INST-016 requires the condition of a conditional component to be an evaluable Boolean parameter expression. mark_disabled_component_if_needed disables the component only when evaluate_component_condition returns Some(false); when the evaluator cannot decide (None) the component is silently instantiated. A model whose condition rumoca fails to fold (but another tool evaluates to false) gets extra variables/equations and a wrong or unbalanced flat model, with no diagnostic pointing at the unevaluated condition.
- **Evidence:** // If the condition cannot be evaluated here, keep component instantiated.
let condition_is_false = comp.condition.as_ref().is_some_and(|cond| {
    ...
    evaluate_component_condition(&eval_ctx, cond) == Some(false)
});
if !condition_is_false { return false; }
- **Verifier note:** Re-read mark_disabled_component_if_needed (lib.rs ~1117): uses is_some_and(... == Some(false)), so evaluate_component_condition returning None yields condition_is_false=false and the component is instantiated. evaluate_component_condition (eval_instantiate/mod.rs:121) genuinely returns None for depth-exceeded and unresolvable refs. Call site (component_loop.rs:39) emits no diagnostic on the false return. No warning/error path found via grep. Project spec SPEC_0022 INST-016 requires "Condition expression must be evaluable Boolean scalar," so silently defaulting to enabled with no diagnostic is a real deviation. Bounded to conditions rumoca fails to fold -> medium.
- **Remediation:** Distinguish Some(true)/Some(false)/None: on None, emit a hard error (or at minimum a warning diagnostic recording the unevaluable condition) per INST-016 rather than defaulting to enabled.

#### [MEDIUM] equalityConstraint output size defaults to hardcoded 3

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-instantiate/src/inheritance.rs:1204` — MLS 9.4.1 · SPEC_0022 CONN-024, CONN-025 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** equality_constraint_output_size returns Some(3) when the equalityConstraint function is found but its output component is not (e.g. the output is inherited from a base function, which the local scan over eq_func.components misses). The overconstrained-connector equation count then silently assumes the MultiBody Orientation case; any overconstrained type whose constraint size differs (or whose function inherits its output) gets a wrong residual count, corrupting the global balance check and the generated connection equations.
- **Evidence:** // If we found the function but no output component, default to 3
// (common case for Orientation's equalityConstraint returning Real[3])
Some(3)
- **Verifier note:** Re-read inheritance.rs:1185-1204: the function scans only local eq_func.components for an Output causality; on miss it returns hardcoded Some(3) with a comment admitting it's the Orientation guess. Sole caller (lib.rs:1918) feeds this straight into overconstrained_eq_size with no correction. get_effective_components exists and is used elsewhere, so inherited outputs are resolvable but not resolved here, and no downstream code re-derives the size. Real correctness gap. Trigger is narrow (function found but output not locally declared, and true size != 3); standard MSL Orientation happens to match the guess, so impact is bounded — medium is appropriate.
- **Remediation:** Resolve the function's effective components (get_effective_components) to find inherited outputs, and return None (with a diagnostic at the use site) instead of guessing 3 when no output can be found.

#### [MEDIUM] Per-modifier full inheritance recomputation and cache-hit deep clones

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-instantiate/src/mod_env.rs:397` — MLS 7.2 · SPEC_0021 (avoid needless recomputation) · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** apply_component_modifier calls modifier_target_component for EVERY modifier of every component, and that calls get_effective_components(tree, target_class) which builds a FRESH InheritanceCache and re-merges the target class's whole inheritance hierarchy, cloning every component — then clones the one target component again. Combined with process_extends_with_cache deep-cloning the entire InheritedContent on every cache hit ((**cached).clone(), inheritance.rs:1070) and classes_are_compatible rendering full Modelica source text for comparison (inheritance.rs:1329), instantiation cost is superlinear in modifier count times hierarchy depth for MSL models, independent of the array-expansion cost.
- **Evidence:** Ok(get_effective_components(eval_ctx.tree, target_class)?  // fresh InheritanceCache + full merge per modifier
    .get(target_name)
    .cloned())
(inheritance.rs:1070) return Ok((**cached).clone()); // deep clone on every cache hit
- **Verifier correction:** classes_are_compatible only renders to_modelica("") when def_ids differ or are absent (def_id equality is a fast path); it does not always render source text.
- **Verifier note:** Verified: modifier_target_component (mod_env.rs:390) calls get_effective_components which allocates a fresh InheritanceCache::default() (inheritance.rs:1845) per call; it runs once per modifier via the loop at mod_env.rs:117-123, redoing the target_class inheritance merge for each modifier only to .get().cloned() one component. process_extends_with_cache deep-clones full InheritedContent on every cache hit (inheritance.rs:1070). An existing ctx.template_cache caches effective components per def_id (templates.rs:62-95) but isn't reused here. Real, compile-time-only, bounded efficiency issue. Minor overstatement: classes_are_compatible (1329) has a def_id fast-path and only renders to_modelica on mismatch, but that's a secondary claim.
- **Remediation:** Thread the ctx.template_cache / a shared InheritanceCache into modifier_target_component (the template for target_class is usually already computed); return Arc<InheritedContent> from process_extends_with_cache and make callers clone only the pieces they mutate; replace to_modelica-based class equality with DefId + hashed structural comparison.

#### [LOW] Component arrays fully scalarized at instantiation (per-element instances and modifiers)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-instantiate/src/array_expansion.rs:98` — MLS 5.6 · SPEC_0032 §1 (domain payloads must not serialize one entry per scalar iteration) · category: tensor
- **Claim:** expand_array_component materializes every index tuple (generate_array_indices returns Vec<Vec<i64>>, O(N·rank) allocation) and calls instantiate_component once per element, cloning the component, re-indexing bindings/start/modifiers, and pushing/restoring mod_env state per element. 'Resistor r[1000]' produces 1000 full instance subtrees in the overlay. While equations elsewhere are being made range-preserving per SPEC_0032, the instance overlay itself remains O(total scalar components), which dominates compile time and memory on MSL-scale arrays and forces every downstream phase to re-discover array structure from indexed names.
- **Evidence:** let indices = super::generate_array_indices(dims);
...
for idx in indices {
    scalar_comp.start = indexed_array_component_start(...)?;
    ...
    ctx.push_path_part(name, idx.clone());
    let inst_result = instantiate_component(scope.tree, &scalar_comp, ...);
    ctx.pop_path();
- **Verifier correction:** Code behavior is real and verified, but it is NOT a SPEC_0032 violation and not high severity. SPEC_0032 scopes strictly to structured EQUATION/derivative/solve-tensor families in Flat/DAE/Solve IR; the instantiate phase runs before Flat and the component instance overlay is outside SPEC_0032's ownership tables. Per-element component instantiation is MLS-consistent (an array component is an array of instances) and correctness-preserving. MLS 5.6 cite is wrong (Balanced Models). Downgrade to a design/perf improvement opportunity (make generate_array_indices an iterator; consider ranged instance representation), no measured bottleneck.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read array_expansion.rs:43,94-168: scalar_comp cloned once (line 94) then per-index start/binding/mods re-derived and instantiate_component called per element in a loop over generate_array_indices; confirmed the fn returns Vec<Vec<i64>> (function_eval.rs:997). So scalarization is real. But SPEC_0032_RANGE_PRESERVING_TENSORS §Summary/§1/§5 explicitly govern equation-family/domain IR across Flat/DAE/Solve, not the pre-Flat instantiate overlay; §1's serialize rule is about equation domain payloads. No correctness/soundness impact; perf claim unmeasured; array_parent_dims registered for downstream. Behavior confirmed, severity/spec-framing overstated.
- **Remediation:** Introduce a ranged component-instance representation in the overlay (one InstanceData plus a StructuredIndexDomain from rumoca-core, per SPEC_0032 §5), expanding to scalar elements only where element-specific modifiers/bindings actually differ; make generate_array_indices an iterator to at least remove the O(N) tuple materialization in the interim.

### A.typecheck — Type checking (7 confirmed)

#### [HIGH] Equation compatibility check no-ops for most expression forms and mixed user/builtin operands

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-typecheck/src/typechecker/late_methods.rs:1532` — MLS 6.7, 8.3.1 (EQN-025, TYPE-013, TYPE-032) · SPEC_0022 §4.5 EQN-025, §4.9 TYPE-013/014 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** infer_expression_type returns None for Binary, Unary, If, and non-record FunctionCall expressions, and check_equation_type_compatibility early-returns when either side's type is None — so any equation with an arithmetic/logical/conditional side (e.g. `Real x; equation x = b and c;` or `x = sin(s)` with String s) is never type-checked. Additionally, the mismatch error only fires when both roots are user-defined (record/enum) or both are incompatible builtins: `enumVar = 1` and `myRecord = 1.0` escape through the `!is_user_defined(lhs) || !is_user_defined(rhs)` branch, which then only compares shapes. Invalid models flow to later phases where diagnostics are worse or absent.
- **Evidence:** late_methods.rs 1531-1532: `Expression::Range { start, .. } => ...,  _ => None` (Binary/Unary/If unhandled); equation_compat.rs 20: `let (Some(lhs_ty), Some(rhs_ty)) = (lhs_ty, rhs_ty) else { return; };` and 38-45: `if !builtin_mismatch && (!Self::is_user_defined_compatibility_type(..lhs_root) || !Self::is_user_defined_compatibility_type(..rhs_root)) { ...shape check...; return; }` — enum-vs-Integer and record-vs-Real pairs take this branch and are accepted.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read late_methods.rs infer_expression_type (lines 1503-1534): `_ => None` for Binary/Unary/If confirmed; infer_function_call_result_type returns Some only for Record/Alias/Enum. equation_compat.rs:20 early-returns on None, so arithmetic/logical/conditional sides are unchecked. Traced mixed-root branch: builtin_roots_incompatible needs both roots builtin; is_user_defined_compatibility_type is enum/record-only — so enum=1 and record=1.0 fall into shape-only branch, no ET002. Grepped crate: no logical-op or math-arg type checks elsewhere; SPEC_0022 lists EQN-025/TYPE-013/014/032 as required. Claim survives.
- **Remediation:** Extend infer_expression_type to at least propagate builtin roots through Binary/Unary/If (numeric unification per MLS 6.7, Boolean for relational/logical ops), and tighten the mixed-root branch: an Enumeration or Record root paired with any different root should be a mismatch (only Real/Integer coercion is legal among builtins).
- **Resolution (2026-07-24):** Resolved. Expression inference now propagates
  `Binary`, `Unary`, `If`, array/index, named-argument, and scalar
  function-call result types; operator validation enforces Boolean and numeric
  operands; elementary builtin and user-function arguments are checked; and
  mixed user/builtin roots are incompatible except for the defined
  Integer/Real numeric conversion. Production regressions cover `Real =
  Boolean and Boolean`, `sin(String)`, and a user function receiving `String`
  for a `Real` input.

#### [HIGH] Production instanced path skips algorithms, component-class equations, bindings, and variability/causality validation

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-typecheck/src/instanced.rs:276` — MLS 3.8, 4.5, 11.2 (EXPR-012, ALG contracts) · SPEC_0022 §4.4 EXPR-012, §4.6 ALG · category: architecture
- **Claim:** check_instanced_equations walks only the root model class's equations/initial_equations. walk_statements is never called in the instanced path, so algorithm sections are not type-checked in production at all; equations inside component classes (library models used as components) are also unchecked; component binding expressions are deliberately skipped (acknowledged in a comment) pending per-instance scope maps; and validate_variability_constraints / validate_causality_constraints / mark_structural_parameters are only invoked from check_class, which only the standalone (non-production) typecheck() path reaches. The production pipeline (rumoca-compile session -> typecheck_instanced) therefore gets none of these checks.
- **Evidence:** instanced.rs 276-282: `walk_equations(self, &model_class.equations, ...); walk_equations(self, &model_class.initial_equations, ...); // Note: component *bindings* are not walked here. ... Binding checks need per-instance scope maps first.` — no walk_statements call anywhere in instanced.rs; late_methods.rs 780-797 shows validate_variability_constraints/validate_causality_constraints/algorithm walking only inside check_class.
- **Verifier note:** Verified instanced.rs: check_instanced_equations walks only root model_class.equations/initial_equations (lines 276-277); no walk_statements anywhere in the file; binding-skip comment present (278-282). walk_statements + validate_variability_constraints/validate_causality_constraints/mark_structural_parameters exist only in check_class (late_methods.rs 780-797), reached only via typecheck() (api.rs:71), which has zero non-test callers; rumoca-compile/session.rs imports only typecheck_instanced. Downstream: flatten's type_roots is used solely for connect() compatibility; grep found no type-mismatch diagnostics in flatten/dae; param_variability.rs is an optimization. SPEC_0022 lists EXPR-012 and 22 ALG contracts as obligations. Not handled elsewhere; claim survives.
- **Remediation:** Walk algorithm sections of the model class (and initial algorithms) in check_instanced_equations; iterate component type classes reachable from the overlay and check their equations/algorithms once per class; build per-instance scope maps so bindings can be checked without false positives; and run the variability/causality validators in the instanced path (or document explicitly that flatten owns them and delete the dead standalone-only versions).
- **Resolution (2026-07-24):** Resolved. The instanced path now checks the root
  and reachable component/base class bodies once, including ordinary/initial
  equations and algorithms, and runs the variability/causality validators.
  Overlay bindings are walked and checked against their resolved instance
  type under a concrete structured instance scope. Algorithm assignments use
  the same compatibility contract as equations. Focused regressions prove
  invalid algorithm assignments, bindings, and component-class equations are
  rejected by the production entry point.

#### [MEDIUM] Op-insensitive shape merging misclassifies vector dot product and produces false matrix-power errors

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-typecheck/src/typechecker/equation_shape.rs:162` — MLS 10.6.4, 10.6.8 (ARR-033) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** merge_binary_shapes ignores the binary operator: for `*` on two known equal-shaped vectors it returns [n], but MLS 10.6.4 defines vector*vector as the scalar dot product. The eval-side helper infer_dims_from_binary_with_scope has the same defect (its `_ => lhs_dims.or(rhs_dims)` arm yields [n] for [n]*[n], and `(Some(ld), None) => ld` guesses the unknown operand away). Concrete false positive: for a legal equation `x = (a*b)^2` with Real a[3], b[3], check_matrix_power infers base shape [3] and emits ET009 "matrix power base must be a square matrix, got dimensions [3]" — rejecting valid Modelica. The same wrong [n] shape also silently satisfies cross()/skew() 3-vector checks for scalar-valued dot products.
- **Evidence:** equation_shape.rs 166-171: `(Some(lhs), Some(rhs)) if lhs == rhs => Some(lhs), ... (Some(shape), None) | (None, Some(shape)) => Some(shape)` with no `op` parameter; builtin_function_checks.rs 501-508: `if shape.len() != 2 || shape[0] != shape[1] { self.emit_expression_error(base, format!("matrix power base must be a square matrix, got dimensions {shape:?} ..." ...`; rumoca-eval-ast dimension_inference.rs 234-241 Mul arms omit the [n]*[n]->scalar case.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read equation_shape.rs:162-173: merge_binary_shapes has no op param and returns [n] for [n]*[n]; called for all Binary exprs (line 56). Eval fallback dimension_inference.rs:232-242 Mul arm lacks [n]*[n]->scalar and guesses unknown operands. check_power_operators runs on every equation side (late_methods.rs:51), PowerOpCollector grabs all `^`, and check_matrix_power (builtin_function_checks.rs:495-508) emits ET009 for the inferred [3] base of (a*b)^2 — legal per MLS 10.6.4. Grep confirms no dot-product handling elsewhere. Claim survives; false diagnostics only, so medium.
- **Remediation:** Thread the operator into shape inference: for OpBinary::Mul implement MLS 10.6.4 cases ([n]*[n]->scalar, [m,n]*[n]->[m], [n]*[n,p]->[p], [m,n]*[n,p]->[m,p]) and return None when an operand shape is unknown instead of guessing; mirror the fix in rumoca-eval-ast::infer_dims_from_binary_with_scope, which feeds overlay dims consumed by flatten.

#### [MEDIUM] Per-model whole-library recomputation and pervasive cloning of modifier-target/member maps

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-typecheck/src/modifier_targets.rs:71` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** Every typecheck_instanced call rebuilds, for the entire class tree (all of MSL when loaded): the cloned TypeTable (`tree.type_table.clone()`), the dotted-suffix index over every type name and every suffix segment, type roots for every TypeId (twice: checker-local and overlay), and component modifier targets for every DefId. collect_component_modifier_targets clones its full HashSet<String> on every cache hit and again on insert, and validate_component_modifier_names clones the allowed-roots set per component. This is O(classes x members) allocations per compiled model, repeated for each model in a session; the multi-pass dimension loop additionally re-attempts integer/boolean/real/enum evaluation of every overlay component's binding (4 eval calls each) up to 10x5 passes over string-keyed FxHashMaps of flattened instance paths.
- **Evidence:** modifier_targets.rs 71-72: `if let Some(existing) = cache.get(&def_id) { return Some(existing.clone()); }` and 101: `cache.insert(def_id, names.clone());`; lib.rs 659: `let mut type_table = tree.type_table.clone();`; late_methods.rs 870: `let allowed_roots = allowed_roots.clone();`; instanced.rs 57: `build_component_modifier_targets(tree)` over all `tree.def_map` keys per model.
- **Verifier correction:** The "4 eval calls each up to 10x5 passes" detail is slightly overstated: reevaluate_boolean_real_and_enum_parameters skips names already in eval_ctx (contains_key guards); only integer re-evaluation is unconditional each pass, and the 10-pass loop breaks early on no progress and iterates the model-sized overlay, not the whole library. The whole-library per-model rebuild and pervasive cloning claims are fully accurate.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read all cited sites: modifier_targets.rs clones HashSet on cache hit (71-72) and insert (101) and iterates all def_map keys; lib.rs:659 clones type_table per build_type_context; suffix index (lib.rs:767) covers every type name+suffix; type roots built twice (lib.rs:993, 1008) over full type_table; late_methods.rs:870 clones allowed_roots per component. api.rs:108 makes a fresh TypeChecker per typecheck_instanced, called per model from compile_support.rs:304 on the shared library-wide ClassTree; ClassTree caches none of this. Minor overstatement only in the multi-pass detail.
- **Remediation:** Cache the type context, suffix index, and modifier-target/member maps on the ClassTree (they depend only on the tree, not the model) and share across typecheck_instanced calls; return &HashSet from the memo instead of cloning (e.g. Rc/interned sets); restrict build_component_modifier_targets to overlay-reachable DefIds like the member-type builder already does.

#### [MEDIUM] Variability violations are warning-level, reuse the ET004 code, and never run in production

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-typecheck/src/typechecker/late_methods.rs:1457` — MLS 3.8 / 4.5 (EXPR-012, FUNC-008) · SPEC_0008 (stable diagnostic codes) · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** check_binding_variability emits a CommonDiagnostic::warning for a binding whose expression variability exceeds the component's (e.g. `parameter Real p = time;`), where MLS 3.8 requires rejection. The diagnostic reuses code "ET004", which is also the error code for UnevaluableDimensions (lib.rs to_diagnostic), so one code means two unrelated conditions at two severities — breaking code-based triage per SPEC_0008. Because the validator is only called from check_class (standalone path), the production pipeline relies entirely on flatten's param_variability for EXPR-012, making this a divergent, weaker duplicate.
- **Evidence:** late_methods.rs 1457-1460: `self.diagnostics.emit(CommonDiagnostic::warning("ET004", format!("variability violation: {} has {} variability but binding references {} variables (MLS §4.5)", ...` vs lib.rs 205-207: `Self::UnevaluableDimensions { .. } => CommonDiagnostic::global_error("ET004", ...)`.
- **Verifier correction:** Minor: flatten's param_variability classifies algorithm assignment families for demotion; it is not an EXPR-012 binding check. Production has no EXPR-012 rejection at all — e.g. `parameter Real p = time;` is accepted silently by the instanced pipeline.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read late_methods.rs: check_binding_variability emits warning("ET004",...) at ~1457; UnevaluableDimensions uses ET004 as error in lib.rs:205 and late_methods.rs:713 — code collision confirmed, and MSL balance tooling keys ET004 to dimension failures. validate_variability_constraints is called only from check_class → standalone typecheck(), whose only callers are tests; production uses typecheck_instanced (compile_support.rs:304), which never runs it. Found NO production EXPR-012 enforcement anywhere (flatten errors.rs has no variability variant), so the claim is if anything understated.
- **Remediation:** Give the variability diagnostic its own code (e.g. ET003, which already exists for VariabilityError) and emit it as an error for clear violations; either wire it into the instanced path or remove it in favor of the flatten-phase check to avoid two half-owners of EXPR-012.

#### [LOW] Suffix/short-name type resolution fallback can silently bind the wrong type

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-typecheck/src/typechecker/late_methods.rs:1866` — MLS 5.3 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** When no DefId anchor is available, resolve_type_name falls back to a global unique-dotted-suffix match and then a unique short-name (leaf) match. Uniqueness is global-by-coincidence, not lexical: a model-local type `Foo` that happens to be the only class named `Foo` anywhere resolves regardless of visibility, and adding an unrelated library class named `Foo` later flips resolution to UNKNOWN (or, worse, a name visible only in another package silently wins). This trades MLS 5.3 lexical lookup for a heuristic; it is guarded by DefId-first resolution but remains a silent-mistyping path for components whose type_def_id is missing.
- **Evidence:** late_methods.rs 1863-1877: `// Fall back to a unique dotted-suffix match. ... let short_name = crate::path_utils::class_name_leaf(name); if let Some(type_id) = self.type_suffix_index.get(short_name).copied().flatten() { return type_id; }` — no scope/visibility constraint on the match.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read late_methods.rs:1845-1878: after DefId-anchor and exact-name lookup fail, resolution falls through to type_suffix_index with no scope/visibility filter, then a leaf-name lookup. lib.rs:767-810 confirms the index is global over the whole TypeTable; collisions poison entries to None, so a globally-unique-but-invisible class binds silently and a later same-named class flips it to UNKNOWN — exactly as claimed. Not handled upstream: resolve (contents.rs:127-192) leaves type_def_id=None on failure and only bumps stats.types_unresolved, emitting no diagnostic, so the fallback path is reachable (components are also built with type_def_id: None in instantiate/type_overrides.rs and dae_lowering.rs). Non-lexical per MLS 5.3, but guarded by DefId-first resolution; low severity stands.
- **Remediation:** Treat suffix/short-name fallback as diagnostic-only (suggest candidates in the ET001 message) or constrain candidates to scopes visible from the referencing class; long term, guarantee type_def_id is populated by resolve so the fallback becomes dead.

#### [LOW] Bare enum literal ordinals use first-wins global registration

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-typecheck/src/enum_context.rs:95` — MLS 4.9.5 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** collect_enum_ordinals registers every literal under both its qualified name and its bare name with `entry(lit_name).or_insert(ordinal)`. Classes are processed in sorted-name order, so when two enumeration types share a literal name (common in MSL: Linear, None, etc.) the alphabetically-first type's ordinal is silently used for any bare-literal reference in dimension/structural evaluation, potentially evaluating a structural expression with the wrong ordinal instead of failing or resolving lexically.
- **Evidence:** enum_context.rs 93-97: `ctx.enum_ordinals.insert(full, ordinal); ctx.enum_ordinals.entry(lit_name.to_string()).or_insert(ordinal);` — the bare-name entry is global and first-wins across all enum types in the tree.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read enum_context.rs: class_entries are sorted (line 32) and collect_enum_ordinals does insert(full) + entry(bare).or_insert (lines 93-97) — first-wins global bare names exactly as claimed. Consumers do use bare entries: lookup_by_scope falls back to map.get(name), feeding eval_integer_with_scope (mod.rs:1241), EvalLookup::lookup_integer, and scalar_value_known_with_scope (dimension_inference.rs:110). Grep across crates found no ambiguity guard (unlike type_suffix_index's Option scheme). Trigger is narrow (bare-ident collision with a literal name), so low severity stands.
- **Remediation:** Mark bare literal names ambiguous when registered by more than one enum type (mirror the Some/None scheme used by type_suffix_index) so evaluation falls back to unresolved rather than a wrong ordinal.

### A.flatten — Flattening & connections (11 confirmed)

#### [CRITICAL] rooted()/Connections.rooted ignores spanning-tree proximity

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/vcg.rs:592` — MLS 9.4.3 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** MLS defines rooted(A.R) — for Connections.branch(A.R, B.R) — as true iff A.R is closer to the root of the spanning tree than B.R. build_vcg never constructs a spanning tree or parent/depth relation (despite the doc comment claiming 'BFS from root to build spanning tree'); it sets rooted(N) = !is_root(N) && component-has-a-root, so BOTH endpoints of a mid-chain branch report rooted=true. In MSL MultiBody, force/joint elements use rooted(frame_a.R) to decide which frame's orientation to propagate; any component attached 'backwards' (frame_b toward world) gets the wrong branch selected, producing wrong kinematics on valid models. The check also uses the global potential_roots list rather than the component's own.
- **Evidence:** vcg.rs:588-593: `let node_is_root = definite_roots.contains(node) || (!has_definite_root && node == root); ... let node_rooted = !node_is_root && (has_definite_root || !potential_roots.is_empty());` — no parent/depth computation exists anywhere in the file; boolean_eval.rs:494-501 serves this map directly to `Connections.rooted` folding.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read vcg.rs: build_vcg (lines 583-594) sets rooted = !is_root && (has_definite_root || !potential_roots.is_empty()) with no parent/depth; the only parent structure (VcgEdgeForest) is a union-find for zero-constraint equality emission, unrelated to rooted. Doc comment promises BFS spanning tree that doesn't exist. Traced vcg_rooted: flatten_pipeline.rs:879 -> boolean_eval.rs lookup_vcg_rooted serves the per-node map directly; no depth-based evaluation anywhere else in crates/. MLS 9.4.3 defines rooted(A.R) via proximity vs B.R in the branch; rumoca returns true for both mid-chain endpoints, so flipped/loop MultiBody topologies get wrong orientation propagation silently. Global potential_roots misuse also confirmed at line 592.
- **Remediation:** Build the actual spanning tree (BFS parent + depth per component, definite root(s) as sources, required edges first). Evaluate rooted(x) in the context of the branch(a,b) statement it appears in: rooted(a) = depth(a) < depth(b). This requires threading the enclosing branch pair into boolean_eval instead of a per-node map.

#### [CRITICAL] Stream connectors: inStream mixing formula and actualStream unimplemented

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/connections/equation_generation.rs:386` — MLS 15.2, 15.3 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** Only two-connector stream sets are handled: rewrite_instream_for_pairs substitutes inStream(a)->b for exact unsubscripted VarRef pairs. For connection sets with 3+ stream connectors the MLS 15.2 positive-flow-weighted mixing formula (max(-m_flow,eps) weights) is never generated — the code comment admits it is 'left to the runtime passthrough until that lowering exists' — and actualStream (MLS 15.3, if m_flow>0 then inStream(...) else ...) is lowered nowhere in the pipeline (phase-dae merely whitelists both names as opaque builtins). Additionally, stream ConnectionSets are formed by textual heuristics (classify_stream_vars_by_presence scans equation LHS strings) rather than MLS semantics. Any fluid/media model with a junction or actualStream produces wrong or unevaluable results.
- **Evidence:** equation_generation.rs:386-389: 'Larger mixing sets need the positive-flow weighted formula and are left to the runtime passthrough until that lowering exists.' Rewrite guard at 626-634 requires `[a, b] = set.as_slice()` and `subscripts.is_empty()`. Only other pipeline hit: phase-dae/src/analysis/variable_analysis.rs:962-963 lists "actualStream" | "inStream" as known call names.
- **Verifier correction:** One sub-claim is overstated: raw_stream_groups used for the inStream pair rewrite come from a union-find over connect() endpoints (semantic, MLS-consistent), not textual heuristics. classify_stream_vars_by_presence (connections/mod.rs:1334-1408) inspects Expression VarRefs (residual LHS of Sub binaries), not equation strings, and feeds only the balance-oriented Stream ConnectionSets used to mark variables connected — it does not determine inStream substitution. The core defects (no 15.2 mixing formula for 3+ sets, actualStream identity passthrough, silent wrong results instead of hard error) are all confirmed as stated.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read equation_generation.rs: comment at 385-389 admits 3+ stream sets are "left to the runtime passthrough"; rewrite_instream_for_pairs (590-644) only binds exact 2-element sets with unsubscripted VarRef args. Grepped all crates: actualStream is never expanded to its MLS 15.3 if-expression anywhere — phase-solve lowers it as pure argument passthrough (helpers.rs:1265-1267, function_calls.rs:139-144, array_values.rs:969-974) and eval-dae evaluates actualStream(x)/inStream(x) as x (state_accessors.rs:62-70). No error is raised for unsupported sets, so junctions and any actualStream use silently produce wrong values. Severity critical stands.
- **Remediation:** Implement MLS 15.2 lowering at connection expansion: for each stream set compute inStream substitution using the mixing formula over the set's flow variables (with the eps regularization of 15.2's recommended form), and expand actualStream via its if-expression definition. Reject (hard error) models whose stream sets exceed supported forms instead of passing calls through.

#### [HIGH] Nonempty equalityConstraint break edges keep full equalities (no replacement residuals)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/vcg.rs:886` — MLS 9.4 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** MLS 9.4 requires that connect() equations on VCG break edges (edges removed from the spanning tree) be replaced by `R1.equalityConstraint(R2) = zeros(n)` residuals. Rumoca implements this only for n=0 (ZeroConstraintEquationForest drops the equalities). For nonempty constraints (e.g. MultiBody Orientation, n=3), zero_constraint_record_path returns None (it requires oc_eq_constraint_size == Some(0)), so retain_generated_equality keeps ALL scalar equalities (12 for Orientation T+w) on the break edge; only the balance metadata is corrected via compute_break_edge_scalar_count. Every closed kinematic loop therefore produces a structurally overdetermined equation system rather than the reduced constraint set.
- **Evidence:** vcg.rs:920-927: `(variable.is_overconstrained && variable.oc_eq_constraint_size == Some(0)).then_some(...)` gates the forest to zero-sized constraints; comment at 886-891: 'Nonempty constraints remain unresolved metadata until their replacement equations are lowered.' Test `nonempty_equality_constraint_break_is_left_for_replacement_lowering` (vcg.rs:1511-1525) asserts the equality is retained.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified vcg.rs: zero_constraint_record_path requires oc_eq_constraint_size==Some(0) (line 925), so retain_generated_equality returns true for nonempty constraints; test at 1511-1525 asserts retention. Grepped all crates: compute_break_edge_scalar_count feeds only balance.rs metadata (clamped subtraction at balance.rs:173-189), never removes equations. SpanningTree.removed_edges and EqualityConstraint IR types (rumoca-ir-flat/src/connections.rs:293,357) have zero producers/consumers — no replacement lowering exists anywhere. phase-dae/overconstrained_interface.rs only computes balance correction. Structural eliminate removes only 0-unknown equations, so 9 excess Orientation loop equalities survive into matching, violating MLS 9.4 break-edge replacement.
- **Remediation:** Select break edges for nonempty-constraint components in the same forest pass, suppress their generated equalities, and emit a call-equation family `equalityConstraint(a, b) = 0` (n scalar rows) referencing the record's resolved constraint function; remove the oc_break_edge_scalar_count balance patch once real replacement exists.
- **Remediation status (2026-07-24): resolved on the current branch.** `OverconstrainedEquationForest` now makes one record-edge decision across all primitive fields, retains selected tree edges, omits zero-width break edges, and replaces a nonempty break edge exactly once with the resolved record-type `equalityConstraint(lhs, rhs)` residual family of the declared output width. Focused tests cover required-edge cycles, per-field decision reuse, and one-time nonempty replacement.

#### [HIGH] Connection engine identity is string tokenization of rendered flat names

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/connections/path_index.rs:87` — SPEC_0007 Stage-2 cross-cutting rules · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** SPEC_0007 states post-resolution identity is keyed by DefId and 'splitting a.b.c text inside compiler logic means structure was lost too early'. The entire connection subsystem does exactly that: endpoints round-trip through QualifiedName::to_flat_string() into VarName strings; matching is done by re-parsing brackets/dots (path_index.rs is 580 lines of it: extract_suffix, split_trailing_index_groups, balanced_index_groups, index_signature); element names are synthesized by string concatenation (format!("{}{}", var, idx)); VCG nodes, branches, definite_roots, scopes, and ir-flat::Model.optional_edges are all String paths. Beyond spec violation this is the root cause of the fragile 'collapsed connector-array' special cases and makes subscript expressions inside names (e.g. [data.medium]) need bespoke bracket-depth parsers.
- **Evidence:** connections/mod.rs:1228-1231: `let path_a = conn.a.to_flat_string(); ... let var_a = rumoca_core::VarName::new(&path_a);`; path_index.rs:87-124 (extract_suffix reparses segments/indices); mod.rs:1202: `conn_b = rumoca_core::VarName::new(format!("{}{}", conn_b.as_str(), idx_suffix))`; ir-flat/lib.rs:138-149 stores `definite_roots: IndexSet<String>`, `branches: Vec<(String,String)>`.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read path_index.rs (582 lines of bracket/dot reparsing: extract_suffix line 87, balanced_index_groups, split_trailing_index_groups), connections/mod.rs (to_flat_string→VarName::new at 493-496/1228-1231/1520-1525; format! name synthesis at 1017-1033/1097/1202), vcg.rs (all String-keyed maps, index_signature), ir-flat/lib.rs 138-146 (String roots/branches/optional_edges). SPEC_0007 lines 98-99 state the quoted rules verbatim. Typed DefId scaffolding in ir-flat/connections.rs is unused outside ir-flat; QualifiedName.parts carries structured Vec<i64> indices that are discarded then reparsed. Claim survives.
- **Remediation:** Rework connection expansion over structured data: ComponentPath/ComponentReference segments with typed index vectors (the instantiate overlay already has QualifiedName parts with Vec<i64> indices), and key VCG nodes by (DefId chain, index tuple). The unused typed scaffolding in ir-flat/connections.rs (ConnectedVariable, GraphNode with DefId) is the natural target representation.

#### [HIGH] Connect over arrays scalarizes; connection equations never form structured families

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/connections/mod.rs:1088` — SPEC_0032 §1 · category: tensor
- **Claim:** SPEC_0032 explicitly lists 'connection-generated array equations that are naturally ranged' as structured families that must stay compact. Instead: (a) for-equation connects and range-subscript connects are exploded to one InstanceConnection per element at instantiate time; (b) arrays-of-components pair element-by-element via string-synthesized names (connect_array_to_expanded creates "v[1]","v[2]"... union-find entries, one equality Equation per element); (c) even whole-array connector equalities only carry an opaque scalar_count on Equation::new_array — no StructuredEquationFamily/domain is ever pushed by connection generation, so DAE/Solve cannot form Map/AffineStencil kernels from connection equations and per-element rows dominate large array models (MultiPhase, discretized domains).
- **Evidence:** mod.rs:1088-1110: loops over expanded_vars creating `VarName::new(format!("{}{}", array_var.as_str(), idx_str))` per element; instantiate connections.rs:100-105: 'Check if either side has range subscripts... expand into individual scalar connections'; equation_generation.rs:125: `flat::Equation::new_array(residual, span, origin, scalar_count)` with no add_structured_equation call anywhere in the connections module.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified all three evidence legs: instantiate connections.rs:100-105/138-149 expands range and for-loop connects to per-element InstanceConnections; connect_array_to_expanded (flatten connections/mod.rs:1077-1110) synthesizes per-element names via format! into union-find/flow_pairs; equation_generation.rs uses only Equation::new_array with scalar_count, and no structured-family push exists anywhere in the connections module. SPEC_0032 §1 verbatim requires connection-generated ranged array equations as structured families. Not handled elsewhere: structural scalarize.rs:1093-1119 expands scalar_count>1 rows without synthesizing families, and solve stencil.rs:3 emits tensor nodes only from structured families (spec §4 forbids rediscovery from anonymous rows). Correctness unaffected, so high (spec violation + perf), not critical.
- **Remediation:** Keep vectorized connects symbolic: represent connect(a.p, b.p) over component arrays as one connection with a StructuredIndexDomain binder, emit equality/flow families via add_structured_equation with the domain (per SPEC_0032 the scalar rows become derived views), and only fall back to element expansion for genuinely irregular index patterns.

#### [MEDIUM] Expandable connectors (MLS 9.1.3) reduced to a balance-exemption flag

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/variables.rs:268` — MLS 9.1.3 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** The only expandable-connector support in flatten/instantiate is tracking `from_expandable_connector: bool` so unconnected members are exempt from balance counting. None of the mandatory 9.1.3 semantics exist: no augmentation of each expandable connector with the union of elements present in its connection set, no materialization of undeclared elements referenced via connect (a connect to an element that no variable carries silently expands to nothing in process_connection because find_sub_variables returns empty), no input/output causality deduction, and no CONN-009/010/011/012/020/021 diagnostics. Control-bus models (MSL Blocks/StateGraph buses, VehicleInterfaces) flatten silently wrong instead of erroring.
- **Evidence:** variables.rs:268: `from_expandable_connector: instance.from_expandable_connector,` — grep over phase-flatten/phase-instantiate shows only scope-frame tracking (instantiate lib.rs:434-436, 1423) and the ir-flat flag (ir-flat/lib.rs:472-476); no augmentation, deduction, or 9.1.3 validation code exists. connections/mod.rs:1256 falls through when `subs_a`/`subs_b` are empty, generating no equations and no error.
- **Verifier correction:** The MLS 9.1.3 elaboration gap (no element-union augmentation, no undeclared-member materialization, no causality deduction) is confirmed. However: (1) "flattens silently wrong instead of erroring" is incorrect — connects referencing undeclared expandable members are rejected at typecheck with ET001 before flatten (conn_contracts.rs conn_011/012/021), so bus models error loudly; (2) "no CONN-009/010 diagnostics" is incorrect — ER058 enforces the flow-member restriction (semantic_checks/mod.rs:620) and ER059 the expandable-peer restriction (semantic_checks/expr.rs:400-444); (3) expandable buses with declared members flatten correctly (tiers_c.rs:846 t10k_07). The recommendation's minimum (hard error instead of silent drop) is already effectively met. Residual silent-drop risk is limited to connects between expandable connectors of different declared types with non-overlapping declared members.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read variables.rs:268, instantiate lib.rs, connections/mod.rs process_connection, resolve semantic_checks, and contract tests. The feature gap (no 9.1.3 augmentation/materialization/causality deduction) is real and admitted in conn_contracts.rs:839. But the impact evidence is wrong: undeclared-member connects are hard-rejected at typecheck (ET001; conn_011/012/021 tests), CONN-009/010 diagnostics exist (ER058/ER059 in phase-resolve), and declared-member buses flatten correctly (tiers_c.rs t10k_07). Models fail loudly, not silently wrong.
- **Remediation:** Implement expandable elaboration before connection-set building: compute the element union per expandable connection set, materialize undeclared members with deduced causality, then run normal expansion; at minimum, emit a hard unsupported-feature error when a connect endpoint resolves to an expandable connector member that produced no variables, instead of silently dropping the connection.
- **Remediation status (2026-07-24): resolved by explicit rejection on the current branch.** `rumoca-phase-flatten` now checks expandable connector member coverage before validation and connection-set construction. Identically declared buses remain supported, while disjoint or partially overlapping declared member sets report the spanned `EF020` diagnostic because they require MLS §9.1.3 union augmentation. Focused regressions cover all three cases and run with strict connection validation disabled to prove the guard is unconditional.

#### [MEDIUM] Missing per-subgraph CONN-013 root check; silent alphabetical root fallback and phantom VCG nodes

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/vcg.rs:845` — MLS 9.4 (CONN-013) · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** CONN-013 requires every VCG subgraph to have at least one definite or potential root, else error. validate_overconstrained_roots (flatten_pipeline.rs:1036) is only a 'tier-1' global check (branches exist AND no roots anywhere); a model with two components where only one has a root passes, and select_root silently promotes the alphabetically-first node to root — masking a required diagnostic with an arbitrary choice that changes generated equations. Separately, expand_optional_edges_for_suffix returns the raw unresolved string pair when exactly one endpoint resolves to known VCG nodes, injecting phantom nodes into the graph that can change component structure and root selection.
- **Evidence:** vcg.rs:843-850: `// Alphabetically first as tiebreaker\n component.iter().copied().min().expect(...)`; vcg.rs:437-440: `if a_nodes.is_empty() || b_nodes.is_empty() { return vec![(a_oc.to_string(), b_oc.to_string())]; }` — the fabricated pair flows into collect_all_nodes (749-752) as graph nodes.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read vcg.rs and flatten_pipeline.rs. validate_overconstrained_roots (lines 1036-1066) errors only if branches exist and NO roots exist anywhere; its own comment calls it a Tier-1 check of CONN-013, whose contracts.toml requirement is per-subgraph. No per-component root check exists elsewhere (grepped flatten/structural crates; only test is the global case). select_root (vcg.rs:844-849) silently picks min() alphabetical node and build_vcg marks it is_root=true, masking the required error. expand_optional_edges_for_suffix (vcg.rs:438-440) does return the raw pair when one endpoint resolves, and both strings enter the graph via collect_all_nodes (749-753). Phantom-node impact is bounded (only affects rootless/degenerate components), so medium stands.
- **Remediation:** In build_vcg, error (FlattenError with the component's node spans) when a connected component containing required branch edges has neither a definite nor potential root; drop half-resolved optional edges instead of fabricating endpoints, or resolve them strictly.
- **Remediation status (2026-07-24): resolved on the current branch.** VCG
  pre-scan now retains each required branch's source span, and flattening
  validates every connected component containing a required branch before
  `build_vcg` can select an alphabetical fallback. A component with only a
  root in a different subgraph receives a spanned error, and half-resolved
  optional edges are discarded rather than materialized as phantom nodes.
  Focused regressions cover both failure modes.

#### [MEDIUM] Equality/flow scalar-count heuristics silently alter semantics for size-mismatched sets

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/connections/equation_generation.rs:96` — MLS 9.2 (CONN-008) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** generate_equality_equations 'caps at the smaller size' when the two sides have different array sizes, emitting residual `a - b` (no subscripts, no offset) with scalar_count = min — only correct for 1-based prefix slices, wrong for any other overlap, and it converts what CONN-008 declares an error into a silently truncated equation when validation didn't catch the pair (subscripted/collapsed vars return None from get_validation_var_info and skip all checks). Similarly generate_flow_equation folds a mixed scalar+one-array flow set into a single scalar Kirchhoff row (scalar_count=1 sums whole arrays), and with multiple differing array sizes it just takes the first size.
- **Evidence:** equation_generation.rs:96-101: `// cap at the smaller size. let rhs_size = ...; if let Some(b) = rhs_size.filter(|&b| scalar_count > 1 && b > 1 && b < scalar_count) { scalar_count = b; }`; 174-178: `let scalar_count = if has_scalar_flow && array_sizes.len() == 1 { 1 } else { array_sizes.into_iter().next().unwrap_or(1) };`
- **Verifier correction:** Two details corrected: (1) subscripted vars do NOT return None from get_validation_var_info — mod.rs:567-587 handles them with remaining-dims; only collapsed/unresolvable names skip checks. (2) The mixed scalar+one-array flow fold (scalar_count=1) is correct Kirchhoff semantics for the legitimate case of a scalar port union-found with per-element array flows into one node; the real defect in generate_flow_equation is narrower — taking the first array size when multiple differing array sizes are present (line 177). Also note direct primitive-to-primitive size mismatches ARE hard errors under default strict_connection_validation=true; silent truncation requires the validation-skipped routes above or strict=false.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read equation_generation.rs (cap at 96-101, flow count at 168-178: quotes exact). Refutation attempts: (1) CONN-008 validation exists and strict defaults true, but collapsed array-component endpoints (empty find_sub_variables) skip validation yet still form potential sets via connect_array_output_variables; validate_expanded_connector_connection silently continues on match failure. (2) Instantiate expands evaluable ranges correctly, but drops unevaluable/mismatched range subscripts (subscript_to_i64→None for Range), yielding whole-array connects where the offset-less min cap is wrong for non-1-based ranges. (3) scalarize.rs:1069 may override scalar_count via IR shape but never restores offsets. Claim survives.
- **Remediation:** Make size mismatch in a potential set a hard error (dims must already match per CONN-008 after instantiate expands range subscripts); if legitimate collapsed-array cases remain, carry explicit slice offsets/domains on the generated equation rather than a bare min count, and derive flow scalar_count from validated uniform dims.

#### [MEDIUM] Parameter/constant connector members: connects dropped without MLS assert generation

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/connections/mod.rs:1131` — MLS 9.3.1 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** connect_sub_variable and connect_primitive_vars return early whenever either endpoint is a parameter or constant, generating nothing. MLS 9.3.1 requires connections of parameter/constant primitives to produce equality assertions (the values must be checked equal, e.g. Plug.m across MultiPhase components). Rumoca emits neither an equation nor an assert nor a compile-time equality check, so inconsistent parameter values across connected connectors go undetected and simulate with silently divergent settings.
- **Evidence:** mod.rs:1131-1138: `// Skip parameters and constants — they are structural, not equation unknowns. ... matches!(var_info.variability, Parameter(_) | Constant(_)) { return; }`; same skip at 1291-1300 in connect_primitive_vars. No assert_equations are produced on this path (assert_equations.rs has no connection-sourced entries).
- **Verifier correction:** The MLS sentence ("Constants or parameters in connected components yield the appropriate assert statements to check that they have the same value; connections are not generated") is in MLS §9.2 (Generation of Connection Equations), not §9.3.1. Also note the gap is not tracked in the project's own contract catalog (SPEC_0022 has no CONN entry for this requirement), so adding a contract ID alongside the fix would be consistent with project practice.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read connections/mod.rs: both connect_sub_variable (lines 1128-1138) and connect_primitive_vars (1290-1300) silently return on Parameter/Constant variability, emitting nothing. Grepped flatten/instantiate crates and the connections module: no connection-sourced AssertEquation, no compile-time value comparison, and the project contract catalog (SPEC_0022 CONN-007/CONN-028 + conn_contracts.rs tests) covers only parameter-typed connector components and variability matching, not the MLS-required equal-value assert. Claim survives; only the MLS section cite is slightly off.
- **Remediation:** For parameter/constant pairs in a connection set, evaluate both sides when possible and raise a flatten error on mismatch; otherwise emit an AssertEquation (condition a==b) into flat.assert_equations so the check survives to runtime, matching 9.3.1.

#### [MEDIUM] Redundant recomputation and cloning in connection processing

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/connections/equation_generation.rs:728` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** Per model, the interface flow-var map is computed twice from identical inputs: process_connections builds interface_flow_vars_by_scope (line 356), then generate_external_unconnected_flow_equations rebuilds it internally (line 728) instead of taking the existing map. Each of validate_connections, process_connection, collect_flow_vars_by_scope, and collect_interface_flow_vars_by_scope independently re-expands the same connection endpoints, and find_sub_variables_indexed clones the whole child Vec<VarName> on every hit. ConnectionVarIndex allocates a String key per (variable x prefix-length) plus a Vec<String> of parsed parts per variable — O(V x depth) owned strings for large MSL models on top of build_prefix_children's O(V x depth) VarName clones.
- **Evidence:** equation_generation.rs:355-362 computes `interface_flow_vars_by_scope`; 720-734: `generate_external_unconnected_flow_equations(...) { let interface_flow_vars_by_scope = collect_interface_flow_vars_by_scope(connections, flat, prefix_children, var_index, interface_connector_roots_by_scope); ... }` — same five inputs, second full pass. mod.rs:766-768: `if let Some(children) = prefix_children.get(prefix) { return children.clone(); }`.
- **Verifier correction:** Minor cost overstatement: VarName is interned (Arc + u32 id), so build_prefix_children's O(V x depth) clones and find_sub_variables_indexed's Vec clones are refcount bumps plus a Vec allocation, not deep string copies. The owned-String costs are real but confined to ConnectionVarIndex. All work is once per compile pass and bounded.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read equation_generation.rs and mod.rs: line 356 builds interface_flow_vars_by_scope; line 728 rebuilds it inside generate_external_unconnected_flow_equations from the same five inputs (intervening flat mutations touch equations/connected flags, not the .flow flags the collector reads, so the result is identical and the map owns its data — passing it in is trivially feasible). Verified 4 independent endpoint expansions via find_sub_variables_indexed (mod.rs 520/1253, collect_flow_vars_from_conn_path:502), the children.clone() at mod.rs:766-768, and ConnectionVarIndex's per-(var x prefix) String keys plus Vec<String> parsed parts (mod.rs:89-113), redundant with VarName's precomputed segmentation.
- **Remediation:** Pass the already-computed interface map into generate_external_unconnected_flow_equations; expand each connection endpoint once into a memoized per-path expansion cache shared by validation/set-building/flow collection; return &[VarName] slices instead of cloned Vecs; intern segment vectors instead of per-prefix String keys.

#### [MEDIUM] Typed connection-graph IR in ir-flat is dead scaffolding; inside/outside re-derived heuristically

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-ir-flat/src/connections.rs:79` · category: architecture
- **Claim:** ir-flat exports a full typed model of MLS 9 machinery — ConnectedVariable with is_inside and sign(), ConnectionSet/ConnectionSets, ConnectionGraph with DefId-bearing GraphNode/RootStatus, SpanningTree, EqualityConstraint — but the flatten phase uses none of it (zero references outside ir-flat and one error-name in phase-dae): it has a private string-based ConnectionSet and FxHashMap<String,bool> VCG results instead. Consequently inside/outside status, which instantiate knows exactly at each connect site, is discarded and later re-approximated by is_outside_flow_var_for_scope path/scope heuristics — the source of several special-cased sign paths and a risk when inner/outer redirects reset scope to root.
- **Evidence:** ir-flat/connections.rs:79-127 defines `ConnectedVariable { ..., is_inside: bool, ... } ... pub fn sign(&self) -> i32 { if self.is_inside { 1 } else { -1 } }`; grep for ConnectedVariable/SpanningTree/ConnectionGraph across phase-flatten returns nothing; equation_generation.rs:226-243 re-derives outside-ness from `interface_flow_vars_by_scope` string sets with an array-index-stripping fallback.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified all evidence: ir-flat/connections.rs defines ConnectedVariable{is_inside}/sign(), ConnectionSets, ConnectionGraph, SpanningTree, EqualityConstraint, re-exported in lib.rs:50-52; full-repo grep shows zero consumers — only a doc comment in phase-flatten/connections/mod.rs:1496 and the error-name InvalidConnectionGraph in phase-dae (which builds its own graph logic). phase-flatten uses a private string-scoped ConnectionSet (mod.rs:326) and equation_generation.rs re-derives outside-ness via is_outside_flow_var_for_scope over IndexMap<String,FlowVarSet> with a strip_embedded_array_indices fallback. Dead scaffolding + dual representation is real; no wrong-results shown, so medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Either populate the typed graph (record is_inside per endpoint at instantiate where connect context is known, carry DefIds) and generate equations from it, or delete the scaffolding so the crate's public API reflects reality; do not keep two divergent representations.

### A.dae — DAE lowering, events & initialization (12 confirmed)

#### [CRITICAL] delay() silently evaluated as identity — transport delay unimplemented

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-dae/src/eval/builtin_runtime.rs:88` — MLS 3.7.4 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** The delay(expr, delayTime[, delayMax]) operator is accepted by the typechecker (check_delay_builtin validates arity) but is never implemented as a transport delay anywhere in the pipeline: the DAE evaluator returns the first argument unchanged, and Solve lowering merely re-evaluates the expression in Pre mode (previous step/event value, not time - delayTime). Models using delay compile, simulate, and produce wrong trajectories with no diagnostic.
- **Evidence:** builtin_runtime.rs:88: `rumoca_core::BuiltinFunction::NoEvent | rumoca_core::BuiltinFunction::Delay => { Some(arg(0)?) }` — delay treated exactly like noEvent. phase-solve/lower/scalar_ops.rs:254 confirms the only other path: `BuiltinFunction::Delay => ... self.lower_expr_in_mode(&args[0], scope, call_depth, ValueMode::Pre)`. No history buffer/interpolation of past trajectory exists in either runtime.
- **Verifier correction:** Claim fully accurate; only nit is the MLS section number — the repo's own comments cite delay as MLS §3.7.2 rather than §3.7.4, which does not affect the finding.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read builtin_runtime.rs:88 (Delay grouped with NoEvent, returns arg(0)) and scalar_ops.rs:254 (Delay lowered as Pre-mode of args[0], delayTime discarded) — both verbatim. ir_primitives.rs:919 doc comment confirms intentional stub: "returns expr (no delay in continuous sim)". Grepped all crates: no history buffer/interpolation exists; only rejection is FmiEvalError::Unsupported("delay") in fmi_metadata_values.rs (metadata path only, not simulation). Resolve/typecheck only validate arguments (ER055/ER096), never diagnose unimplemented semantics. Models using delay silently produce wrong trajectories.
- **Remediation:** Until a real delay buffer (dense-output interpolation of expr over [t-delayMax, t], as OpenModelica does) is implemented, reject delay() at ToDae with an explicit ED-class unsupported-operator error instead of silently mis-simulating; then implement it as DAE/runtime metadata (a history-buffer runtime special) analogous to the existing external-table machinery.

#### [HIGH] terminal() is hardwired to false — terminal events never fire

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-dae/src/eval/builtin_runtime.rs:86` — MLS 3.7.5 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** terminal() must return true at the terminal event of a successful simulation, activating `when terminal()` clauses (commonly used for final assertions/outputs). Rumoca evaluates terminal() as constant 0 (false) in the DAE evaluator, Solve lowering emits const 0.0, and condition_activation treats it as never-active; no runtime hook exists to run a terminal event pass. Models using terminal() silently never execute those clauses.
- **Evidence:** builtin_runtime.rs:86: `rumoca_core::BuiltinFunction::Terminal => Some(T::zero())`; phase-solve/lower/scalar_ops.rs:291: `BuiltinFunction::Terminal => self.emit_const_at(0.0, span)`; condition_activation.rs:7-11 maps `Initial | Terminal` to `Some(false)`. grep shows no terminal-event pass in rumoca-eval-solve/sim_driver or runtime_events.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read all three sites: builtin_runtime.rs:86 hardwires Terminal to T::zero() while Initial reads env.is_initial (VarEnv at eval/mod.rs:616 has is_initial, no is_terminal field exists anywhere); scalar_ops.rs:291 emits const 0.0 with a comment admitting "terminal-event handling can override this phase marker explicitly when that event is implemented" — i.e. not implemented; condition_activation.rs:8-11 maps Initial|Terminal to Some(false). Grepped all 50+ crates: no terminal flag or end-of-simulation event pass in rumoca-eval-solve (sim_driver.rs, runtime_events.rs), rumoca-eval-dae, or rumoca-sim; phase-dae tests only verify terminal()-gated relations create no continuous roots, not that any terminal pass runs. Per MLS 3.7.5 terminal() must be true at the termination event, so when-terminal() clauses silently never fire. Severity high fits the rubric (MLS violation, silent); not critical since continuous results are otherwise correct.
- **Remediation:** Add an end-of-simulation event pass in the sim driver that evaluates the discrete partitions once with a terminal flag set (mirroring the existing env.is_initial mechanism for initial()), and route terminal()-guarded when clauses/event actions through it; alternatively reject terminal() explicitly until supported.

#### [HIGH] Semantic decisions driven by human-readable origin strings

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/algorithm_lowering.rs:657` — SPEC_0007 (post-resolution identity keyed by DefId, not strings) · category: architecture
- **Claim:** Equation.origin is documented as a 'human-readable origin description (for debugging)', yet it drives load-bearing semantics: f_m/f_z canonicalization merges branch-split when-updates only when origin strings are byte-equal, reinit equations are exempted from self-ref rewriting via origin.starts_with("guarded reinit"), when_guard skips guarded definitions via origin.starts_with("guarded "), and balance filtering keys off origin prefixes (connect(, connection equation:, binding equation for). Any formatting change to these strings silently changes lowering and balance results, and unrelated equations that happen to share an origin string can be erroneously merged.
- **Evidence:** algorithm_lowering.rs:639 `!equation.origin.starts_with("guarded reinit")`; :654-657 `.any(|equation| is_connection_equation_origin(&equation.origin)) || ... equation.origin != first.origin` gates merge_branch_split_discrete_assignments; balance.rs:1056-1062 `origin.starts_with("connect(") || origin.starts_with("connection equation:")`, and balance.rs:341 `eq.origin.starts_with("binding equation for")` all change balance arithmetic.
