- **Verifier correction:** Minor refinement: strings are compiler-generated from the existing typed flat::EquationOrigin enum (identifiers cannot contain spaces/colons), so user-name prefix collisions are essentially impossible; the concrete risks are Display-format drift silently changing lowering/balance results and byte-equal-origin merges conflating distinct when-clauses in the same component. The fix is smaller than claimed: carry the already-existing typed origin across the flat-to-DAE boundary (convert.rs:1176) instead of inventing a new enum.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified every cited site: ir-dae lib.rs:1199 doc says origin is debug-only; algorithm_lowering.rs:639 starts_with("guarded reinit"), :652-658 merge gated on origin byte-equality and is_connection_equation_origin (contains("connection equation:")); when_guard.rs:28 starts_with("guarded "); balance.rs:341/1056-1061 prefix checks drive balance arithmetic; pattern duplicated in phase-structural direct_demotion.rs:39. SPEC_0007 lines 95-99 explicitly forbid string-keyed identity/display-string parsing. Refutation fails: typed EquationOrigin exists in ir-flat (lib.rs:729) but is to_string()'d at convert.rs:1176, so semantics ARE string-driven downstream; WhenAssignment/ComponentEquation Display collapses distinct when-clauses to equal strings, enabling the claimed erroneous merge.
- **Remediation:** Introduce a typed EquationOrigin/EquationKind enum (Connection, Binding, GuardedWhen{clause_id}, GuardedReinit, Component{DefId}, ...) on dae::Equation carried through lowering, keep the display string purely diagnostic, and key merge/filter decisions on the enum plus when-clause identity.

#### [MEDIUM] Debug-format of subscript expressions used as union-find identity keys in balance

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/balance.rs:644` — MLS 4.7 · SPEC_0007 §Flat cross-cutting rules · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** Discrete connection balance counting builds union-find node names by rendering subscripts with Rust Debug formatting (`format!("{expr:?}")`) into VarName strings. Debug output is not stable identity: two semantically different expressions can render alike (merging distinct nodes and under-counting equations) and the same expression can render differently across refactors, changing balance results. This is exactly the string-keyed identity SPEC_0007 forbids after resolution.
- **Evidence:** balance.rs:638-649: `let rendered = subscripts.iter().map(|subscript| match subscript { ... Subscript::Expr { expr, .. } => format!("{expr:?}"), }) ... VarName::new(format!("{name}[{rendered}]"))` feeding ConnectionUpdateRank::add_edge node identity.
- **Verifier correction:** Mechanism is more accurately span-induced node splitting than expression collision; practical reachability is narrow because post-flatten subscripts are typically constant Index values.
- **Verifier note:** balance.rs:644 renders `Subscript::Expr` via `format!("{expr:?}")` into a VarName that becomes union-find node identity (add_edge, lines 611/546-580). Expression derives Debug with per-variant `span: Span` fields (ir_primitives.rs:1257+), so the key embeds spans and is not stable semantic identity. SPEC_0007 line 98 explicitly forbids hashing rendered names/display strings after resolution. No other site computes these nodes. Finding's collision framing is imprecise (Debug is near-injective; real risk is span-induced splitting), and reachability is narrow (scalar connection updates normally carry Index subscripts; scalar_count>1 with Expr returns None), so impact is real but bounded — medium.
- **Remediation:** Key connection-rank nodes on structured identity: (DefId or VarName, evaluated integer index vector) — the caller already has variable metadata and scalar counts; reject or conservatively skip non-evaluable subscripts instead of Debug-rendering them.

#### [MEDIUM] When-equations with unroutable LHS are silently dropped

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/algorithm_lowering.rs:239` — MLS 8.3.5 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** route_discrete_event_equations matches the when-equation target against states/discrete_reals/discrete_valued; if the target is in none of these partitions (e.g. a variable that also has a continuous defining equation, which MLS makes illegal, or a classification miss), the guarded update is discarded with `None => {}` — no error, no diagnostic. The model then simulates with the when-assignment simply absent, and the balance gate cannot notice because the dropped equation never entered any partition.
- **Evidence:** algorithm_lowering.rs:234-240: `match discrete_equation_bucket_for_lhs(dae, &lhs) { Some(DiscreteEquationBucket::DiscreteValued) => dae.discrete.valued_updates.push(guarded), Some(DiscreteEquationBucket::DiscreteReal) => dae.discrete.real_updates.push(guarded), None => {} }` — the None arm drops a user equation.
- **Verifier note:** Verified algorithm_lowering.rs:234-240: the None arm (`None => {}`) drops the guarded when-update. Reachability confirmed: find_when_only_vars (variable_analysis.rs:223-226) excludes any when-target that is also continuously/algorithm defined, so it never lands in discrete_reals/discrete_valued, making discrete_equation_bucket_for_lhs return None. Downstream appendix_b validators only check equations already pushed into partitions, and the balance gate counts the continuous defining equation as balanced, so neither notices the drop. On MLS-illegal dual-definition input (or a subscript classification miss) the model silently simulates without the event assignment instead of erroring. Medium: valid models unaffected, but silently-wrong output on illegal/edge input.
- **Remediation:** Make the None arm a hard ToDaeError (target of when-equation is neither a state nor discrete-time variable), which also implements the MLS 8.3.5 single-definition restriction diagnostically instead of silently ignoring the event update.

#### [MEDIUM] O(sites x relations) deep semantic-equality scans in condition lowering

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/condition_lowering.rs:344` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** Condition candidate interning does a linear scan of all previously collected candidates with deep structural comparison for every relational subexpression occurrence in every continuous/discrete equation, and rewrite_continuous_event_conditions repeats a linear expressions_semantically_equal scan over all relations for every relational node in every f_x equation. On MSL-scale models with hundreds to thousands of relations this is quadratic in deep tree comparisons, in a phase that already has a hashing dedup utility available.
- **Evidence:** condition_lowering.rs:344-346: `if out.iter().any(|existing| rumoca_core::expressions_semantically_equal(&existing.expr, &candidate.expr)) { return; }`; :638-641 `relations.iter().position(|relation| rumoca_core::expressions_semantically_equal(relation, expr))` invoked per relational Binary in every continuous equation via ConditionRewriter.
- **Verifier correction:** Minor nuance only: UniqueExpressions is pub(super) inside runtime_precompute and stores bare Expressions, so reuse for candidate interning needs a visibility widening plus carrying ConditionCandidate metadata (span/source) alongside the fingerprint buckets — a small adaptation, not a drop-in reuse.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read condition_lowering.rs: insert_condition_candidate (line 344) does out.iter().any(expressions_semantically_equal) per candidate, and ConditionCandidateCollector inserts one candidate per relational Binary node across all continuous+discrete equations — quadratic interning. matching_relation_index (line 640) linearly scans relations per relational node in every continuous equation via ConditionRewriter (rewrite_continuous_event_conditions loops all f_x eqs). expressions_semantically_equal (ir_primitives.rs:1607) is a deep recursive compare; expression_semantic_fingerprint (1651) and hash-bucketed UniqueExpressions (runtime_precompute/expression_identity.rs) exist in the same crate, so the fix path is real. Not handled elsewhere. Correctness unaffected; cost bounded to relations in one phase, so medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Reuse the hash-bucketed UniqueExpressions from runtime_precompute/expression_identity.rs (semantic hash + equality within bucket) for both candidate interning and the relation->index lookup, making both passes near-linear.

#### [MEDIUM] Balance discrete counting iterates all variables per equation

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/balance.rs:820` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** collect_update_target_scalar_counts, add_matching_variable_scalar_counts, and insert_complete_connection_variable_names each loop over the entire discrete variable partition for every equation LHS/reference (reference_overlaps_variable per pair), and expression_references_included_variable additionally calls variable_overlaps_any (another full partition scan) per candidate — O(E x V) to O(refs x V^2) behavior. Balance runs at least once per compile and twice when diagnostics are produced, so this dominates ToDae time on large event-heavy models.
- **Evidence:** balance.rs:816-825: `for eq in equations { ... for (candidate, variable) in variables { if reference_overlaps_variable(dae_model, lhs, candidate, variable) { *counts.entry(...) += eq.scalar_count; } } }`; balance.rs:837-843 nests `variables.iter().any(...)` inside a per-reference loop with `variable_overlaps_any` scanning `excluded` for each hit.
- **Verifier correction:** Minor: the O(refs x V^2) bound for expression_references_included_variable is more precisely O(refs x V x X), where X is the excluded (inputs) partition scanned by variable_overlaps_any — same order but cross-partition. "Dominates ToDae time" is plausible for event-heavy models but not statically provable; the scans are confined to discrete partitions, so medium (real but bounded) stands.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read balance.rs: collect_update_target_scalar_counts (810-827), add_matching_variable_scalar_counts (927-939), insert_complete_connection_variable_names (907-925) each scan the full discrete partition per equation/reference with no short-circuit; expression_references_included_variable (829-844) nests a full-partition any() per ref, calling variable_overlaps_any which scans the excluded partition per hit. lib.rs:467-468 runs balance once per compile and re-runs balance_detail via equations_unknowns on unbalanced, uncached. BalanceSymbolSet index exists in the same file but is unused here, so the fix is feasible and nothing mitigates it elsewhere.
- **Remediation:** Build one index up front per balance run: VarName -> partition entry plus DefId -> variable (and ancestry closure), then resolve each reference via hash lookup + ancestry walk instead of scanning every variable per equation.

#### [MEDIUM] Evaluator stores arrays as per-element rendered string keys

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-dae/src/eval/mod.rs:338` — SPEC_0032 §2; SPEC_0007 §Flat cross-cutting rules · category: tensor
- **Claim:** The DAE evaluator's VarScope is a layered IndexMap<String, T>; every array element lives under a formatted key like `x[1,2]` produced by scalar_name_text_for_flat_index, so each element access in init/precompute/function evaluation pays string formatting + hashing, and whole-array operations decay to per-element string lookups. This both loses tensor structure at the evaluation boundary and makes eval-dae the slow path for array-heavy initialization (pre-seeding alone formats one key per scalar element).
- **Evidence:** eval/mod.rs:336-341: `struct VarScopeFrame<T> { local: IndexMap<String, T>, parent: Option<VarScope<T>>, ... }`; pre_seed.rs:27-29: `for flat_idx in 0..sz { let key = dae::scalar_name_text_for_flat_index(name, &var.dims, flat_idx); if let Some(value) = lookup_pre_value_in(&env.runtime, &key)...` — one formatted string per element per seed.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified: VarScopeFrame uses local: IndexMap<String,T> (mod.rs:338); array elements stored under formatted keys x[i]/x[i,j] via format_subscript_key + scalar_name_text_for_flat_index (lib.rs:1031-1053); set_array_entries (mod.rs:905) and pre_seed.rs:28 write one string-keyed slot per element; set_env_key inserts key.to_string() into the map, so hashing occurs per element access. cached_indexed_keys (mod.rs:255) only memoizes the flat name[i] key strings — hashing still per-access, pre-seed and multi-dim subscript forms re-format uncached. Tensor structure is lost at the eval boundary as claimed. Purely a perf/architecture concern (correct results), so medium (real but bounded) stands; not high.
- **Remediation:** Store array variables as contiguous Vec<T> slots keyed once by base identity (VarName or interned id) with (dims, offset) addressing, keeping string keys only at display/trace boundaries; the existing collect_var_dims metadata already provides the shapes needed.

#### [MEDIUM] Rendered-name string parsing pervades semantic lookups

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/analysis/discrete_partition.rs:236` — SPEC_0007 §Flat cross-cutting rules · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** Discrete-bucket resolution, balance symbol matching, phantom expansion, and pre-target resolution all recover name hierarchy by tokenizing flattened strings: subscript_fallback_chain peels bracketed indices from rendered names per query, strip_all_subscripts normalizes text, and get_top_level_prefix re-parses brackets containing dots. SPEC_0007 explicitly states that splitting rendered `a.b.c` text inside lowering logic means structure was lost too early; besides fragility (a mini string parser must handle `bus[data.medium].pin`), each lookup allocates a chain of new VarName strings in hot classification loops.
- **Evidence:** discrete_partition.rs:232-243: `dae.variables.discrete_valued.contains_key(&flat_to_dae_var_name(name)) || subscript_fallback_chain(name.as_str()).into_iter().any(|candidate| ...)` — string-peeling fallback per target per equation; path_utils.rs:107-117 test shows the chain allocates `a[1].b[2].c`, `a[1].b.c`, `a.b.c` per call.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read discrete_partition.rs:228-261 — the quoted contains_key||subscript_fallback_chain pattern is present for both discrete buckets. subscript_fallback_chain (rumoca-core/lib.rs:431) allocates a new VarName String per peeled layer per call; phase-dae path_utils.rs tests 107-118 match the cited chain. SPEC_0007 "Cross-cutting rules (Flat through DAE)" verbatim forbids splitting rendered a.b.c text in lowering logic; phase-dae is semantic, not a boundary. Grep confirms the pattern in balance.rs, name_resolution.rs, when_analysis.rs, algorithm_lowering.rs, variable_analysis.rs — no DefId index exists as an alternative. No wrong-result bug demonstrated (bracket-dot cases are handled), so medium is correct.
- **Remediation:** Carry structured ComponentReference/DefId on equation targets end-to-end (Equation.lhs already holds a Reference) and resolve partition membership via DefId/ComponentPath indexes built once per DAE, reserving string peeling for display and legacy wire boundaries.

#### [MEDIUM] Algorithm for-loops fully unrolled into guarded expression trees

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/algorithm_lowering/for_lowering.rs:349` — SPEC_0032 §1 · category: tensor
- **Claim:** Model-algorithm for-statements are lowered by expanding every iteration binding at compile time and folding each assignment into a nested guarded if-expression per target, bounded by MAX_GUARDED_ALGORITHM_EXPR_NODES. Loop structure (the compact iteration domain SPEC_0032 preserves for equations) is destroyed, expression size grows with iteration count times body complexity, and large-but-legal loops fail with a ForLoopGuardedExpressionTooLarge error rather than lowering to a ranged construct.
- **Evidence:** for_lowering.rs:348-353: `let iteration_bindings = expand_for_bindings(nested.indices, flat, &merged_bindings)?; for iteration in iteration_bindings { ... lower_for_statement_sequence_with_guard(...) }`; :106-113 `expression_exceeds_node_budget(expr, MAX_GUARDED_ALGORITHM_EXPR_NODES)` turns the blowup into a hard error.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified in for_lowering.rs and algorithm_lowering.rs. expand_for_bindings (l.976) recursively materializes the full Cartesian product of all iteration tuples at compile time; lower_nested_for_with_guard/lower_for_statement_assignments loop over every tuple, folding each assignment via guarded_expr into nested if-trees. expression_budget_error hard-fails at MAX_GUARDED_ALGORITHM_EXPR_NODES = 1,000,000. All factual claims accurate. Not a correctness bug (semantically faithful to imperative algorithm semantics) and not strictly a SPEC_0032 §1 violation — that section governs structured equation families, not algorithm sections, as the finding concedes. Real but bounded design/perf-parity limitation: medium.
- **Remediation:** For the common affine-assignment subset (target[i] := f(i) with evaluable ranges), lower algorithm loops to structured/ranged DAE families or keep them as bounded loop constructs for Solve to lower, falling back to unrolling only for genuinely irregular bodies.

#### [LOW] Balance gate is a clamped heuristic, not MLS 4.7 local balance

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/balance.rs:181` — MLS 4.7 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** equations_unknowns_from_detail clamps interface-flow and overconstrained-interface contributions to 'deficit only' (they can only add equations up to the number needed), and equation_counts_for_balance excludes continuous equations that reference no continuous unknown or input. Consequently entire classes of over-determined models (e.g. a redundant constraint among parameters/time, or excess interface-flow equations) pass the balance gate, and the reported equations/unknowns pair is a back-computed artifact rather than the MLS-defined local count.
- **Evidence:** balance.rs:181-186: `let iflow_needed = (unknowns as i64 - base_without_iflow).max(0); let effective_iflow = (detail.interface_flow_count as i64).min(iflow_needed); ... let effective_oc_interface = available_oc_interface.min(oc_needed);` — corrections can never create a surplus. balance.rs:331-346 skips equations not referencing a continuous unknown/input from f_x counting.
- **Verifier note:** Verified in balance.rs: lines 181-186 deficit-clamp iflow/oc (min of needed), line 188 surplus-clamps brk; equation_counts_for_balance (314-346) excludes continuous equations referencing no continuous unknown/input. Doc comment 67-69 and tests confirm this is intentional. So the described over-determined classes can pass the gate. But core f_x/f_z/f_m/f_c are unclamped (genuine redundant real-unknown equations still caught), downstream structural analysis catches true singularities, and the finding is framed as an additive strict-mode enhancement, not a correctness bug. "MLS 4.7 violation" overstates a diagnostic-completeness gap; severity is low.
- **Remediation:** Keep the heuristic as the permissive default gate, but add a strict MLS 4.7 counting mode (all equations counted, flow contributions computed from connector structure rather than clamped) used for library validation, and report when the clamps were exercised so masked imbalances are visible.

#### [LOW] Discrete and condition partitions carry no structured equation families

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/dae_lowering.rs:860` — SPEC_0032 §1, §3 · category: tensor
- **Claim:** StructuredEquationFamily metadata (compact index domains for scalar views) exists only for continuous.equations and initialization.equations; DaeDiscretePartition (f_z/f_m) and DaeConditionPartition (f_c) have no structured_equations field. Array-valued when-updates and connection-generated discrete array equations keep only a flat scalar_count, and once the phantom scalarizer expands them into per-element rows all range structure is unrecoverable — exactly the 'scalar rows without a structured owner' state SPEC_0032 prohibits, and it blocks tensor-native event kernels downstream.
- **Evidence:** dae_lowering.rs:860: `// The discrete and condition partitions carry no structured families.` followed by scalarize_equation_list calls on discrete.real_updates/valued_updates/conditions.equations with the returned span maps discarded; rumoca-ir-dae/src/lib.rs:508-518 DaeDiscretePartition has only `real_updates`/`valued_updates` vectors.
- **Verifier correction:** Factual observation (missing field, discarded spans) is accurate, but it is a design/architecture gap for future tensor-native event codegen, not a SPEC_0032 violation or correctness bug. Discrete array updates remain compact via scalar_count and scalarize deterministically downstream.
- **Verifier note:** Verified: DaeDiscretePartition/DaeConditionPartition (ir-dae/lib.rs:508-529) lack structured_equations; dae_lowering.rs:861-878 scalarizes discrete/condition lists and discards spans (no family remap), matching evidence. But impact is overstated: array when-updates become a single compact Equation with scalar_count>1 (when_conversion.rs:126-135), not orphaned scalar rows; flat when_clauses never held StructuredEquationFamily, so nothing compact is dropped; SPEC_0032 doesn't mandate discrete/condition structured families; no wrong sim results. This is a forward-looking gap for not-yet-existing tensor event kernels, not a correctness/MLS defect. Downgraded to low.
- **Remediation:** Add structured_equations to DaeDiscretePartition and DaeConditionPartition (bumping the DAE schema_version), populate them from flat when/for structured families during when conversion, and remap them in scalarize_phantom_vector_equations the same way f_x/init already do.

### A.structural — Structural analysis: index reduction, matching, tearing (11 confirmed)

#### [CRITICAL] Demoted-state der() references silently compile to 0.0

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/dae_prepare/state_row_reduction.rs:402` — MLS Appendix B.1 · SPEC_0007 Stage 3 contract (No der() on RHS) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** demote_states_without_assignable_derivative_rows demotes any state that loses the state-to-row bipartite matching (e.g. two states sharing one coupled derivative row, or rows rejected by the all_active_derivative_args_are_states gate) without verifying that no der(state) references remain in the system. The later expand_compound_derivatives pass is best-effort (returns silently when the chain-rule map cannot resolve a derivative), and Solve lowering compiles any surviving der(...) call to the constant 0.0. The result is a zeroed derivative term and silently wrong trajectories with no diagnostic, exactly the failure mode this heuristic pipeline should error on.
- **Evidence:** state_row_reduction.rs:402 demotes unmatched states: `let matched_states = states_with_assignable_derivative_rows(dae, &state_names); ... dae.variables.states.shift_remove(&name)` with no check that der(name) is gone. Downstream, crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/scalar_ops.rs:273: `rumoca_core::BuiltinFunction::Der => self.emit_const_at(0.0, span),` — surviving der() becomes literal zero instead of an error.
- **Verifier correction:** SPEC_0007's "No der() on RHS" row cannot literally mean no Der builtin in eq.rhs (DAE residuals carry der(state) in rhs by design); the enforced invariant is the expand_compound_derivatives doc contract that only der(state) survives to Solve lowering. Also the hazard is broader than demoted states: any der() reaching the scalar builtin fallback (scalar_ops.rs:273) — including der(state) in an unhandled non-ODE row shape — lowers to constant 0.0 silently. Additionally the demotion loop cascades: demoting one state of a coupled derivative row un-gates the row for the remaining matched state, which is then demoted too, leaving multiple orphan der() calls.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read state_row_reduction.rs:402-442 (demotion via matching loss, no der-ref check; cascades on coupled rows via all_active_derivative_args_are_states), symbolic.rs:1569-80 (unresolved der kept silently), scalar_ops.rs:273 (Der => Const 0.0, unconditional). Refutation attempts failed: incidence.rs builds DerState unknowns only for states and counts der(alg) operands as plain variable occurrences, so BLT can match without singularity; der-of-algebraic diagnosis exists only in --inspect structure_report; derivative_coefficient_expr returns 0.0 coefficient without error; no validation pass rejects surviving der(non-state). Spec citation loose but core claim intact.
- **Remediation:** After the final demotion pass, scan all runtime partitions for der(v) where v is not a state and fail with a spanned StructuralError (or re-promote). Separately, make phase-solve scalar lowering reject Der instead of emitting Const(0.0); the SPEC_0007 DAE contract already declares der() on RHS a bug.

#### [HIGH] Regular-family incidence validation is dead code; release fallback silently degrades

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/incidence.rs:136` — SPEC_0032 §1 (structured families stay authoritative) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** The debug invariant promised for the corner-synthesized incidence path is a no-op: debug_check_one_regular_family computes the synthesized rows, compares, and just returns on mismatch — no debug_assert, no log. Its doc comment still claims it 'Panics (debug builds only)'. In release, apply_regular_family_corner_incidence leaves placeholder interior incidence rows when a family is not corner-derivable ('degraded, but no panic'). A misclassified `regular` family therefore produces wrong incidence rows in all build types, which corrupts matching/BLT and can either report spurious singularity or build a wrong evaluation order silently.
- **Evidence:** incidence.rs:136-149: `fn debug_check_one_regular_family(...) { ... for (offset, synthesized_row) in ... { if eq_unknowns.get(row) != Some(&synthesized_row) { return; } } }` — comparison result discarded; doc says 'Panics (debug builds only, via the single caller's cfg! guard) when ... a synthesized row disagrees'. incidence.rs:113-117 comment: 'leave the placeholder rows in release (degraded, but no panic in production)'.
- **Verifier correction:** One overstatement: 'wrong incidence rows in all build types' — the non-corner-derivable cheapened case DOES panic in debug builds via the debug_assert at incidence.rs:116, and materialized families keep their correct walked incidence in every build. The genuinely silent exposure is (a) release builds with cheapened non-uniform families proceeding on placeholder incidence, and (b) loss of the debug/CI oracle for corners-uniform-but-interiors-differ misderivations, which the dead check was documented to catch. Core defect and recommendation stand.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read incidence.rs: debug_check_one_regular_family (136-149) discards its comparison (mismatch -> bare return), contradicting its 'Panics' doc; git -L shows it shipped dead in c76c15b, unchanged. apply pass (107-129) keeps placeholder rows in release when synthesize declines; cheapening is the production default (flatten lib.rs:146). classify_regular_for_family accepts any affine coeffs, but uniform_translation needs one shared delta per row, so legal models (e.g. der(x[i])=y[2*i]) hit the release fallback: interior incidence collapses to {der(x[cell])}, corrupting matching/BLT. Not handled elsewhere (solve stencil path is independent).
- **Remediation:** Restore the debug_assert (or better: return a StructuralError) on synthesized/materialized mismatch, and in release treat a non-corner-derivable cheapened family as a hard error or force re-materialization — never proceed with placeholder incidence rows. Note the file may be mid-edit by another agent; verify before fixing.

#### [HIGH] Substitution application is quadratic with whole-DAE clones per step

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/eliminate/substitution_application.rs:33` — SPEC_0021 · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** apply_substitutions_to_expr_with_derivatives iterates the entire substitution list per expression (O(S·|expr|)); the boundary scan applies the running list to every equation per pass (O(E·S)); resolve_boundary_equations_to_fixpoint re-applies the cumulative list to all DAE partitions after every pass; and apply_substitutions_to_remaining_once / apply_substitutions_to_dae_partitions each clone the entire DAE just to serve as a derivative source. dae_prepare compounds this: index_reduce_missing_state_derivatives clones the DAE per round, and apply_constrained_dummy_derivative_plan clones the whole DAE per single-state demotion. For MSL-scale models with thousands of connection aliases this is O(S²·|expr|) plus O(D) full-DAE clones — a compile-time wall OpenModelica avoids with union-find alias sets.
- **Evidence:** substitution_application.rs:33: `let derivative_source = dae.clone();` (again at line 97); eliminate/mod.rs:1357: `for sub in substitutions { if expr_contains_substitution_target(&out, sub) { ... } }` per expression; eliminate/mod.rs:273 comment 'Reapply the cumulative set'; dae_prepare/mod.rs:2021: `let mut staged = dae.clone();` per constrained-dummy plan; state_row_reduction.rs:466/633: `let mut reduced = dae.clone();` per index-reduction round.
- **Verifier correction:** index_reduce_missing_state_derivatives (state_row_reduction.rs:633) clones the DAE once per invocation and then iterates in place; the per-call clone at line 466 belongs to the `_once` variant, which is only called from tests. All other evidence points are accurate, and the per-expression cost is understated: apply_record_field_aggregate_substitutions also rebuilds aggregate/complex substitution groups from the full list for every expression.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified every cited site: per-expression loop over all substitutions with a contains-walk each (eliminate/mod.rs:1364), plus per-expression rebuild of aggregate substitution groups (worse than claimed); running-list application per equation in boundary scan (boundary_scan.rs:67); cumulative re-application to all partitions each fixpoint pass (mod.rs:273); dae.clone() at substitution_application.rs:33/97 solely as derivative context; one full-DAE clone per single-state constrained-dummy demotion (dae_prepare/mod.rs:2021, caller commits one plan per round). No name-indexed or union-find substitution mechanism exists in eliminate/dae_prepare. One detail wrong: index_reduce_missing_state_derivatives clones once per invocation, not per round.
- **Remediation:** Index substitutions by target name (one hash lookup per VarRef during a single rewrite walk instead of S passes), close the substitution set transitively once (union-find for pure aliases), and thread a borrowed derivative context instead of cloning the DAE. Commit multiple demotion plans per round where independence is provable.

#### [MEDIUM] All structural algorithms run on materialized scalar incidence, O(N·E) in array size

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/incidence.rs:334` — SPEC_0032 §1 (domain payloads compact) · category: tensor
- **Claim:** Unknown columns are materialized one per scalar element via rendered names (scalar_name_text_for_flat_index), incidence rows are one HashSet per scalar equation (even corner-synthesized families expand to num_cells*per_cell HashSets), and Kuhn's matching is O(V·E) on that scalarized graph. BLT, Tarjan, tearing, and state-to-row matching all operate at scalar granularity. For an N-cell discretized model this makes structural analysis O(N) memory and up to O(N·E) time, defeating the compact-domain goal: the regular-family metadata proves uniform translation structure that could give a family-level matching/BLT in O(corners), but nothing exploits it beyond incidence construction.
- **Evidence:** incidence.rs:334-344: `for flat_index in 0..size { let scalar_name = dae::scalar_name_text_for_flat_index(name.as_str(), &var.dims, flat_index); push_unknown(...) }`; incidence.rs:205-221 synthesizes one HashSet per cell (`for cell in 0..num_cells { ... synthesized.push(...) }`); matching.rs:19-29 runs per-equation augmenting paths over all scalar rows.
- **Verifier correction:** Not a spec violation: SPEC_0032 §2 explicitly sanctions scalar views in "structural phases", and §1's compactness rule targets IR payload serialization with a carve-out for materialized scalar views. Also, family metadata is exploited beyond nothing — corner synthesis reconstructs interior incidence without walking interior bodies (though rows are still O(N)). Real but bounded compile-time scalability limit, correct results; downgrade high → medium. The family-level matching fast path is a valid optimization recommendation, not a defect fix.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read incidence.rs, matching.rs, lib.rs, blt/tarjan/tearing. All cited evidence holds: per-scalar unknown columns via scalar_name_text_for_flat_index, num_cells*per_cell HashSets even for corner-synthesized families (written back by apply_regular_family_corner_incidence), scalar Kuhn matching with a vec![false; n_var] per equation (Θ(n_eq·n_var) floor), and zero family awareness in blt.rs/tarjan.rs/tearing.rs or any other crate. Claim survives; only its severity framing over-reaches.
- **Remediation:** Add a family-level fast path: match a regular family's rows against its translated unknown-block wholesale (one decision per family, not per cell), and only fall back to scalar Kuhn matching for irregular residue. SPEC_0032 permits scalar views, but the O(N) decision layer is the main obstacle to the range-preserving goal at scale.

#### [MEDIUM] Structural identity is string-keyed and re-parses flattened names

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/dae_prepare/state_row_reduction.rs:301` — SPEC_0007 Flat→DAE cross-cutting rules (DefId keying; no string tokenizing) · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** The phase's semantic keys are rendered strings throughout: DefiningExprIndex = IndexMap<String, ...>, der maps HashMap<String, Expression>, state-name HashSet<String>, and scalar identity built by formatting "base[i,j]" strings (expression_exact_name/append_subscripts) then re-parsed with rumoca_core::parse_scalar_name and dae::component_base_name (31 non-test uses of string-parsing helpers crate-wide). SPEC_0007 explicitly calls hashing rendered names after resolution a phase-boundary bug and forbids recovering name hierarchy by tokenizing flattened strings inside semantic phases. Beyond compliance, string keys make every hot map lookup hash a full path string and make shadowing/rename hazards possible.
- **Evidence:** state_row_reduction.rs:301-329: `fn expression_exact_name(...) -> Option<String> { ... Some(format!("{base_name}.{field}")) }` and `append_subscripts` building `format!("{base}[{}]")`; line 235-237: `rumoca_core::parse_scalar_name(&exact_name).is_some_and(|scalar| scalar.base == state_name.as_str())`; dae_prepare/mod.rs:27-28: `type DefiningExprIndex = IndexMap<String, Vec<IndexedDefiningExpr>>; type AliasSafetyCache = IndexMap<(String, Option<usize>), bool>;`.
- **Verifier correction:** Evidence and spec violation confirmed, but severity is medium not high: these are compile-time passes (not simulation-hot), VarName interning already gives cheap hashing where used, string re-parsing in the cited path fails conservatively (skips reduction) rather than producing wrong results, and helper-use count is ~27 non-test, not 31.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read state_row_reduction.rs (expression_exact_name/append_subscripts building "base[i,j]" strings at 301-347, parse_scalar_name re-parse at 235), mod.rs:27-28 String-keyed DefiningExprIndex/AliasSafetyCache, derivative_map.rs HashMap<String,Expression>, HashSet<String> in direct_demotion/connection_alias; 27 non-test string-parsing helper uses (claim said 31; tree in flux). SPEC_0007 cross-cutting rules verbatim forbid both patterns. Not mitigated elsewhere: interned VarName exists but these maps bypass it with plain String.
- **Remediation:** Introduce a structural key type (DefId or interned VarId + flat index) for all dae_prepare/eliminate maps and replace parse_scalar_name/component_base_name calls with structured (base, indices) keys carried from scalarization metadata. This is a prerequisite for both SPEC_0007 compliance and cheap hashing in the hot passes.

#### [MEDIUM] eliminate_derivative_aliases skips initialization and event-action partitions

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/dae_prepare/mod.rs:553` — MLS 8.6 (initialization uses all equations) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** When a derivative-alias variable is eliminated (alias equation removed, variable dropped from algebraics), the substitution alias→der(state) is applied to continuous, discrete, conditions, relations, synthetic roots, and clock constructor expressions — but not to dae.initialization.equations, clocks.triggered_conditions, or events.event_actions. The sibling helper rewrite_state_derivative_everywhere covers exactly those three surfaces, showing the omission is an inconsistency, not policy. An initialization equation or assert condition referencing the removed alias variable is left dangling; the IC solver then reads an undefined key (typically defaulting to 0), producing wrong initial conditions silently.
- **Evidence:** dae_prepare/mod.rs:553-575 substitution loop covers `continuous.equations, discrete.real_updates, discrete.valued_updates, conditions.equations, conditions.relations, events.synthetic_root_conditions, clocks.constructor_exprs` — no `initialization.equations`, no `events.event_actions`, no `clocks.triggered_conditions`. Contrast rewrite_state_derivative_everywhere (mod.rs:931-961) which includes all three. The alias var is then removed: `dae.variables.algebraics.shift_remove(var_name)` (line 586).
- **Verifier correction:** Failure mechanism differs slightly from the claim: the IC solver does not read an undefined key defaulting to 0. Instead, initial_residual_equations (rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/initial_residual.rs:51-58) silently filters out an initialization equation whose only reference is the removed alias (e.g. steady-state `der_T = 0`), yielding silently wrong/underdetermined initial conditions; if the equation also references a live unknown, lowering fails loudly with LowerError::MissingBinding. Impact (silent wrong ICs or spurious rejection) and the recommended fix stand.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read mod.rs:495-589: substitution loop covers exactly the 7 partitions cited, omitting initialization.equations, clocks.triggered_conditions, events.event_actions; sibling rewrite_state_derivative_everywhere (931-961) covers all three. Pipeline (structural_lowering.rs:50) runs after init equations are populated; inline_hidden_component_algebraics skips vars referenced in init eqs, so the alias survives. Downstream, initial_residual.rs:55 silently drops an init equation whose only ref is the removed var (wrong ICs), or lowering hard-errors with MissingBinding. Not handled elsewhere.
- **Remediation:** Route eliminate_derivative_aliases through the same partition-complete rewrite helper used by rewrite_state_derivative_everywhere (or a shared all-partitions visitor) so no runtime/initialization surface can retain a reference to the removed variable.

#### [MEDIUM] Any substitution touching a structured family drops it; BLT elimination skipped for array models

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/eliminate/mod.rs:487` — SPEC_0032 §1/§4 (structured families authoritative; AffineStencil is source-proven) · category: tensor
- **Claim:** drop_structured_families_touching_equations discards a family's compact-domain metadata whenever any substitution changes any of its rows — even a uniform substitution (e.g. a parameter alias) applied identically across all cells that provably preserves regularity. Independently, eliminate_trivial's Phase B (`blocks.filter(|_| !uses_scalar_view)`) skips BLT elimination entirely when any equation has scalar_count != 1. The combined effect on array-native models: either tensor structure is destroyed by elimination or elimination is not performed — alias-heavy discretized models get the worst of both, since boundary-phase substitutions routinely touch family rows.
- **Evidence:** eliminate/mod.rs:487-500: `fn drop_structured_families_touching_equations(...) { dae.continuous.structured_equations.retain(|family| { ... !touched_sorted.iter().any(|&idx| idx >= family.first_equation_index && idx < block_end) }) }` with comment 'the safe structural-elimination behavior is to scalarize rewritten families'. mod.rs:221: `if let Some(blocks) = blocks.filter(|_| !uses_scalar_view) {` — Phase B gated off whenever scalar views are in use.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read eliminate/mod.rs:487-500 — drop is unconditional on any body change, uniform or not; both call sites (substitution_application.rs:86, :393) confirmed; no family-template rewrite or regeneration pass exists anywhere (grepped crates/). Phase B gate `blocks.filter(|_| !uses_scalar_view)` confirmed at mod.rs:221 with uses_scalar_view = any scalar_count != 1; sim pipeline runs eliminate_trivial before scalarize and never again, so array models get no BLT/tearing elimination. SPEC_0032 §1/§4 forbid downstream stencil rediscovery, so dropped families force permanent scalar fallback. Correctness preserved (scalar rows intact), so bounded perf/structure loss: medium stands.
- **Remediation:** For substitutions whose replacement is cell-invariant (parameter/constant/scalar alias), rewrite the family template instead of dropping the family; and allow Phase B elimination on the rows outside family blocks even when uses_scalar_view is true.

#### [MEDIUM] Constrained-dummy analysis bakes parameter values into float coefficients with 1e-12 pruning

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/dae_prepare/dummy_state_metadata.rs:39` — MLS Appendix B (structural analysis should not depend on parameter values) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** LinearRow accumulates equation coefficients as f64 with parameter values evaluated at compile time, and drops any term whose accumulated coefficient magnitude is <= 1e-12. Structural state-selection decisions (which state is a constrained dummy, which constraint defines it) therefore depend on runtime-parameter magnitudes: a legitimate tiny coefficient (1e-15 F capacitance, unit-scaled coefficients) is treated as structurally absent, and cancellation to ~1e-12 from rounding flips structure. The pass pins baked parameters as non-tunable, which is honest, but the epsilon threshold can misclassify the structure itself, silently selecting a wrong dummy state or missing a constraint term.
- **Evidence:** dummy_state_metadata.rs:15: `const LINEAR_EPSILON: f64 = 1.0e-12;`; lines 37-44: `fn add_term(&mut self, name: VarName, coeff: f64) { let next = ... + coeff; if next.abs() <= LINEAR_EPSILON { self.terms.shift_remove(&name); } ... }`; line 65: `self.terms.retain(|_, coeff| coeff.abs() > LINEAR_EPSILON);`.
- **Verifier correction:** Two overstated details: (1) the "1e-15 F capacitance" example cannot trigger this — scalar_linear_rows skips any residual containing der(), so capacitor/derivative equations never enter the linear-row analysis; only parameter-scaled algebraic constraints (ratios, gains, unit conversions) do. (2) A uniformly tiny row is dropped entirely (empty-terms filter), yielding a missed demotion with fallback to the dummy-derivative-group path, not a wrong selection. The real risk is mixed-magnitude rows and near-1e-12 cancellation during elimination, where partial pruning silently alters candidate selection and the substituted der(dummy) expression. Original constraint equations are preserved, bounding the error to the derivative substitution and state choice.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read dummy_state_metadata.rs: LINEAR_EPSILON=1e-12 (l.15), add_term drops |coeff|<=eps (l.37-44), retain (l.65); structural_numeric_constant (l.425-482) bakes parameter start values into f64; Gaussian elimination pivots on |coeff|>1e-12 (l.692-709). No downstream re-verification: plan (mod.rs:1701) checks only smoothness/der-map; apply (mod.rs:2017) substitutes der(dummy) with float-literal reconstruction. Structure thus depends on parameter magnitudes; pruning is silent. Claim survives.
- **Remediation:** Make the structural decision symbolic (is the coefficient expression identically zero?) and use numeric magnitude only as a tie-breaking preference; at minimum scale the epsilon by the row's max coefficient (relative threshold) and emit a diagnostic when a term is pruned.

#### [MEDIUM] stateSelect=always/never not enforced in matching-based demotion passes

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/dae_prepare/state_row_reduction.rs:402` — MLS 4.9.7 (StateSelect) · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** MLS defines stateSelect=always as 'do use it as a state' and never as 'never use it as a state'. The early demotion paths honor Always (direct demotion and constrained-dummy plans skip StateSelect::Always), but the late cleanup passes — demote_states_without_derivative_refs, demote_states_without_assignable_derivative_rows, demote_states_without_retained_derivative_rows — demote any unmatched state regardless of stateSelect, silently overriding an explicit always annotation with no diagnostic. Conversely nothing ever demotes a stateSelect=never variable that has a plain der() row; never is only a rank tie-breaker in alias-representative selection.
- **Evidence:** state_row_reduction.rs:402-441: demotion loop `for name in to_demote { ... dae.variables.states.shift_remove(&name) ... }` contains no state_select check; contrast direct_demotion.rs:244-247: `if state.state_select == rumoca_core::StateSelect::Always { counters.n_skip_always_state += 1; return None; }` and dummy_state_metadata.rs:104-111 with the same guard.
- **Verifier correction:** Claim is accurate as stated. Severity stays medium: the Always override only triggers on the fallback path where bipartite matching finds no assignable derivative row (after index reduction has already run), and the resulting dynamics are typically still consistent — the defect is silent non-compliance with an explicit annotation plus a missing error, not generally wrong results.
- **Verifier note:** Read state_row_reduction.rs in full: demote_states_without_derivative_refs (l.375), demote_states_without_assignable_derivative_rows (l.402), demote_states_without_retained_derivative_rows (l.449) demote unmatched states via states.shift_remove with no state_select check and no diagnostic. Contrast guards exist exactly as cited: direct_demotion.rs:244 (n_skip_always_state), dummy_state_metadata.rs:104-111, mod.rs:1709, dummy_derivative_group.rs:177-185 (Always excluded from dummy candidates). Grep across crates found no diagnostic for overriding Always and no pass demoting Never states with der() rows — Never appears only as rank tie-breaker (alias_metadata.rs:5, mod.rs:843) and candidate filter. Not handled elsewhere.
- **Remediation:** In the matching-based demotion passes, treat StateSelect::Always states as undemotable (error with span if no derivative row can be assigned, since that is a genuine model/reduction failure) and prefer demoting Never/Avoid states first when the matching leaves a choice.

#### [LOW] ODE sign normalization ignores multiplied coefficients and assumes positional state↔row pairing

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/dae_prepare/state_row_reduction.rs:736` — MLS Appendix B.1a · category: correctness
- **Claim:** normalize_ode_equation_signs pairs state i with equation i positionally (an undocumented reordering precondition shared with ic_plan's n_x convention) and uses der_sign_in_expr, whose Mul rule returns the sign of whichever operand contains der() while ignoring the sign of the multiplier: `(-2)*der(x)` yields +1. Rows whose der coefficient is a negative product (e.g. `0 = f + (-k)*der(x)` after substitution folding) are not negated even though the mass-matrix formulation documented in the function requires coefficient +1, and rows negated by position can be the wrong row entirely if the reordering precondition is violated upstream.
- **Evidence:** state_row_reduction.rs:738-742: `for (i, state_name) in state_names.iter().enumerate() { if i >= dae.continuous.equations.len() { break; } let sign = der_sign_in_expr(&dae.continuous.equations[i].rhs, state_name, 1);` — positional pairing. Lines 698-704 (Mul case): `let l = der_sign_in_expr(lhs, ...); if l != 0 { return l; } der_sign_in_expr(rhs, state_name, current_sign)` — multiplier sign never inspected.
- **Verifier correction:** The described sign-walk and positional-pairing flaws are accurately reported, but normalize_ode_equation_signs is dead code in the production pipeline: its only caller is its own unit test (state_row_reduction.rs tests, ~line 906). der_sign_in_expr has no other callers. Active coefficient handling goes through split_linear_der_target in dae_prepare/symbolic.rs. Severity drops from medium to low: a latent bug in an exported-but-unused helper (plus stale doc comments in mod.rs:361,485 referencing a nonexistent reorder_equations_for_solver pass), not an active correctness defect.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read state_row_reduction.rs: positional state-i/equation-i pairing (lines 738-742) and Mul-arm sign blindness (698-704) are both exactly as claimed; traced (-2)*der(x) to +1. However, git grep across working tree AND HEAD shows normalize_ode_equation_signs has zero production call sites — only its own unit test invokes it (re-exported from mod.rs:73 but unused). The referenced reorder_equations_for_solver pass exists only in comments. Defect is real in the code but latent/dead, so no simulation impact today.
- **Remediation:** Compute the actual structural coefficient of der(state) (reuse split_linear_der_target from symbolic.rs, which already tracks multiplicative factors) instead of a sign walk, and key the pass on the recorded ODE-row assignment rather than positional index i.

#### [LOW] Symbolic solvers limited to unit coefficients; unmatched BLT unknown uses "???" sentinel

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/eliminate/solve_for_unknown.rs:60` — SPEC_0021 (no string sentinels) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** try_solve_for_unknown and split_unit_affine_residual only solve residuals where the unknown's coefficient is exactly +1/-1 (plus one scaled-product pattern in dae_prepare); `2*z + y = 0` is unsolvable, so such equations escape boundary elimination, tearing causalization (stable_solution_for_unknown returns None, aborting the whole torn block), and IC ScalarDirect blocks, falling back to Newton or remaining as loops. Bounded impact (missed optimization, never wrong results). Relatedly, scc_to_block fabricates an UnknownId named "???" for an unmatched scalar block instead of a typed absent-match representation — a string sentinel that lib.rs's own docs admit working around.
- **Evidence:** solve_for_unknown.rs:66-70: `match coef { 1 => Some(negate_expr(...)), -1 => Some(remainder), _ => None }`; blt.rs:30: `None => UnknownId::Variable(rumoca_core::VarName::from("???")),`; lib.rs:106-109 doc: 'without relying on string sentinels or unmatched `???` BLT blocks'.
- **Verifier correction:** Spec citation is loose: SPEC_0021 is a code-complexity/maintainability spec and contains no explicit "no string sentinels" rule; the authority for the sentinel complaint is the codebase's own doc comment at lib.rs:109. All code-level evidence otherwise verified verbatim.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read solve_for_unknown.rs: coef match is exactly {1, -1, _=>None}; splitter handles only Add/Sub with bare VarRef target, so 2*z+y=0 returns None. tearing_elimination.rs:58 returns Ok(()) on None, aborting the whole torn block. Callers confirmed in eliminate/mod.rs (737/832/1334) and ic_plan.rs ScalarDirect. dae_prepare's LinearRow solver handles real coefficients but only for dummy-state/state-row paths, not eliminate/tearing/IC. blt.rs:30 "???" sentinel and lib.rs:109 doc admitting the workaround both present; sentinel-bearing Scalar blocks are consumed downstream (eliminate, ic_plan, phase-solve). Newton fallback bounds impact — low severity holds.
- **Remediation:** Extend the affine splitter to divide by nonzero literal/parameter coefficients (guarding zero via structural bindings), which directly improves tearing causalization depth; replace the "???" sentinel with Option<UnknownId> in BltBlock::Scalar.

### A.solve-lower — Solve lowering (phase-solve + ir-solve) (11 confirmed)

#### [CRITICAL] der() silently lowered to constant 0.0 in general expression position

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/scalar_ops.rs:273` — MLS 3.7.4 / Appendix B (v contains x-dot) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** Any der() expression that reaches generic scalar or array lowering (root conditions, discrete update rows, observations, initial residuals, event action conditions) is silently replaced by the constant 0.0. MLS Appendix B's variable vector v explicitly includes x-dot, so relations like `when der(x) > 0` are legal; phase-dae condition_lowering.rs contains no Der rewriting and phase-solve appendix_b_validation.rs (1088 lines) has no Der gate, so such expressions flow through and produce a permanently-false root function or wrong values instead of either the correct derivative or a diagnostic. The adjacent Pre arm correctly errors, proving the intended pattern.
- **Evidence:** scalar_ops.rs:273: `rumoca_core::BuiltinFunction::Der => self.emit_const_at(0.0, span),` while the next arm errors: `BuiltinFunction::Pre => Err(unsupported_at(format!("pre() must be lowered to {} parameters...")))`. Array path array_values/builtins.rs lower_der_array_like_values: `let zero = self.emit_const_at(0.0, span)?; values.resize(count, zero);`. grep confirms zero `Der` matches in phase-solve/src/appendix_b_validation.rs and phase-dae/src/condition_lowering.rs.
- **Verifier correction:** Claim is accurate as stated. Minor note: no test model in the repo exercises der() in a condition/initial position, so the bug is latent on untested-but-legal MLS input; the phase-dae appendix_b_validation.rs (1456 lines) also lacks a Der gate, not just the phase-solve one cited.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified: scalar_ops.rs:273 emits const 0.0 for Der (Pre arm errors); array path resizes with 0.0. Traced dispatch lower.rs:792 -> lower_builtin with no der-slot interception. Confirmed no upstream gate: zero Der matches in both appendix_b_validation.rs files and condition_lowering.rs; phase-resolve only rejects der on discrete vars; ir-dae conditions store raw Expressions; dae_prepare der rewrites are alias/demotion-scoped only. Root conditions, initial residuals, discrete updates all use the generic builder, so legal `when der(x)>0` yields a constant-false root silently.
- **Remediation:** Make Der an error in generic lowering (mirroring Pre), and add an explicit lowering: when the argument is a state whose derivative row exists, load/inline that derivative RHS (or a dedicated der-slot); otherwise reject at appendix-B validation so upstream must rewrite der() out of relations/discrete partitions.

#### [HIGH] Dense O(n_x^2) identity mass matrix built and serialized for every runtime model

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/solve_model.rs:1353` — SPEC_0007 (mass-matrix form is solver-facing derived metadata) · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** state_identity_mass_matrix allocates a Vec<Vec<f64>> identity of size state_count^2 on every Runtime-profile lowering, and ContinuousSolveArtifacts stores/serializes it densely. Since the solve pipeline already reduces to x' = f(x,p,t) (the comment says exactly this), the matrix is always identity here - yet a 10k-state discretized PDE pays 800 MB and a 100k-state model is simply infeasible, killing the tensor-native scaling goal at the packaging step.
- **Evidence:** solve_model.rs:1353-1367: `for idx in 0..state_count { let mut row = ...; resize(state_count); row[idx] = 1.0; mass.push(row); }`; ir-solve lib.rs:1645: `#[serde(default)] pub mass_matrix: Vec<Vec<f64>>,`; invoked unconditionally at solve_model.rs:407 when profile.needs_solve_artifacts().
- **Verifier correction:** Claim is accurate with two refinements: (1) per-step solver cost is unaffected because PreparedMassMatrix detects identity and stores no dense data at runtime — the cost is confined to lowering-time allocation (twice, producer plus consumer normalization scan) and to serialization of SolveModel (wasm diffsol JSON path; codegen templates are lazy and none currently read mass_matrix); (2) the fix is even cheaper than the proposed enum: PreparedMassMatrix::new already treats an empty mass slice as Identity, so emitting an empty Vec (or the compact enum for wire-format hygiene) works without consumer changes.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read solve_model.rs (dense identity built at fn state_identity_mass_matrix, invoked unconditionally under Runtime profile at the needs_solve_artifacts branch), ir-solve lib.rs:1645 dense serde field, and a twin dense builder at phase-solve/lib.rs:886. Refutation attempts failed: consumer PreparedMassMatrix compresses to Identity but only after allocating a second dense n^2 copy (normalized_matrix), and it already accepts an empty vec as Identity, proving the dense build is pure waste; SolveModel with the dense matrix is JSON-serialized in production via wasm lower_model_to_solve_json for the diffsol addon. SPEC_0007 permits materializing at the runtime boundary, so the defect is representation, not spec violation. Impact is latent (negligible at current MSL model sizes) but an unguarded O(n^2) memory/wire wall against the project's tensor-scaling goals.
- **Remediation:** Replace the payload with a compact enum (Identity { n } | Diagonal(Vec<f64>) | Sparse{coo}) mirroring the existing SparsityPattern design; emit Identity{n} here and let backends expand only if their API requires a dense matrix.
- **Remediation status (implemented):** `rumoca-ir-solve` schema 19 defines `MassMatrix::{Identity, Diagonal, Sparse}` with the dimension supplied by the accompanying `SolveLayout` and COO entries for the general sparse form. Both `lower_solve_artifacts` and runtime-profile Solve-model lowering emit the zero-payload `Identity` variant, deleting both dense builders and their optional-capacity helper. The wire-format regression asserts identity serializes only as `{"kind":"identity"}`, and `solve_artifacts_keep_identity_mass_matrix_compact_for_large_state_counts` proves artifact lowering remains constant-size even with `state_scalar_count = usize::MAX`.

#### [HIGH] String-keyed identity throughout Solve layout and lowering (format/parse round trips)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-ir-solve/src/layout.rs:17` — SPEC_0007 cross-cutting rules (DefId keying; no tokenizing flattened strings) · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** VarLayout binds scalars by rendered name strings (IndexMap<String, ScalarSlot>) and recovers array-element slots by re-parsing names (parse_scalar_name inside binding()); SolveLayout's solver maps are Vec<String> + IndexMap<String,usize> + base_to_indices; lowering renders one name string per scalar element of every array (scalar_name_text_for_flat_index / format_subscript_binding_key at 81 non-test call sites) and then looks those strings back up. Also note binding()'s base-slot+shape derivation fallback exists, but the builder (phase-solve/src/layout.rs:367-369) still inserts per-element string bindings, so the O(N) allocation cost is currently real, not avoided. The same string-identity debt also exists upstream (ir-dae keys variables by VarName), so this is pipeline-wide migration debt, with solve layout/lowering the heaviest consumer.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified every citation: layout.rs:17-18 IndexMap<String,ScalarSlot>; binding() falls back to parse_scalar_name (layout.rs:395-401); ir-solve lib.rs:1946 parse_scalar_name; SolverNameIndexMaps is Vec<String>+IndexMap<String,usize>+base_to_indices (lib.rs:1762-1767); phase-solve builder still materializes one rendered String per array element (phase-solve/src/layout.rs:357-370) and lib.rs:1516-1518 renders per-element names. SPEC_0007 lines 95/98/99 forbid display-string parsing in solver-layer scalarization and string-keyed post-resolution identity. ComponentReferenceKey exists but is only a partial parallel path. Scope ("Flat through DAE" header) and staleness refutations fail; only the 81-call-site count is inflated (I count ~53 non-test).
- **Remediation:** Finish the migration the ComponentReferenceKey (DefId + structured subscripts) type began: key VarLayout/SolveLayout by (DefId, flat-offset) with base-slot + shape derivation (already implemented for the fallback path), and keep rendered strings only in a display-boundary side table for FMI/tooling output.

#### [MEDIUM] Dummy-derivative elimination is a DAE-to-DAE rewrite inside phase-solve

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/dummy_derivative.rs:37` — SPEC_0007 Stage 4 'Do NOT do here' + Structural Lowering Placement; SPEC_0029 §12 · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** eliminate_dummy_derivatives performs an expression-level symbolic rewrite (der(x) -> di substituted across all non-defining continuous equations) and clones the entire DAE to do it, at the top of lower_solve_problem. SPEC_0007 states solve lowering must not perform DAE-level structural transformations or expression-level symbolic rewrites and that all DAE structural lowering MUST live in rumoca-phase-structural. The pass also only recognizes scalar, subscript-free states/algebraics, so array-state dummy derivatives silently miss the treatment and can still yield the structurally singular Newton system the pass exists to prevent.
- **Evidence:** dummy_derivative.rs:46-55: `let mut rewritten = dae_model.clone(); for (idx, equation) in rewritten.continuous.equations.iter_mut()... equation.rhs = rewriter.rewrite_expression(&equation.rhs);` invoked from lib.rs:294: `let dummy_eliminated = dummy_derivative::eliminate_dummy_derivatives(dae_model);`. scalar_var_ref_name only matches `VarRef { subscripts: [] }`.
- **Verifier correction:** Claim is accurate except two nuances: (1) the array-state gap yields a hard solver failure (structurally singular LU, unfactorable), not silently wrong results, and per the module doc that failure is loud; (2) the clone is one-time per lowering and only when dummy definitions exist, so it is not a major perf issue. This is a project-spec placement violation (explicit MUST in SPEC_0007/SPEC_0029 §12), not an MLS violation, so medium rather than high under the given scale. Also: dummy_derivative.rs:26 doc-links crate::ensure_implicit_system_determined, which no longer exists anywhere in the workspace.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read dummy_derivative.rs: lines 46-55 clone the full DAE and rewrite der(x)->di across non-defining equations, called from lib.rs:294 at top of lower_solve_problem. SPEC_0007 Stage 5 verbatim bans "DAE-level structural transformations ... expression-level symbolic rewrites" in phase-solve, and Placement requirement + SPEC_0029 §12 mandate such DAE-to-DAE rewrites live in rumoca-phase-structural. Not handled elsewhere: phase-structural's reduce_constrained_dummy_derivatives is a different pass. scalar_var_ref_name (subscripts.is_empty()) confirms scalar-only coverage.
- **Remediation:** Move the pass into rumoca-phase-structural as a finalizing DAE-to-DAE transformation (where index reduction and state selection already live), extend it to array states, and delete the full-model clone by rewriting equations in place during that phase.

#### [MEDIUM] Tensor nodes recovered by lowering all scalar rows then reassembling

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/residual_compute_block.rs:29` — SPEC_0032 §2 'No scalar-row reassembly'; SPEC_0007 Stage 4 AffineStencil rule · category: tensor
- **Claim:** build_residual_compute_block first lowers every scalar row of every equation family (cloning each row's op vector and cloning the family expression per row to build a per-row access proof), then push_structured_programs re-groups rows into Map/AffineStencil nodes by proving uniformity. SPEC_0032 §2 explicitly forbids scalar-row reassembly in Solve lowering; even with provenance attached, the process is O(total scalar rows) in time and peak memory per family, so a 1M-cell grid pays 1M scalar lowerings + proofs to produce one compact node. An in-code comment admits this path previously caused O(N^2)/O(N^3) compile blowups.
- **Evidence:** residual_compute_block.rs:31-63: `for row_offset in 0..scalar_count { let Some(ops) = residual_rows.get(residual_index).cloned() ... access_proof: residual_row_access_proof(...) }` where residual_row_access_expression does `expression.clone()` per row; stencil.rs:196-205 comment: 'each call re-scanning the entire remaining family (the O(N^2)/O(N^3) compile blowup on large grids)'.
- **Verifier correction:** Claim overstates cost: (1) per-row cloning is of the per-cell/per-element expression, not the whole family expression; (2) a 1M-cell grid pays 1M cheap placeholder rows, not 1M full scalar lowerings, for cheapened regular families (state-derivative and parameter-variability bodies, the dominant PDE cases) since flatten defaults to materialize_structured_families=false; full per-row lowering remains only for non-cheapenable algebraic/non-regular families; (3) the O(N^2)/O(N^3) blowup is historical and fixed (batch scalarization + corner-only stride derivation); remaining cost is linear with nontrivial constants. The spec-letter violation of SPEC_0032 §2 stands, but §4's prohibition targets "anonymous" rows and these rows are provenance-tagged, so severity is medium (real but bounded, known transitional P1/P3 migration), not high.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read residual_compute_block.rs (per-row loop cloning ops and building per-row access proofs), stencil.rs push_structured_programs/structured_tensor_at (regroups rows into Map/AffineStencil), and SPEC_0032 §2 which verbatim forbids "No scalar-row reassembly" in Solve lowering. The O(N)-row intermediate vec is real. But rows carry family provenance (not anonymous per §4), strides come from corner rows only, and default cheapening (materialize_structured_families=false) makes interior rows O(1) placeholders for state-derivative/param families; the O(N^2)/O(N^3) comment is a fixed historical bug.
- **Remediation:** Lower structured DAE families directly: emit one base program from the family's binder-symbolic body plus stride terms derived from the StructuredIndexDomain (which the DAE already owns), and generate scalar rows only as the eval-solve fallback view - never as the intermediate representation inside phase-solve.

#### [MEDIUM] Compact tensor-node bookkeeping enumerates the full index domain

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-ir-solve/src/lib.rs:470` — SPEC_0032 §1 'Domain payloads are compact / avoids O(N) metadata' · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** TensorOutputMap::output_indices and output_count call StructuredIndexDomain::index_tuples(), which materializes Vec<Vec<i64>> for every point of the domain, and output_count then scans for the max. ComputeBlock::output_count (hence .len()) calls this for every Map/AffineStencil node, and it runs in release paths (derivative_rhs.len() in lower_solve_problem) plus twice more per node in shape validation. For a compact node over a 1M-point grid this allocates ~1M small vectors just to compute a length that is derivable in closed form from binder extents and strides.
- **Evidence:** lib.rs:474-476: `let index_tuples = domain.index_tuples().map_err(...)?;` inside output_indices; lib.rs:490-495 output_count takes `self.output_indices(domain)?.into_iter().max()`; core/structured_domain.rs:268: `pub fn index_tuples(&self) -> Result<Vec<Vec<i64>>, ...>`; called from ComputeBlock::output_count at lib.rs:733 and validate_tensor_output_map at lib.rs:1022-1037.
- **Verifier correction:** The "twice more per node in shape validation" portion is not a standard release cost: validate_tensor_output_map runs inside validate_shape_contract, which is gated by ir_boundary_validation_enabled() (debug_assertions, test, or strict-ir-validation feature; phase-solve lib.rs:504,515). Also SPEC_0032 §1's "Domain payloads are compact / avoids O(N) metadata" row targets IR serialization, not in-memory computation, so the spec citation is indirect. The unconditional release-path hit is derivative_rhs.len() via build_implicit_rhs_compute_block — a one-time-per-lowering cost, not per timestep.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read ir-solve/lib.rs: output_indices (line 470) calls domain.index_tuples() (structured_domain.rs:268) materializing one Vec<i64> per domain point; output_count (486) maxes over the full vector. ComputeBlock::output_count (713) invokes it per Map/AffineStencil node, and derivative_rhs.len() at phase-solve implicit_rhs.rs:94 runs unconditionally in release lowering (phase-solve lib.rs:454). Closed-form max from strides/binder counts is valid (output_ordinal_delta, binder_value_count); corner_ordinals() shows O(rank) precedent. Cost is one-time per lowering, not per step — medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Compute output_count analytically: for each stride term, max contribution = stride * (value_count-1) for positive strides (0 for negative), summed with start; validate bounds by checking the min/max corners of the domain instead of enumerating all tuples.

#### [MEDIUM] Dynamic Y-array subscripts lower to O(N) select chains

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower.rs:1277` — MLS 10.5 · category: tensor
- **Claim:** lower_dynamic_subscripted_binding handles a runtime subscript by emitting one comparison + select per candidate array element (lower_dynamic_indexed_binding_candidates loads every element slot), unless the target is a contiguous constant parameter array (LoadIndexedP fast path). There is no LoadIndexedY analogue, so a runtime index into a state/algebraic array of size N costs ~3N ops per access - quadratic total for a family that indexes dynamically per row - and destroys any stencil uniformity for the containing family. Additionally the shared clamp semantics (resolve_indexed_slot rounds then saturates) silently return the boundary element for out-of-range indices where MLS 10.5 requires a bounds violation to be detected.
- **Evidence:** lower.rs:1284-1295: `for (indices, value) in candidates { ... let cond = self.emit_subscript_match_at(&selectors, &indices, emit_span)?; merged = self.emit_select_at(cond, value, merged, emit_span)?; }`; linear_op.rs:318-331: `resolve_indexed_slot`: `if rounded < 0.0 { 0 } else if rounded as usize >= count { count - 1 }`.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read lower.rs: lower_dynamic_subscripted_binding falls back to a per-element compare+select chain (lines ~1284-1296) after try_lower_indexed_param_load, whose grid helpers accept only constant-parameter slots. Grep of all crates shows the LinearOp enum has LoadIndexedP/LoadIndexedSeed but no dynamic Y load, and no downstream pass collapses Y select chains. resolve_indexed_slot (linear_op.rs:318-331) round-then-saturates; the select chain silently yields 0.0 on no match. Project spec SPEC_0022 ARR-024 explicitly requires the MLS 10.5 bounds assertion, which neither path generates.
- **Remediation:** Add a LoadIndexedY op symmetric to LoadIndexedP (states/algebraics are contiguous per base, as VarLayout::derive_array_element_binding already assumes), and give indexed loads an optional bounds-check mode that raises a runtime event/assertion instead of clamping, for MLS 10.5 conformance.

#### [MEDIUM] Visible observations silently dropped on typed 'unsupported' declines

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/solve_model/visible.rs:191` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** lower_visible_observations skips any observation whose lowering fails with DynamicSubscript, ForRangeUnknownDimension, MissingActualArgument, DynamicBindingBase, or MissingFunction - the variable simply disappears from visible_names/visible_value_rows with no warning surfaced. A user plotting a reconstructed variable that hits any of these declines sees it vanish from results rather than an error or NaN column, which reads as data loss and can mask genuine lowering regressions (a new decline variant silently shrinks output coverage).
- **Evidence:** visible.rs:78-79: `Err(err) if should_skip_unbound_observation(layout, &err) => {} Err(err) if should_skip_unsupported_observation(&err) => {}` and visible.rs:191-203 lists the five silently-skipped variants.
- **Verifier correction:** One nuance: the claim's "a new decline variant silently shrinks output coverage" masking argument is overstated — the classification is a closed variant list and any error NOT in it fails the compile (doc comment at visible.rs:188-190), so a new LowerError variant would surface as a hard error unless someone also adds it to the skip list. The core issue stands: existing declines drop variables from visible_names/result columns and from visible_values_for_names lookups with no warning or record anywhere in the pipeline.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read visible.rs: lines 78-79 discard errors matching should_skip_unsupported_observation (DynamicSubscript, ForRangeUnknownDimension, MissingActualArgument, DynamicBindingBase, MissingFunction at lines 191-203) with empty arms; no name/reason is recorded. Grepped workspace: skip predicates used only here, no warn!/tracing/report in phase-solve, no LoweringReport for skips. Downstream, runtime.rs visible_values_for_names silently `continue`s on missing names (~line 1598) and rk45/diffsol result columns come straight from visible_names, so the variable vanishes from output with no signal. Intentional (doc comment + tests lower_skips_visible_observation_*) but genuinely unsurfaced.
- **Remediation:** Record skipped observations (name + decline reason) in the SolveModel or a lowering report and emit a tracing warning, so tooling can show 'variable not recorded: dynamic subscript unsupported' instead of dropping the signal.

#### [MEDIUM] Per-scalar visible expressions and observation rows materialize O(N) scalar view in payload

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/solve_model/visible.rs:334` — SPEC_0032 §2 (scalar views are derived, compact owners upstream) · category: tensor
- **Claim:** visible_expressions_for_dae synthesizes one Expression + name String per scalar element of every state/algebraic/output/input/discrete array, and lower_visible_observations lowers each into an individual scalar program stored in SolveModel.visible_value_rows (most are trivial LoadY/LoadP identity rows). For grid models this puts O(N) expressions, O(N) strings, and O(N) two-op scalar programs into every runtime payload, even though the identity mapping is fully derivable from VarLayout shapes - the scalar view is materialized at the owner instead of derived at the consumer.
- **Evidence:** visible.rs:350-369: `for idx in 0..size { ... expressions.push(visible_expression_for_variable_scalar(name, var, dae::scalar_name_text_for_flat_index(...), subscripts)?) }`; visible.rs:95-124 emits a per-scalar `[Load*, StoreOutput]` row for each identity observation.
- **Verifier correction:** Two refinements: (1) the O(N) VisibleExpression list is transient lowering state — the payload stores O(N) names, O(N) 2-op rows plus one Span per row, not the expressions themselves; (2) VarLayout.bindings already serializes one string-keyed entry per scalar (phase-solve/src/layout.rs:367-368, 402-403), so the payload is O(N) regardless — visible rows add a constant factor plus O(N) interpreted ops per output sample, not a new asymptotic class. Also, SPEC_0032 §1 carves out "explicitly materialized scalar view", so this is ownership/design debt (spec assigns scalar-view generation to rumoca-eval-solve) rather than a hard spec violation. Medium (real but bounded) is correct.
- **Verifier note:** Verified in visible.rs: visible_expressions_for_variable loops 0..size building one Expression+String per scalar (lines ~350-369), and lower_visible_observations emits a per-scalar [Load*, StoreOutput] row for identities (~95-125). SolveModel serializes visible_names + visible_value_rows; rk45/diffsol/eval-solve consume them row-by-row (eval-solve runtime.rs:1540-1542 interprets each identity row per sample) — no compact/derived identity path exists anywhere. SPEC_0032 §2/§5 assigns scalar-view generation to rumoca-eval-solve. Claim survives.
- **Remediation:** Represent identity observations compactly (a slice descriptor per array base: base slot + shape -> contiguous outputs) and only lower genuine reconstruction expressions to scalar programs; let eval-solve expand the identity view on demand.

#### [MEDIUM] Projection BLT unknown resolution is a quadratic linear scan

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lib.rs:1310` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** projection_y_index resolves each BLT unknown back to its solver-Y index with a linear position() scan over incidence.unknown_names, and it is called once per unknown of every scalar/loop block in lower_blt_projection_blocks. For an algebraic subsystem with U unknowns this is O(U^2) UnknownId comparisons; on discretized models where most cells are projected algebraics this becomes a measurable compile-time term on top of the already O(rows*ops) def-use scan in lower_projection_plan.
- **Evidence:** lib.rs:1313-1319: `projection_incidence.incidence.unknown_names.iter().position(|candidate| candidate == unknown).and_then(|idx| projection_incidence.unknown_y_indices.get(idx).copied())` invoked per unknown from lower_blt_projection_blocks (lib.rs:1334, 1412).
- **Verifier note:** Re-read lib.rs: projection_y_index (now ~1309-1319) does exactly the cited linear position() scan over incidence.unknown_names, called per Scalar-block unknown (1335) and per AlgebraicLoop unknown (1412). BLT unknowns partition U total unknowns, and U = solver_scalar_count - state_scalar_count (all projected algebraics, line 996-1009), so total work is O(U^2). No cached map exists: algebraic_projection_incidence builds unknown_positions (BTreeMap, line 1253) but drops it instead of storing it in ProjectionIncidence. Worse, unknown_names[i] is always UnknownId::SolverY(unknown_y_indices[i]), so resolution could be O(1) by matching the SolverY variant plus a membership check. Runs once per lowering with cheap enum compares, so medium (bounded compile-time cost) is the right severity.
- **Remediation:** Build one HashMap<UnknownId, usize> (or reuse the local-index mapping already constructed in algebraic_projection_incidence) before iterating blocks, making resolution O(1) per unknown.

### A.runtime — Runtime evaluation engines (11 confirmed)

#### [HIGH] Instantiate-time function eval silently skips while/break, producing wrong structural constants

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-ast/src/eval_instantiate/function_eval.rs:228` — MLS 11.2.2.3, 12.4 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** The integer function interpreter used during instantiation (which feeds array-dimension and structural-parameter evaluation via eval_integer_function_call / try_eval_integer_shape_expr) treats Statement::While and Statement::Break as successfully-evaluated no-ops (Some(false) = continue) instead of returning None (cannot evaluate). A pure function whose output is computed in a while loop const-evaluates to the pre-loop value; a for loop containing break runs to completion. The wrong integer is then used silently, e.g. as an array dimension.
- **Evidence:** ast::Statement::Empty
| ast::Statement::Break { .. }
