| ast::Statement::While(_)
| ast::Statement::When(_)
| ast::Statement::Reinit { .. }
| ast::Statement::Assert { .. } => Some(false),
— contrast eval-flat/constant/function_eval.rs which fully implements While (eval_while_statement) and Break (FlowControl::Break). Some(false) here means 'handled, continue', so the loop body is skipped yet evaluation reports success.
- **Verifier correction:** Exposure is narrower than claimed: the primary component-dimension path (resolve_component_dimensions in rumoca-phase-instantiate/src/dims.rs) treats any non-literal shape expr as needs_late_recompute and discards the early eval, deferring to later phases whose evaluator (rumoca-eval-flat/src/constant/function_eval.rs) handles while/break correctly. The silent-wrong-value paths are instantiate-time consumers without that guard: non-primitive array expansion and zero-sized-array dropping in component_loop.rs:50, type-alias dimensions in dims.rs:76, and other try_eval_integer_expr uses with function calls. Also, outputs with no binding/start and no pre-loop assignment yield None (safe refusal), so the bug requires a seeded output. Real, silent, structural when triggered, but bounded => high rather than critical.
- **Verifier note:** Verified lines 226-231 of eval_instantiate/function_eval.rs: While/Break => Some(false) = "handled, continue" (Some(true)=return, None=abort), while the bool interpreter in the same file implements While (interpret_bool_function_while, MAX_FUNCTION_LOOP_ITERATIONS) and returns None for For. Wrong value is reachable: initialize_function_locals seeds outputs from binding/start, so a while-computed output folds to its pre-loop seed and reports success; interpret_function_for runs full range past Break. The value IS trusted: component_loop.rs:50 uses evaluate_array_dimensions (no late-recompute guard) for zero-size dropping and non-primitive array expansion, and dims.rs:76 (resolve_type_alias_dimensions) trusts it. eval-flat's evaluator implements both correctly, confirming the asymmetry.
- **Remediation:** Return None for While and Break in interpret_function_statement so evaluation aborts conservatively (matching the bool interpreter's For => None convention), or implement while/break with the same iteration limit used elsewhere (MAX_FUNCTION_LOOP_ITERATIONS). Add a regression test: a function computing its output via while must either evaluate correctly or refuse to fold.
- **Resolution (2026-07-24):** Resolved. The duplicate integer-only
  instantiation statement walker was removed. Integer and Boolean scalar
  functions now use the same `MixedLocals` interpreter and the same
  `FunctionStmtFlow` contract as the typecheck-time AST evaluator. `while`
  consumes `break` at the loop boundary, nested `return` propagates to the
  function boundary, unsupported statements fail conservatively, and focused
  seeded-output regressions cover both `while`/`break` and nested `return`.

#### [HIGH] sign(0) evaluates to 1 at runtime (f64::signum), violating MLS and diverging from compile-time eval

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-solve/src/lib.rs:1914` — MLS 3.7.3 (sign) · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** BuiltinFunction::Sign lowers to UnaryOp::Sign (phase-solve/lower/scalar_ops.rs:94), and the Solve runtime evaluates it as value.signum(), which returns 1.0 for +0.0 and -1.0 for -0.0. MLS defines sign(0)=0. At event instants (friction, switching models) the argument is exactly 0 by construction, so this yields wrong values precisely where it matters. eval-flat's constant evaluator (builtins.rs eval_sign) and the phase-solve test oracle (lower/tests.rs apply_unary) both correctly return 0 — the evaluators have drifted.
- **Evidence:** pub(crate) fn eval_unary(op: UnaryOp, value: f64) -> f64 {
    match op {
        ...
        UnaryOp::Sign => value.signum(),
— while crates/rumoca-eval-flat/src/constant/builtins.rs eval_sign returns 0.0 for x==0, and crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/tests.rs:1054 uses partial_cmp with `_ => 0.0`. phase-solve compile_time.rs:249 and layout.rs:759 also use f64::signum.
- **Verifier correction:** Claim is correct but understates the spread: besides eval-solve and the two cited phase-solve sites, sign(0) is also folded via f64::signum in rumoca-core/src/modelica_builtins.rs:198, phase-solve/lower/statements/compile_time.rs:305, and phase-flatten (constant_injection.rs:639,1379; conditional_and_eval.rs:520). Only eval-flat, the phase-solve test oracle, and the Cranelift JIT backend are MLS-correct, making results backend-dependent.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read eval-solve lib.rs:1909-1932: UnaryOp::Sign => value.signum(), so sign(0)=±1 at runtime (interpreter, linear fast path, reverse.rs). MLS requires sign(0)=0; the repo's own eval-flat eval_sign (builtins.rs:161-184) and phase-solve test oracle (tests.rs:1080-1084) return 0, and critically the Cranelift JIT (emit.rs:1048-1056) emits the correct three-way select — so the same IR gives different sign(0) per backend. No zero-guard rewrite exists in lowering (scalar_ops.rs:94 maps directly). Cited signum folds in compile_time.rs:249/layout.rs:759 verified; more exist (core modelica_builtins.rs:198, phase-flatten constant_injection.rs:639/1379).
- **Remediation:** Replace with the MLS-correct three-way comparison (>0 → 1, <0 → -1, else 0) in eval_unary, and fix the sibling f64::signum uses in phase-solve compile_time/layout so folding and runtime agree. Add a sign(0) parity test across eval-flat, eval-solve, and any JIT backend.

#### [HIGH] Guarded division rewrites x/0: 0/0→0 and -x/0→+Inf, diverging from IEEE and from compile-time folding

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-solve/src/lib.rs:1949` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** BinaryOp::Div uses guarded_division: any nonzero numerator over 0 returns +INFINITY (sign of the numerator is discarded, so -1/0 = +Inf, which is simply the wrong limit), and 0/0 returns 0.0 instead of NaN, silently masking structural singularities that the NaN-tracing and non-finite validation layers are designed to catch. The test eval_row_guarded_division_matches_jit_semantics shows this is deliberate cross-backend policy, but eval-flat's evaluators refuse to fold division by zero (phase_constant: (r != 0.0).then_some(l / r)), so whether a subexpression is constant-folded changes observable simulation behavior.
- **Evidence:** fn guarded_division(lhs: f64, rhs: f64) -> f64 {
    if rhs == 0.0 {
        if lhs == 0.0 { 0.0 } else { f64::INFINITY }
    } else {
        lhs / rhs
    }
}
— and tests.rs:367 eval_row_guarded_division_matches_jit_semantics asserts 0/0 == 0.0.
- **Verifier correction:** The divergence is miscited: eval-flat/eval-ast refusing to fold ((r != 0.0).then_some(l/r)) defers to guarded runtime semantics, so that path does not diverge. The actual compile-time/runtime divergence is crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/statements/compile_time.rs:260, which folds Div as raw IEEE l/r (0/0->NaN, -1/0->-Inf), plus SPEC_0034_GALEC_EFMI_EXPORT.md T14 mandating silent IEEE +/-inf in exported code. All other evidence and the recommendation stand.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read guarded_division in rumoca-eval-solve/src/lib.rs:1949 and rumoca-exec-cranelift/src/emit.rs:1128: both return 0 for 0/0 and unsigned +Inf otherwise; test at tests.rs:367 pins it; reverse.rs:388 mirrors it in AD. It applies to every Div in both backends (emit.rs:750, lib.rs:971/1136), not just Newton probes, and is undocumented. nan_trace.rs exists to name NaN/inf sources; 0/0->0 bypasses it. Fold/runtime divergence is real via phase-solve compile_time.rs:260 (raw IEEE l/r), and SPEC_0034 T14 requires IEEE +/-inf for GALEC export.
- **Remediation:** At minimum preserve the numerator sign (lhs.signum()*INFINITY) and let 0/0 produce NaN so the existing non-finite diagnostics (non_finite_value_error, nan_trace) fire with a named variable. If guarded semantics must stay for solver-probe robustness, confine them to the Newton-probe path and document the deviation; ensure compile-time and runtime division semantics match.

#### [HIGH] Five drifting expression evaluators; two pairs duplicate evaluation over the same IR type

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-flat/src/phase_constant/mod.rs:637` — SPEC_0029 §3b/§5 · category: architecture
- **Claim:** SPEC_0029 §5 sanctions one eval crate per IR, but the area contains parallel evaluators over the same representation: eval-flat/constant (Result<Value> evaluator over rumoca_core::Expression) and eval-flat/phase_constant (a second Option<f64/i64/bool> evaluator over the same rumoca_core::Expression, reimplementing unary/binary/if/builtin/function-call dispatch), plus eval-ast/eval (typecheck-time) and eval-ast/eval_instantiate (instantiate-time) as two more independent AST walkers, and phase-solve's own compile-time folders. These have already drifted: sign(0) is 0 in eval-flat/constant but 1 in phase-solve folding and the Solve runtime; division-by-zero errors at compile time but is guarded at runtime; while-loops work in eval-flat but are skipped in eval-ast.
- **Evidence:** phase_constant/mod.rs try_eval_real_with_context re-implements the operator table over rumoca_core::Expression:
rumoca_core::Expression::Binary { op, lhs, rhs, .. } => { ... match op { OpBinary::Add => Some(l + r), ... OpBinary::Div => (r != 0.0).then_some(l / r), ... } }
— the same crate's constant/mod.rs contains a full parallel evaluator for the identical Expression type, and eval-ast holds two more (TypeCheckEvalContext walker and IntegerEvalEnv/MixedLocals walkers).
- **Verifier correction:** SPEC_0029 §5 governs crate-level alignment; constant and phase_constant both live inside rumoca-eval-flat, so no literal crate-boundary violation exists and §3b's single-source table does not list these evaluators — the spec citation overreaches. Also, eval-ast's eval_instantiate does interpret while-loops in its bool-function path (function_eval.rs:435) while skipping them in another statement path (line 228), so "while skipped in eval-ast" is only partially accurate — though that internal inconsistency itself reinforces the drift thesis. The sign(0)=1 behavior in phase-solve folding and the Solve runtime is a genuine MLS 3.7.3 deviation, justifying high severity despite the softened spec framing.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read phase_constant/mod.rs (try_eval_real_with_context at ~609 with its own OpBinary match, Div guarded via then_some at 644, second table ~1801) and constant/mod.rs (full Value evaluator over the same rumoca_core::Expression, DivisionByZero at 895-914, eval_sign returning 0 for zero). Verified sign(0)=1 via f64::signum in phase-solve compile_time.rs:249, layout.rs:759, statements/compile_time.rs:305 and eval-solve eval_unary — a real MLS 3.7.3 divergence. eval-ast has two independent walkers; While skipped at function_eval.rs:228 but interpreted at 435. Duplication and drift confirmed.
- **Remediation:** Collapse phase_constant's expression walking into the constant Value evaluator (a thin typed adapter over one core), and make eval-ast's two walkers share one statement/expression interpreter parameterized by lookup environment. Put a builtin-semantics conformance table (sign/div/mod/rem/floor/range) in one crate with cross-evaluator parity tests so the remaining evaluators cannot drift.
- **Resolved (2026-07-24):** `ast_scalar` now owns integer, Real, and Boolean
  syntax dispatch for the typecheck, instantiation, and shape-evaluation
  policies, and function execution shares `FunctionStmtFlow`. Flat
  `phase_constant` scalar entry points now delegate to the single `Value`
  interpreter; the module retains only shape inference and typed compatibility
  adapters. Solve evaluation remains correctly separate for its distinct IR
  and shares builtin semantics through `rumoca-core`.

#### [MEDIUM] Map/AffineStencil tensor nodes are eagerly scalarized at prepare time; interpreter has no native loop kernel

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-solve/src/compute_block_scalarize/affine.rs:41` — SPEC_0032 §4 · category: tensor
- **Claim:** PreparedComputeBlock lowers ComputeNode::Map and ComputeNode::AffineStencil to a PreparedScalarProgramBlock by materializing every index tuple (domain.index_tuples()) and cloning the full base_ops program per tuple with rewritten load indices (prepared.rs prepared_affine → scalarize_affine_rows). The compact SPEC_0032 domain that survived Flat/DAE/Solve dies at the evaluator: memory is O(domain_size × program_len), and execution pays per-row register-file reinit and dispatch instead of a fused stride loop. Only MatMul/LinSolve get native kernels. This is spec-legal (eval-solve owns the scalar-view fallback) but means the flagship tensor-native pipeline currently executes structured families element-by-element in the reference runtime.
- **Evidence:** for index_tuple in &index_tuples {
    let mut ops = cloned_linear_ops(base_ops, kind, span)?;
    for stride in load_strides { apply_affine_load_stride(&mut ops, stride, ...)?; }
    ...
    rows.push(ops);
}
— and prepared.rs:1178-1181 routes both Map and AffineStencil through this into PreparedComputeNode::ScalarPrograms; contiguous_output_range() returns None for ScalarPrograms, so no tensor fast path exists for these nodes.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read affine.rs (scalarize_affine_rows_with_span clones base_ops per index tuple, lines 41-66) and prepared.rs (prepared_affine at 1155-1184 wraps rows into PreparedComputeNode::ScalarPrograms; Map and AffineStencil both route there at 1235-1260; the enum at 1019-1042 has no loop variant; contiguous_output_range returns None for ScalarPrograms). Per-row PreparedRowEval dispatch confirmed. Native rendering exists only in codegen backends (WGSL), not the interpreter, matching SPEC_0032 §4/§5 which sanctions eval-solve as the scalar fallback. Claim is factually accurate and spec-legal as stated; medium severity fits (real bounded perf/memory cost, no correctness impact).
- **Remediation:** Add a PreparedComputeNode::AffineLoop variant that keeps one base program plus stride metadata and iterates the domain at eval time (recomputing LoadY/LoadP indices and Const offsets in registers), eliminating the O(N) program clones and giving the interpreter a true tensor execution path; keep the current scalarization only for consumers that genuinely need materialized scalar rows.

#### [MEDIUM] Per-RHS-call Vec allocations in the algebraic refresh hot loop

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-solve/src/runtime.rs:464` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** refresh_slots_with_plan runs on every ODE RHS evaluation (eval_state_derivatives → refresh_derivative_dependencies) and on every root-condition evaluation, and it allocates a fresh solver-length Vec snapshot each call via copy_runtime_values (support.rs builds a new Vec with try_reserve + extend). For any model with algebraic slots this is a heap allocation (plus copy) per RHS call, in the single hottest loop of the simulator. update_rows.rs similarly allocates two fresh y/p snapshots per fixed-point iteration, and eval_scalar_program_block allocates its output Vec per call (eval_dynamic_time_event_rows runs per event-window query).
- **Evidence:** let incoming = copy_runtime_values(args.solver_y, "algebraic projection snapshot")?;
— support.rs: pub(super) fn copy_runtime_values(...) -> Result<Vec<f64>, _> { let mut copy = Vec::new(); reserve_runtime_vec_capacity(&mut copy, values.len(), context)?; copy.extend_from_slice(values); Ok(copy) } — called unconditionally whenever plan.rows or the simultaneous plan is non-empty, i.e. once per derivative/root evaluation.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read runtime.rs: refresh_slots_with_plan (line 464) calls copy_runtime_values, which support.rs:15 implements as a fresh Vec::new + try_reserve_exact + extend per call, whenever plan.rows or simultaneous_plan is non-empty. It sits on the RHS path (eval_state_derivatives_at_solver_y:1694 → refresh_derivative_dependencies:423) and both root-condition paths (834, 874); rk45 lib.rs:669 and diffsol ode.rs:591/lib.rs:584+ call these per step/stage. The snapshot is only read on error-rollback paths (470, 498) yet allocated unconditionally — worse than claimed. update_rows.rs:27-28 allocates two snapshots per fixed-point iteration; runtime.rs:1095 allocates an output Vec per eval_scalar_program_block call. The RefCell scratch pattern (refresh_probe_scratch:284, root_scratch:288) and copy_runtime_values_into already exist, so the fix is idiomatic here and the concern is not handled elsewhere. One alloc+memcpy per RHS call is real but bounded: medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Add a RefCell<Vec<f64>> snapshot scratch on SolveRuntime (the pattern already used for refresh_probe_scratch/root_scratch) and copy into it instead of allocating; do the same for the update-row snapshots (two reusable buffers on the runtime) and let eval_dynamic_time_event_rows write into a caller/scratch buffer.

#### [MEDIUM] Scalarized LinSolve costs O(n^4): LinearSolveComponent re-runs full Gaussian elimination per component and allocates per op

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-solve/src/linear_solve.rs:47` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** When a LinSolve node is consumed through the scalar path (to_scalar_program_block emits one LinearSolveComponent per solution component; the runtime's implicit_scalar_rhs — used for every refresh-row and per-row Jacobian evaluation — is exactly such a scalarization), each component op rebuilds the augmented matrix (heap-allocating AugmentedMatrix::zeroed) and re-runs the full O(n^3) elimination, so one n-dimensional solve costs O(n^4) plus n allocations per evaluation. The tensor path (solve_all_unchecked) correctly factors once; the scalar fallback does not share the factorization. MultiBody-class models with 6x6+ kernel solves hit this on every refresh-row evaluation.
- **Evidence:** pub(super) fn solve_component_unchecked(...) -> Result<f64, EvalSolveError> {
    validate_component(n, component)?;
    let mut matrix = build_augmented_matrix_unchecked(regs, matrix_start, rhs_start, n)?;
    if gaussian_eliminate(&mut matrix).is_none() { ... }
    Ok(matrix.solution_component(component))
}
— AugmentedMatrix::zeroed allocates a fresh Vec every call; the op executes once per component within the same row program over identical matrix registers.
- **Verifier note:** Verified chain: scalarize_linsolve (dense.rs:21-31) emits one LinearSolveComponent per component sharing matrix_start/rhs_start/n. Runtime eval (lib.rs:1128) routes each to solve_component_unchecked, which build_augmented_matrix_unchecked heap-allocs AugmentedMatrix::zeroed (Vec) and re-runs full gaussian_eliminate O(n^3) with no cached factorization -> O(n^4)+n allocs per solve. Tensor solve_all_unchecked factors once. Runtime refresh hot path uses implicit_scalar_rhs (PreparedScalarProgramBlock from to_scalar_program_block), evaluated per row (refresh_batch.rs:112), running all n solve ops. No interpreter memoization. Efficiency-only, bounded; medium correct.
- **Remediation:** Memoize the factorization within a single program evaluation (e.g., cache keyed by (matrix_start, rhs_start, n) in RowEvalScratch, invalidated per program run), or lower scalarized linsolve to one solve-all op writing n registers; also reuse a scratch matrix buffer instead of allocating per op.

#### [MEDIUM] String-keyed evaluation contexts with dotted-path splitting in eval-ast/eval-flat

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-ast/src/eval/mod.rs:30` — SPEC_0007 Stage 2 cross-cutting rules · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** SPEC_0007 requires post-resolution identity to be keyed by DefId and forbids recovering name hierarchy by tokenizing flattened strings inside evaluator logic. eval-ast's TypeCheckEvalContext keys every value map by String and resolves scope by repeatedly splitting the dotted scope string (lookup_by_scope / find_map_top_level_splits_rev); eval-flat's EvalContext keys parameters by rendered flat paths ("component.subcomponent.param") and phase_constant resolves record fields by format!("{base_name}.{field}") lookups. Beyond the spec violation, these string-hash maps sit in flatten-phase hot loops (per-equation constant folding), affecting compile-time scaling on large MSL models.
- **Evidence:** fn lookup_by_scope<'a, T>(name: &str, scope: &str, map: &'a FxHashMap<String, T>) -> Option<&'a T> {
    ...
    if let Some(val) = rumoca_core::find_map_top_level_splits_rev(scope, |base, _suffix| try_scope(base)) { return Some(val); }
— and eval-flat constant/mod.rs: /// Parameter values by name (e.g., "component.subcomponent.param" -> value)
pub parameters: EvalIndexMap<Value>, — plus phase_constant/mod.rs: resolve_varref_real(&format!("{base_name}.{field}"), ctx).
- **Verifier note:** Re-read eval/mod.rs: lookup_by_scope splits dotted scope strings over String-keyed FxHashMaps via find_map_top_level_splits_rev, verbatim as claimed. eval-flat constant/mod.rs keys parameters by rendered flat-path Strings; phase_constant/mod.rs:145,627 build format!("{base_name}.{field}") lookups. SPEC_0007 Stage-2 cross-cutting table verbatim forbids string-keyed post-resolution identity and tokenizing flattened strings "inside compiler/evaluator/lowering logic". Both contexts are used in Stage-2 phases (typecheck_instanced, phase-flatten constant_injection.rs:1653, equations/mod.rs). No exemption found in SPEC_0029; core helper location does not exempt semantic-phase use. Not MLS-wrong-results, so medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Key instantiate/flatten-time evaluation environments by DefId (or VarNameId per SPEC_0029 §3a) with structured (id, field-path) keys for record members; keep string rendering only at diagnostic/display boundaries. This is a prerequisite for consolidating the duplicated evaluators anyway.

#### [MEDIUM] MSL random generators are hash substitutes, not the specified algorithms; impure-random cache grows unboundedly

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-solve/src/random_runtime.rs:117` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** RandomResult/RandomState claim to implement Modelica.Math.Random.Generators.Xorshift64star/128plus/1024star, but random_result_and_state hashes the state array together with the generator name string and derives both the sample and the 'next state' from splitmix-style scrambling — not the xorshift recurrences the MSL defines in normative Modelica code. Sequences, statistical structure, and cross-tool reproducibility all diverge from Dymola/OpenModelica; state components are additionally clamped to u31. Separately, impure_random_sample caches every sample keyed by (t.to_bits(), call_site) with no eviction, so an impure random evaluated in the continuous RHS grows a HashMap entry per distinct solver time — unbounded memory over long runs.
- **Evidence:** let mut state = seed_values.iter().fold(0u64, |acc, value| acc ^ scramble_seed(value.to_bits()))
    ^ scramble_seed(random_generator_name(generator).bytes().fold(...));
...
Ok((unit_from_u64(xorshift64star_next(&mut sample_state)), out))
— and impure_random_sample: let key = (t.to_bits(), call_site); ... stream.cached_by_event_call.insert(key, value); with no pruning (only SimulationRuntimeState::clear at simulation end).
- **Verifier note:** Re-read random_runtime.rs: random_result_and_state (lines 117-143) hashes state via splitmix-style scramble_seed plus an FNV hash of the generator name — not the MSL xorshift recurrences — and clamps state to u31 (clamp_i64_to_positive_u31). runtime_intrinsics.rs routes the actual MSL names (Xorshift64star/128plus/1024star .random/.initialState) to this substitute; grep across crates/ found no real xorshift implementation (rumoca-eval-dae runtime_specials.rs:431 is the same hash scheme). impure_random_sample inserts into cached_by_event_call keyed by (t.to_bits(), call_site) with no eviction; the only clear is SimulationContext::drop (lib.rs:511). Both halves verified; medium fits (bounded, no crash, still pseudo-random).
- **Remediation:** Implement the actual MSL xorshift64*/128+/1024* state transitions (they are short, fully specified algorithms) so results are tool-reproducible; document any intentional deviation. Bound the impure cache (e.g., keep only entries for the current event/accepted-step time, clearing on time advance).

#### [MEDIUM] Real range evaluation accumulates the step and uses absolute epsilon, dropping endpoints (e.g., 0:0.1:0.3)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-flat/src/constant/function_eval.rs:1080` — MLS 10.4.3 (range vector) · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** eval_range_values builds Real ranges by repeated accumulation (v += step) and terminates with an absolute f64::EPSILON tolerance. MLS defines the element count as n = floor((stop-start)/step) computed once (with tolerance proportional to the step). With accumulation, 0:0.1:0.3 gives 0.1+0.1+0.1 = 0.30000000000000004 > 0.3 + 2.2e-16, yielding 3 elements instead of 4; long ranges accumulate drift in the element values themselves. Since constant ranges feed for-equation expansion and array constructors at flatten time, this silently changes array sizes.
- **Evidence:** let mut v = s;
if step_f > 0.0 {
    while v <= e + f64::EPSILON {
        values.push(Value::Real(v));
        v += step_f;
    }
}
— absolute epsilon (2.2e-16) is smaller than one ULP of typical endpoints, and v carries accumulated rounding error (0.1*3 accumulation exceeds 0.3).
- **Verifier correction:** The example is backwards: 0:0.1:0.3 yields 4 elements with this code (accumulation error 4.4e-17 < f64::EPSILON), and the strict MLS 10.4.3 count formula floor((0.3-0)/0.1)+1 = 3 actually expects 3. The real failure is ranges with endpoints >= ~1 where absolute epsilon is below one ULP: 1:0.1:2 produces 10 elements ending at 1.9000000000000008, dropping 2.0, while the MLS formula floor((2-1)/0.1)+1 = 11 includes it. Also note MLS 10.4.3 specifies no tolerance; "tolerance proportional to step" is a tool convention already used by the project's sibling evaluators (rumoca-eval-dae extend_range_values, rumoca-phase-solve compile_time.rs eval_range: |step|*1e-9+1e-12), making eval-flat inconsistent with the rest of the pipeline. Recommendation (count-once via s + i*step, mirrored tests) stands.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read eval_range_values (function_eval.rs:1077-1089): accumulation + absolute f64::EPSILON confirmed. Simulated in IEEE doubles: the claimed example is wrong — 0:0.1:0.3 gives 4 elements (drift 4.4e-17 < eps), and strict MLS floor formula gives 3 there. But the defect is real: 1:0.1:2 drops the 2.0 endpoint (10 vs MLS-formula 11) since eps is sub-ULP above 1.0, and values drift on long ranges. Sibling evaluators (eval-dae extend_range_values, phase-solve compile_time) use |step|*1e-9+1e-12 tolerance, so lengths disagree across pipeline stages. Mechanism confirmed; example corrected.
- **Remediation:** Compute n = max(0, floor((e - s)/step + eps_rel)) + 1 with eps_rel proportional to |step| (or the MLS-recommended formulation), then generate elements as s + i*step. Mirror the same fix in any sibling range evaluators (eval-ast, phase_constant) and add 0:0.1:0.3 / 1:0.1:2 regression tests.

#### [MEDIUM] build_eval_context represents known 1-D array dims as zero-filled placeholder arrays and drops multi-dim arrays

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-flat/src/phase_constant/mod.rs:52` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** The flatten-phase EvalContext builder encodes 'array with known dimensions' by materializing a Vec of Value::Integer(0) placeholders for 1-D arrays only (v.len() == 1); multi-dimensional arrays get no entry at all. Two hazards: (a) any later constant evaluation that indexes such a parameter (arr[i]) receives 0 rather than 'unknown', silently folding a wrong constant into conditions or dimensions; (b) size()/shape queries on 2-D+ parameters fail even when dims are known. It also allocates O(N) heap values just to carry a length — the antithesis of the SPEC_0032 compact-domain philosophy applied at compile time.
- **Evidence:** for (k, v) in array_dims {
    if v.len() == 1 {
        let arr: Vec<Value> = (0..v[0]).map(|_| Value::Integer(0)).collect();
        eval_ctx.add_parameter(k.clone(), Value::Array(arr));
    }
}
— constant/mod.rs applies subscripts to parameter Values directly, so arr[2] on a placeholder yields Integer(0), indistinguishable from a real zero.
- **Verifier correction:** Hazard (b) is largely mitigated: size()/shape on multi-dim params is resolved by the primary path (eval_size_integer_with_context consulting array_dims), so it does not "fail when dims are known" except in the rarely-reached fallback. The core valid concern is hazard (a): indexing a 1-D placeholder yields a confident Integer(0) instead of unknown. The O(N) allocation note is a minor style point.
- **Verifier note:** Verified in code: build_eval_context materializes Value::Array([Integer(0);N]) only for v.len()==1; multi-dim omitted. This eval_ctx is the fallback in eval_integer_param_bindings (ctx_and_tests.rs:733). try_eval_integer_with_context has no Index arm, so indexed reads always hit the fallback, where eval_var_ref+apply_subscripts (constant/mod.rs:1209/1235) returns Integer(0) — a confident wrong constant, not None. Hazard (a) is real. Hazard (b) is overstated: multi-dim size(M,dim) is handled by primary eval_size_integer_with_context via array_dims, independent of the placeholder. Reachability of (a) depends on indexed array-param reads surviving to this fallback (uncertain vs. prior scalarization), so medium (bounded) is apt, not critical.
- **Remediation:** Introduce an explicit shape-only value (e.g., Value::ArrayShape(Vec<usize>) or a separate dims map consulted by size/ndims) so element reads on shape-only entries return 'not constant' instead of 0, and so multi-dimensional shapes are representable without materialization.

### A.solvers — Integrators & simulation runtime (10 confirmed)

#### [HIGH] Periodic time events silently truncated at 200,000 ticks

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver/src/timeline.rs:115` — MLS 3.7.5 (sample) · SPEC_0032 §1 (compact domain payloads) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** SolveStopSchedule::new materializes every periodic event tick over the entire [t_start, t_end] horizon up front (schedule.rs collect_solve_scheduled_events -> periodic_event_times), and collect_periodic_event_times silently stops after 200,000 accumulated events. For a long horizon with a fast sample rate (e.g. sample(0, 1e-4) to t_end=100 => 1,000,000 ticks), all sample() events after tick 200,000 are dropped with no error: the sampled controller silently stops updating partway through the run while integration continues. The cap is also shared across all schedules, so one fast clock starves the others. A production stack computes the next event time on the fly.
- **Evidence:** timeline.rs:114-117: `tick += 1.0; if !tick.is_finite() || events.len() > 200_000 { break; }` inside collect_periodic_event_times, called from schedule.rs:156 `periodic_event_times(schedules, t_start, t_end)` where t_start/t_end are the full simulation horizon (SolveStopSchedule::new). No error or warning is produced when the cap is hit.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read timeline.rs:94-119: silent break at events.len() > 200_000 in collect_periodic_event_times, shared Vec across schedules (starvation real: cap check inside each schedule loop). schedule.rs:156 materializes over full horizon; verified callers sim_driver.rs:213, rk45 lib.rs:617, diffsol runtime.rs:550 all pass (t_start, t_end) and never rebuild mid-run. No fallback: periodic_schedule_matches_time/scheduled_root_indices_at_time only run at stop times the capped schedule supplies. Single occurrence of cap, no error/warn path. Claim fully survives.
- **Remediation:** Replace whole-horizon materialization with an on-demand iterator that computes the next tick per schedule from (period, phase, current_t) - the tick arithmetic already exists in periodic_schedule_matches_time. If a cap must remain, return an error instead of silently dropping events.

#### [HIGH] max_wall_seconds ignored by default BDF/diffsol path; no budget in shared driver

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver-diffsol/src/lib.rs:180` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** SimOptions::max_wall_seconds is honored only by the rk45 backend (TimeoutBudget in rk45/lib.rs:616). The default Auto->Bdf path (simulate_prepared -> simulate_state_targets) contains no budget check anywhere: neither the diffsol step loop nor the shared sim_driver output/event loop consults a deadline, and SolverDeadlineGuard/panic_on_expired_solver_deadline are exported but never installed by any production code. A chattering or Zeno model (e.g. ideal diode without hysteresis) on the default solver loops root->reset->root forever despite the user-supplied timeout; rumoca-test-msl's plot_compare sets max_wall_seconds believing it bounds runs, and the Python binding exposes it, but on the default backend it is a silent no-op.
- **Evidence:** grep over non-test code: the only TimeoutBudget::new consumer is `crates/rumoca-solver-rk45/src/lib.rs:616: budget: TimeoutBudget::new(opts.max_wall_seconds)`. sim_driver.rs simulate_state_targets (the BDF driver loop) has no budget/deadline reference; SolverDeadlineGuard::install has zero call sites outside its definition.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified: TimeoutBudget::new(opts.max_wall_seconds) is consumed only in rk45 (lib.rs:616, checked at :846). Default SimSolverMode::Auto|Bdf routes to rumoca-solver-diffsol (solve_lowering/entry.rs:197); simulate_prepared->simulate_with_states->simulate_state_targets (sim_driver.rs:203) has zero budget/deadline/wall-clock refs; diffsol crate's only Instant use is perf counters, and its SimError::Timeout is never constructed. SolverDeadlineGuard::install and panic_on_expired_solver_deadline have no call sites outside timeout.rs (only re-exports in rumoca-solver/rumoca-sim lib.rs). plot_compare.rs:233 sets max_wall_seconds then calls in-process simulate_dae (diffsol only; run_command_with_timeout guards only the OMC side); python model.rs:203 likewise. Not an MLS issue, but the documented timeout is a silent no-op on the default backend, allowing unbounded hangs.
- **Remediation:** Thread a TimeoutBudget through simulate_state_targets (check per backend.step() and per event iteration) or install SolverDeadlineGuard around diffsol calls and call panic_on_expired_solver_deadline from the rhs/jac closures; error out with the existing Timeout variant.

#### [HIGH] Interactive BDF session leaks a full ODE problem on every root event

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver-diffsol/src/session.rs:420` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** BdfSession's event_reset_fn rebuilds the entire solver stack after each root event - OdeModel::new (clones all prepared compute blocks), SolveRuntime::new, a new OdeSolverProblem - and then `Box::leak`s the problem to satisfy diffsol's 'static borrow. Every root event in a long-running interactive session (scheduled_sim real-time loop, web viewer) permanently leaks the whole problem plus its captured model clones; an event-dense or lightly chattering model grows memory without bound until the process dies. The rk45 interactive session has the same pattern once per session (rk45/lib.rs:219 Box::leak of SolveRuntime), which also never frees.
- **Evidence:** session.rs:399-434 (inside event_reset_fn closure, executed per root event): `let ode_model = Arc::new(OdeModel::new(&event_reset_model)?); let reset_runtime = SolveRuntime::new(&event_reset_model)?; ... let problem_ref = Box::leak(Box::new(problem)); ... let rebuilt = solver_call("BDF new", ...)` - contrast with the batch path, which reuses one problem and resets solver state via reset_solver_state.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read session.rs: advance_to invokes event_reset_fn on every hit_root (line 526); the closure rebuilds OdeModel::new (5 fresh PreparedCompute/ScalarProgram blocks + cloned external tables/plans, ode.rs:178-219), SolveRuntime::new, a new OdeSolverProblem, then Box::leak at line 420. No Box::from_raw anywhere; old leaked problems are never freed. Batch path (lib.rs:803 DiffsolAdvanceBackend::reset) reuses the problem via reset_solver_state, proving an alternative. rk45 lib.rs:219 leaks once per session as claimed. BdfSession is exposed via rumoca-sim to the wasm web viewer advance_to loop, so growth is unbounded over session lifetime.
- **Remediation:** Reuse the batch path's reset_solver_state (fresh BdfState on the existing leaked problem, as DiffsolAdvanceBackend::reset does) instead of rebuilding+leaking a problem per event; alternatively use a self-referential owner (ouroboros/Rc) so problems are dropped with the session.

#### [MEDIUM] Global panic-hook swap under a process mutex on every BDF step

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver-diffsol/src/bdf.rs:41` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** catch_solver_panic locks a process-global mutex, calls std::panic::take_hook()/set_hook() (each taking std's global hook lock and allocating a boxed hook), runs the closure under catch_unwind, then swaps the hook back - and solver_call("BDF step", ...) wraps every single integration step (lib.rs:718) and every session step (session.rs:852). In bulk simulation (rumoca-sim/bulk.rs runs worker threads), all workers serialize on SOLVER_PANIC_HOOK_LOCK at every step, destroying parallel throughput; additionally, while the hook is swapped, panics from any unrelated thread in the process are routed to the silent location-capturing hook, so their normal panic output is swallowed.
- **Evidence:** bdf.rs:38-56: `let _guard = SOLVER_PANIC_HOOK_LOCK.lock()...; let previous_hook = std::panic::take_hook(); std::panic::set_hook(Box::new(|info| {...})); let result = std::panic::catch_unwind(...); std::panic::set_hook(previous_hook);` called from lib.rs:718 `solver_call("BDF step", || self.solver.step())` - i.e., per step, per thread.
- **Verifier correction:** The code pattern and per-step call sites are exactly as claimed, but the bulk-simulation impact is overstated: run_prepared_simulation_pipeline (rumoca-sim/bulk.rs) has no non-test callers, so no in-repo production path currently runs concurrent BDF steps; serialization would only bite parallel tests or future bulk wiring. The live defects today are (a) per-step global hook swap overhead (minor vs. an implicit BDF step) and (b) panics on concurrent non-solver threads (e.g. scheduled_sim's WS/viewer/external-interface threads) being routed to the silent hook and losing their output during any solver step. Recommendation (install hook once via OnceLock, keep only catch_unwind per step) is sound.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read bdf.rs: catch_solver_panic does hold SOLVER_PANIC_HOOK_LOCK across the whole step and swaps the global panic hook per call; lib.rs:718 and session.rs:852 wrap every BDF step. Silent-hook side effect on unrelated threads is real (scheduled_sim spawns WS/viewer threads). However, bulk.rs's worker pipeline has no non-test caller wiring solver jobs, and the only diffsol consumers are the main-thread sim loop and single-threaded wasm, so "destroys parallel throughput" is latent, not a current major perf problem. Mechanism confirmed; severity bounded.
- **Remediation:** Install the location-capturing panic hook once per process (e.g. OnceLock, chaining to the previous hook and filtering by a thread-local 'in solver' flag); keep only catch_unwind on the per-step path.

#### [MEDIUM] Event right-limit epsilon 1e-6*(1+|t|) is a tolerance-independent error floor that grows with time

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver/src/timeline.rs:225` — MLS 8.5 (event semantics) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** event_right_limit_time steps time forward by max(1e-6*(1+|t|), eps) after events, and the driver bridges [root_t, right_t] by first-order forward-Euler extrapolation of the full state (bracket_event_limits_kernel). The epsilon is relative to absolute simulation time: at t=100 the shift is ~1e-4 s, at t=1000 ~1e-3 s, regardless of rtol/atol. Discrete updates and recorded samples are applied at right_t, so every event carries a state error ~shift*|y'| and a time bias that rtol cannot reduce - visible as trace divergence vs reference tools on long runs (MSL trace parity), and events closer together than the shift alias. CVODE/IDA-class stacks restart exactly at t_event and rely on discrete guard state instead of shifting time.
- **Evidence:** timeline.rs:224-227: `let step = (1.0e-6 * (1.0 + t_event.abs())).max(f64::EPSILON * (1.0 + t_event.abs())); next.max(t_event + step)`; sim_driver.rs:422-429 advances y across the interval with `*slot += dt * d` (forward Euler on the derivative guess). Same epsilon reused for the left limit in runtime.rs:622-624.
- **Verifier correction:** Evidence sub-claim is stale: the left limit does NOT reuse the 1e-6 epsilon — event_left_limit_time (timeline.rs:216-218) uses previous_representable_time (1 ULP), and rumoca-eval-solve/src/runtime.rs:622-624 contains unrelated algebraic-refresh code. Also right_t is capped by the next target time via .min(target_t) in runtime_root_event_application_time (rumoca-solver/src/runtime/event.rs:74), slightly bounding the shift. Core claim otherwise accurate; additionally the same 1e-6 shift is baked into generated FMI2 C code (model.c.jinja:444,483).
- **Verifier note:** Re-read timeline.rs:220-228: step = max(1e-6*(1+|t|), eps*(1+|t|)), tolerance-independent, grows with |t|; test asserts >=1e-6. sim_driver.rs:410-430 bridges [root_t,right_t] by forward Euler on the pre-event derivative; handle_root_crossing (752-821) applies the discrete update, records, sets current_t, and resets the solver at right_t, never at t_event, so crossings inside the shift are skipped. No tolerance coupling exists elsewhere; relation-memory overrides don't remove the shift. Same pattern emitted in fmi2 model.c.jinja:444,483. Claim survives.
- **Remediation:** Tie the right-limit step to solver resolution (a few ULPs, or min(c*h_current, c*rtol*(1+|t|))) rather than a fixed 1e-6 relative-to-t offset, and rely on relation-memory overrides (already present) for guard re-evaluation instead of a large time shift; at minimum make the epsilon relative to time-since-t_start.

#### [MEDIUM] rk45 root localization: 48 blind bisections of re-integrated steps, dense output unused

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver-rk45/src/lib.rs:969` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** bisect_root always runs exactly 48 iterations (no early exit on interval width), and each iteration re-integrates a single uncontrolled RK step from lo_t to mid_t (trial_step_from, error_norm discarded) plus a root-condition evaluation that itself embeds a Newton algebraic refresh (up to 32 iters at tol 1e-10). locate_simultaneous_crossings then re-runs the full 48-iteration bisection for every candidate crossing including the one already located. Cost per event is ~(1+k)*48*(7 stages + refresh) = hundreds to thousands of derivative evaluations, versus ~10 with the free 4th-order DOPRI5 dense output (the k1..k7 stages are already computed) plus Brent/regula-falsi on the interpolant. The single-step re-integration over a potentially large bracket also has uncontrolled local error, so the located state can be less accurate than the accepted trajectory.
- **Evidence:** lib.rs:969 `for _ in 0..ROOT_BISECTION_ITERS {` (const = 48, line 35) with `self.trial_step_from(lo_t, &lo_state, mid_t - lo_t, event_boundary)?.y_next` per iteration and no tolerance-based early exit; lib.rs:1005-1011 re-invokes bisect_root per crossing in locate_simultaneous_crossings.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read crates/rumoca-solver-rk45/src/lib.rs: ROOT_BISECTION_ITERS=48 (line 35); bisect_root loop (lines 969-988) has no early exit and each iteration calls trial_step_from (7-stage DOPRI5, error_norm discarded, only y_next used) plus eval_root_conditions with UPDATE_MAX_ITERS=32/tol 1e-10 refresh. locate_simultaneous_crossings (lines 1006-1008) re-runs full 48-iter bisection per crossing including the already-located first one. Refutation attempts failed: derivative_cache is single-entry and never written by derivatives_at, so no caching saves the cost; no dense-output interpolant exists anywhere in the crate (only a line-3 comment). Severity medium is correct: results remain correct, cost is real but bounded.
- **Remediation:** Build the standard DOPRI5 dense-output interpolant from the already-available stages and root-solve on it (Brent), exiting when the bracket is below max(ulp, c*atol/|dg/dt|); reuse the first bisection's bracket for simultaneity checks instead of re-bisecting each crossing.
- **Remediation status (implemented):** `crates/rumoca-solver-rk45/src/dense_output.rs` now builds the standard DOPRI5 quartic continuous extension from the seven stages retained by `TrialStep`. `locate_step_roots` and `locate_dense_root` in `lib.rs` evaluate only that interpolant and the root conditions, use guarded secant candidates with a bisection fallback, exit on root residual or a tolerance derived from ULP and `atol/|dg/dt|`, select the earliest localized crossing, and reuse each localized time/tolerance for simultaneity clustering. Unit coverage includes constant-derivative interpolation, exact agreement with the DOPRI5 endpoint weights, and `rk45_dense_root_localization_selects_earliest_crossing_and_state`. The 27-test RK45 suite, workspace lint gate, and 176 architecture/policy checks pass.

#### [MEDIUM] Dense mass matrix with explicit inverse, rebuilt per derivative-guess call

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver-diffsol/src/bdf.rs:272` — SPEC_0032 §1 (domain payloads are compact) · category: tensor
- **Claim:** The mass matrix travels as dense Vec<Vec<f64>> in SolveArtifacts and PreparedMassMatrix::new always materializes a normalized n_state x n_state dense matrix (even just to detect identity), computing a Gauss-Jordan explicit inverse for the non-diagonal case (numerically inferior to a factorization). bdf_derivative_guess constructs a fresh PreparedMassMatrix - O(n^2) allocation plus O(n^3) inversion - on every call, and it is called per event reset (reset_vectors), per derivative_guess in the event bracket kernel, and at initialization. For structurally sparse multibody/PDE models with thousands of states this is quadratic memory and cubic time at every event for what is nearly always an identity or diagonal operator, contradicting the range-preserving/compact-payload target.
- **Evidence:** bdf.rs:271-274: `let mass_matrix = PreparedMassMatrix::new(&model.artifacts.continuous.mass_matrix, state_count)?; let state_dy = mass_matrix.solve(&rhs[..state_count])?;` inside bdf_derivative_guess (hot per-event path); mass_matrix.rs:118-129 normalized_matrix builds `vec![vec![0.0; n]; n]` unconditionally, :155 invert() computes a full dense inverse.
- **Verifier correction:** Per-event cost is O(n^2), not O(n^3): both lowering producers (phase-solve solve_identity_mass_matrix, solve_model state_identity_mass_matrix) only ever emit dense identity matrices, so is_identity short-circuits before invert() and the Gauss-Jordan explicit inverse is currently dead code for pipeline artifacts. The confirmed defects are: dense n^2 identity payload stored in SolveArtifacts for every model, fresh O(n^2) PreparedMassMatrix rebuild per event/init/input-refresh call in bdf_derivative_guess, and a latent explicit-inverse path should a non-diagonal mass matrix ever be produced.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read mass_matrix.rs (normalized_matrix :118 builds n×n unconditionally; invert :155 explicit Gauss-Jordan), bdf.rs :271-274 (fresh PreparedMassMatrix per bdf_derivative_guess call), call sites (reset_vectors lib.rs:781, derivative_guess lib.rs:860 via sim_driver.rs:422/623/813 per event; init bdf.rs:214/248; session.rs:653), and ir-solve lib.rs:1645 (Vec<Vec<f64>>). Also found lowering always emits a dense n² identity, so the empty short-circuit never fires — but is_identity then avoids the O(n³) inversion, making per-event cost O(n²) not cubic.
- **Remediation:** Prepare the mass matrix once per OdeModel (store PreparedMassMatrix alongside the other prepared blocks), represent it sparsely (identity/diagonal/CSR) end-to-end in SolveArtifacts, and solve via LU factorization instead of an explicit inverse.
- **Remediation status (implemented):** `OdeModel::new` now prepares and owns the mass matrix once; `bdf_derivative_guess` and the Diffsol mass callback reuse that prepared value. `PreparedMassMatrix` keeps identity and diagonal forms compact, applies sparse matrices directly from COO entries, and stores a reusable partial-pivoted nalgebra LU factorization for sparse solves. The former normalization copy, Gauss-Jordan inverse, and hot-path preparation call are gone. Unit tests cover diagonal and sparse solve/application plus malformed dimension and index rejection.

#### [MEDIUM] nominal attribute never used for per-variable tolerance scaling

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver-diffsol/src/ode.rs:698` — MLS 4.8.6 (nominal attribute) · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** Both diffsol problem builders pass a uniform absolute-tolerance vector (`vec![opts.atol; n]`), and the rk45 error norm uses scalar atol/rtol. The Modelica nominal attribute - whose stated purpose is to give solvers the variable scale for error control - reaches the runtime only as a Debug-formatted string in SimVariableMeta and is never consumed numerically. For stiff industrial models mixing scales (Pa vs K vs rad/s spanning 1e-5..1e7), a single atol makes error control either too loose on small-scale variables or step-crushing on large-scale ones; per-variable atol_i = atol*nominal_i is standard in CVODE/IDA-based Modelica runtimes. min/max bounds are likewise never enforced on Newton iterates.
- **Evidence:** ode.rs:697-698 (general path): `let n_total = model.solver_scalar_count(); let atol = vec![opts.atol; n_total.max(1)];` and ode.rs:572 (state-only path) `let atol = vec![opts.atol; state_count.max(1)];`; rumoca-sim/src/lib.rs:505 stores nominal as `source.var.nominal.as_ref().map(truncate_meta_expr)` (a Debug string) - no numeric consumer exists.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified all cited evidence: ode.rs builds uniform atol vectors in both paths (`vec![opts.atol; state_count.max(1)]` at ~572, `vec![opts.atol; n_total.max(1)]` at ~698, passed to `.atol(atol)`); rk45 lib.rs:795 error_norm uses scalar atol/rtol fields. Workspace-wide grep shows nominal exists only as `Option<String>` metadata (ir-solve, solver, sim, report_payload) produced by truncate_meta_expr = `format!("{expr:?}")` — no numeric consumer; no clamp of iterates to min/max in diffsol/eval-solve. Severity: MLS 4.8.6 nominal is advisory ("may be used"), so this is a bounded quality/accuracy gap, not an outright spec violation — medium is right.
- **Remediation:** Lower evaluated nominal (and min/max) values into SolveLayout as per-scalar f64s and build the atol vector as atol*|nominal_i| (default 1); optionally clamp Newton/projection iterates to [min, max].

#### [LOW] Interactive BDF advance_to hard-fails after 256 events per call

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver-diffsol/src/session.rs:511` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** BdfSession::advance_to processes at most SESSION_ADVANCE_EVENT_LIMIT=256 root events per call and then returns the error 'event processing did not settle before t=...'. A legitimately event-dense model (e.g. a 1 kHz switching guard) advanced by a large dt (>0.256 s here) fails outright rather than continuing, even though every event was handled successfully. The limit conflates Zeno protection with normal event density; batch paths have no such cap.
- **Evidence:** session.rs:29 `const SESSION_ADVANCE_EVENT_LIMIT: usize = 256;` and session.rs:511-531: `for _ in 0..SESSION_ADVANCE_EVENT_LIMIT { ... } Err(SimError::SolverError(format!("event processing did not settle before t={target_time}")))` - the loop exits with an error even when each iteration made forward time progress.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read session.rs: SESSION_ADVANCE_EVENT_LIMIT=256 (line 29); advance_to loops 256x then unconditionally errors with no time-progress check. step_solver_by returns hit_root on the first RootFound, so each iteration = one root event; 257 time-advancing events in one call hard-fail. Batch driver simulate_state_targets (rumoca-eval-solve/sim_driver.rs) uses an unbounded while-loop; EVENT_UPDATE_MAX_ITERS in lib.rs caps single-instant discrete fixed-point iteration, not per-interval root count. No caller subdivides targets. Claim survives.
- **Remediation:** Only error when time fails to advance between successive events (true accumulation/Zeno); otherwise keep processing until target_time, optionally bounded by a wall-clock budget rather than an event count.

#### [LOW] truncate_meta_expr byte-slices a Debug string and can panic on UTF-8 boundary

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-sim/src/lib.rs:438` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** truncate_meta_expr renders an expression with format!("{expr:?}") and truncates with &rendered[..160]. If byte 160 falls inside a multi-byte UTF-8 character (Modelica descriptions/units/string literals routinely contain characters like the degree sign, and Debug rendering embeds them), the slice panics, taking down variable-metadata construction for the whole simulation result. Debug formatting is also not stable output for user-facing start/min/max/nominal metadata.
- **Evidence:** rumoca-sim/src/lib.rs:434-441: `let rendered = format!("{expr:?}"); if rendered.len() <= 160 { rendered } else { format!("{}...", &rendered[..160]) }` - a direct byte-index slice of a UTF-8 string with no char-boundary check.
- **Verifier correction:** Evidence and mechanism are correct, but the likelihood argument is overstated: unit and description strings (the common carriers of characters like °) are cloned directly into the metadata structs and never pass through truncate_meta_expr. The panic requires multi-byte UTF-8 inside a start/min/max/nominal expression itself (e.g. a String-typed start attribute or a quoted Unicode identifier) whose Debug rendering exceeds 160 bytes with a multi-byte char straddling byte 160 — reachable but uncommon.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read rumoca-sim/src/lib.rs:434-441: code is exactly `format!("{expr:?}")` then `&rendered[..160]` with no char-boundary check; grep shows no is_char_boundary/floor_char_boundary anywhere in rumoca-sim. Expression carries Literal::String, FieldAccess field, and Reference names (ir_primitives.rs:1258+), and derived Debug leaves printable non-ASCII (°, α) as raw multi-byte UTF-8, so a byte-160 mid-char slice panics. Not handled upstream. Minor overstatement: descriptions/units are cloned directly and never pass through this function — only start/min/max/nominal do — so triggering needs non-ASCII in those attribute expressions, which is rare. Low severity stands.
- **Remediation:** Truncate on a char boundary (e.g. rendered.char_indices().nth-based or rendered.floor_char_boundary(160)) and prefer a display/pretty-printer over Debug for user-facing attribute metadata.

### A.codegen — Code generation & execution backends (11 confirmed)

#### [HIGH] MLIR runtime linear solve overflows fixed 64x64 stack buffer

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-exec-mlir/src/compile.rs:68` — MLS Appendix B (B.1a algebraic loop blocks) · SPEC_0007 Stage 4 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** The C runtime helper linked into every MLIR-compiled model copies the caller's n*n matrix and n-vector into fixed stack arrays of RUMOCA_LS_MAXN=64 without any bounds check. A model whose algebraic loop block (LinearSolveComponent/LinSolve) has n > 64 unknowns writes past the stack buffers — undefined behavior (stack smashing) inside the loaded shared library, with no diagnostic at compile or load time.
- **Evidence:** #define RUMOCA_LS_MAXN 64 ... double A[RUMOCA_LS_MAXN * RUMOCA_LS_MAXN]; double x[RUMOCA_LS_MAXN]; ... for (i = 0; i < n * n; i++) A[i] = Ain[i]; for (i = 0; i < n; i++) x[i] = bin[i]; — `n` comes straight from the emitted LinearSolveComponent op; nothing rejects n > 64 anywhere in the MLIR pipeline.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read compile.rs: C helper copies n*n/n elements into fixed A[64*64]/x[64] stack arrays with no guard. Traced n end-to-end: mlir.mlir.jinja emits arith.constant {{nn}} from the op with no check or fail(); scalarize_linsolve (dense.rs:11) and ad.rs:503 build LinearSolveComponent with unbounded n (only overflow checks). Grepped exec-mlir and codegen for any 64/MAXN cap — only the #define exists. Dense LinSolve is the documented fallback path, so n>64 is reachable; result is stack smashing UB in the loaded .so.
- **Remediation:** Either reject n > RUMOCA_LS_MAXN at template-render time (the MLIR template knows `nn` statically and the env has a fail() function), or allocate the work arrays dynamically (VLA/malloc) in the runtime helper.

#### [HIGH] DAE-level templates silently emit 0.0 when equation pattern-matching fails

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-codegen/src/codegen/render_c.rs:293` — MLS Appendix B.1a (residual semantics) · SPEC_0008 (no silent defaults) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** ode_rhs_for_state and alg_rhs_for_var extract explicit RHS expressions from B.1a residuals by pattern matching (der(x)-expr, var-expr, linear-system solve). When no pattern matches, they return the string "0.0 /* WARNING: no equation found ... */" instead of a render error. The fmi2 target uses alg_rhs_for_var for algebraics (model.c.jinja:747,752) and outputs (line 853), and jax/onnx use ode_rhs_for_state for derivatives — so an FMU or JAX model whose equations do not match the expected shapes compiles cleanly and silently simulates with zeroed variables. This contradicts the project's own no-silent-defaults stance (SPEC_0008 / GAL-007) and the strict-undefined template philosophy used everywhere else.
- **Evidence:** render_c.rs: Ok(format!("0.0 /* WARNING: no equation found for {} */", name_str)) — and fmi2/model.c.jinja:853: m->w[{{ ns_out.offset }}] = {{ alg_rhs_for_var(source_ref(name, var.dims, i + 1), dae.f_x, cfg) }}; with template comment admitting the assumption: "If the model has been through structural preparation, outputs have explicit equations."
- **Verifier note:** Re-read render_c.rs: ode_rhs_for_state_function and alg_rhs_for_var_function return Ok("0.0 /* WARNING: no equation found */") on no-match (and bare "0.0" if iteration fails), never an error, despite fail_function being registered (mod.rs:1296). Confirmed consumers in fmi2/fmi3 model.c.jinja (algebraics + outputs, incl. line 853), jax.py.jinja and onnx.py.jinja (derivatives). No pipeline stage scans output for the warning; only 3 fixture regression tests do — proving the path fires on real models. SPEC_0008 and GAL-007 explicitly forbid silent defaults. Refutation failed.
- **Remediation:** Make the no-match case a CodegenError (the environment already registers a fail() function for templates); if a lenient mode is genuinely needed for prototyping targets, gate it behind an explicit template flag rather than defaulting to silent zeros in the FMI export path.

#### [MEDIUM] Shipped MLIR target emits only unrolled scalar rows; linalg path unused

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-codegen/src/templates/mlir/mlir.mlir.jinja:231` — SPEC_0032 §5 (native tensor rendering by backend/codegen target); SPEC_0007 Stage 4 (MLIR linalg listed as tensor consumer) · category: tensor
- **Claim:** The built-in `mlir` target — the backend SPEC_0007 designates for native tensor kernels — renders every tensor ComputeNode (Map, AffineStencil, MatMul, LinSolve) as scalar fallback rows, one fully-unrolled SSA block per scalar output, plus a fully unrolled zero-init store per output index. The linalg.matmul renderer (render_matmul_mlir_function, dense_solve_render.rs:137) and render_linsolve_mlir exist and are registered, but no shipped template references them (grep over src/templates/ shows only WGSL uses native families). Large array models therefore produce O(total scalar elements) lines of textual MLIR, and mlir-opt/llc compile time and code size scale with element count rather than tensor rank.
- **Evidence:** // eval_derivative — ... // Emits scalar fallback rows for every tensor ComputeNode until native MLIR tensor emission is enabled by the target manifest. Plus: {% for output_index in range(0, drv_output_count) %} ... memref.store %drv_zero, %out[...] {% endfor %} — one store statement per scalar output, no scf/affine loops, no linalg for Map/AffineStencil.
- **Verifier correction:** Facts confirmed, framing corrected: neither cited spec mandates native emission for the mlir target (SPEC_0007 Stage 4 explicitly permits scalar expansion; SPEC_0032 §5 only assigns ownership), and the scalar fallback is self-declared in target.toml (matmul="scalar", linsolve="scalar", readiness_level=1) and in the template comment. Also, LinSolve is not fully unrolled Gaussian elimination — each component calls runtime @rumoca_solve_linear_component, though A/b assembly is unrolled per component. Real O(elements) IR-size/compile-time scalability gap, but documented, correctness-neutral, and bounded: medium, not high.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read mlir.mlir.jinja: comment at 231-232, unrolled zero-init stores 239-242, all four functions emit per-row scalar SSA via emit_op; no scf/affine/linalg. Grep confirms render_matmul_mlir/render_linsolve_mlir are registered (mod.rs:1279-80) but called only from tests; rumoca-exec-mlir compile.rs/gpu_blob.rs render this same template, so no native path ships. But SPEC_0007 says backends "can choose scalar expansion or native tensor ops" and target.toml declares matmul/linsolve="scalar", readiness_level=1 — a documented staged limitation, not a spec violation.
- **Remediation:** Wire the existing native-family partition (native_family_template_partition) into the MLIR template the same way wgsl-solve does: emit scf.for/affine.for or linalg.generic per Map/AffineStencil family and call render_matmul_mlir/render_linsolve_mlir for MatMul/LinSolve nodes, keeping scalar fallback only for genuine scalar rows.

#### [MEDIUM] Division-by-zero semantics diverge between interpreter/cranelift and all text backends

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-exec-cranelift/src/emit.rs:1128` — MLS 10.6 (elementwise division; no saturation semantics defined) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** rumoca-eval-solve (lib.rs:1949) and the cranelift JIT implement 'guarded division': x/0 returns +INFINITY regardless of the sign of x, and 0/0 returns 0.0. Every text backend (C render_solve_ops.rs:48 '((lhs)/(rhs))', MLIR arith.divf, WGSL, WASM F64Div, Python) emits IEEE-754 division: sign-correct ±inf and NaN for 0/0. The same Solve-IR therefore produces different trajectories near singularities depending on backend — e.g. -1.0/0.0 is +inf under cranelift/interpreter but -inf in an exported FMU, which can flip zero-crossing/event detection. The guarded value is also mathematically wrong in sign for negative numerators, so the 'reference' interpreter is the deviant one.
- **Evidence:** emit.rs guarded_division: let rhs_zero_value = fb.ins().select(lhs_zero, zero, inf); fb.ins().select(rhs_zero, rhs_zero_value, safe) — always +inf for nonzero lhs. render_solve_ops.rs:48: "Div" => Ok(format!("(({lhs}) / ({rhs}))")) — plain IEEE division in generated C.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified all cited sites: cranelift emit.rs guarded_division (lines 1128-1140) returns +INFINITY for nonzero lhs regardless of sign and 0.0 for 0/0, wired into general emit_binary_op; eval-solve lib.rs:1949 has identical logic used by eval_binary (line 1939) and mirrored in reverse.rs AD; test eval_row_guarded_division_matches_jit_semantics institutionalizes it. All text backends emit raw IEEE division: render_solve_ops.rs Py/WGSL/C/Rust lines 48/114/177/223, MLIR arith.divf (dense_solve_render.rs:651), WASM F64Div (exec-wasm emit.rs:590). No guard exists in any codegen path; SPEC_0022 defines no div-by-zero semantics. Claim survives.
- **Remediation:** Pick one semantics (IEEE division is what every generated target already does and what solvers expect) and align eval-solve and cranelift to it; the cranelift interpreter cross-validation will then also guard the C parity instead of institutionalizing the divergence.

#### [MEDIUM] dae_template_json enumerates every scalar array element into strings, hard-capped at 1M

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-codegen/src/codegen/mod.rs:317` — SPEC_0032 §1 (domain payloads compact, no O(N) metadata) · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** Every DAE-target render builds symbol_refs/symbol_aliases by enumerating each array variable element-by-element into formatted strings (name[i,j,...]) across all eight variable partitions plus function outputs. This is O(total scalar elements) string allocations per render — the crate itself documents it as 'costs seconds on large models' (solve_renderer.rs LazyDaeTemplateJson) — and source_ref_scalar_count returns a hard CodegenError above SOURCE_REF_ENUMERATION_LIMIT = 1_000_000 elements per variable, so a 100x100x100 field variable cannot be rendered by any DAE-level target at all. This rebuilds at the codegen boundary exactly the O(N) scalar metadata SPEC_0032 removes from the IR.
- **Evidence:** for flat_index in 1..=total { refs.insert(format!("{}[{}]", name, source_subscript_suffix(&dims, flat_index)?)); } and const SOURCE_REF_ENUMERATION_LIMIT: usize = 1_000_000; if count > SOURCE_REF_ENUMERATION_LIMIT { return Err(..."exceeds enumeration limit"...) }
- **Verifier correction:** Two details: (1) the check is `count > 1_000_000`, so a 100x100x100 variable (exactly 1M elements) renders fine; the cap first bites above 1M (e.g. 101x100x100). (2) SPEC_0032 §1 forbids O(N) scalar entries in IR domain payloads but explicitly allows "an explicitly materialized scalar view", and §5 assigns scalar fallback/native rendering to the codegen boundary — so this is a real efficiency problem plus a hard functional cap, but a design tension with the spec's intent rather than a literal spec violation.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read mod.rs: add_source_refs_for_var (line ~317) enumerates 1..=total formatting "name[i,j]" strings for all 8 partitions plus function outputs/inputs/locals; source_ref_scalar_count errors when count > SOURCE_REF_ENUMERATION_LIMIT=1_000_000 (lines 131, 345-363), and tests at codegen_tests.rs:607+ exercise the rejection. solve_renderer.rs:19 self-documents "costs seconds on large models"; laziness only defers, it does not remove the O(N) build for FMI/DAE targets (render_dae_context at mod.rs:569 always materializes). DAE variables retain dims through scalarize_equations (equations only), so enumeration is reachable. Claim survives.
- **Remediation:** Make symbol_refs lazy/on-demand (a minijinja Object resolving name+indices at lookup time, as already done for scalar rows), or emit per-variable dims and let templates compute element names, removing both the O(N) materialization and the 1M hard cap.

#### [MEDIUM] LinearSolveComponent renders a full Gaussian elimination per component (O(n^4))

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-codegen/src/codegen/render_solve.rs:1362` — SPEC_0007 Stage 4 (LinSolve tensor node with scalar fallback) · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** Solve-IR represents an n-unknown linear block as n separate LinearSolveComponent ops, and every backend renders each op as a complete independent solve: the C dialect emits __rumoca_solve_linear_component((double[]){all n^2 entries}, (double[]){n entries}, n, comp) once per component (n^2-element compound literals duplicated n times in the source text, O(n^3) runtime each → O(n^4) per block per evaluation), and cranelift's emit_dense_linear_solve inlines the entire O(n^3) select-based branchless pivoting elimination as straight-line IR per component op — for n=20 that is ~8k instructions duplicated 20 times in one JIT function.
- **Evidence:** render_solve.rs: Self::C => Ok(format!("__rumoca_solve_linear_component((double[]){{{matrix}}}, (double[]){{{rhs}}}, {n}, {component})")) — called once per component op; emit.rs lower_linear_solve_component → emit_dense_linear_solve(fb, ...) fully unrolled per op.
- **Verifier correction:** Two scope nuances: (1) Solve-IR proper holds a single tensor ComputeNode::LinSolve; the n per-component ops arise only in the scalar-fallback program produced by scalarization, per SPEC_0007's tensor-node-with-scalar-fallback design. (2) "every backend" is overbroad: MLIR (dense_solve_render.rs whole-node rendering) and cuda-nvrtc (linsolve="native") solve once per block, and the eval-solve prepared runtime keeps LinSolve as one node (prepared.rs:1143). The O(n^4)/duplication applies to c-solve, rust-solve, Python dialect, cranelift JIT, and the LinearOp interpreter.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified: scalarize_linsolve (compute_block_scalarize/dense.rs) emits n LinearSolveComponent ops per block; render_solve.rs render_linear_solve_component inlines all n^2 matrix expr strings into a __rumoca_solve_linear_component call per component; the c-solve template helper runs full pivoted elimination per call returning x[component] (O(n^3) x n = O(n^4)); cranelift lower_linear_solve_component (emit.rs:899) calls emit_dense_linear_solve fresh per op, fully unrolled select-based pivoting, no memoization by matrix_start, discarding n-1 of n computed solutions (line 928). target.toml declares linsolve="scalar" for c-solve/rust-solve/cranelift-solve-jit, so this is the live path.
- **Remediation:** Solve once per block: emit a single call/kernel that factorizes A and back-substitutes all components (return the vector, or cache the factorization keyed by the op's matrix_start within a row), and in cranelift emit one elimination whose n solution values feed the n component registers.

#### [MEDIUM] FMI3 template silently projects Modelica Complex to its real part

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-codegen/src/templates/fmi3/model.c.jinja:78` — MLS 4.7 / MSL Complex record semantics · category: correctness
- **Claim:** The FMI3 C template defines `#define Complex(re, im) (re)`, so any surviving Complex record constructor in the DAE renders as its real part only. A model using the MSL Complex type (Modelica.ComplexBlocks, ComplexMath) compiles into an FMU that silently discards imaginary parts instead of failing with an unsupported-feature diagnostic.
- **Evidence:** /* Complex number support — Modelica Complex record projected to real part. */\n#define Complex(re, im) (re)
- **Verifier correction:** The claim's mechanism is exact, but "a model using MSL Complex compiles into an FMU that silently discards imaginary parts" is overbroad: IR-level record decomposition (lower_record_function_params_dae, scalarize project_constructor_component, eliminate ComplexFieldSubstitutionGroup) preserves both fields on the primary data flow. The silent loss occurs only when a Complex constructor survives in a scalar C expression context; the recommendation to reject residual constructors (mirroring the external-function guard in codegen/mod.rs:515) is apt.
- **Verifier note:** Verified fmi3/model.c.jinja:77-78 has `#define Complex(re, im) (re)`; render_function_call emits surviving constructors literally so the macro silently drops im; only external functions are rejected (no Complex guard), and functions still holding record params stub to `return 0.0`. FMI2 template comments confirm this is a knowing stub. However prepare_dae_for_codegen record-param lowering plus scalarize constructor projection and eliminate field substitution carry .re/.im through the main MSL paths, so the projection only bites residual whole-record-in-scalar contexts — bounded, hence medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Reject models whose DAE still contains Complex constructors for this target (mirror reject_external_functions_for_simulation_template), or implement genuine complex record lowering; a silent real-part projection is never a correct simulation.

#### [MEDIUM] CUDA path runs the entire model RHS on a single GPU thread

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-exec-mlir/src/cuda_driver.rs:275` — SPEC_0032 §5 · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** launch_eval_derivative launches the compiled eval_derivative kernel with grid (1,1,1) and block (1,1,1): the entire scalar-unrolled RHS executes serially on one CUDA thread. batch_euler_cuda parallelizes only across per-trajectory streams, each still a 1-thread launch (1/32 warp occupancy), and CudaGpuOdeModel::eval_state_derivatives allocates and frees all device buffers on every call. The GPU backend is thus a functional proof-of-concept that will be dramatically slower than the CPU path for any single trajectory and severely underutilized even for Monte Carlo batches.
- **Evidence:** cuda_driver.rs: (self.cu_launch_kernel)(func.0, 1, 1, 1, // grid: 1 block ... ; cuda_gpu_model.rs eval_state_derivatives: let d_y = drv.alloc_f64(...)?; ... drv.free(d_y)?; per call.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read cuda_driver.rs: launch_eval_derivative passes grid (1,1,1) block (1,1,1) to cuLaunchKernel (lines ~273-280); launch_euler_update by contrast uses 256-thread blocks. cuda_gpu_model.rs eval_state_derivatives allocs d_y/d_p/d_out and frees them every call (lines 31-57). Both batch_euler_cuda and batch_euler_cuda_device loop per-trajectory streams, each a 1-thread eval launch. gpu_blob.rs confirms the kernel is the straight-line CPU eval_derivative merely tagged via inject_nvvm_annotation — no thread indexing exists, so 1 thread is forced; fixing it needs row-indexed emission as recommended. Not handled elsewhere; batch_euler_cuda_device only removes host round-trips. Correctness is unaffected and the GPU path is test/experimental (compile.rs: "GpuCuda target not yet wired up"), so medium (real but bounded) is right.
- **Remediation:** Parallelize within the kernel (thread per scalar row / per native-family domain point, which requires emitting row-indexed kernels rather than one straight-line function), batch trajectories into one grid dimension instead of streams, and hoist device buffer allocation out of the per-step path (batch_euler_cuda_device with the euler_update kernel is the right direction — make it the default).

#### [LOW] MLIR template silently skips unsupported LinearOps producing opaque toolchain failures

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-codegen/src/templates/mlir/mlir.mlir.jinja:216` — SPEC_0008 (phase-local structured errors) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** The emit_op macro is an if/elif chain with no else: ops it does not know (LoadIndexedSeed, TableBounds/TableLookup/TableLookupSlope/TableNextEvent, Random*/ImpureRandom*) render as nothing, leaving their destination registers undefined. Downstream uses then reference undefined SSA values and the failure surfaces as an mlir-opt-18 parse/verifier error with no source span, instead of a rumoca diagnostic naming the unsupported feature (as cranelift and wasm both do).
- **Evidence:** {%- elif op.StoreOutput is defined %} ... {%- endif %}{%- endmacro %} — no else branch; the environment registers a fail() template function (mod.rs env.add_function("fail", fail_function)) but the MLIR template never uses it.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read mlir.mlir.jinja emit_op (lines 107-217): if/elif chain ends at StoreOutput with no else; inner Unary/Binary chains also fall through silently. LinearOp (rumoca-ir-solve/src/linear_op.rs) has LoadIndexedSeed/Table*/Random*/ImpureRandom* variants unmatched. fail() is registered (codegen/mod.rs:1296) but unused in this template, while the Rust MLIR helper emit_one_linear_op_mlir errors explicitly on unknown ops. Refutation attempt failed: capability validation (codegen_target.rs:490-495) only fires on external_tables=false/random=false, which mlir/target.toml omits (host_callbacks=false is never enforced), and LoadIndexedSeed is reachable via forward_ad=true AD rows since LoadIndexedP is supported in primal form. Failure surfaces as an mlir-opt undefined-SSA error, not a rumoca diagnostic — loud but opaque, so low severity stands.
- **Remediation:** Add {% else %}{{ fail("unsupported LinearOp for MLIR target: " ~ op) }}{% endif %} to emit_op (and to the unary/binary macros), matching the explicit-unsupported-op behavior of the cranelift and wasm adapters.

#### [LOW] GAL-008 letter vs practice: C statement text is printed in Rust

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-galec-codegen/src/c_print.rs:1` — SPEC_0034 GAL-008/D2 · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** GAL-008 states the producer 'emits typed serializable context only — never C/XML strings in Rust', with C text owned by minijinja templates. In practice c_print.rs is a full GALEC-AST→C99 statement/expression printer in Rust whose pre-rendered C strings are placed into c_template_context for the embedded-c-galec/galec-production templates to splice. The printer is well-tested and errors are typed (ET023), so this is a spec-text/implementation divergence rather than a defect — but unlike the .alg printer, this exception is not recorded (GAL-009 records the .alg one).
- **Evidence:** c_print.rs header: "GALEC AST → C99 printer for the `embedded-c-galec` export (SPEC_0034 GAL-024). Scope: exactly the AST shape crate::lower emits today..." — Rust code producing C text that GAL-008 says must never be produced in Rust.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read SPEC_0034 line 45: GAL-008 says the producer "emits typed serializable context only — never C/XML strings in Rust". c_print.rs (881 lines) has statement_lines() emitting literal C statements ("{target} = {expr};"); emit.rs packs them as CStatement.c_lines into c_template_context, and model.c.jinja just splices lines. GAL-009/D1 and SPEC_0029 §12 record ONLY the .alg printer exception; grep of spec/ finds no C-printer exception. Refutation attempts failed: "typed context" doesn't cover pre-rendered C strings; GAL-024 governs conformance claims, not text ownership; emit.rs's "D2/GAL-008 split" gloss contradicts the spec's literal wording rather than being sanctioned by it.
- **Remediation:** Amend SPEC_0034 to record the C-expression-printer exception explicitly (mirroring the GAL-009 .alg exception), or move statement-level assembly into the templates leaving only expression atoms in Rust — currently a reviewer cannot tell whether this is sanctioned.

#### [INFO] WGSL backend computes in f32 with 3.4e38 standing in for infinity

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-codegen/src/templates/wgsl-solve/model_solve.wgsl.jinja:36` — MLS 3.7 Real semantics (double precision expectation) · category: tensor
- **Claim:** The WGSL solve target stores y/p/out as array<f32> and evaluates all rows in single precision, with non-finite constants replaced by 3.4028235e38 (render_solve.rs SolveRowDialect::Wgsl infinity()). The header honestly documents drift, but stiff systems, long-horizon integration, and root-condition sign tests evaluated in f32 can produce qualitatively different results (missed/spurious events) from every other backend; there is no f64 option even on hardware supporting shader-f64, and no per-model precision warning is emitted.
- **Evidence:** @group(0) @binding(0) var<storage, read> y: array<f32>; ... // Precision note: WGSL baseline is f32; reference solvers integrate in f64. Expect single-precision drift on long horizons. And render_solve.rs: Self::Wgsl => "3.4028235e38".
- **Verifier correction:** Facts stand, but: (1) shader-f64 does not exist in WebGPU/WGSL — f64 kernels are impossible in this target language, so the "detect shader-f64" recommendation should be dropped; (2) missed/spurious event concern is already surfaced via target.toml capability metadata (runtime_events=false, readiness_level=0, dtypes=["f32"]) directing hosts to refuse/warn; event iteration is not in the WGSL kernel.
