Remaining actionable slice is only a compile-time courtesy warning for models with root conditions; severity is info, not low.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read model_solve.wgsl.jinja (array<f32> bindings, f32 time uniform, header drift note) and render_solve.rs:1263 (Wgsl infinity = "3.4028235e38"); all quoted evidence is accurate and no wgsl-specific warning/admissibility gate exists in crates/. However, WGSL/WebGPU has no f64 type or shader-f64 feature, so the recommendation is infeasible, and target.toml already declares readiness_level=0, dtypes=["f32"], runtime_events=false with "hosts must refuse or warn" — the event risk is declared out of this target's contract. Documented platform limitation, not a compiler defect.
- **Remediation:** Detect and use the shader-f64 WebGPU capability when present, or at minimum implement compensated (Kahan-style) accumulation for reductions and surface a structured warning when a model with root conditions targets wgsl-solve.

### A.tensor — Cross-cutting tensor-nativeness audit (11 confirmed)

#### [CRITICAL] Dropped cheapened families leave der=0 placeholder rows live

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/eliminate/mod.rs:487` — SPEC_0032 §2 (views carry provenance; no fragile recovery) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** With the production default materialize_structured_families=false, interior cells of regular state-derivative for-families carry placeholder bodies `der(x[i]) - 0.0`, valid only while the family metadata survives so solve/incidence rebuild them from corners. But drop_structured_families_touching_equations (called from substitution_application.rs:86 after every boundary-elimination pass) and shift_structured_families_after_equation_removal (mod.rs:456) drop a family whenever any row in its block is touched by a substitution or removed — e.g. an eliminated algebraic alias referenced by the family's corner rows. The placeholder interior rows then remain as ordinary anonymous equations; solve lowers them as ScalarPrograms computing der(x_interior)=0, silently freezing interior states. The fail-early guard mentioned in flatten (equations/mod.rs:1047-1050) covers only algebraic/parameter-promotion families, not state-derivative ones, and no downstream check detects orphaned placeholder rows.
- **Evidence:** fn drop_structured_families_touching_equations(dae: &mut Dae, touched_sorted: &[usize]) { ... dae.continuous.structured_equations.retain(|family| { ... !touched_sorted.iter().any(|&idx| idx >= family.first_equation_index && idx < block_end) }); } — while flatten's cheapen_equation_bodies produced `lhs = 0.0` interior bodies (equations/mod.rs:1327-1341) that only solve's corner reconstruction knows are fake.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified every link: default cheapens (compile_support.rs:147); interior rows emitted as der(x[i])=0.0 with count preserved (cheapen_equation_bodies mod.rs:1327, comment 1208-1216); drop fns (mod.rs:627,658) retain purely on touched index range, no interiors_materialized guard, no re-materialize; corners rewritten by substitution get pushed to touched_equations (subst_app.rs:84-88); solve's structured_slots_for_rows returns None for orphaned rows (stencil.rs:353) → scalar lower to der=0; incidence fix (incidence.rs:107) only runs while family present; DAE promotion guard covers only algebraic families (promote_parameter_variable.rs:234). Silent frozen interior states under production default. Reachability is conditional (corner must reference an eliminable algebraic) but the pattern is plausible.
- **Remediation:** When a family with interiors_materialized == false must be dropped or invalidated, either (a) re-materialize its interior bodies from the ComprehensionTemplate before dropping, or (b) return a hard StructuralError instead of degrading. At minimum add a post-elimination validation pass that errors if any equation whose origin lies inside a formerly-cheapened family block survives without its family.

#### [HIGH] Whole-array, slice, and connection equations never form structured families

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/scalarize.rs:1094` — MLS 3.7 §8.4, §10.6 · SPEC_0032 §1 · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** StructuredEquationFamily is constructed only in flatten's expand_for_equation (equations/mod.rs:1117-1128) and its nested-family lift. Whole-array equations (`der(x) = -x` with x[1000]), sliced equations, and connection-generated array equations (equation_generation.rs:125 uses Equation::new_array with no family) reach the structural phase as single compact equations with scalar_count metadata, and scalarize_equations then expands them into per-element scalar rows with no provenance. Solve's stencil recovery requires a family slot (MissingStructuredSlot decline), so these become permanent ScalarPrograms. SPEC_0032 §1 explicitly lists 'whole-array equations, slices, ... and connection-generated array equations that are naturally ranged' as structured family sources. Consequence: the canonical rubric model compiles to 1000 scalar kernels, while the semantically identical explicit for-loop compiles to one Map node — the idiomatic form is penalized.
- **Evidence:** scalarize.rs: `for i in 1..=scalar_count { ... expanded.push(Equation { lhs: scalarized_equation_lhs(eq, target, i, eq.span)?, rhs: project_rhs_for_scalar_target(&eq.rhs, i, ...)?, ... }) }` — no family is attached; grep confirms flat::StructuredEquationFamily is only built at phase-flatten equations/mod.rs:1119 and structured_domain.rs:82.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified the full chain. Flatten's simple-equation path (equations/mod.rs:475) and connection generation (equation_generation.rs:125) both emit Equation::new_array with structured_equations empty. Grep confirms the only non-test StructuredEquationFamily construction sites are mod.rs:1119 (inside expand_for_equation) and structured_domain.rs:82 (nested lift) — both for-loop only. DAE phase merely remaps existing families (remap_flat_structured_equations), never synthesizes from compact whole-array/connection eqs. scalarize.rs:1094 expands into scalar_count:1 rows with no family. Solve stencil.rs:427-430 returns MissingStructuredSlot -> ScalarPrograms. SPEC_0032 §1 explicitly lists whole-array and connection-generated array equations as required family sources. Correct results but real spec-compliance + perf gap; high is right.
- **Remediation:** Synthesize a StructuredEquationFamily (domain from the variable dims, template from the whole-array residual, regular from trivially-affine elementwise access) for whole-array equations at flatten or during DAE conversion, and for uniform connection array equations. This is the single highest-leverage change for the OpenModelica head-to-head: it makes `der(x) = -x` produce one Map node.

#### [MEDIUM] Non-regular family preservation is O(N^2) shrinking-prefix search with full tuple materialization

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/stencil.rs:889` — SPEC_0032 §4 · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** For families without a `regular` classification, max_structured_affine_domain first materializes ALL index tuples (structured_domain_index_tuples — O(N) Vec<Vec<i64>>), then runs `for count in (2..=row_indices.len()).rev()`, re-running body-shape comparison, access-proof stride derivation, and output-map checks over up to count rows per prefix — O(N^2) worst case per family when it ultimately declines. The code comments themselves identify this as 'the O(N^2)/O(N^3) blowup on large grids' and mitigate it only for regular families (corner validation). Any family that loses regularity (a non-affine boundary iteration, a min/max clamp in one cell) silently pays the quadratic price at every compile.
- **Evidence:** `let index_tuples = structured_domain_index_tuples(&family.domain, family.span)?; ... for count in (2..=row_indices.len()).rev() { ... if !structured_dae_body_shapes_match(rows, row_indices, dae_equations)? { ... continue; } if affine_strides_from_access_proofs(rows, row_indices, &domain, family.span)?.is_none() { ... continue; } ... }`
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified stencil.rs. max_structured_affine_domain (line 852): regular families short-circuit via corner validation (line 865); non-regular families fall to line 887 which materializes all index tuples O(N), then the reverse loop (889) runs count from N..2, each iteration doing O(count) work in structured_dae_body_shapes_match, affine_strides_from_access_proofs, and output_map_for_rows over count rows. Early return only on output_map success; a declining family runs every prefix -> Σcount = O(N^2). Matches the finding and the code's own O(N^2)/O(N^3) comments. Compile-time, bounded, non-correctness -> medium is fair.
- **Remediation:** Replace the shrinking-prefix scan with a single forward pass that finds the maximal affine run (compare each row's access proof delta against the base incrementally, O(N) total), or split the family into affine sub-domains during flatten classification where boundary iterations are known statically.

#### [MEDIUM] Reference interpreter and C codegen scalarize tensor nodes; no native execution

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-solve/src/prepared.rs:1178` — SPEC_0032 §4, §5 · category: tensor
- **Claim:** prepared_affine converts every Map/AffineStencil into a PreparedComputeNode::ScalarPrograms by cloning base_ops once per domain point at prepare time (`scalarize_affine_rows`), so runtime memory and prepare time are O(N x ops) per family — the compact IR buys nothing at simulation time on the default path. The C codegen path does the same ('An affine stencil renders as its scalarized expansion', solve_lazy.rs:193-201). Only WGSL has a native parametric kernel. SPEC_0032 §4 permits scalar fallback, but with the reference solver being the primary consumer, the tensor pipeline currently improves only compile-side metadata, not simulation throughput or memory, for CPU targets.
- **Evidence:** `let rows = scalarize_affine_rows(domain, base_ops, load_strides, const_strides, span)?; let block = ScalarProgramBlock::with_output_indices(rows, spans, output_indices)?; Ok((PreparedComputeNode::ScalarPrograms(...)))` — Map/AffineStencil both route here (prepared.rs:1235-1260).
- **Verifier note:** Re-read all cited code. prepared_affine (prepared.rs:1155-1184) routes both Map and AffineStencil to scalarize_affine_rows, yielding PreparedComputeNode::ScalarPrograms. scalarize_affine_rows (affine.rs:41-66) clones base_ops per index_tuple into Vec<Vec<LinearOp>> = O(N x ops) memory/prepare cost. PreparedComputeNode enum (1019-1042) has no native Affine variant. C codegen (solve_lazy.rs:191-201) also scalarizes; only WGSL has native kernels (render_solve_native_family_wgsl). SPEC_0032 §4 permits scalar fallback, §5 marks native rendering target-local, so no correctness/MLS violation. Genuine bounded perf/memory limitation on CPU path: medium.
- **Remediation:** Add a PreparedComputeNode::Affine variant that keeps base_ops + strides and evaluates in a loop with register-index offsetting per domain ordinal (O(ops) memory, better cache behavior), and a C loop renderer mirroring render_native_family_expr_wgsl. This is where the OpenModelica comparison will be measured at runtime.

#### [MEDIUM] Scalar views lack spec-required provenance metadata

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-ir-solve/src/lib.rs:83` — SPEC_0032 §2 · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** SPEC_0032 §2 requires scalar views to 'preserve parent structured/tensor id, index tuple, scalar row id, and instantiated lhs/rhs or output expression'. ScalarProgramBlock carries only programs, program_spans, and output_indices; the scalarized rows produced by compute_block_scalarize and prepared_affine record no parent family/tensor identity and no index tuple. Diagnostics for a wrong scalar row inside a 100k-point family cannot name the family or the (i,j) coordinate, and fallback rows are indistinguishable from natively scalar rows in emitted artifacts.

The scalarize visitor pushes only ops, span, sequential output index; the domain index tuple is used for strides then discarded. SPEC_0032 RANGE_PRESERVING_TENSORS §2 (ACCEPTED) verbatim requires scalar views to preserve parent tensor id + index tuple + row id + output expr; parent id and index tuple are absent. Grep across crates found no struct storing them. Real spec gap, and it surfaces in serialized artifacts (implicit_jacobian_v_scalar). But it is diagnostics/traceability only — correctness unaffected, and tensor structure is retained in the parent ComputeNode. Medium (bounded) fits.
- **Remediation:** Add an optional per-row provenance record (family/tensor node id + domain ordinal, from which the index tuple is recomputable in O(rank)) to generated scalar views, populated by the shared scalarization in rumoca-eval-solve.

#### [MEDIUM] Simulation lowering makes 4+ full DAE clones and re-applies substitutions in a fixpoint

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-sim/src/solve_lowering/structural_lowering.rs:167` — SPEC_0021 (compile-time scaling) · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** prepare_structural_daes clones the entire DAE three times (source_dae = dae_model.clone(), lowered = source_dae.clone(), metadata_dae = lowered.clone()); eliminate_trivial clones it a fourth time (sort_input, eliminate/mod.rs:181); and apply_substitutions_to_dae_partitions clones it yet again per invocation (substitution_application.rs:97 `let derivative_source = dae.clone()`), where resolve_boundary_equations_to_fixpoint calls it once per pass with the CUMULATIVE substitution list — O(passes x substitutions x equations) rewriting plus O(passes x model) cloning. On a scalarized 1e5-row model this dominates compile time and peak memory; the profiling hooks (log_eliminate_profile 'clone_sort_input') show the team already measures it.
- **Evidence:** structural_lowering.rs:161-176: `let mut source_dae = dae_model.clone(); ... let mut lowered = source_dae.clone(); ... let metadata_dae = lowered.clone();` plus eliminate/mod.rs:262-274: loop { let pass = resolve_boundary_equations(dae)?; ... apply_substitutions_to_dae_partitions(dae, &result.substitutions)?; } — cumulative set re-applied every pass.

- **Verifier note:** Re-read the cited code. prepare_structural_daes does three sequential full-DAE clones (structural_lowering.rs:188,194,203). eliminate_trivial clones a fourth (mod.rs:184, sort_input, with a clone_sort_input profile hook). apply_substitutions_to_dae_partitions clones dae per call (substitution_application.rs:99). The boundary fixpoint (mod.rs:427-439) loops re-applying the CUMULATIVE substitution set per pass, each pass full-DAE-cloning. Minor drift: the fixpoint calls the sibling apply_aggregate_substitutions_to_dae_partitions (also clones per call), not apply_substitutions_to_dae_partitions; line numbers off by ~2. Substance holds: repeated full clones + cumulative re-application scale with passes×model. Compile-time efficiency issue, not correctness; medium is apt.
- **Remediation:** Apply only the current pass's new substitutions each iteration (composing substitutions instead of re-running the full list), share immutable partitions via Arc between source/metadata/lowered copies, and pass the derivative cache by reference instead of cloning the DAE inside apply_substitutions_to_dae_partitions.

#### [MEDIUM] BLT elimination silently disabled for any model containing array equations

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/eliminate/mod.rs:221` — SPEC_0032 §2 · category: correctness
- **Claim:** eliminate_trivial scalarizes a clone (sort_input) to run sort_dae when any equation has scalar_count != 1, but then discards the resulting BLT blocks for exactly those models: `blocks.filter(|_| !uses_scalar_view)` means Phase B (eliminate_via_blt) never runs when array equations are present. Every array-bearing model — i.e. every model this tensor audit cares about — pays the full clone+scalarize+sort cost and receives none of the BLT-based alias/trivial elimination, leaving larger systems for the solver. This is a silent optimization cliff keyed on array-ness, not a correctness-checked condition.
- **Evidence:** `let uses_scalar_view = sort_input.continuous.equations.iter().any(|equation| equation.scalar_count != 1); if uses_scalar_view { crate::scalarize::scalarize_equations(&mut sort_input)?; } ... if let Some(blocks) = blocks.filter(|_| !uses_scalar_view) { ... eliminate_via_blt(dae, &blocks, &state_names)?; }`
- **Verifier correction:** Two caveats: (1) the scalarize+sort is NOT "pointless" — its blt_error is consumed by eliminate_trivial_with_state_selection (lines 288-374) to drive state selection, so the "skip the work" recommendation is unsound; only the block-remapping recommendation is valid. (2) The !uses_scalar_view guard is a correctness necessity: blocks come from the scalarized sort_input clone, whose EquationRef/UnknownId indices don't map to the unscalarized `dae` that eliminate_via_blt mutates. Phase A boundary/alias elimination still runs for array models; only Phase B (BLT scalar-block) is skipped. Impact is performance/system-size, not wrong results (system is still scalarized and solved downstream).
- **Verifier note:** Re-read eliminate_trivial lines 199-256. uses_scalar_view = any(scalar_count != 1) is true for array equations; blocks.filter(|_| !uses_scalar_view) at line 241 gates eliminate_via_blt, so Phase B never runs for array models, which still incur the clone (184), drop (192), scalarize (205), and sort (208) cost. Factually accurate. It is a bounded performance limitation (correct results, larger algebraic system), deliberately guarded because scalarized block indices can't map to the unscalarized dae. Medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Either map scalarized BLT blocks back to the unscalarized equations (the spans vector from scalarize_equations provides the row mapping) so Phase B can run on array models, or skip the pointless scalarize+sort work when the result will be discarded.

#### [MEDIUM] delay() lowered as pre()/pass-through — no transport delay

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/scalar_ops.rs:254` — MLS 3.7 §3.7.2 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** BuiltinFunction::Delay is lowered to the delayed expression evaluated in ValueMode::Pre (previous event iterate), and eval-dae treats Delay identically to NoEvent (builtin_eval.rs:120). MLS §3.7.2 requires delay(expr, delayTime) to return expr(time - delayTime) — a transport delay with history interpolation. Models using Modelica.Blocks.Nonlinear.FixedDelay or delay() in equations (common in thermo-fluid and control MSL models) will simulate with zero delay and produce silently wrong trajectories rather than a compile error. The ir_primitives doc comment codifies the wrong behavior ('returns expr (no delay in continuous sim)'). Nearby, `BuiltinFunction::Der => self.emit_const_at(0.0, span)` (line 273) similarly substitutes a silent constant where an internal-contract error would be safer.
- **Evidence:** // MLS §3.7.2: delay(expr, delayTime) reads expr at a previous time instant... rumoca_core::BuiltinFunction::Delay => { ... self.lower_expr_in_mode(&args[0], scope, call_depth, ValueMode::Pre) } — delayTime argument is ignored entirely.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified all cited code. scalar_ops.rs:254-261 lowers Delay via lower_expr_in_mode(args[0], ValueMode::Pre); delayTime arg is never referenced. eval-dae builtin_eval.rs:120 and builtin_runtime.rs:88 group Delay with NoEvent, returning arg(0)/left-limit with no time offset. ir_primitives.rs:919 doc comment explicitly says "returns expr (no delay in continuous sim)". Grep across crates/ finds no transport-delay/history-buffer implementation (the two ring-buffer hits are unrelated stubs). So delay() silently simulates with zero delay, violating MLS §3.7.2. Medium is apt: wrong results but bounded to models using delay().
- **Remediation:** Until a history-buffer implementation exists, reject delay() with a clear unsupported-feature diagnostic instead of mis-simulating; when implemented, the solver already has the event/step infrastructure to carry a ring-buffer per delay site.

#### [MEDIUM] Arrays of structured components expand per element at instantiate

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-instantiate/src/array_expansion.rs:98` — MLS 3.7 §10.1 · category: tensor
- **Claim:** expand_array_component clones the component declaration and re-runs modifier resolution once per array index (`for idx in indices { scalar_comp.start = indexed_array_component_start(...) ... }`), so `Body body[N]` or a connector array in a MultiBody/power-grid model costs O(N) instantiation with full per-element modification environments, and — more importantly for the tensor goal — erases the array identity before flatten, so no equation family can ever form across component-array elements even when all N instances are homogeneous. This is the same architecture as every scalarizing compiler and is the hard ceiling on O(1) compilation for component arrays (as opposed to scalar-typed arrays, which stay whole). It is architectural, not incidental: fixing it requires homogeneity analysis of modifiers before expansion.
- **Evidence:** // MLS §10.1: Array component expansion creates indexed instances. ... let indices = super::generate_array_indices(dims); ... let mut scalar_comp = comp.clone(); ... for idx in indices { scalar_comp.start = indexed_array_component_start(...)?; ... }
- **Verifier note:** Verified array_expansion.rs: scalar_comp = comp.clone(); loop `for idx in indices` re-runs indexed_array_component_start + modifier distribution + full instantiate_component per element (O(N)). component_loop.rs:66 gates expansion on `!is_primitive && non-empty dims`, so primitive arrays stay whole while structured/component arrays scalarize, matching the finding. array_parent_dims is only recorded for array-equation expansion, not to preserve component-instance array identity, so families cannot form across elements. Accurate; not a correctness bug (standard scalarizing behavior, correct results) but a real bounded architectural ceiling for the tensor goal. Medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Long-term: detect modifier-homogeneous component arrays (no per-index modifications, no index-dependent bindings) and instantiate a single template instance plus a domain, expanding only on demand. Short-term: record the array provenance (base path + index) on the generated instances so flatten could later group homogeneous per-element equations into families.

#### [LOW] Flatten materializes every for-equation domain point, O(N) always

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/equations/mod.rs:1236` — SPEC_0032 §1 (domain payloads are compact; no parallel scalar owners) · category: tensor
- **Claim:** collect_for_iterations recursively iterates every value of every binder range, cloning and substituting the full AST equation body per outer-index value (`substitute_index_in_equation` per cell) and pushing one flat Equation per cell into Model::equations — even in cheapened mode, where only the leaf body is replaced with a placeholder after the O(N x body) substitution walk has already run. The flat equation vector therefore serializes one entry per scalar iteration, and ir-flat's own doc admits 'Current Flat lowering still materializes deterministic scalar views in Model::equations' (ir-flat lib.rs:843-845), i.e. the scalar rows — not the family — are the physical owner. This caps end-to-end compilation at Θ(N) time and memory per family and violates the spec's compact-payload rule outside an 'explicitly materialized scalar view'.
- **Evidence:** `for value in range_values { let substituted: Vec<ast::Equation> = equations.iter().map(|eq| substitute_index_in_equation(eq, index_name, value)).collect(); ... collect_for_iterations(...) }` — full body clone+substitution per domain point, before the cheapen decision applies at the leaf (mod.rs:1213-1218).
- **Verifier correction:** Real O(N) materialization, but it is NOT a SPEC_0032 §1 violation: the spec's "explicitly materialized scalar view" exception covers it, the compact domain owner already exists, and the behavior is documented as intentional transitional state. This is a known scalability limitation / tech debt with a defined migration path, not a high-severity defect.
- **Verifier note:** Mechanism verified: collect_for_iterations (mod.rs:1235-1251) iterates every domain point, substitutes per cell, and pushes one flat Equation per cell; cheapen_equation_bodies (1331-1340) only swaps rhs to a 0.0 literal, preserving O(N) row count. So the O(N) time/memory claim is factually true. But the spec-violation/high framing fails: SPEC_0032 §1 explicitly permits "an explicitly materialized scalar view" as the exception, the compact StructuredIndexDomain payload IS compact, and ir-flat lib.rs:842-846 documents these rows as exactly that transitional view with the structured family authoritative. No wrong results; documented, spec-permitted tech debt, not a defect.
- **Remediation:** Invert ownership per SPEC_0032: store the ComprehensionTemplate + domain as the authoritative family and emit scalar views lazily (the corner cells only, which build_cheapen_plan already identifies). The template capture (capture_comprehension_template) already exists; the remaining work is making downstream row-count consumers (balance, incidence sizing) read family.point_count() instead of counting materialized rows.

#### [LOW] Per-scalar String-keyed maps and linear name scans in structural hot paths

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/dae_prepare/derivative_map.rs:237` — SPEC_0021 · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** After scalarization the structural phase manipulates per-scalar identities as plain Strings: compute_full_derivative_map returns HashMap<String, Expression> keyed by rendered names ('x[999]'), scalarize builds var_dims/var_spans HashMap<String,...> per call, and helpers like record_array_length (phase-dae variable_analysis.rs:461-468) do `format!("{}.")` + linear `starts_with` scans over all flat variables per query. With N-element arrays these maps hold N heap strings each and hashing re-hashes full rendered names, while VarName interning (which exists precisely for this) is bypassed. Bounded cost, but it multiplies the O(N) row blowup by string-allocation constants on the compile-critical path.
- **Evidence:** pub fn compute_full_derivative_map(dae: &Dae) -> HashMap<String, Expression> — String-keyed per-scalar map; variable_analysis.rs: `let prefix_dot = format!("{}.", base); flat.variables.iter().find(|(name, _)| name.as_str().starts_with(&prefix_dot))` — O(V) scan per record-array query.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified all cited code. derivative_map.rs:237 `compute_full_derivative_map` returns HashMap<String,Expression> keyed by rendered names. variable_analysis.rs:461-465 `record_array_length` does format!("{}.",base) + linear starts_with scan over flat.variables per query. scalarize.rs:151/172 build_var_dims_map/build_var_spans_map return HashMap<String,...> per call. VarName interning exists but is bypassed here. Accurate efficiency observation of bounded cost, not a correctness bug. Low severity appropriate.
- **Remediation:** Key structural maps by interned VarName/VarNameId (already Arc-shared with precomputed segmentation), and precompute a base-name -> children index once per phase instead of prefix-scanning flat.variables per lookup.

### A.efficiency — Cross-cutting efficiency audit (11 confirmed)

#### [HIGH] Substitution application is O(n_eq x n_subs x expr) in eliminate_trivial

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/eliminate/mod.rs:1364` — SPEC_0029 §3a (VarNameId for hash-heavy paths) · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** apply_substitutions_to_expr_with_derivatives iterates the ENTIRE substitution list per expression, doing a full expression traversal (expr_contains_substitution_target) per substitution, and additionally rebuilds aggregate/complex substitution groups from the full list for every expression (apply_record_field_aggregate_substitutions at line 1363 -> aggregate_alias_substitution_groups at 1491). boundary_scan.rs:67 calls this per scanned equation with the cumulative in-pass substitution list, so a single boundary pass on an alias-heavy model (s ~ n aliases, typical for MSL connector networks) is O(n^2 * expr). This is the dominant compile-time cost for a 10k-equation model; OpenModelica does alias elimination in near-linear time with union-find + one substitution sweep.
- **Evidence:** mod.rs:1363-1372: `let mut out = apply_record_field_aggregate_substitutions(expr, substitutions, ...); for sub in substitutions { if expr_contains_substitution_target(&out, sub) { ... rewrite_expression(&out)?; } }` and boundary_scan.rs:67: `let eq_rhs = apply_substitutions_in_order(ctx.dae, &expr, &state.substitutions)?;` — executed for every equation in every boundary pass.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read mod.rs 1348-1393: per-expression loop over the full substitution list with a full expr traversal per sub (SubstitutionTargetChecker), plus aggregate_alias_substitution_groups + complex_field_substitution_groups rebuilt from the whole list on every call. boundary_scan.rs:67 invokes this per equation with the monotonically growing state.substitutions, so alias-heavy passes are O(n²·expr); resolve_boundary_equations_to_fixpoint additionally reapplies the cumulative set to all partitions each iteration and apply_substitutions_to_remaining_once clones the whole DAE. No target-keyed index or union-find exists anywhere in eliminate/. Only caveat: structural matchers (aggregate subscripts, embedded alias indices) mean the proposed VarNameId map needs a base-name key plus a per-hit structural check, not an exact-key lookup.
- **Remediation:** Index substitutions by target VarName (HashMap<VarNameId, &Substitution>) and rewrite each expression in ONE walk that looks up each VarRef/der() in the map, chasing substitution chains with memoized closure (union-find/path compression for pure aliases). Build the aggregate/complex groups once per pass, not per expression.

#### [HIGH] Elimination fixpoint re-applies cumulative substitutions and deep-clones the DAE each pass

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/eliminate/substitution_application.rs:97` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** resolve_boundary_equations_to_fixpoint (eliminate/mod.rs:262-274) calls apply_substitutions_to_dae_partitions with the CUMULATIVE substitution list after every pass, re-rewriting all previously-substituted equations again; each call first executes `let derivative_source = dae.clone();` — a full deep clone of the entire DAE (all equations, variables, functions, events) just to serve as a read-only derivative lookup source. apply_substitutions_to_remaining_once (line 33) does the same. With k fixpoint passes the cost is k full-DAE clones plus k * (all partitions) * (cumulative substitutions) rewrites.
- **Evidence:** substitution_application.rs:97: `let derivative_source = dae.clone();` inside apply_substitutions_to_dae_partitions; eliminate/mod.rs:262-274: `loop { let pass = resolve_boundary_equations(dae)?; ... result.substitutions.extend(pass.substitutions); apply_substitutions_to_dae_partitions(dae, &result.substitutions)?; }` — comment confirms the cumulative set is deliberately reapplied every pass.
- **Verifier correction:** Minor nuance: previously-substituted equations are not fully re-rewritten (a per-substitution contains-check skips non-matching rewrites), but each pass still scans every expression against the entire cumulative substitution list and rebuilds aggregate substitution groups per expression, plus two full-DAE clones per productive pass (apply_substitutions_to_remaining_once at line 33 runs inside every resolve_boundary_equations pass via finish_boundary_elimination, mod.rs:509). The asymptotic claim stands.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read substitution_application.rs (clones at lines 33 and 97; Dae derives Clone with all partitions/symbol tables) and eliminate/mod.rs:258-275 (cumulative extend + reapply per pass, deliberate per comment; reapplied again at :239 and re-entered by the demotion loop at :277-305). Attempted refutation: a contains-target pre-check (mod.rs:1359) skips redundant rewrites, but it is itself a full traversal per substitution per expression, and apply_record_field_aggregate_substitutions rebuilds groups from the whole cumulative list per expression — cost is as claimed or worse. No incremental/composed mechanism exists elsewhere.
- **Remediation:** Apply only the new pass's substitutions each iteration (resolving them against the already-applied set first, i.e. compose substitutions instead of reapplying), and split the borrow so the derivative source does not require cloning the DAE (precompute a derivative map of state -> ODE rhs once per pass, or pass the immutable variables/symbols tables separately from the mutated equation partitions).

#### [MEDIUM] Simulation lowering deep-clones the whole DAE at least 7 times

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-sim/src/solve_lowering/structural_lowering.rs:161` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** One structurally_lower_dae_for_simulation call clones the full DAE as source_dae (line 161), lowered (167), metadata_dae (176), state_selection_dae (257), observation_dae (291); eliminate_trivial clones it again as sort_input (eliminate/mod.rs:181), and phase-solve's compatibility entry clones once more (solve_model.rs:286 lower_dae_to_solve_model_owned(dae_model.clone())). The DAE's Expression trees are Box-per-node, so each clone is a full-graph allocation storm. For a 10k-equation model this rebuild-the-world pattern is second only to elimination in compile-time cost, and three of the copies (metadata/state-selection/observation) exist only to run substitutions or scalarization that the main copy also runs.
- **Evidence:** structural_lowering.rs:161 `let mut source_dae = dae_model.clone();` :167 `let mut lowered = source_dae.clone();` :176 `let metadata_dae = lowered.clone();` :257 `let mut state_selection_dae = metadata_dae.clone();` :291 `let mut observation_dae = source_dae.clone();` plus eliminate/mod.rs:181 `let mut sort_input = dae.clone();` — each is instrumented with its own clone timer, showing the team already suspects the cost.
- **Verifier correction:** The simulation hot path performs 6 full-DAE deep clones, not 7: lower_dae_to_solve_model(&dae) at solve_model.rs:283-286 (which clones) is a compatibility wrapper called only from tests; rumoca-sim uses the owned *_with_visible_expressions_and_metadata entries. All other cited clone sites are accurate and currently live.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read structural_lowering.rs: all five cited clones exist at lines 161/167/176/257/291, each with its own timer; eliminate/mod.rs:181 clones sort_input. Expression uses Box-per-node (ir_primitives.rs:1261+) and Dae has no Arc sharing, so clones are deep. state_selection_dae is used only to derive a name set then dropped; observation_dae only to enumerate visible expressions (comment at :303 admits the duplication). One overstatement: solve_model.rs:286's clone is a test-only compatibility entry — sim path uses owned variants (entry.rs:190/198), so the hot path has 6 clones, not 7.
- **Remediation:** Restructure the funnel around one owned DAE plus cheap derived views: share immutable sections (variables, symbols, functions) via Arc, derive metadata/state-selection facts as side tables (sets of demoted state names) instead of cloned-and-rewritten DAEs, and compute the observation/visible expressions from the substitution map directly rather than cloning + scalarizing source_dae.

#### [MEDIUM] remove_duplicate_continuous_equations is O(n^2) with full-expression String keys

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-sim/src/solve_lowering/expr_util.rs:12` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** Duplicate-equation removal renders every equation's whole expression tree to a String (expression_key builds nested format! results, itself quadratic in tree depth) and then checks membership with keys.contains(&key) — a linear scan over a Vec<String> — for each of n equations. For 10k equations that is ~5x10^7 long-string comparisons plus n full-tree string renders on every simulation lowering. The `_ => format!("{expr:?}")` fallback also embeds spans, so semantically equal equations from different locations silently fail to dedup for non-covered variants.
- **Evidence:** expr_util.rs:7-19: `for eq in dae.continuous.equations.drain(..) { let key = duplicate_equation_key(&eq); if keys.contains(&key) { continue; } keys.push(key); ... }` with `fn expression_key(...) -> String { ... format!("bin:{op:?}:{}:{}", expression_key(lhs), expression_key(rhs)) ... _ => format!("{expr:?}") }`.
- **Verifier correction:** All technical details verified correct. Severity is medium, not high: the pass runs once per simulation lowering (compile-time), not per solver step; the worst functional effect of the span-sensitive fallback is a missed dedup (duplicates from flatten expansion share spans, so common cases still dedup), and the O(n^2) scan is seconds-scale only on very large (10k+ equation) models.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read expr_util.rs: keys is Vec<String> with keys.contains() per equation (O(n^2) compares), expression_key recursively nests format! (O(size*depth) per tree), and the `_ => format!("{expr:?}")` fallback does embed spans — Expression derives Debug and all variants carry span (ir_primitives.rs:1258-1360), with Literal/FunctionCall/If/Array/etc uncovered. expression_semantic_fingerprint exists (ir_primitives.rs:1651, span-insensitive) and the pattern is used in runtime_precompute/expression_identity.rs:11; nothing else mitigates this pass, which runs on every lowering (structural_lowering.rs:170-173). Claim fully accurate; severity lowered: once-per-lowering, bounded, no wrong results.
- **Remediation:** Replace the String keys with rumoca_core::expression_semantic_fingerprint (already exists, span-insensitive) bucketed in a HashSet<u64> with expressions_semantically_equal to confirm on collision — the exact pattern already used in runtime_precompute/expression_identity.rs — making the pass O(n * expr).

#### [MEDIUM] BLT elimination is skipped entirely for models with structured (array) equations

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/eliminate/mod.rs:221` — SPEC_0032 §1-§2 · category: tensor
- **Claim:** eliminate_trivial scalarizes a cloned sort_input to run sort_dae when any equation has scalar_count != 1, but then gates Phase-B elimination with blocks.filter(|_| !uses_scalar_view), so eliminate_via_blt never runs for array-carrying models. Exactly the models where SPEC_0032 tensor preservation matters (MultiBody, discretized PDE grids) get only boundary elimination: trivial algebraic chains that BLT would remove stay in the runtime system, inflating solver_y and every refresh/projection at simulation time. The scalarize call on the clone is also pure throwaway work when the filter then discards the blocks.
- **Evidence:** eliminate/mod.rs:196-221: `let uses_scalar_view = sort_input.continuous.equations.iter().any(|equation| equation.scalar_count != 1); if uses_scalar_view { crate::scalarize::scalarize_equations(&mut sort_input)?; } ... if let Some(blocks) = blocks.filter(|_| !uses_scalar_view) { ... eliminate_via_blt(dae, &blocks, &state_names)?; }`
- **Verifier correction:** Two sub-claims need trimming: (1) the scalarize call on the clone is not "pure throwaway work" — sort_dae on the scalarized view supplies structural-singularity detection (blt_error consumed as a hard error in structural_lowering.rs:135); only sorted.blocks.clone() is wasted for array models. (2) Array models do not get zero array-level elimination: Phase A's boundary fixpoint already performs whole-array alias and shape-matched array-definition elimination via aggregate_alias.rs, so the recommendation's "at least whole-array alias elimination" partially exists. What array models actually lose is Phase B's BLT topological scalar-block elimination and tearing-based elimination of algebraic-loop members.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read eliminate/mod.rs:196-236: gate matches evidence verbatim; blocks.filter(|_| !uses_scalar_view) means eliminate_via_blt (mod.rs:1114, incl. loop tearing) never runs when any equation has scalar_count != 1. Gate introduced in ef5f071 (MultiBody tensor commit); no other pass re-runs BLT elimination. However, Phase A boundary fixpoint DOES already do whole-array alias/definition elimination (aggregate_alias.rs), and the scalarize+sort_dae on the clone is not pure throwaway: it feeds blt_error, a hard error in rumoca-sim/solve_lowering/structural_lowering.rs:135. Core efficiency gap real but bounded; correctness unaffected.
- **Remediation:** Either run eliminate_via_blt on the scalar view and lift scalar substitutions back to family-level substitutions when the whole family is uniform (keeping SPEC_0032's single structured owner), or implement family-level alias elimination directly on structured equations so array models get at least whole-array alias/zero elimination.

#### [MEDIUM] Full scalarization runs three times on three DAE copies per simulation compile

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-sim/src/solve_lowering/structural_lowering.rs:147` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** scalarize_equations — which itself builds seven whole-model maps (var dims/spans/structural ints/complex fields/projection maps) and expands every array equation element-by-element with per-element expression cloning — executes up to three times per compile: on the cloned sort_input inside eliminate_trivial (when families exist), on the lowered solve DAE (line 147), and on the cloned observation DAE (line 296). With SimOptions::default().scalarize == true (rumoca-solver/src/solver.rs:154) this triple expansion is the default path, tripling the largest single-pass allocation cost in structural lowering for a 10k-row model.
- **Evidence:** structural_lowering.rs:147 `scalarize_solver_view(&mut lowered, opts, "structural.scalarize_solve_dae")?;` and :296 `rumoca_phase_structural::scalarize::scalarize_equations(&mut observation_dae)` plus eliminate/mod.rs:202 `crate::scalarize::scalarize_equations(&mut sort_input)?;` — scalarize.rs:1014-1024 rebuilds all projection maps from scratch on each call.
- **Verifier correction:** Minor undercount only: scalarize_equations rebuilds ten whole-model maps per call (var dims, var spans, structural ints, complex fields, component-index, function-output index/dims, dynamic function-output, record-field projection, constructor-input), not seven — which slightly strengthens the claim. The eliminate-path scalarization is gated by array-family presence (uses_scalar_view), independent of opts.scalarize, exactly as claimed.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read all three sites: eliminate/mod.rs:181-202 scalarizes the cloned sort_input when uses_scalar_view; structural_lowering.rs:147 scalarizes `lowered` (never scalarized earlier in this path); :291-301 scalarizes a fresh clone of pre-elimination source_dae. scalarize_equations (scalarize.rs:1014-1024) rebuilds ten whole-model maps per call with no caching, and no shared scalar view exists elsewhere (grepped crates/). SimOptions::default().scalarize==true (solver.rs:154), so triple expansion is the default path. Bounded 3x cost, no correctness impact: medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Scalarize once and reuse: run the scalar view generation a single time after elimination and derive both the solver view and the observation/visible view from that one expansion (or from the substitution map plus the structured families), and share the projection-map construction across calls when multiple scalarizations are genuinely needed.

#### [MEDIUM] Flatten finalize re-runs whole-model passes up to 10 times without dirty tracking

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-flatten/src/pipeline/flatten_pipeline.rs:956` — SPEC_0021 (maintainability: pass sequence is also very hard to reason about) · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** finalize_flat_model executes collect_functions twice back-to-back (lines 957/959) plus up to 8 more inside collect_rewritten_functions_to_fixed_point, mark_record_constructor_calls five times, substitute_known_constants_in_flat three times, inject_referenced_qualified_class_constants and collapse_index_refs_to_known_varrefs twice each, and ctx.build_parameter_lookup at least five times across prepare+finalize. Each is a full traversal of all equations/variables; substitute_known_constants_in_flat additionally rebuilds an FxHashSet<String> copy of every variable name and clones every residual before rewriting (postprocess.rs:816-831). The result is a ~25-30x whole-model traversal constant factor on the flatten tail for large models.
- **Evidence:** flatten_pipeline.rs:956-1012: `functions::collect_functions(flat, ...)?; rewrite_function_extends_aliases...; functions::collect_functions(flat, ...)?; mark_record_constructor_calls(flat, tree); ... substitute_known_constants_in_flat(flat, ctx)?; ... [fixpoint] ... substitute_known_constants_in_flat(flat, ctx)?;` and postprocess.rs:816-819: `let live_vars: FxHashSet<String> = flat.variables.keys().map(|name| name.as_str().to_string()).collect();` rebuilt per call.
- **Verifier correction:** Lines 957/959 are not back-to-back — rewrite_function_extends_aliases_in_flat_functions (958) sits between and makes the second call semantically necessary; the cost is full re-traversal, not redundancy. build_parameter_lookup runs unconditionally only 2x across prepare+finalize (lines 851, 979); worst case 6 with conditionals (828, 841, 854, 981), so "at least five" is wrong as a floor. The fixpoint loop typically converges in 1 iteration; 8 is the worst-case cap.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read finalize_flat_model (flatten_pipeline.rs:925-1031): collect_functions at 957/959 plus 1-8 in the fixpoint loop (limit 8) = up to 10; mark_record_constructor_calls 3-5x; substitute_known_constants_in_flat 2-3x, each rebuilding live_vars FxHashSet from all variable names (postprocess.rs:816-820) and cloning every residual. Each pass verified as a full-model traversal; grep confirms no dirty/worklist tracking in the crate. Minor overstatements: 957/959 have a rewrite between them (2nd call needed), and build_parameter_lookup floor is 2 (max 6), not "at least 5".
- **Remediation:** Convert the finalize tail to a worklist: track which equations/functions were touched by each rewriting pass and re-run dependent passes only over the touched subset; hoist live_vars/parameter-lookup construction out of repeated calls and invalidate incrementally.

#### [MEDIUM] Structural incidence resolves unknowns through formatted-string keys per reference

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/incidence.rs:553` — SPEC_0029 §3a · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** Every variable reference in every equation goes through canonical_var_ref_key -> format_subscript_key, allocating a formatted "base[i,j]" String, then a HashMap<String,usize> lookup (ScalarUnknownResolver.exact/base_all), and resolve_name_all returns a fresh Vec per reference. Unknown registration likewise formats one scalar-name String per array element (scalar_name_text_for_flat_index, incidence.rs:334-345). There are 65 non-test call sites of format_subscript_key/scalar_name_text_for_flat_index across structural/solve/eval-solve — string formatting and string-keyed maps are the identity mechanism of the structural hot path, contradicting SPEC_0029's requirement to use compact VarNameId for equality/hash-heavy paths and costing an allocation per reference in build_incidence, which runs multiple times (sort_dae in eliminate, report, sparsity).
- **Evidence:** incidence.rs:548-560: `pub(crate) fn resolve_var_ref_all(&self, name, subscripts) -> Vec<usize> { if let Some(canonical) = canonical_var_ref_key(name, subscripts) { let resolved = self.resolve_name_all(&canonical); ... } }` with canonical_var_ref_key building `Some(dae::format_subscript_key(name.as_str(), &indices))` per reference and `exact: HashMap<String, usize>`.
- **Verifier correction:** Call-site spread is slightly misstated: format_subscript_key/scalar_name_text_for_flat_index have zero direct uses in rumoca-eval-solve; the ~90 non-test mentions span rumoca-phase-structural, rumoca-phase-solve, rumoca-phase-dae, rumoca-eval-dae (including runtime eval paths in eval_expr_impl.rs), rumoca-ir-dae, and rumoca-phase-codegen. The core claim about the structural incidence path is exact as cited.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read incidence.rs: resolve_var_ref_all (~:548) -> canonical_var_ref_key (~:585) allocates a String per reference (even subscript-free refs, :590 to_string()), keys ScalarUnknownResolver.exact/base_all HashMap<String,_> (:458-459), and resolve_name_all returns vec![idx]/cloned Vec per lookup (:539-546). from_dae/build_unknown_resolvers even convert already-interned VarNames back to owned Strings (:444-445, :484). SPEC_0029 §3a (lines 53-59) mandates interned VarName with VarNameId for equality/hash-heavy paths; VarNameId exists in rumoca-core/ir_primitives/var_name.rs. build_incidence runs in sort_dae (called from eliminate/mod.rs:205), build_structural_report, analyze_structure; build_solver_sparsity_triplets builds another resolver. No caching or ID-based path elsewhere. Compile-time only, so bounded: medium holds.
- **Remediation:** Key scalar unknowns by (VarNameId, flat_index) computed arithmetically from subscripts and dims — no string materialization — and return indices via a SmallVec or callback to avoid the per-reference Vec. Keep string names only for diagnostics rendering.

#### [MEDIUM] Kuhn matching and Tarjan SCC: O(V*E) with per-call re-sorting, plus unbounded recursion

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/matching.rs:46` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** maximum_matching is Kuhn's algorithm (O(V*E) worst case vs Hopcroft-Karp's O(E*sqrt(V))) and each augment() invocation re-collects and re-sorts the equation's HashSet into a fresh Vec — during a single augmenting path search the same equations may be re-sorted many times — plus a vec![false; n_var] visited allocation per start equation (O(n^2) total memset for 10k eqs). Both augment() and TarjanState::strongconnect (tarjan.rs:37) recurse without depth bounds: a 10k-long causal chain (cascade models, discretized 1-D PDEs) produces recursion depth ~n, risking stack overflow (a crash on valid input) at the ~50-100k-equation scale rumoca targets with tensor models. Matching runs multiple times per compile (sort_dae in eliminate, ic_plan x4, report).
- **Evidence:** matching.rs:46-47 inside recursive augment(): `let mut vars: Vec<usize> = eq_vars[eq].iter().copied().collect(); vars.sort_unstable();` and :19-21 `for eq in 0..n_eq { let mut visited = vec![false; n_var]; augment(...) }`; tarjan.rs:44-47 recurses `self.strongconnect(w, adj)` per edge.
- **Verifier correction:** Minor context the claim omits: the worker binaries (rumoca-worker, rumoca-sim-worker) already run compilation on 64 MB stacks and the rayon pool uses 16 MB stacks, so the stack-overflow risk is partially mitigated in the primary deployment paths; it remains for the plain rumoca CLI main thread and at the extreme (~100k+) chain depths. The determinism comment at matching.rs:43-45 explains the sort but does not justify redoing it per call — precomputed sorted adjacency is equally deterministic.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read matching.rs and tarjan.rs: Kuhn's algorithm confirmed; per-augment HashSet collect+sort_unstable at lines 46-47 (re-executed on every recursive re-entry); vec![false; n_var] per start equation at lines 19-20; recursion in augment (line 63) and strongconnect (tarjan.rs:46) with no depth guard or explicit stack. Confirmed ~10 maximum_matching call sites (lib.rs x5, ic_plan.rs x5) plus tarjan in blt.rs and diagnostics.rs. Refutation attempt: only mitigation found is stack sizing — 64 MB worker threads (rumoca-worker.rs:1440) and 16 MB rayon stacks (session/config.rs:47) — which lowers but does not remove the overflow risk (plain rumoca CLI main() runs on the default 8 MB main thread), and does nothing for the algorithmic costs.
- **Remediation:** Precompute sorted adjacency Vec<Vec<usize>> once before matching (also removes HashSet iteration entirely); reuse a timestamped visited array instead of reallocating; convert augment and strongconnect to explicit-stack iterative forms; consider Hopcroft-Karp for the initial matching on large systems.

#### [MEDIUM] Per-RHS-evaluation Vec allocation and full solver_y copy in refresh path

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-solve/src/runtime.rs:464` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** refresh_slots_with_plan — executed on every derivative evaluation (eval_state_derivatives -> refresh_derivative_dependencies) and every algebraic refresh, i.e. multiple times per integrator step for the whole simulation — allocates a fresh Vec<f64> snapshot of solver_y via copy_runtime_values (support.rs:19 `let mut copy = Vec::new(); ... try_reserve_exact`). refresh_slots_once also allocates `let mut row_outputs = Vec::new()` per call (line 688). The rest of the runtime is scrupulously allocation-free via RefCell scratch (refresh_probe_scratch, reverse_scratch), so these are the remaining per-step heap allocations in an otherwise clean hot loop; for 10k solver slots and 1e5+ RHS evals it is ~10^9 doubles of avoidable alloc+copy traffic.
- **Evidence:** runtime.rs:464: `let incoming = copy_runtime_values(args.solver_y, "algebraic projection snapshot")?;` inside refresh_slots_with_plan, which refresh_derivative_dependencies (line 415) routes every state-derivative evaluation through; support.rs:19-22 shows a fresh Vec per call.
- **Verifier correction:** Two details overstated: (1) row_outputs Vec::new() at line 688 does not heap-allocate unless a shapeless output segment actually pushes, and the batched fast path (line 675) skips it entirely; (2) the snapshot cannot be skipped outright for causal-certified plans — restore_after_causal_seed_error (line 470) consumes it before the certified early-return at line 474, so the fix must be a RefCell scratch or a lazily-taken snapshot, not omission.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read runtime.rs: line 464 allocates a fresh Vec via copy_runtime_values (support.rs:19-22, Vec::new+extend per call) inside refresh_slots_with_plan, reached on every derivative eval (eval_state_derivatives_at_solver_y:1694 and :795 -> refresh_derivative_dependencies:415) and every algebraic refresh (:443) whenever the plan is non-empty. Contrasts with the RefCell scratch pattern used everywhere else (refresh_probe_scratch:284/647, derivative_scratch:287 explicitly "to avoid per-call allocation", reverse_scratch:291). No pooling elsewhere; empty-plan early return (461) only spares refresh-free models. Correctness unaffected; bounded constant-factor cost, so medium stands.
- **Remediation:** Add a RefCell<Vec<f64>> rollback-snapshot scratch on SolveRuntime (mirroring refresh_probe_scratch) and use copy_runtime_values_into; skip the snapshot entirely when the plan is causal-certified with no projection fallback (the common non-loop case where rollback can never trigger).

#### [LOW] Regular-family debug incidence check silently does nothing

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/incidence.rs:136` — SPEC_0032 §1 (structured families stay authoritative) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** debug_check_one_regular_family is documented as the debug-build guard that makes a mis-classified 'regular' family 'fail loudly in debug builds rather than silently lowering wrong' (comment on debug_check_regular_family_incidence, line 84-99), but its body contains no assertion: on a row mismatch it just `return;` and on decline it just `return;`. The function computes the synthesized incidence, compares, and discards the result — the entire debug validation pass is a no-op that still costs O(family rows) per materialized family in debug builds. The doc-comment ('Panics (debug builds only...)') directly contradicts the body. This may be a half-finished edit in the current working tree, but as committed it means the P3 corner-derivation contract has no enforcement at the point the comments claim it does.
- **Evidence:** incidence.rs:136-149: `fn debug_check_one_regular_family(...) { let Some(synthesized) = ... else { return; }; for (offset, synthesized_row) in synthesized.into_iter().enumerate() { let row = ...; if eq_unknowns.get(row) != Some(&synthesized_row) { return; } } }` — no debug_assert!, no panic, no logging, no return value.
- **Verifier correction:** Minor nuance only: this is not a half-finished edit in the working tree — incidence.rs is unmodified vs HEAD and the function has lacked the assertion since its introduction in commit c76c15b; the doc/body contradiction was committed from the start. Everything else in the claim is accurate.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read incidence.rs:136-149: body computes synthesized incidence, and on both decline and row mismatch just `return;` — no debug_assert/panic/log, return type is unit and the caller ignores it, so the check is a pure no-op costing O(family rows) in debug builds, contradicting its own doc ("Panics...") and the caller comment ("fails loudly"). Sibling apply_regular_family_corner_incidence (line 116) shows the intended debug_assert pattern. Not a concurrent half-edit: file is clean vs HEAD, and git -S shows the function was born as a no-op in c76c15b (PR #278). No other code validates materialized regular-family incidence against corner synthesis (grep across crates/ found only solve-phase stencil tests, a different layer).
- **Remediation:** Either restore the debug_assert!/panic on mismatch (matching the documented contract), or if mismatch is now intentionally treated as a decline, make the check feed that decision back (e.g. clear family.regular) and fix the stale comments — a validation that observes and ignores is worse than none.

### A.project-spec — Project-spec conformance audit (9 confirmed)

#### [MEDIUM] phase-solve mutates DAE math via dummy-derivative substitution

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/dummy_derivative.rs:37` — SPEC_0007 §Structural Lowering Scope; SPEC_0029 §12 · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** lower_solve_problem internally runs a DAE-to-DAE symbolic rewrite: eliminate_dummy_derivatives substitutes der(x) -> di in every non-defining continuous equation and produces a rewritten dae::Dae that is then lowered. SPEC_0007's placement requirement states 'All DAE structural lowering/transformation MUST live in rumoca-phase-structural' and 'rumoca-phase-solve only lowers a finalized DAE to Solve-IR; it does not mutate DAE mathematical structure' (also SPEC_0029 §12: 'Lowering only, not structural mutation'). Because the rewrite happens inside solve lowering, the DAE actually integrated differs from the 'externally stable' finalized DAE that FMI/codegen/other consumers receive, undermining SPEC_0031's single-contract property.
- **Evidence:** rumoca-phase-solve/src/lib.rs:294: "let dummy_eliminated = dummy_derivative::eliminate_dummy_derivatives(dae_model); let dae_model = dummy_eliminated.as_ref().unwrap_or(dae_model);" and dummy_derivative.rs doc: "return a rewritten copy with `der(x) -> di` substituted in all non-defining continuous equations" — an expression-level symbolic rewrite of DAE structure inside rumoca-phase-solve.
- **Verifier correction:** Placement violation is real: eliminate_dummy_derivatives (dummy_derivative.rs:37) is a DAE-to-DAE rewrite (substitutes der(x)->di in equation.rhs, returns a new dae::Dae) invoked inside phase-solve lowering (lib.rs:294), contradicting SPEC_0007 §Structural Lowering Scope and SPEC_0029 line 243 ("Lowering only, not structural mutation"). However the finding's single-contract/divergence claim is overstated: the rewrite is transient (never re-emitted as the finalized DAE), applied at the shared solve entry so all Solve-IR consumers see it uniformly, and semantically neutral (di == der(x)), so SPEC_0031 externally-stable shape is not broken and results are unaffected. Hence medium, not high.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read dummy_derivative.rs: fn returns Option<dae::Dae>, clones the DAE and mutates equation.rhs via der->dummy rewriter — a DAE-to-DAE structural rewrite. Confirmed it runs in phase-solve lib.rs:294. Spec text (SPEC_0007 L308-315, SPEC_0029 L243) explicitly forbids structural mutation in phase-solve, so the placement claim holds. But the pass is correctness-preserving and necessary (avoids singular Newton system) and doesn't alter the emitted DAE-IR other consumers get, so no wrong results/perf harm — bounded → medium.
- **Remediation:** Move the dummy-derivative alias substitution into rumoca-phase-structural (it already owns dae_prepare/dummy_derivative_group.rs and state selection) so the finalized DAE handed to phase-solve is the one actually solved; alternatively amend SPEC_0007 to explicitly sanction this pass at the solve boundary and document the resulting DAE divergence.

#### [MEDIUM] Phantom vector equation pass reassembles arrays from rendered names and scalarizes in phase-dae

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/dae_lowering.rs:821` — SPEC_0032 §1-§2; SPEC_0007 cross-cutting rules · category: tensor
- **Claim:** scalarize_phantom_vector_equations rebuilds array structure by string-stripping subscripts out of rendered variable names (base 'sineVoltage.plug_p.pin.v' recovered from 'pin[1].v'/'pin[2].v'/... entries), then expands vector equations into per-element scalar rows inside DAE lowering and afterwards re-points structured families at shifted row indices. This violates SPEC_0032 §1/§2 ('structured equation families stay authoritative', 'no scalar-row reassembly'; scalar rows are derived views) and the SPEC_0007 cross-cutting rule that semantic phases must not recover name structure by tokenizing flattened strings. It also documents that connector-array elements are already materialized as per-element scalar variables with subscripts baked into name strings — the scalarized owner SPEC_0032 forbids.
- **Evidence:** Doc comment at 808-821: "connector arrays like `plug_p.pin[3]` produce scalarized variables (`sineVoltage.plug_p.pin[1].v`, ...) but some component-level equations reference the unsubscripted base name... This pass detects equations with `scalar_count > 1`... and expands each into `scalar_count` scalar equations"; build_phantom_expansion_map builds "map from stripped base name -> sorted list of actual indexed names" from strings.
- **Verifier correction:** Severity high→medium: genuine SPEC_0007/SPEC_0032 violation with string-tokenization fragility risk, but no demonstrated miscompile, MLS violation, or perf problem — a bounded architectural conformance issue.
- **Verifier note:** Code verified. scalarize_phantom_vector_equations (dae_lowering.rs ~821, called live from lib.rs:397) builds phantom map via build_phantom_expansion_map, which calls rumoca_core::strip_all_subscripts — pure char-level string tokenization of rendered names — to regroup connector-array elements under a stripped base. It then expands scalar_count>1 vector equations into per-element scalar rows during DAE lowering and calls remap_structured_families_after_expansion to re-point families at shifted rows. This squarely contradicts SPEC_0007 cross-cutting rules ("Arrays stay symbolic through Flat and DAE... not via display-string parsing"; "Semantic phases do not recover name hierarchy by tokenizing flattened strings") and SPEC_0032 §1/§2 ("families stay authoritative", "no scalar-row reassembly"). Real, active spec violation. Downgraded to medium: it is an architectural/conformance violation with latent fragility, not a demonstrated wrong-simulation or MLS violation, so it does not meet the review's "high" bar.
- **Remediation:** Keep connector arrays as shaped variables with a StructuredIndexDomain through flatten so 'phantom' base references are ordinary whole-array references; represent connection-generated array equations as structured families (SPEC_0032 lists them explicitly) instead of expanding to scalar rows and patching family row maps afterwards.

#### [MEDIUM] rumoca-eval-solve depends upward on rumoca-solver and hosts the simulation driver

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-solve/Cargo.toml:14` — SPEC_0029 §Dependency Tiers, §12; SPEC_0031 §Known Risk · category: architecture
- **Claim:** SPEC_0029 places rumoca-eval-* in Tier 3 and the solver family in Tier 5 with 'dependencies flow downward', yet rumoca-eval-solve's [dependencies] include rumoca-solver — the only upward tier edge in the workspace. The crate has also outgrown 'Solve-IR row evaluation': sim_driver.rs implements the backend-neutral simulation orchestration (output grid, root finding, event boundaries), runtime.rs/runtime_events.rs hold event state machinery, and jacobian/sensitivity live here — responsibilities SPEC_0029 §12 assigns to the runtime-contract (rumoca-solver) and simulation layers. It even hard-codes a concrete backend's tracing namespace, the bundled-solver drift SPEC_0031 flags as a known risk. No architecture-hardening test covers this edge, so the tier table is silently dead here.
- **Evidence:** Cargo.toml [dependencies]: "rumoca-solver = { workspace = true }"; sim_driver.rs:1 "//! Backend-neutral simulation output / event / root driver"; sim_driver.rs:149 "const EVENT_TRACE_TARGET: &str = \"rumoca_solver_diffsol::bdf\";" — a Tier 3 eval crate naming a Tier 5 concrete backend.
- **Verifier correction:** All cited facts hold: Cargo.toml:14 has the rumoca-solver dep; the tier table (T3 eval-*, T5 solver, "downward" + Rule 2) makes this a literal upward edge; sim_driver.rs is backend-neutral orchestration; EVENT_TRACE_TARGET hard-codes "rumoca_solver_diffsol::bdf"; no test pins eval-solve's dep set. But the edge is intentional — the arch test (686-698) bans only the reverse (solver depending on eval-solve), and §12 assigns shared runtime policy to rumoca-solver::runtime as a contract eval-solve consumes. Real defect is a tier-table-vs-reality doc inconsistency plus a hard-coded-backend smell, no correctness/MLS/perf impact. Severity high → medium.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read Cargo.toml, SPEC_0029 tier table + Rule 2, sim_driver.rs (dep imports, EVENT_TRACE_TARGET line 149), and architecture_hardening_test.rs. Evidence is accurate and survives refutation: the upward edge and hard-coded diffsol namespace are real. But the arch test intentionally enforces only solver-must-not-depend-on-eval-solve, and §12/122 frame rumoca-solver as a runtime-contract crate eval-solve legitimately consumes, so this is a spec-classification inconsistency + drift smell, not a functional/MLS violation. Bounded, no simulation impact → medium, not high.
- **Remediation:** Either move sim_driver/runtime-event orchestration into rumoca-solver (which §3b already names as owner of event-boundary/no-state/pre-param policy) leaving eval-solve as a pure row evaluator, or amend the SPEC_0029 tier table to re-classify eval-solve as a runtime-tier crate; add an architecture test pinning the eval-solve dependency set either way, and remove the diffsol-specific trace-target constant.

#### [MEDIUM] Solver-facing VarLayout is String-keyed; ~30 sites re-parse element indices from names

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-ir-solve/src/layout.rs:18` — SPEC_0007 cross-cutting rules; SPEC_0029 §3a; SPEC_0032 §2 · category: tensor
- **Claim:** VarLayout.bindings and shapes are IndexMap<String, ...> keyed by rendered flat names ('u[i,j]' with subscripts baked into the string), and rumoca_core::parse_scalar_name / dae::component_base_name are called from 10 files in phase-structural, 8 in phase-dae, and 7 in phase-solve to recover base/index structure from those strings. SPEC_0007's cross-cutting rule says post-resolution identity is keyed by DefId not strings and that tokenizing flattened strings inside compiler/lowering logic 'means structure was lost too early'; SPEC_0029 §3a mandates VarNameId for equality/hash-heavy paths. String keys also make every hot lookup hash full path text.
- **Evidence:** layout.rs:18-20: "bindings: IndexMap<String, ScalarSlot>, shapes: IndexMap<String, Vec<usize>>"; stencil.rs:104: "let base = dae::component_base_name(name).unwrap_or_else(|| name.clone());" recovering array base identity by parsing the rendered name inside Solve lowering.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified layout.rs 18-20: bindings/shapes are IndexMap<String,_> keyed by rendered flat names; binding()->derive_array_element_binding calls rumoca_core::parse_scalar_name (line 396). stencil.rs:104 parses names via dae::component_base_name inside Solve lowering — confirmed verbatim. SPEC_0007 lines 98-99 explicitly call hashing flat names a "phase-boundary bug" and forbid tokenizing flattened strings in lowering logic; SPEC_0029 §3a mandates VarNameId for hash/equality paths. Partial mitigation (indexed_bindings keyed by DefId-carrying ComponentReferenceKey) exists but String maps remain primary, and the finding already accounts for it. File counts are understated (15/14/16 vs 10/8/7). Functionally correct (tests pass), no wrong results, so medium — a real spec-conformance + minor hashing concern, not high/critical.
- **Remediation:** Key VarLayout bindings by VarNameId (interned) or extend the existing ComponentReferenceKey (which already carries DefId) to be the primary binding key with the String form serialization-only; carry (base, index-tuple) metadata on scalar slots so phases never call parse_scalar_name on layout keys.

#### [LOW] File-size budget gamed: 43 files packed at 1900-2000 lines, 4 over the CI gate

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-core/src/ir_primitives.rs:1` — SPEC_0021 §File Size · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** SPEC_0021 marks >2000-line files 'Action Required: split by concern' and xtask enforces max_lines=2000 in CI. - **Evidence:** wc -l: 2086 rumoca-core/src/ir_primitives.rs; 2069 rumoca-phase-structural/src/dae_prepare/mod.rs; 2057 rumoca-phase-structural/src/eliminate/mod.rs; plus 43 non-generated files in [1900,2000] and 32 in [1950,2000] — a distribution spike directly below xtask's `#[arg(long, default_value_t = 2000)] max_lines` (crates/xtask/src/main.rs:80).

SPEC_0021 line 114 frames File Size as a "Guideline" and line 125 exempts "cohesive modules", though the automated gate excludes only /generated/ paths (main.rs:767). Over-gate count is now 5, not 4 (lower.rs crossed to 2046 during concurrent edits).
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified every fact. SPEC_0021:121 marks >2000 "Split by concern"; xtask default_value_t=2000 bails on violation. wc -l confirms 5 non-generated files over 2000 (eliminate 2281, ir_primitives 2086, dae_prepare 2070, lower 2046, runtime/tests 2001) and 42 in [1900,2000] with a dense 1993-1997 spike — the clustering thesis holds. Corrections: "CI" is actually git hooks; spec is a guideline with a cohesive-module exemption the gate ignores. No correctness/functional/perf impact — a maintainability/process observation — so severity is low, not medium.
- **Remediation:** Split the 3 production files currently over the gate by concern (ir_primitives.rs is the shared-vocabulary crate and should not be a 2k-line monolith), and lower the xtask threshold stepwise (e.g. 1500) with a tracked exception list so the gate drives decomposition instead of line-shaving.

#### [LOW] phase-solve LowerError has no error codes and span-less variants; SPEC_0008 code table omits structural/solve

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/error.rs:2` — SPEC_0008 §Error Code Ranges, §Source Traceability · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** Unlike the miette-coded errors of resolve/typecheck/flatten/dae (ER/ET/EF/ED with spans and MLS help), phase-solve's LowerError is a plain enum of string reasons — Unsupported { reason: String }, MissingBinding { name } and DynamicSubscript carry no Span at all, and no variant has a stable diagnostic code. Meanwhile SPEC_0008's error-code range table stops at codegen (EC0xx): it assigns no range to the structural phase (which already ships ES001/ES002 in diagnostics.rs) or to solve lowering. Both directions are drift: the spec is stale, and the newest phase regressed to uncoded string errors that cannot be aggregated in CI/MSL reports or clicked to source.
- **Evidence:** error.rs: "pub enum LowerError { Unsupported { reason: String }, ... MissingBinding { name: String }, ... DynamicSubscript," — no #[diagnostic(code(...))], no Span on several variants; contrast phase-dae errors.rs "#[diagnostic(code(rumoca::todae::ED001), help(\"MLS §4.9...\"))]". SPEC_0008 table lists EP/ER/ET/EI/EF/ED/EC only, while crates/rumoca-phase-structural/src/diagnostics.rs:10 defines ES001/ES002.
- **Verifier correction:** Characterization "regressed to uncoded string errors" understates the intentional span-propagation design (with_fallback_span/Spanned/WithContext) that attaches spans at boundaries; impact is limited to missing stable codes and CI/MSL aggregation, not lost traceability or wrong output.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified in code: lower/error.rs LowerError has zero #[diagnostic(code(...))] and no with_code (grep found none in phase-solve), while phase-dae errors.rs carries ED0xx codes+help. Variants Unsupported/MissingBinding/DynamicSubscript are span-less; SPEC_0008 table lists only EP..EC (no ES/solve), yet structural/diagnostics.rs already ships ES001/ES002 — both drifts real. But LowerError has a deliberate span model (source_span, with_fallback_span, Spanned wrappers, documented span-free rationale), so spans aren't silently lost, and there's no correctness/MLS impact. Facts hold; severity is diagnostics/spec-consistency only, hence low not medium.
- **Remediation:** Assign an official code range for structural (ES0xx, already de-facto) and solve lowering (e.g. EL0xx/ES1xx) in SPEC_0008, convert LowerError to coded miette diagnostics, and require a Span (or documented span-free justification) on every variant per the Source Traceability table.

#### [LOW] SPEC_0008 sanctions panic! for invariants; CI bans it outright

- **Claim:** SPEC_0008's error-propagation table lists 'panic! / expect("invariant")' as the approved mechanism for internal invariant violations in 'any phase, any location', but test_production_code_has_no_panic_todo_or_unimplemented fails CI on any panic!/todo!/unimplemented! in non-test code, directing authors to 'return a typed error or document an invariant with expect instead'.
