### A.core-api — Core foundation, contracts, drivers & bindings (11 confirmed)

#### [MEDIUM] Scalar variable identity is a rendered string; structure recovered by re-parsing names

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-core/src/ir_primitives.rs:611` — SPEC_0032 §2 · category: tensor
- **Claim:** The foundation's variable identity for flattened scalars is an interned rendered string with subscripts baked into the text. Element references are minted by string formatting (Reference::with_appended_index), and rumoca-core exports an inverse parser family (parse_scalar_name, strip_trailing_subscript_suffix, subscript_fallback_chain, strip_all_subscripts) that 20+ files in rumoca-phase-structural and rumoca-phase-solve use to recover base/indices from names. Flat IR still materializes deterministic scalar views by default alongside structured families. This is the opposite of SPEC_0032 §2's 'scalar rows are derived views, not recovered structure': identity should be (array/tensor id, index tuple) with the rendered name as a display view. It also costs one global-interner entry per scalar element of every array (O(total scalars) strings for MultiBody/PDE-scale models).
- **Evidence:** ir_primitives.rs:611 `let rendered = format!("{}[{index}]", self.as_str());` (with_appended_index); ir_primitives.rs:751 `pub fn parse_scalar_name(name: &str) -> Option<ScalarNameRef>` parses "x[1,2]" back into base+indices; rumoca-ir-flat/src/lib.rs:843 "Current Flat lowering still materializes deterministic scalar views in the equation list"; grep shows parse_scalar_name/strip_scalar_name_subscripts used in 20+ structural/solve files.
- **Verifier correction:** Severity overstated. The description is accurate but this is acknowledged transitional architecture debt against SPEC_0032 (ACCEPTED, not fully implemented — flat lib.rs comment says so), not an MLS violation or wrong-result bug. Also, Reference keeps a structured component_ref with Subscripts in lockstep (ir_primitives.rs:612-618) and StructuredEquationFamily is the intended authoritative owner, so structure is not truly unavailable — it's just that VarName identity/keying and 20+ consumers still key off/re-parse the rendered string. The O(total scalars) interner cost is real but bounded.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified every cited line: with_appended_index at 611 uses format!("{}[{index}]"); parse_scalar_name at 751 re-parses; consumers eliminate/mod.rs:1710,1795 and solve/helpers.rs:270 call parse_scalar_name on rendered names; VarName uses a static process-local interner (var_name.rs OnceLock<RwLock>), so one entry per scalar element. Flat lib.rs:~843 comment confirms scalar views still materialized. Grep confirms broad structural/solve usage. All factual claims survive. Downgraded high->medium: real, bounded design-conformance deviation, no correctness/MLS impact.
- **Remediation:** Introduce a typed scalar-element identity in rumoca-core — e.g. ScalarVarId { array: VarNameId, ordinal: u32 } carried alongside (not inside) the rendered VarName — and migrate structural/solve consumers of parse_scalar_name to it, leaving string rendering as a display/serialization boundary only. This is the single highest-leverage change for the SPEC_0032 goal.

#### [MEDIUM] Path segmentation ignores quoted identifiers (Q-IDENT), corrupting names containing '.', '[', ']'

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-core/src/ir_primitives/var_name.rs:46` — MLS 2.3.1 · SPEC_0029 §3a · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** MLS 3.7 §2.3.1 allows quoted identifiers containing any characters, including '.', '[' and ']', and the parser accepts Q-IDENT (rumoca-phase-parse/src/equations.rs Ident::QIdent). But every path utility in rumoca-core — VarNameData::new, visit_top_level_path_segments, find_last_top_level_dot, split_trailing_subscript_suffix — tracks only bracket depth and has no single-quote state. A component or function named 'a.b' or 'x[2]' produces a flattened path like m.'a.b'.v whose segmentation, scope_split, and subscript stripping are wrong, silently corrupting variable identity. MSL itself ships quoted operator-function names ('+', '*', '0' in Complex/operator records), so quoted segments do flow through these paths today; only the dot/bracket-containing subset miscomputes.
- **Evidence:** var_name.rs:46-59 VarNameData::new: `match byte { b'[' => bracket_depth += 1, b']' => ..., b'.' if bracket_depth == 0 => top_level_dots.push(...) }` — no b'\'' case; grep for quote handling in rumoca-core/src returns zero hits; parser accepts Q-IDENT: crates/rumoca-phase-parse/src/equations.rs:13 `Ident::QIdent(tok)`.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read var_name.rs:46-65 and lib.rs:161-210 plus ir_primitives.rs:779: every helper tracks only bracket_depth, no b'\'' case. grep for quote handling in rumoca-core/src returns zero hits. Parser accepts Q-IDENT (equations.rs:13) and quotes are preserved in flat paths (is_quoted_identifier checks starts_with('\'')). A component 'a.b' mis-segments; 'x[2]' mis-strips its subscript. Bug is genuine. Impact bounded: MSL's quoted names ('+','*','0') lack dots/brackets so don't trigger it; only pathological dot/bracket-in-quote names (absent from any std lib) corrupt. Medium is defensible; low also arguable given near-zero real occurrence.
- **Remediation:** Add quote-state tracking (b'\'' toggles an in-quote flag, honoring the \' escape) to VarNameData::new and the lib.rs path-segmentation helpers, with contract tests for components named 'a.b' and 'x[1]'; or normalize quoted segments to an escaped internal form at the parse boundary.

#### [MEDIUM] expressions_semantically_equal is not span-insensitive for function calls (Reference PartialEq compares spans)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-core/src/ir_primitives.rs:1889` — SPEC_0029 §3b · category: correctness
- **Claim:** The documented contract of Expression::semantically_eq_ignoring_spans is 'Source spans are intentionally ignored', and SPEC_0029 §3b designates this as the single shared Flat/DAE expression identity. But function_calls_semantically_equal compares `lhs_name == rhs_name` using Reference's derived PartialEq, which includes component_ref — and ComponentReference/ComponentRefPart derive PartialEq including their `span: Span` fields, plus the `generated` flag and resolved_function. Two textually identical calls written at different source locations therefore compare unequal. Consumers include phase-dae runtime_precompute expression_identity (fingerprint-bucketed dedup), condition_lowering, phase-solve visible.rs, and structural eliminate — all get conservative false negatives: missed dedup, duplicate condition/root slots, and extra runtime work rather than wrong values, but the shared identity helper violates its own contract.
- **Evidence:** ir_primitives.rs:1889 `lhs_name == rhs_name && ...` in function_calls_semantically_equal; Reference::eq (ir_primitives.rs:710-717) compares `self.component_ref == other.component_ref`; component_refs_and_functions.rs:19-27 `#[derive(..., PartialEq, ...)] pub struct ComponentReference { pub span: Span, pub parts: Vec<ComponentRefPart>, ... }` with ComponentRefPart also holding `span: Span`. Contrast var_refs_semantically_equal which deliberately compares only var_name.
- **Verifier note:** Verified in code: function_calls_semantically_equal (line 1889) compares full Reference via Reference::eq (710-717), which includes component_ref; ComponentReference/ComponentRefPart derive PartialEq over span:Span, and Span::eq compares source/start/end. The paired fingerprint hashes FunctionCall only by name.as_str()+is_constructor+args (span-insensitive), so identical calls at different spans share a bucket but compare unequal. Doc contract (l.1533) and SPEC_0029 §3b designate this as span-insensitive structural identity — genuine violation for FunctionCall. Consumers (expression_identity, causal_factor, visible, condition_lowering) get conservative false negatives (missed dedup/redundant slots), not wrong values. Medium (real but bounded) holds.
- **Remediation:** In function_calls_semantically_equal, compare `lhs_name.var_name() == rhs_name.var_name()` plus resolved_function (matching the VarRef rationale comment), or add a span-insensitive Reference::semantically_eq used by both; add a regression test with two identical calls at different spans.

#### [MEDIUM] FlatBuffers codec panics on malformed wire bytes and schema files (unchecked index arithmetic)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-codec-flatbuffers/src/codec.rs:210` — SPEC_0008 (fail-fast, no silent defaults) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** UnpackCodec::unpack runs on untrusted bytes arriving over UDP/WebSocket lockstep transports, but its bounds checking is incomplete: after checking `vtable < buf.len()` it calls `get_u16(buf, vtable)` (reads vtable+1) and `get_u16(buf, vtable + op.vtable_slot)` with no upper-bound check, so a truncated or adversarial datagram indexes out of bounds and panics, aborting the simulation process (remote DoS of a SIL/HIL loop). The bfbs schema parser (bfbs.rs read_u8/read_u16/read_i32/read_u32, vtable_of) has no bounds checks at all beyond an 8-byte magic check, so a corrupt .bfbs file panics instead of returning the anyhow::Result the API promises. Additionally unpack() silently returns a partial SignalFrame on the failures it does detect, with no error signal to the caller.
- **Evidence:** codec.rs:209-214: `let vtable_size = get_u16(buf, vtable) as usize;` guarded only by `vtable >= buf.len()` (vtable+1 == len panics); `let field_off = get_u16(buf, vtable + op.vtable_slot as usize);` unchecked; bfbs.rs:205-231 raw `buf[off]` indexing helpers; parse_bfbs checks only `buf.len() < 8` and the 'BFBS' magic. UnpackCodec::unpack signature returns SignalFrame, not Result.
- **Verifier correction:** apply_fb_datagram (executor.rs:1253) drops datagrams whose length != recv_expected, so the pure "truncated datagram" vector is mitigated; the surviving vector is an exactly-sized but adversarially-crafted datagram (e.g. root_off=len-1), which still reaches the unchecked reads and panics. bfbs path is a config-supplied local file, lower exposure than the network unpack path.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read both files. unpack's guards are incomplete: get_i32(buf,table_off) (l202), get_u16(buf,vtable) (l210) and get_u16(buf,vtable+slot) (l214) all read past bounds on adversarial input (vtable_size is attacker-controlled; table_off/vtable only checked >=len, missing the +1..+4 span). Reachable via UDP recv_blocking->apply_fb_datagram->unpack (executor.rs:1256). bfbs.rs helpers use raw buf[off] with only len<8+magic checks, panicking instead of returning its anyhow::Result. Rust panics = DoS abort, not UB/wrong results, so medium (not critical) is right.
- **Remediation:** Make unpack return Result (or drop-and-count malformed frames) and route every read through checked accessors (buf.get(off..off+n)); do the same for bfbs.rs, which already returns anyhow::Result. Fuzz both entry points with truncated/bit-flipped inputs.

#### [MEDIUM] Two parallel source-location systems; AST Location carries an owned String file_name per node

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-core/src/ir_primitives.rs:291` — SPEC_0029 §3a · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** rumoca-core defines both Span (SourceId + byte offsets, 24 bytes, the SPEC_0029 §3a identity) and a legacy Location { start_line, start_column, ..., file_name: String } used throughout the AST: every Token and every AST node's `location` owns a heap-allocated copy of the file name (parser sites clone it per token/node — helpers.rs:28, recovery.rs:72/184/527). For MSL-scale parsing (thousands of files, millions of tokens/nodes) this is millions of redundant small String allocations and clones, inflates parse-cache size on disk, and keeps two location vocabularies alive that phases must convert between. SPEC_0029 §3a says SourceId/Span are the owned source identity and phases must carry Span through transformations.
- **Evidence:** ir_primitives.rs:291-300 `pub struct Location { ... pub file_name: String }`; ir_primitives.rs:312-320 `pub struct Token { pub text: Arc<str>, pub location: Location, ... }`; rumoca-phase-parse/src/helpers.rs:28 `file_name: start.location.file_name.clone()`; rumoca-ir-ast/src/nodes.rs:122 `pub location: Location` on ClassDef and many other nodes.
- **Verifier note:** Verified in code: ir_primitives.rs:299 `Location { ... pub file_name: String }`; Token embeds Location (313-320); helpers.rs:28 `file_name: start.location.file_name.clone()`; nodes.rs:122 and 25+ AST sites use `location: Location`. Span (SourceId+BytePos) exists separately at 195. SPEC_0029 §3a confirms SourceId/Span are the owned identity phases must carry. Two parallel location vocabularies and per-token/node owned-String allocation are real. Efficiency-only, no correctness impact, real but bounded at MSL scale -> medium.
- **Remediation:** Replace Location.file_name with SourceId (line/col can stay for LSP convenience, or be derived lazily from SourceMap), or at minimum switch to Arc<str> to share one allocation per file. This shrinks the AST, the persisted parse cache, and the dependency-fingerprint hashing input.

#### [MEDIUM] Global VarName interner: single process-wide RwLock contended by parallel compilation, unbounded growth

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-core/src/ir_primitives/var_name.rs:260` — SPEC_0029 §3a · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** Every VarName::new goes through one static RwLock<VarNameInterner> (IndexMap<String, VarNameId> + Vec<Arc<VarNameData>>). Under compile_models_with_cache's rayon par_iter (and parallel MSL runs), every flattened-name creation across all workers serializes on this lock — writes exclude all readers, and flattening is exactly the phase that mints millions of names. Combined with finding 1 (one interned string per scalar array element), a large MultiBody/PDE model floods the interner, and SPEC_0029 §3a's intentional no-reset policy means LSP/worker/wasm hosts retain every name ever interned for the process lifetime. The lock is a scalability ceiling and the growth is a memory ceiling for long-lived sessions.
- **Evidence:** var_name.rs:260-277: `static VAR_NAME_INTERNER: OnceLock<RwLock<VarNameInterner>>`; intern path takes read lock, then write lock on miss, for every VarName::new; session_impl.rs:1538 compiles cache-miss models with `.par_iter()` so all workers hit the same lock; SPEC_0029 §3a: 'Interned text is retained until process or WASM worker teardown... intentionally no public reset API.'
- **Verifier note:** Verified all cited code: var_name.rs:262-277 does read-lock-then-write-lock per intern on a single static RwLock<VarNameInterner>; session_impl.rs:1538 compiles cache-miss models via .par_iter(); SPEC_0029 §3a confirms monotonic, no-reset interner retained until teardown. The lock is a shared read on hits but exclusive write on each miss (flatten mints new names), so parallel model compilation genuinely serializes on the write lock. Concern is real but bounded: write critical section is tiny, and cross-model parallelism (not intra-model) limits contention; growth is explicitly by-design per spec. Efficiency/medium is correct; not a correctness/soundness bug.
- **Remediation:** Shard the interner (e.g. N-way by hash, as rustc/lasso do) or use a lock-free concurrent interner; longer term, reduce interner pressure by not minting per-scalar-element names (finding 1). Measure flatten-phase throughput at 8+ rayon workers before/after.

#### [LOW] SourceMap::add_shared silently aliases a SourceId hash collision to a different file

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-core/src/lib.rs:764` — SPEC_0008 (fail-fast error semantics) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** SourceId is a 64-bit FNV-1a hash of the normalized file name. In add_shared, if a new name hashes to an id already registered for a *different* name (exact-name matches are handled earlier), the code maps the new name onto the existing id and returns without storing the new content — all diagnostics for the second file would render against the first file's text, with no warning. The collision probability is negligible (~n²/2⁶⁵), but SPEC_0008 prohibits silently substituting a plausible value when the real one is missing; this branch institutionalizes exactly that instead of failing loudly.
- **Evidence:** lib.rs:763-769: `let id = SourceId::from_source_name(name); if self.id_to_index.contains_key(&id) || self.files.iter().any(|(source_id, _, _)| *source_id == id) { self.name_to_id.insert(name.to_string(), id); return id; }` — returns the other file's id and never stores this file's content.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified lib.rs add_shared (lines 751-775): after exact-name checks at 752 and 755, line 763 computes id via SourceId::from_source_name, and lines 764-769 — on id_to_index/files already containing that id from a DIFFERENT name — insert name_to_id mapping and return WITHOUT pushing the new content. Confirmed SourceId is a 64-bit FNV-1a hash (ir_primitives.rs:164-174, offset basis 0xcbf29ce484222325, prime 0x100000001b3). So a collision aliases file B's diagnostics onto file A's text silently. SPEC_0008 line 53/59 prohibits silent substitution of a plausible value. Real but 64-bit collision probability is negligible; low severity is correct.
- **Remediation:** On collision with a different stored name, panic with an invariant message (per SPEC_0008 this is a compiler-internal impossibility worth failing loudly on) or disambiguate by salting the hash with a sequence number and recording the mapping.

#### [LOW] Python bindings hold the GIL for the entire compile

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-bind-python/src/session.rs:44` · category: architecture
- **Claim:** Model.simulate correctly wraps the solver in py.allow_threads (model.rs:496), but the compile entry points (Session.load_file/load/loads → compile_source_in_session → compile_model_strict_reachable_with_recovery) run the full multi-second pipeline (parse, resolve, instantiate, flatten, DAE, structural) while holding the GIL. Any Python application compiling MSL-scale models from a worker thread freezes every other Python thread (GUI event loops, asyncio executors, notebook kernels) for the duration.
- **Evidence:** grep across rumoca-bind-python shows allow_threads appears exactly once (model.rs:496, around simulate_with_diagnostics); session.rs:44 `fn load_file(&mut self, path: &str, model: Option<&str>) -> ApiResult<Model>` calls the compile pipeline directly with no GIL release.
- **Verifier note:** Verified: `allow_threads` appears exactly once in the crate (model.rs:496, wrapping the solve). session.rs load_file (and thus load/loads/from_scenario) calls compile_source_in_session directly with no GIL release. That function (lib.rs:547) operates only on the Rust CompileSession and returns Rust types (HighLevelCompilationResult, String); no Python objects are touched during the parse/resolve/instantiate/flatten/DAE/structural pipeline, so the whole compile runs under a held GIL. The claim survives. This is an architecture/perf concern, not a correctness/MLS bug and does not affect simulation results, so low severity is correct.
- **Remediation:** Wrap the pure-Rust compile call in py.allow_threads (the Session state is &mut self and not a Python object graph, so no Python objects are touched inside), mirroring the simulate path.

#### [LOW] rumoca-worker resolves its executable by invoking `cargo build` at runtime

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-worker/src/lib.rs:539` · category: architecture
- **Claim:** resolve_model_worker_exe_inner falls back to shelling out to `cargo build -p rumoca-worker` from the running process when CARGO_BIN_EXE_rumoca-worker is absent. In an installed/production environment there is no cargo (spawn fails with a confusing 'failed to build rumoca-worker' error rather than 'worker binary not found'), and when invoked from within a cargo test/build already holding the target-directory lock, the nested cargo build blocks until the lock frees. Development-harness bootstrapping is baked into library API used by the CLI/MSL stages.
- **Evidence:** lib.rs:539 `build_model_worker_exe(profile_dir)?;` inside resolve_model_worker_exe_inner; lib.rs:569-596 spawns `cargo build -p rumoca-worker --bin rumoca-worker` with the current profile.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read lib.rs. resolve_model_worker_exe_inner (line 510) unconditionally calls build_model_worker_exe(profile_dir)? at line 539 before the candidate binary search (541-561). build_model_worker_exe (569-596) spawns `cargo build -p rumoca-worker --bin rumoca-worker` (+--release for release profile). In an installed env without cargo, spawn errors as "failed to build rumoca-worker: {error}" rather than a clear not-found message, and the build even runs when a sibling binary already exists. Evidence and line refs match exactly. Not a simulation/MLS issue, so low severity is correct.
- **Remediation:** Gate the cargo-build fallback behind cfg(debug_assertions) or a dev-only feature, and make the release path search only for an installed sibling binary with a clear 'rumoca-worker not found; install it next to rumoca' error.

#### [LOW] Codec pack/unpack byte-exactness tests hardcode a developer-machine path and silently skip

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-codec-flatbuffers/src/codec.rs:703` · category: testing
- **Claim:** The tests that verify hand-rolled FlatBuffers layout byte-exactness (pack_sim_input_matches_handcoded, pack_sim_input_byte_exact, unpack_motor_output, the UDP round-trip) all load fixtures from /home/micah/cognipilot/ws/cerebri/... and return early with an eprintln when the path is absent. On CI and every other developer machine these tests pass vacuously, so the entire wire-format implementation — the crate's core claim — is effectively untested outside one machine, while the crate simultaneously has panic-prone parsing (see codec panic finding).
- **Evidence:** codec.rs:703-706 and bfbs.rs:448-456: `let path = Path::new("/home/micah/cognipilot/ws/cerebri/build-native_sim/generated/flatbuffers/cerebri2_topics.bfbs"); if !path.exists() { eprintln!("skipping test: bfbs not found"); return; }` — pattern repeated in every substantive test in the crate.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified in the tree. codec.rs load_test_schema() (lines 700-714) points at /home/micah/cognipilot/ws/.../cerebri2_topics.bfbs and cerebri2_sil.bfbs, returning None if absent; all 5 codec.rs tests (716,814,856,963,1129) use the `else { eprintln!(\"skipping...\"); return; }` pattern. bfbs.rs's 2 tests (446,488) do the same via path.exists(). No .bfbs/.fbs fixtures are checked into the crate. So all 7 tests in the crate pass vacuously off that one machine, leaving the wire-format layout untested on CI. Severity low is apt: a test-coverage gap, not a runtime correctness bug.
- **Remediation:** Check a small generated .bfbs fixture (or the flatc-generated bytes as a const array) into the repo's test data so layout tests run everywhere; keep the cerebri paths as optional extra coverage.

#### [LOW] SignalFrame unpack allocates a String per signal per packet on the lockstep hot path

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-codec-flatbuffers/src/codec.rs:225` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** UnpackCodec::unpack builds a fresh string-keyed SignalFrame per packet and inserts `op.var.clone()` (heap String) for every routed signal, and PackCodec::pack does string lookups per signal. In a real-time lockstep loop (e.g. 1 kHz SIL with dozens of signals) this is tens of thousands of allocations per second in the latency-critical path, for keys that are fixed at codec-compile time.
- **Evidence:** codec.rs:225 `values.insert(op.var.clone(), raw * op.scale);` inside the per-packet op loop; ops and their var names are precompiled once in UnpackCodec::compile but re-cloned every unpack call.
- **Verifier note:** Verified codec.rs: unpack() builds a fresh SignalFrame (IndexMap<String,f64>) per call and line 225 inserts op.var.clone() (heap String) inside the per-op loop; var names are precompiled once in UnpackOp but re-cloned every unpack. pack() line 489 does values.get(&op.var) string lookups per signal. SignalFrame confirmed as IndexMap<String,f64>. Both called in lockstep executor loop (executor.rs:971 pack, :1256 unpack). Real but bounded efficiency issue; not a correctness/MLS problem. Low severity accurate.
- **Remediation:** Precompute Arc<str> or interned keys in UnpackOp (SignalFrame keyed by Arc<str> or a small-index slot map negotiated at compile time), and let unpack write into a caller-provided reusable frame.

### A.tests — Testing strategy & MSL validation (7 confirmed)

#### [MEDIUM] ModelicaTest semantic gate contains exactly one model

The committed CI target list contains a single model, ModelicaTest.Blocks.MuxDemux, so the gate that is supposed to catch wrong-answer semantic regressions (as opposed to the MSL example gate's mostly-structural coverage) validates almost nothing. The CI job (ci.yml 'ModelicaTest Semantic Gate', lines 1241-1347) overlays the full ModelicaTest source tree and runs with --require-selected-targets-success, so the infrastructure for a real gate exists; only the target list is vestigial.

SPEC_0025 §4: "Run the separate ModelicaTest semantic gate ... ModelicaTest is an assertion-heavy semantic suite".
- **Verifier correction:** Severity should be medium: a real, spec-relevant coverage gap in a working gate, but bounded and does not itself cause incorrect results or an MLS violation.
- **Verifier note:** Verified directly: modelica_test_targets_ci.json = ["ModelicaTest.Blocks.MuxDemux"] (single model, only ModelicaTest target list in tree). SPEC_0025 §4 mandates a separate assertion-heavy ModelicaTest semantic gate over "selected ModelicaTest.* models" (plural). ci.yml:1325-1343 runs msl-parity with --include-modelica-test --require-selected-targets-success on that one-model file. So the spec-mandated semantic gate is effectively vestigial — claim holds. Downgrading high→medium: this is a bounded CI test-coverage gap, not wrong sim results/MLS-compiler violation; other gates (MSL example, pinned modelica_models) give overlapping coverage.
- **Remediation:** Grow the target list to the ModelicaTest models that currently pass (the harness hard-fails on missing/typoed names, so this is safe), and add a ratchet so the list can only grow. Prioritize ModelicaTest.Math, ModelicaTest.Blocks and ModelicaTest.MultiBody assertion models, which encode expected numeric results via assert().

#### [MEDIUM] Trace metric misclassifies near-zero reference channels (1e-12 normalization floor)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-sim/src/sim_trace_compare.rs:9` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** For continuous channels the normalization scale is max(p95-p05 reference range, 1e-12). A channel whose OMC reference is (nearly) constant zero — ground currents, ideal node voltages, LossPower of ideal elements — gets scale 1e-12, so numerically irrelevant absolute noise (1e-9 A in a circuit with ~1 A currents) yields bounded_normalized_l1_error ~ 1.0 and is counted as a bad AND severe channel. This inflates the headline bad/severe-channel percentages (11.1%/1.96% in the current triage), pollutes the shape taxonomy (the triage worst-channel lists are full of `ground.p.i [unknown]`, `V.n.i`, `Ground1.p.i`), and can flip model agreement bands (High requires deviation-channel share <= 1%), spuriously tripping or masking the trace gate.
- **Evidence:** sim_trace_compare.rs:9 `const NORMALIZATION_SCALE_EPS: f64 = 1.0e-12;` and :680-684 `let normalization_scale = if use_step_hold { reference_range.max(1.0)... } else { reference_range.max(NORMALIZATION_SCALE_EPS) };`. dev/msl-triage-current.md worst channels: "ground.p.i [unknown]" (HBridge_R, IdealTriacCircuit), "G.p.i [unknown]" (CauerLowPassAnalog) — classic constant-zero references. Discrete channels get a 1.0 floor (line 681) but continuous ones do not.
- **Verifier correction:** Severity overstated. It is a genuine metric-quality defect but does not affect simulation correctness or MLS compliance; impact is polluted triage taxonomy and channel-share inflation in a baseline-relative gate. Medium.
- **Verifier note:** Verified in code: use_step_hold == is_discrete_channel (lines 233-241). Continuous channels get normalization_scale = reference_range.max(1e-12) (line 683) while discrete get .max(1.0) (681). robust_reference_range returns (p95-p05).abs() ~0 for constant-zero refs, so scale=1e-12; a ~1e-9 error normalizes to ~1000 -> bounded ~1.0 -> severe. Triage doc (dev/msl-triage-current.md) corroborates: worst-channel lists dominated by ground.p.i, G.p.i, V.n.i. Percentages feed the trace gate. Real defect, but confined to triage/gate metric tooling (area tests), not simulation results or MLS; gate is baseline-relative so bias is shared. Bounded -> medium, not high.
- **Remediation:** Give continuous channels an absolute floor tied to signal magnitude: scale = max(range, alpha * max(|p05|,|p95|), Modelica `nominal` attribute, global_abs_floor) — the approach used by the Modelica Association csv-compare tube algorithm. Re-baseline bad/severe channel counts afterwards; expect a visible drop in false severe channels.

#### [MEDIUM] Event-heavy benchmark cohort excluded from correctness validation by CI timeouts

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-test-msl/tests/balance_pipeline/mod.rs:58` — MLS 8.5 (events), MLS 3.8.5 · SPEC_0032 §5 (failing models must stay visible and classified) · category: testing
- **Claim:** Per-model budgets (10s solver via rumoca_worker::MSL_SIM_TIMEOUT_SECS, 10s IR-solve, 45s phase watchdog) cause the canonical event/friction benchmarks — Mechanics.Rotational.Examples.Friction, CoupledClutches, Backlash, SimpleGearShift, Translational.Friction/Vehicle — to land in sim.timeout on shared CI runners (20 models in the current triage). These are exactly the models that stress event iteration, mode switching, and reinit semantics, so the suite has no continuous evidence about rumoca's event correctness on the canonical cohort; the timeout bucket structurally conflates 'slow' with 'unvalidated'. The gate treats them as permanent failures, so a wrong-event-semantics regression in these models is also invisible (they cannot regress further).
- **Evidence:** mod.rs:58 `pub(super) const MODEL_ATTEMPT_TIMEOUT_SECS: f64 = 45.0;`; rumoca-worker/src/lib.rs:13 `pub const MSL_SIM_TIMEOUT_SECS: f64 = 10.0;`; dev/msl-triage-current.md lines 89-93: Backlash "45.758s in phase Sim", CoupledClutches "46.087s", Friction "47.206s", SimpleGearShift, Translational Friction — all `sim.timeout`.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified all cited evidence: MODEL_ATTEMPT_TIMEOUT_SECS=45.0 (mod.rs:58) and MSL_SIM_TIMEOUT_SECS=10.0 (worker lib.rs:13) exist as stated. msl-triage-current.md line 37 confirms 20 models in sim.timeout; the named friction/event cohort (Backlash 45.758s, CoupledClutches 46.087s, Friction 47.206s, SimpleGearShift, Translational Friction/Vehicle) all time out in phase Sim, consistent with event-iteration cost. No nightly or long-budget lane handles this (only ci.yml; no long-budget constant found). The coverage gap is real and bounded: already-timing-out models yield no continuous event-correctness signal and cannot visibly regress. Testing gap, not a wrong-sim-result defect, so medium (real but bounded) is appropriate.
- **Remediation:** Add a small nightly (or per-merge) long-budget lane (e.g. 300s) for the known-slow event cohort so their traces are actually compared against OMC, and separately profile why 1-second-horizon friction models need >45s (likely event-iteration thrashing — itself a correctness signal).

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-test-msl/tests/casadi_msl_test.rs:1` — SPEC_0025 §4 (commands not run = untested paths) · category: testing
- **Claim:** The MSL-scale cross-backend suites (casadi_msl_test, fmi2_msl_test, fmi3_msl_test, embedded_c_msl_test, backend_stress_test; ~3,800 LOC, 24-39 curated targets each) are gated behind the `msl-external-tests` feature, which appears nowhere in .github/workflows/ci.yml — the CI 'Template Runtime' matrix runs only the small-model `template-runtime-tests` in the rumoca crate. So generated FMU/CasADi/embedded-C code at MSL scale can silently regress. They also use rumoca's own diffsol trace as the reference, so a shared compiler-side numeric bug passes all backends consistently; only the main gate's OMC parity would catch it, and only for the interpreter path.

Both features defined in Cargo.toml but enabled nowhere (grep across rs/toml/sh/justfile/.github found only the test files + doc-comments). grep of both features in ci.yml returned no matches; only one workflow exists (no nightly). template-runtime-tests job runs `xtask verify template-runtimes` (a different small-model path). Self-compare confirmed: tests build rumoca diffsol trace via simulate_dae and compare to backend trace. LOC 3919 matches. Bounded testing gap → medium.
- **Remediation:** Run at least fmi3_msl_test and casadi_msl_test on a nightly/weekly schedule (they are already curated to ~39 models), and for a subset compare backend traces against the cached OMC references rather than rumoca-vs-rumoca.

#### [LOW] No property-based or fuzz testing anywhere in the workspace

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-parse/Cargo.toml:1` — SPEC_0021 (robustness/determinism) · category: testing
- **Claim:** A workspace-wide grep finds no proptest, quickcheck, arbitrary, or cargo-fuzz usage in any of the 53 crates, and no fuzz/ directory. The parser crate (rumoca-phase-parse) has only 52 unit tests; grammar robustness is validated exclusively by the MSL corpus (well-formed code) plus hand-written cases, so malformed/adversarial .mo input paths (a compiler's primary untrusted surface, also exposed via the LSP and WASM playground on every keystroke of partial code) are untested. Numeric components (trace comparison, solvers, event scheduling) similarly lack property tests for invariants such as interpolation monotonicity, grid-merge idempotence, or metric bounds.

Test counts: rumoca-phase-parse has 52 #[test], rumoca-phase-solve has 839 — matching the finding's ratio. The only "fuzz" hits are fuzzy symbol matching in the LSP, unrelated. The claim is an accurate testing-coverage observation. It is not a code defect (no wrong results), just a genuine untested-input-surface gap, so severity is closer to low than medium. spec_ref labels SPEC_0021 as robustness/determinism but it is actually CODE_COMPLEXITY — minor mislabel, not central.
- **Remediation:** Add a cargo-fuzz target for the lexer/parser (crash/hang-freedom on arbitrary bytes, then round-trip via the formatter), and proptest suites for sim_trace_compare (metric in [0,1), permutation/grid-refinement stability) and solver step-size logic. Even a small nightly fuzz budget would cover the largest untested input surface.

#### [LOW] OMC reference cache permanently reuses transient error/timeout results within a cache key

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-test-msl/src/msl_tools/omc_simulation_reference.rs:1035` — SPEC_0032 §5 (failures must stay visible) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** cached_omc_result_is_reusable treats status "error" and "timeout" as always reusable, so a transient CI failure (runner OOM, cold-cache gcc timeout) while building an OMC reference becomes sticky for the lifetime of the OMC+MSL cache key: that model is silently dropped from trace parity on all subsequent runs until --force or an OMC/MSL upgrade. With TRACE_MODELS_COMPARED_ALLOWED_DROP = 2, up to two models can vanish from parity coverage without failing the gate. Relatedly, the comment at the call site claims --rumoca-sim-ok-only is CI-only ("local runs default to all targets") but the flag is passed unconditionally (balance_pipeline_quality_gate.rs:867-871), so the documented all-targets default of select_omc_simulation_models is dead code in the canonical flow.
- **Evidence:** omc_simulation_reference.rs:1034-1036: `match result.status.as_str() { "error" | "timeout" => true, ... }`; balance_pipeline_quality_gate.rs:861-878 always pushes "--rumoca-sim-ok-only" while its comment says "local runs default to all targets"; runtime.rs:8-10 comment documents the never-used default.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified all evidence. omc_simulation_reference.rs:1035 returns true for "error"|"timeout", and line 706 skips those cached models on reuse, so transient failures stay sticky within an OMC+MSL cache key until --force. balance_pipeline_quality_gate.rs:870 pushes --rumoca-sim-ok-only unconditionally while the line-868 comment claims local runs default to all targets — a real mismatch; runtime.rs:8-13 all-targets default is thus unused in the canonical flow. TRACE_MODELS_COMPARED_ALLOWED_DROP=2 confirmed. Impact is test/parity-coverage infra, not simulation correctness, so low is correct.
- **Remediation:** Retry cached error/timeout OMC results after N runs or when the failure text matches transient patterns (timeout, OOM), and either make --rumoca-sim-ok-only actually conditional on CI or fix the comments and delete the dead default path.

#### [LOW] Quadratic string-matching in per-model discrete-scalar accounting

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-test-msl/tests/msl_tests.rs:352` — SPEC_0021 · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** active_discrete_scalar_count runs for every successfully compiled model (545 per full run): it materializes every variable reference in every DAE partition into a HashSet<String> (a fresh HashSet + String conversions per equation, ~10^4 strings for large Electrical/MultiBody models) and then, for each discrete variable, does `active.iter().any(|a| prefix-match)` — an O(D x A) scan with string prefix comparisons (10^6-10^7 ops for machine/converter models). This sits inside the same 45s per-model phase budget as compilation itself, so harness accounting overhead eats into the compile watchdog margin on shared runners and contributes to borderline timeouts.
- **Evidence:** msl_tests.rs:357-378: `.filter(|(name, _)| active.iter().any(|active_name| names_match_via_component_prefix(active_name, name.as_str())))` over discrete_reals and discrete_valued; :233-279 allocates `let mut refs = HashSet::new();` plus per-name String conversions for every equation in five partitions.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read msl_tests.rs:233-381. Code matches the claim exactly: collect_active_refs_from_dae/_flat build a HashSet<String> with fresh per-equation HashSets and .to_string() conversions; active_discrete_scalar_count then filters discrete_reals+discrete_valued with active.iter().any(prefix-match) — genuinely O(D×A) string-prefix work. Called per successful model from summarize_success_result (balance_pipeline/mod.rs:723). This is test-harness accounting only (rumoca-test-msl test file), so no simulation-correctness impact; the watchdog-timeout angle is speculative. Real but minor. Severity low correct.
- **Remediation:** Collect active names once into a sorted Vec/BTreeSet and answer prefix queries with range scans (O((D+A) log A)), interning names as Arc<str>/SmolStr instead of String; or compute the active-discrete count in the compiler-side balance_detail where the symbol table already exists.

### A.tooling — Developer tooling: LSP, formatter, linter (9 confirmed)

#### [HIGH] LSP panics on lines containing non-ASCII text (UTF-16 column byte-sliced)

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-tool-lsp/src/helpers.rs:43` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** get_word_at_position, get_dotted_token_at_position, and get_text_before_cursor treat the client-supplied LSP Position.character (UTF-16 code units) as a byte index and slice the line with line[..col]. Whenever any non-ASCII character (°, umlaut, Ω, CJK — all common in MSL description strings and comments) precedes the cursor column, col lands mid-code-point and the &str slice panics, unwinding through the tower-lsp handler and killing the server. Hover fires on mouse move and completion on typing, so opening real MSL files makes this reachable constantly. The dedicated rumoca-lsp-position crate solves exactly this but is only used in handlers/diagnostics.rs; completion_metrics.rs line 59 even uses the safe line.get(..) pattern, showing the inconsistency.
- **Evidence:** let col = position.character as usize;
    if col > line.len() {
        return None;
    }
    let start = line[..col]
        .rfind(|c: char| !c.is_alphanumeric() && c != '_')
(helpers.rs:39-46; `position.character` is UTF-16 units, `line[..col]` is a byte slice — e.g. line "éx", hover on x sends character=1, byte 1 is mid-é → panic). Same pattern at get_text_before_cursor: `Some(line[..col].to_string())` (line 210).
- **Verifier correction:** "Kills the server" may be overstated — tower-lsp/tokio can isolate a per-request panic. The panic itself (broken hover/completion on non-ASCII) is real.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read helpers.rs: get_word_at_position (L43), get_dotted_token_at_position (L155), get_text_before_cursor (L210) all slice line[..col] with col=position.character as usize. LSP character is UTF-16 units; the col>line.len() guard does NOT prevent a mid-codepoint byte index, so line[..col] panics on any non-ASCII prefix (e.g. "éx"). Callers: hover/goto_definition/completion/signature_help/server all pass client positions. rumoca-lsp-position::position_to_byte_offset exists with boundary/surrogate handling but is unused here; completion_metrics uses safe line.get(..). Only overstatement: panic may be isolated to the request rather than crashing the whole process, but the reachable panic on common MSL content stands.
- **Remediation:** Route every Position->offset conversion through rumoca_lsp_position::position_to_byte_offset (it already clamps forward to char boundaries), or at minimum replace direct slicing with line.get(..byte_col) after converting UTF-16 units to a byte offset. Add a regression test hovering/completing after a '°' and a CJK character.
- **Remediation status (2026-07-24): resolved on the current branch.** The
  cursor helpers now convert UTF-16 LSP positions through
  `rumoca_lsp_position::position_to_byte_offset` before slicing. Focused tests
  cover degree symbols, CJK, and astral characters for word, dotted-token,
  text-before-cursor, and comment-context queries.

#### [HIGH] Hover runs an uncached strict compile to DAE under the session write lock

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-tool-lsp/src/server.rs:1278` · category: efficiency
- **Claim:** When the hovered word is a top-level model/block/class in the open file, the hover handler acquires the exclusive session write lock and calls flattened_preview_for_model, which invokes compile_model_strict_reachable_uncached_with_recovery — an explicitly uncached full strict pipeline (instantiate, typecheck, flatten, DAE, balance). Every hover over the model name repeats the full compile; for MSL-scale models this blocks the single RwLock and therefore stalls diagnostics, completion, and did_change processing for the duration. It also bypasses the strict work lane that all other strict compiles respect, undermining the server's own lane discipline.
- **Evidence:** && let Some(preview) = {
    let mut session = self.session.write().await;
    flattened_preview_for_model(&mut session, &word)
} (server.rs:1277-1281) and preview.rs:98: `session.compile_model_strict_reachable_uncached_with_recovery(model_name)` — name confirms no caching; hover holds `session.write()` for the whole compile.
- **Verifier note:** Verified end-to-end: hover (server.rs:1277-1281) takes self.session.write() and synchronously calls flattened_preview_for_model, which (preview.rs:98) invokes compile_model_strict_reachable_uncached_with_recovery -> compile_model_strict_reachable_report(name,false) -> finalize_strict_compile_report_from_uncached_targets, a full uncached instantiate/typecheck/flatten/DAE/balance pipeline. It runs on no spawn_blocking and does NOT take work_lanes.strict, unlike simulation compiles (server.rs:749,845). The shared session RwLock is read by document/snapshot queries and written by did_change, so the compile blocks them. Not a correctness bug; a real major-perf/latency issue. High is appropriate (medium defensible since hover is user-triggered and gated to top-level model names, but uncached full compile under the exclusive write lock justifies high).
- **Remediation:** Cache the preview per (model, session revision) like the simulation compile cache, run it on the strict lane via a cloned StrictSessionSnapshot on spawn_blocking instead of under the live session write lock, and/or gate the preview behind a debounce or an explicit code-lens action rather than passive hover.

#### [MEDIUM] Editor formatting and linting ignore SPEC_0018 config files

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-tool-lsp/src/handlers/formatting.rs:8` — SPEC_0018 §Configuration File Names / Hierarchical Lookup · category: project-spec
- **Claim:** The LSP formatting handler hardcodes FormatOptions::default() and the diagnostics handler hardcodes LintOptions::default() (handlers/diagnostics.rs:60). SPEC_0018 defines .rumoca_fmt.toml/.rumoca_lint.toml hierarchical lookup so that editor and CI behavior agree, and the rumoca CLI implements it (fmt_cli.rs loads config and overlays CLI options) — but the editor path never calls find_config/load_config_from_dir. A project configured with profile="canonical" or disabled_rules=["naming-convention"] gets Dymola-profile formatting and the naming rule anyway inside VS Code, producing exactly the editor/CLI divergence the spec exists to prevent. params.options (client tab size) is also ignored.
- **Evidence:** pub fn handle_formatting(source: &str) -> Option<Vec<TextEdit>> {
    let options = FormatOptions::default();
    let formatted = format(source, &options).ok()?; (formatting.rs:7-9)
and diagnostics.rs:60-61: let lint_options = LintOptions::default();
    let lint_messages = lint(source, file_name, &lint_options); — no find_config/load_config call anywhere in rumoca-tool-lsp.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified in code: formatting.rs uses FormatOptions::default() (default profile = Dymola, format_options.rs:140/296); diagnostics.rs:60 uses LintOptions::default(). server.rs:1617-1621 calls handle_formatting(&doc.content) discarding params.options (tab size). Grep confirms the only load_config/find_config calls in rumoca-tool-lsp are for unrelated scenario config; fmt/lint config (.rumoca_fmt.toml/.rumoca_lint.toml) is never loaded there. CLI does load it (fmt_cli.rs:305, cli.rs:1375). SPEC_0018 defines the hierarchical fmt/lint config; editor bypasses it, producing real editor/CLI divergence. Bounded to tooling UX, no simulation impact — medium is correct.
- **Remediation:** In the server, resolve the document's directory from the URI and call rumoca_tool_fmt::load_config_overrides_from_dir / rumoca_tool_lint::load_config_from_dir (cached per directory, invalidated on config file change), falling back to defaults; thread the result into handle_formatting/compute_diagnostics.

#### [MEDIUM] Formatter assignment-spacing rules can rewrite comment text between LHS and RHS

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-tool-fmt/src/formatter/trivia.rs:342` — SPEC_0018 §TOML Format (normalize_equation_spacing semantics) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** assignment_operator_replacement (and the ':=' and compact variants) locate the operator with rfind on the raw trivia-gap text between LHS and RHS spans. If a comment containing '=' sits in that gap — e.g. `x = /* gain == 2 */ y;` under normalize_equation_spacing or the Canonical profile — rfind('=') matches the '=' inside the comment and the whitespace-normalizing replacement rewrites characters inside the comment ("== 2" becomes "= =  2"), altering user comment content. This also breaks idempotence for that input. All other rules (comma/dot/paren) whitelist the entire gap to whitespace+delimiter characters and are safe; only the assignment family scans an arbitrary gap.
- **Evidence:** let assignment_offset = self.text.rfind('=')?;
    let start = self.start + trim_horizontal_whitespace_before(self.text, assignment_offset);
    let end = self.start
        + trim_horizontal_whitespace_after(self.text, assignment_offset + '='.len_utf8());
    Some(TextReplacement { start, end, text: " = " }) (trivia.rs:342-350) — gap text for `x = /* k == 2 */ y` is " = /* k == 2 */ " and rfind('=') hits the comment interior.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read trivia.rs:338-381: assignment/compact/`:=` variants guard only newlines then rfind the operator on raw gap text. Reached from collect_simple_equation_assignment (formatter.rs:629) via gap_between_expressions, gated by normalize_equation_spacing (Canonical). gap_between_spans slices raw source, so inline comments are included. Hand-traced `x = /* gain == 2 */ y`: rfind('=') hits the comment's `=`, replacement rewrites comment interior and breaks idempotence. No comment guard exists (grep empty); comma/dot/binary rules whitelist the whole gap so they bail on comments, confirming the differential. Bounded, tooling-only, no sim impact -> medium.
- **Remediation:** Reject gaps whose text contains "/*" or "//" (mirroring the newline guard), or restrict the assignment rules to gaps whose non-whitespace content is exactly the operator, as the comma/dot rules already do.

#### [MEDIUM] No formatter idempotence or MSL round-trip test

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-tool-fmt/src/formatter/tests.rs:1` · category: testing
- **Claim:** All 61 formatter tests assert a single format() pass against an expected string; there is no test that format(format(s)) == format(s) for either profile, and no harness formats the vendored MSL and re-parses/re-formats it (xtask's fmt check at verify_cmd.rs:1094 is rustfmt for Rust code, not the Modelica formatter). Given the two-phase design (AST-trivia replacements then a stateful line pass) plus known state-machine edge cases (escape parity, comment-gap rewrites), idempotence and parse-preservation are plausible but unverified — a regression here would silently churn user diffs on every save once format-on-save is enabled.
- **Evidence:** grep for 'idempot|twice|format(&format' over crates/rumoca-tool-fmt returns nothing; every test is of the form `let formatted = format(source, &opts); assert_eq!(formatted, expected);`. xtask verify only runs `test_cmd::run_workspace_fmt_check` (rustfmt) — no Modelica-formatter corpus check.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read crates/rumoca-tool-fmt/src/formatter/tests.rs: 122 tests, 61 assert_eq!, every one of the form `let formatted = format(source,&opts); assert_eq!(formatted, expected)`. No double-format (format(format(s))), no reparse, no idempotence/round-trip test anywhere in the crate (grep empty). No integration tests dir. xtask verify's "fmt check" is test_cmd.rs:9 `cargo fmt --all -- --check` = rustfmt on Rust, not the Modelica formatter; no .mo corpus/MSL formatting harness. Claim's facts all hold. Testing gap is real but bounded (no demonstrated bug, idempotence only "plausible"); medium is defensible though borderline low.
- **Remediation:** Add a property-style test that formats every fixture (and the vendored MSL tree, behind the msl-parity harness) twice under both profiles, asserting second-pass stability and that the output still parses to an equivalent AST (token stream ignoring whitespace).

#### [LOW] Linter missing-documentation rule inverts Modelica description-string placement; rules fire inside comments

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-tool-lint/src/lint_rules.rs:105` — MLS 3.7 Annex A (description-string grammar); §4.5 class declarations · SPEC_0018 §TOML Format (lint config) · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** MissingDocumentationRule accepts a declaration as documented only if the PREVIOUS line starts with a quote, but Modelica description strings follow the identifier (`model Foo "doc"` or on the next line) per the MLS grammar (class-specifier ... description-string); no Modelica dialect puts docs on the preceding line. The rule therefore flags essentially all correctly documented code and blesses none — it only avoids user harm because Note-level messages are filtered at the default Warning min_level, i.e. the rule is effectively dead. Additionally, all three rules are raw trimmed-line string heuristics: NamingConventionRule flags prose like 'model foo ...' inside block comments or strings, and MagicNumberRule skips any line containing the substring "start" and misparses exponents (3.5e2 splits into 3.5 and 2). The linter currently provides near-zero real value beyond syntax errors.
- **Evidence:** fn has_doc_on_prev_line(lines: &[&str], line_num: usize) -> bool {
    line_num > 0 && lines[line_num - 1].trim().starts_with('"')
} (lint_rules.rs:105-107) — Modelica puts the description string AFTER the name (same line or following line), never before; `model Foo "docs"` is reported as missing documentation.
- **Verifier correction:** Severity overstated as medium. This is an advisory-only linter with no effect on compilation or simulation correctness; the two most-broken rules (missing-documentation, magic-number) are suppressed at the default Warning min_level, leaving only the naming rule's comment/string false positives visible. Appropriate severity is low.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read lint_rules.rs: has_doc_on_prev_line (105-107) checks the PREVIOUS line for a leading quote, inverted vs Modelica (desc string follows the name). MissingDocumentationRule emits Note (226), MagicNumberRule emits Help (302); default min_level=Warning (lint_options.rs:84) and lint.rs:82 retains only level>=min_level, so both are filtered/dead at default. should_skip_magic_number_check skips substring "start" (145); is_number_char excludes 'e' so 3.5e2 splits. NamingConvention uses raw trimmed lines, no comment stripping, fires at Warning. All evidence holds. Downgraded to low: advisory linter, no simulation/MLS-semantic impact, worst rules level-filtered by default.
- **Remediation:** Rewrite the rules over the parsed AST (which the LSP already has in hand): read ClassDef description strings directly for missing-documentation, use class/component name tokens for naming, and expression literals for magic numbers. Until then, remove or downgrade missing-documentation so it can never ship enabled.

#### [LOW] Formatter line state machine: escape-parity and keyword-prefix misclassification

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-tool-fmt/src/formatter/dymola.rs:273` — MLS 3.7 §2.4 (string escape sequences) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** update_format_state toggles in_string on '"' only when prev_char != '\\', so a string ending in an escaped backslash ("...\\") leaves in_string latched for the rest of the file — silently disabling trailing-whitespace trimming and indentation for everything after (the sibling helper is_position_in_comment in the LSP handles escape state correctly, so the fix pattern exists in-repo). Separately, update_format_section matches lower.starts_with("equation")/("algorithm") so a legal identifier like `equationCount = 1;` at line start flips the section state, and lines inside block comments are not excluded from section switching (only in_string/in_quoted_identifier are checked). All failure directions are conservative (formatting suppressed or indentation repair skipped) but they make Dymola-profile output input-order dependent in rare files.
- **Evidence:** if !state.in_quoted_identifier && c == '"' && state.prev_char != '\\' {
    state.in_string = !state.in_string; (dymola.rs:273-274) — for "a\\" the closing quote's prev_char is '\\' so the string never closes. update_format_section (319-334): `if state.in_string || state.in_quoted_identifier { return; }` — no in_block_comment check; `lower.starts_with("equation")` matches identifier `equationcount`.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read dymola.rs. Line 273 toggles in_string only when prev_char != '\\'; tracing "a\\" the closing quote has prev_char=='\\' so in_string latches for the rest of the file — escaped-backslash parity bug confirmed. update_format_section (319-334) uses lower.starts_with("equation")/("algorithm") (matches identifier equationCount) and only guards in_string/in_quoted_identifier, not in_block_comment, so block-comment lines beginning with a keyword flip the section. Both confirmed. Effects are formatter-only (indentation/trim suppression, section misclassification); no simulation/IR impact, all conservative. Severity low is correct.
- **Remediation:** Track escape parity with a bool toggled on '\\' (as helpers.rs does), require a word boundary after section keywords (starts_with("equation") && next char is whitespace/EOL), and early-return from update_format_section while in_block_comment.

#### [LOW] Inlay hints (incl. array-dimension hints) implemented but never advertised

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-tool-lsp/src/server.rs:224` — SPEC_0032_RANGE_PRESERVING_TENSORS (editor visibility of array structure) · category: architecture
- **Claim:** handlers/inlay_hints.rs implements array-dimension hints (rendering component shapes like ' [3x3]' from ast shape/shape_expr — a nice tensor-structure visibility feature aligned with the SPEC_0032 tensor-native story) plus builtin-call parameter hints, and server.rs implements the inlay_hint request — but server_capabilities() sets inlay_hint_provider: None, so conforming clients will never issue the request. The feature is dead code in production; either it was disabled deliberately (no comment says so) or the capability wiring was forgotten.
- **Evidence:** inlay_hint_provider: None, (server.rs:224) while server.rs:1791 implements `async fn inlay_hint(...)` and handlers/inlay_hints.rs:27-60 builds dimension hints: `label: InlayHintLabel::String(format!(" [{}]", dims))`.
- **Verifier correction:** Core claim verified: inlay_hint_provider is None (server.rs:224) yet inlay_hint() (server.rs:1791) and handle_inlay_hints (handlers/inlay_hints.rs) are fully implemented, so conforming clients never trigger the array-dimension hint feature. Correction: it is NOT forgotten — server/tests.rs:1720-1722 asserts None with the rationale "inlay hints stay disabled until the selective mode is re-enabled," so disabling is deliberate and documented (in the test, not at the capability site).
