So handler code is live but unreachable via capabilities — dead in production. But the "forgotten" framing dies: test at 1720 documents the deliberate choice pending a "selective mode." Low severity: no simulation impact, purely an editor-UX/architecture gap.
- **Remediation:** Advertise InlayHintServerCapabilities (resolve_provider: false), or delete the handler and note why; if latency was the concern, hints here are AST-only and cheap. Keeping tensor-dimension hints visible supports the range-preserving-tensor UX story.

#### [LOW] Navigation/rename ranges use lexer char-columns and byte lengths as UTF-16 without conversion

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-tool-lsp/src/helpers.rs:8` · category: correctness
- **Claim:** token_to_range/location_to_range pass Location start/end columns straight through as LSP UTF-16 characters. The recovery lexer counts columns per Rust char (Unicode scalar), which equals UTF-16 units only for BMP text — astral characters (emoji in comments, mathematical alphanumerics) shift every subsequent range on the line, so rename TextEdits (server/navigation.rs builds edits from location_to_range) would splice at the wrong columns and corrupt the line. Semantic token lengths additionally use token.text.len() (bytes, semantic_tokens.rs:140-195), which diverges for any non-ASCII quoted identifier. Unlike the byte-slicing panic this only mis-positions rather than crashes, and BMP-only files are unaffected, hence low.
- **Evidence:** start: Position { line: token.location.start_line.saturating_sub(1), character: token.location.start_column.saturating_sub(1) } (helpers.rs:10-13) — no UTF-16 conversion; lexer counts columns via `self.column += 1` per char (rumoca-phase-parse/src/recovery.rs:97); semantic_tokens.rs:156 `class.name.text.len() as u32` is a byte length.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified: helpers.rs:8-33 passes lexer start/end_column straight through as LSP Position.character with no UTF-16 conversion; recovery.rs:97 increments column per Rust char (== UTF-16 only for BMP). navigation.rs:81-94 builds rename TextEdits from location_to_range; semantic_tokens.rs uses text.len() (bytes) for lengths. Server negotiates no position_encoding, so LSP default UTF-16 applies — units are genuinely wrong for astral (columns) and any non-ASCII (byte lengths). Project has a correct UTF-16 span_to_range (rumoca-lsp-position) used by diagnostics but NOT these paths, confirming the gap. Mispositions, not crashes, and only for non-ASCII source; low is right.
- **Remediation:** Standardize on byte spans (Location.start/end already exist) plus rumoca_lsp_position::span_to_range everywhere a Range is emitted (navigation, rename, semantic tokens, symbols), retiring the column-arithmetic helpers.

### A.gap:Synchronous/clocked models (MLS ch 16) s — Synchronous/clocked models (MLS ch 16) silently produce grossly wrong traces — the worst-performing cohort in the entire MSL suite, yet no finding covers it (3 confirmed)

#### [HIGH] No dedicated once-per-tick clocked solve; clocked previous() history is committed on every event boundary by the shared pre-param machinery

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver/src/runtime/pre_params.rs:25` — MLS 16.4, 16.5.1 (previous/hold, per-clock partition solving) · dev/msl-triage-current.md:141-146 (Modelica.Clocked SimpleControlledDrive cohort, monotonic_drift/event_time_mismatch) · category: architecture
- **Claim:** Synchronous clocked partitions are folded into the generic when/discrete-event path: previous(v) becomes a __pre__ parameter slot, and commit_pre_params_after_event rewrites ALL pre slots (including clocked previous() history) from post-event state at every event boundary, not exclusively at the owning variable's own clock ticks, so recurrence state and clocked-to-continuous hold outputs are not guaranteed to advance exactly once per tick.
- **Evidence:** commit_pre_params_after_event (pre_params.rs:25-33) calls write_pre_params_from_sources over `model.problem.solve_layout.pre_param_bindings` (all pre slots) and is invoked after scheduled time events (sim_driver.rs:604) and after every zero-crossing root (sim_driver.rs:810). previous() lowers to a Pre-mode __pre__ slot (lower/function_calls.rs:181-186) and clocked equations are classified into ordinary discrete buckets (analysis/discrete_partition.rs). No separate synchronous per-tick solve exists.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified in tree. pre_params.rs write_pre_params_from_sources iterates ALL solve_layout.pre_param_bindings; commit_pre_params_after_event wraps it and is called unconditionally at every boundary — sim_driver.rs:604 (time events) and :810 (zero-crossing roots), plus all backends (diffsol runtime.rs, rk45, no_state, prepared_vectors). previous() lowers to a Pre-mode __pre__ slot (function_calls.rs:180-187); clocked eqs go to generic discrete buckets (discrete_partition.rs). IR confirms no per-clock association: PreParamBinding = {dest_p_index, source} only; PeriodicEventSchedule = {period, phase} only. No per-clock gating found anywhere. Triage md:139-145 shows SimpleControlledDrive cohort worst traces with monotonic_drift/event_time_mismatch. High: MLS-16 synchronous violation, wrong results but bounded to clocked models.
- **Remediation:** Give clocked partitions a distinct once-per-tick evaluation keyed to their own clock schedule, snapshotting previous()/pre() for a clocked variable only when that variable's clock ticks (not on unrelated zero-crossings or other clocks' ticks). This isolates the discrete controller recurrence from the continuous when-event machinery and removes the monotonic drift on recurrence/plant states (PI.x, load.a) seen in the SimpleControlledDrive cohort.
- **Remediation status (2026-07-24): resolved on the current branch.** Solve schema 22 adds the owning `PeriodicEventSchedule` to clocked `PreParamBinding`s. Solve lowering derives the association from authoritative DAE per-variable clock timing (including scalarized array members), and every numeric backend now commits clock-associated history at the semantic event time only when that schedule matches; ordinary `pre()` continues to advance at every event. Focused tests prove unrelated clock boundaries do not advance the history and initialization still seeds every pre slot.

#### [MEDIUM] Clocked partition activation reconstructed from continuous `time` with a 1e-9·period tolerance decoupled from the 1e-12 event scheduler

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/clock.rs:219` — MLS 16.3 (base/sub clock activation) · category: correctness
- **Claim:** The 'is this a clock tick' predicate baked into every clocked equation is recomputed at runtime from the continuous time value using floor(delta/period+0.5) and a 1e-9*max(period,1) tolerance, instead of being driven by the authoritative periodic event that stopped the solver.
- **Evidence:** emit_periodic_tick (clock.rs:219-243): `let delta = time - phase; ... tol = 1e-9 * max(period,1); ... quotient=delta/period; tick_index=floor(quotient+0.5); nearest=tick_index*period; at_tick = |delta-nearest| <= tol; after_start && at_tick`. The scheduler instead matches ticks with `1e-12*(1+|t|)` (timeline.rs:39). The activation is a function of `time`, not of the event schedule, and the two tolerances differ 1000x.
- **Verifier correction:** Claim is accurate; severity high overstates practical impact. Ticks are never missed (scheduler places stops within 1e-12, inside the 1e-9*period window); the only realizable defect is spurious activation when an unrelated stop lands within 1e-9*period of a tick — rare for typical periods (>=1s gives relative 1e-9), material only for very fast clocks. Recommendation to unify with sample_time_match_with_tol / schedule-driven flag remains valid.
- **Verifier note:** Verified verbatim. emit_periodic_tick (clock.rs:219-243) reconstructs the tick from continuous time: delta=time-phase, tol=1e-9*max(period,1), at_tick=|delta-round(delta/period)*period|<=tol, ANDed with after_start. Scheduler uses sample_time_match_with_tol (timeline.rs:39) = 1e-12*(1+max|a|,|b|), and gates ticks via periodic_schedule_matches_time (line 133) — a distinct predicate. So activation IS a function of time, decoupled from the schedule, with a ~1000x wider tolerance. Downgrade to medium: solver stops at ticks within 1e-12 so a tick cannot be missed; only spurious activation at a non-tick stop within 1e-9*period is possible (narrow), worst for sub-microsecond clocks where max(period,1) floors tol at a large fraction of the period. Bounded, not routinely wrong.
- **Remediation:** Drive clocked-equation activation from the actual scheduled clock event that produced the current event boundary (a per-schedule 'active this tick' flag set by the driver), not from a time-reconstructed nearest-tick relation. If a time-based guard is retained, derive its tolerance from the same sample_time_match_with_tol used by the scheduler so activation cannot latch on a non-tick stop within 1e-9*period or miss a tick the scheduler placed.
- **Remediation status (2026-07-24): resolved via the stated shared-predicate alternative on the current branch.** Lowered periodic activation now compares the dimensionless tick coordinate to its nearest integer using the same `1e-12 * (1 + max(abs(a), abs(b)))` formula and shared constant as `periodic_schedule_matches_time`; the former period-scaled `1e-9` window is gone. A regression evaluates a point `1e-10` before a scheduled tick and confirms the row remains inactive.

#### [LOW] Sub-clock/super-clock rational factors collapsed to independent float periods, losing MLS 16.3 exact-rational tick coincidence

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-dae/src/runtime_precompute/clock/timing_inference.rs:144` — MLS 16.3, 16.5.2 (sub/superSample rational clocks) · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** subSample/superSample derive the child clock as base_period*factor / base_period/factor in f64 and emit it as a separate PeriodicEventSchedule, so a super-sampled clock's ticks only coincide with the base grid up to floating tolerance rather than exactly as MLS rational clock arithmetic guarantees.
- **Evidence:** infer_supersample_timing: `valid_positive_period(base.0 / factor).map(|period| (period, base.1))` (:144); infer_subsample_timing: `base.0 * factor` (:131). Solve-IR mirrors this with BinaryOp::Div/Mul (lower/clock.rs:114-117). e.g. superSample(Clock(0.1),3) -> 0.033333.. f64; 3 sub-ticks sum to 0.0999999.. != 0.1 base tick. Base and child are emitted as separate PeriodicEventSchedule entries (rumoca-phase-solve/src/lib.rs:928-935).
- **Verifier correction:** Code facts verified exactly: superSample=base/factor (line 144), subSample=base*factor (line 131), emitted as separate float PeriodicEventSchedule entries (lib.rs:928-937). However, the stated consequence — "losing tick coincidence" — overstates impact. The entire clock runtime is tolerance-based: timeline.rs matches/dedups ticks with sample_time_match_with_tol = 1e-12*(1+|t|). f64 rounding of tick*period is ~2.2e-16*|t|, always ~4 orders of magnitude below that tolerance, so super/sub ticks DO coincide with the base grid (within tolerance) and are correctly merged for all realistic factors. No wrong results arise. Note also the counter-form branch (lines 121-127) already preserves exact rational counter/resolution for Clock(intervalCounter,resolution)-derived clocks. Spec SPEC_0022 4.16.1 scopes clock support and does not mandate exact-rational internals. This is a real representational-purity gap and reasonable robustness recommendation, but bounded to a hypothetical the runtime tolerance already absorbs — low, not medium.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read timing_inference.rs: float div/mul at lines 131/144 confirmed; lib.rs float schedules confirmed. But timeline.rs generates ticks as phase+tick*period and coincidence-matches with tol 1e-12*(1+|t|), which exceeds f64 rounding (~2.2e-16*|t|) by ~10^4, so coincidence is preserved within tolerance in every realistic case — no wrong results. Counter-form clocks already keep exact rationals. Spec does not require exact-rational representation. Claim's code facts survive; its "coincidence lost" consequence is effectively benign, so severity downgraded to low.
- **Remediation:** Represent clocks as exact rationals (a shared base period with integer numerator/denominator sub/super factors) through DAE and Solve IR, and generate derived-clock tick times by integer arithmetic on the base grid so sub/super/shift clocks coincide exactly, per MLS 16.3/16.5.2. At minimum, snap derived-clock stop times onto the base-clock grid instead of independently expanding each float schedule.

### A.gap:Runtime nonlinear-loop and event fixed-p — Runtime nonlinear-loop and event fixed-point solvers are non-robust (no true Newton for torn algebraic loops, no homotopy continuation ever executed) — this is the single largest real-model failure mode and the review only inspected the plumbing, not the numerics (3 confirmed)

#### [MEDIUM] Homotopy continuation is never executed: Solve-IR lowers homotopy(actual,simplified) to a hard binary switch, never a lambda 0->1 sweep

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/scalar_ops.rs:123` — MLS 3.7.2.4 (homotopy operator / homotopy-based initialization) · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** The compiled runtime implements homotopy as a compile-time selection between the simplified expression (during initialization) and the actual expression (otherwise), so the continuation that lets OpenModelica converge hard nonlinear init/algebraic loops is completely absent.
- **Evidence:** scalar_ops.rs:123-125: `BuiltinFunction::Homotopy => arg(self, usize::from(self.is_initial_mode && args.len() > 1))`. This picks arg#1 (simplified) at init, arg#0 (actual) otherwise — a binary switch with no lambda parameter. The only lambda blend (eval-dae builtin_runtime.rs:113, reading INIT_HOMOTOPY_LAMBDA_KEY) is in the tree-walk interpreter and is set to a value ONLY in tests (runtime_specials_more.rs); no production code ever sweeps it. So after initialization the ACTUAL algebraic system must converge from the simplified fixed point with zero continuation.
- **Verifier correction:** Core technical claim is accurate; severity should be medium not high. MLS §3.7.4.3 treats homotopy as a convergence aid where a tool MAY implement the lambda continuation but is permitted to evaluate homotopy(actual,simplified) as actual — so the missing sweep is a bounded convergence-robustness gap on hard init/algebraic loops, not wrong results or unsoundness. (Note: MLS ref given as 3.7.2.4; project spec EXPR-034 cites §3.7.4.3 — minor citation drift, operator identity intact.)
- **Verifier note:** Re-read scalar_ops.rs:123-125: literally `arg(self, usize::from(self.is_initial_mode && args.len()>1))` — binary select of simplified at init, actual otherwise, no lambda. Grep: INIT_HOMOTOPY_LAMBDA_KEY set only in eval-dae tests; the lambda blend lives solely in the tree-walk interpreter (builtin_runtime.rs:118). Production runtime crate rumoca-eval-solve has no homotopy/continuation code (its 'lambda' hits are adjoint multipliers/random scaling). Evidence survives. Severity trimmed to medium since MLS permits evaluating homotopy as actual; impact bounded to hard-to-converge models.
- **Remediation:** Implement homotopy-based initialization: thread a continuation parameter lambda into the initialization/algebraic residual so homotopy lowers to lambda*actual + (1-lambda)*simplified, and have the init/projection driver sweep lambda from 0 to 1 (with step-back on non-convergence), re-projecting the algebraic loop at each lambda. This is the mechanism the PowerConverters (rootMeanSquareVoltage.product.y) models need.

#### [MEDIUM] Algebraic/init projection uses unscaled absolute residual tolerance and line-search; nominal is never consumed numerically

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-solver/src/runtime/projection.rs:1289` — dev/msl-triage-current.md sim.structural.algebraic bucket · category: correctness
- **Claim:** Convergence and the Newton line-search compare raw residual magnitudes against an absolute tol with no per-variable nominal/start weighting, so models with large-magnitude unknowns (e.g. kV-scale PowerConverters voltages) either never reach tol or accept a physically meaningless step.
- **Evidence:** projection.rs:1289 `residual_converged` -> `value.abs() <= tol` (absolute); `accept_algebraic_block_delta` (projection.rs:665-671) accepts a step purely on max-abs residual decrease `after < before` with no nominal weighting. `nominal` metadata exists (rumoca-solver/src/solver.rs:183, solve_ops.rs:239) but is only copied into report_payload — it is never read in projection, refresh, or tolerance logic. The refresh tol/max_iters are passed straight through from SETTLE_TOL/SETTLE_MAX_ITERS with no scaling.
- **Verifier correction:** Line-search at 626-680 is descent-based (accepts steps with after<before), so it is not a "physically meaningless" step but a valid Newton globalization; the real defect is the unscaled convergence/settled test. Triage cases listed are bucketed sim.event, but the sim.structural.algebraic bucket (80) does exist.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read projection.rs: residual_converged (1289-1292) uses absolute value.abs()<=tol; accept_algebraic_block_delta (608-686) and algebraic_selected_residual_norm (701-707) use unweighted max-abs residual, settled only if after<=tol. nominal is Option<String> (solver.rs:183) copied only into solve_ops.rs:239 and report_payload.rs:114; workspace grep found no numeric use. tolerance = args.tol passed straight through (runtime.rs:513). No rtol/nominal/RMS weighting anywhere in the projection path. Real but bounded (affects ill-scaled models only, not a strict MLS mandate): medium.
- **Remediation:** Scale residuals and the convergence test by each row's nominal (or an equilibration factor derived from the Jacobian), and use a weighted RMS norm (nominal/atol/rtol) rather than an unweighted max-abs against a single absolute tol, matching Modelica tool practice.

#### [LOW] Event restart couples discrete rows and algebraic projection by Picard fixed point, not a coupled Newton

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-eval-solve/src/runtime.rs:1314` — dev/msl-triage-current.md sim.event bucket ('event update iteration did not converge') · category: architecture
- **Claim:** The event update re-solves the algebraic loop at the new mode (good), but the discrete<->continuous<->relation-memory coupling is a Gauss-Seidel/Picard sweep capped at max_iters; tightly coupled discrete-continuous systems that need a simultaneous solve stall and emit 'event update iteration did not converge'.
- **Evidence:** runtime.rs:1314-1358 apply_projected_event_update loops `for event_iteration in 0..max_iters`, each pass calling apply_runtime_assignments_until_stable, then `project_algebraics(y,p)` (the algebraic loop IS re-solved), then settle_discrete_rows_for_pre_snapshot, then update_relation_memory — all as separate fixed-point stages, converging only when a whole pass reports `!changed`; otherwise `Err(... 'event update iteration did not converge at t={t}')`. There is no Jacobian coupling discrete parameters to continuous unknowns.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read runtime.rs:1282-1358. apply_projected_event_update is a `for event_iteration in 0..max_iters` Picard/Gauss-Seidel sweep: relation-memory overrides, apply_runtime_assignments_until_stable, project_algebraics(y,p), settle_discrete_rows_for_pre_snapshot, update_relation_memory — all ORing a `changed` flag, converging only on a full no-change pass, else Err "event update iteration did not converge at t=". No simultaneous Jacobian couples discrete params to continuous unknowns (jacobian.rs/Newton exist only for the continuous algebraic projection). Spec citation verified: dev/msl-triage-current.md lines 98,123-124 show this exact error on real MSL fixtures. Severity low/architecture is apt: it fails loud (an enhancement gap), not wrong results.
- **Remediation:** For events where discrete outputs feed back into the algebraic loop within the same instant, detect non-convergence of the Picard loop and escalate to a combined (discrete-frozen) Newton re-solve, or apply the same homotopy continuation at the event restart, rather than only reporting non-convergence.
- **Remediation status (2026-07-23): addressed on the current branch.** `rumoca-eval-solve::runtime::discrete_rows` now escalates a stalled fixed-point settle to `rumoca-solver::solve_coupled_event_newton`. The event adapter builds one square residual inventory from the algebraic projection plan plus accepted Real-valued event update rows, freezes discrete-valued modes/relation-memory overrides, refreshes runtime assignments on each numerical-Jacobian probe, and writes the solved slots back only after convergence. `projected_event_update_recovers_picard_oscillation_with_coupled_newton` pins the formerly divergent `z = d`, `d = 2 - z` case at the simultaneous solution `z = d = 1`; solver-level tests cover convergence and rollback on failure. The affected unit suites (211 tests), lint gate, and architecture gate pass.

### A.gap:State machines (MLS ch 17) are entirely  — State machines (MLS ch 17) are entirely unimplemented past semantic validation, and no finding or area summary states this MLS chapter is unsupported (3 confirmed)

#### [MEDIUM] Implicit operator overloading (MLS §14) is resolved only for the built-in Complex record; user operator-record operators are never applied in expressions

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/function_calls/runtime_intrinsics.rs:211` — §14 · SPEC_0022 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** Binary/unary operator overloading is lowered via a hardcoded whitelist of Complex.'+'/'-'/'*'/'/' only; there is no general resolver that looks up a user-defined operator record's operator functions for `a op b`, so §14 works for Complex alone. OPREC-001..011 are all 'Implemented' but cover only declaration-shape validation.
- **Evidence:** runtime_intrinsics.rs:206-216 parse_complex_operator_projection matches base against literal strings "Complex.'+'", "Complex.'-'", "Complex.'*'", "Complex.'/'" and returns None otherwise; misc_helpers.rs:44 repeats the same Complex-only map. lower.rs:1893 comment: "MLS operator overloading for Complex numbers is flattened into ordinary expression trees." No grep hit for any general operator-function lookup in phase-flatten/typecheck/instantiate. contracts.toml OPREC-001..011 all status='Implemented'.
- **Verifier note:** Re-verified all cited evidence. runtime_intrinsics.rs:211-215 and misc_helpers.rs:44-48 both hardcode a Complex-only whitelist ("Complex.'+'" etc.); lower.rs:1893 comment/logic flattens only Complex Binary/Unary exprs. Grep across phase-flatten/eval-ast/typecheck shows operator-record work is declaration validation only (ER050-056/110/122, check_ambiguous_operator_overloads) plus constructor-signature handling; no operand-type-driven lookup applies a user operator record's '+'/'-'/'*' functions to `a op b`. contracts.toml OPREC-001..011 all status='Implemented' and describe only declaration-shape rules. Constructor half has general handling, but binary/unary operator application is Complex-only. Medium: real bounded §14 gap.
- **Remediation:** Either implement general operator-record overload resolution (lookup of '+','-','*','constructor','String', etc. by operand type) or record the OPREC 'application' half as Partial in the compliance matrix; the current all-'Implemented' rows overstate §14 support to declaration validation only.

#### [MEDIUM] The derivative function annotation (MLS §12.7) is parsed and validated but never consulted during solve-time differentiation; external functions cannot be differentiated

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-phase-solve/src/lower/derivative_rhs/function_projection.rs:249` — §12.7 · SPEC_0022 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** Derivatives of function calls are obtained only by inlining/projecting the function body. External functions (which have no body) short-circuit to Ok(None), and the code never reads function.derivatives, so a state derivative flowing through an external (or body-less library) function that supplies a derivative annotation cannot be projected — the annotation is dead metadata for simulation.
- **Evidence:** function_projection.rs:249 `if !function.pure || function.external.is_some() { return Ok(None); }` — external calls give up on derivative projection. The DerivativeAnnotation struct (ir_primitives.rs:1225) is populated at functions.rs:1816 (func.derivatives = extract_derivative_annotations(...)) and validated in restrictions_decl.rs:1277, but grep for `.derivatives`/`derivative_function` shows zero consumers in phase-solve/eval-solve differentiation. The inverse annotation (MLS §12.8) is not modeled at all.
- **Verifier note:** Re-read function_projection.rs:249: `if !function.pure || function.external.is_some() { return Ok(None); }` — external/impure calls give up derivative projection. Grep across phase-solve/eval-solve/phase-dae shows DerivativeAnnotation/derivative_function/zero_derivative/no_derivative are only written (functions.rs:1816, function_metadata.rs) and validated (restrictions_decl.rs:1277); no differentiation-path reads. eval_at.rs:65 `.derivatives` is the unrelated state-derivative slot list. SPEC_0022's §12.7 entries (FUNC-030/031/032/035) are all validation restrictions, none asserting solver consumption; §12.8 inverse absent. Claim survives. Medium: real bounded gap (annotation is dead metadata for simulation), most MSL functions inline.
- **Remediation:** When projecting the derivative of an external/opaque function call, consult function.derivatives (and zero_derivative/no_derivative) and emit a call to the annotated derivative function instead of returning Ok(None); or mark the FUNC §12.7/§12.8 derivative/inverse-annotation contracts as Partial/NotImplemented so the compliance matrix reflects that the annotations are honored only as validation, not in the solver.

#### [LOW] MLS §17 state-machine elaboration is unimplemented, but all SM contracts are marked 'Implemented' with no NotImplemented/Deferred entry recording the lowering gap

- **Where:** `crates/rumoca-contracts/data/contracts.toml:3322` — §17 · SPEC_0022 · category: mls-compliance
- **Claim:** Every language-level state machine is rejected with ER073 (no lowering past semantic validation), yet the compliance matrix marks SM-001..SM-008 all status='Implemented', and SM-001/SM-008 are 'validated' only by asserting the model is rejected as unsupported.
- **Evidence:** contracts.toml SM-001..SM-008 each carry status='Implemented'. builtin_calls.rs:285 emits ER073 for transition/initialState/activeState/ticksInState/timeInState: "requires Modelica state-machine lowering, which is not implemented yet (MLS §17)". sm_contracts.rs:212-228 sm_001_mixed_clock_state_machine_rejected_as_unsupported asserts code "ER073" (not a clock check), comment: "State machines are not lowered yet ... every model containing transition() is rejected with ER073". state_machines.rs:3 header confirms.
- **Verifier correction:** The gap IS documented narratively in SPEC_0022 §4.16.1, which explicitly disclaims full §17 coverage; only the machine-readable status field over-reads as 'Implemented'. Claim's core (SM-001/SM-008 vacuously satisfied, no Deferred status in contracts.toml) is accurate.
- **Verifier note:** Facts hold: contracts.toml SM-001..SM-008 all status='Implemented' (lines 3317-3386). builtin_calls.rs:284-288 emits ER073 "not implemented yet (MLS §17)". sm_contracts.rs sm_001/sm_008 tests assert "ER073" (blanket rejection), not clock/parallel checks. state_machines.rs:1 validates only SM-002..004,006,007 (ER077/079/078/080/081); SM-001 & SM-008 have no dedicated check, vacuously satisfied. 'Deferred'/'NotApplicable' statuses exist and are used 17/14x elsewhere, so a Deferred entry was available but not used. Downgrade to low: no correctness impact (all SMs safely rejected), 'Implemented' defensible under reject-all-superset reading, and SPEC_0022 §4.16.1 already discloses this scope in prose ("not a claim of full MLS coverage... remain scoped by the SM contracts").
- **Remediation:** Add a §17-elaboration contract (or downgrade SM-001/SM-008 to NotImplemented/Deferred) recording that state-machine lowering/simulation is unsupported so the compliance matrix does not read as full §17 conformance. SM-001 (same clock) and SM-008 (parallel-assignment) are not actually checked — they are vacuously 'satisfied' by the ER073 blanket rejection.
- **Remediation status (2026-07-24): resolved by honest rejection/status on the current branch.** The existing `ER073` fail-fast boundary remains authoritative. The machine-readable matrix now marks `SM-001` and `SM-008` `NotImplemented`, marks the graph/context validations `SM-002`..`SM-007` `Partial`, and records the exact limitation in each contract note. Those IDs were removed from the implemented-contract manifest while the ten semantic/rejection tests remain green.

# Appendix B — Refuted, uncertain, and unverified claims

### B.1 Refuted claims (rejected by adversarial verification)
- **[resolve] Duplicate modifier arguments collapse silently in the AST (INST-003 undiagnosable)** — The core claim ("No phase can emit the mandatory diagnostic") is false. rumoca-phase-parse/src/elements.rs enforces INST-003 at map-build time: store_component_modification (l.917) and the nested-mod paths (l.1127, l.1149) all check value.modifications.contains_key(param_name) BEFORE inserting and return duplicate_modification_error ("duplicate modification of element ...", code EP001). Contract test inst_003_no_duplicate_modifications (inst_contracts.rs:99) verifies `Inner a(p=2,p=3)` yields EP001. The cited inheritance.rs:1824 insert is inheritance/override merging (legal per MLS), not "two arguments of the same modification" (INST-003 scope). This is exactly the parser-side fix the finding recommends — already implemented and tested.
- **[typecheck] Subscript validation flattens subscripts across parts and compares against leaf shape only** — Re-read equation_compat.rs:82-136, late_methods.rs:1556/762-773, lib.rs:605-629, instanced.rs:253-286, component_loop.rs:38-91. Evidence lines are quoted correctly, but the failure cannot occur: instantiate unconditionally expands non-primitive array components (should_expand, component_loop.rs:66), so shape keys are `a[1].b`, never compact `a.b`; the `a.b` lookup misses and validation is silently skipped — no false ET009 today, only a missed bounds check (a lesser, different issue). SPEC_0032 §1 governs structured equations in Flat/DAE/Solve, not typecheck component representation. Under a future compact form, storing flattened dims [3,5] under `a.b` would make the flat concatenation MLS-correct; the claim presumes an unimplemented leaf-shape convention.
- **[flatten] VCG optional-edge fallback pairing uses lexicographic order for indexed nodes** — Evidence lines exist, but resolve_vcg_nodes_for_endpoint forces each side to share identical segment names (vcg_endpoint_matches_node, vcg.rs:514), so lexicographic sort applies the SAME index permutation to both sides; in the reachable signature-mismatch case (explicit prefix index, e.g. adapter[1].pin vs plug.pin), zip still pairs pin[k]<->pin[k] even for k>=10. A divergent permutation needs shape-mismatched connector arrays, which MLS 9.1 forbids (equal dimensions required), so no defined correct pairing is violated. Per-side signatures are distinct, so buckets are singletons and exact matching is a bijection.
- **[solve-lower] phase-solve scalarizes tensor blocks and eagerly builds scalar Jacobian artifacts** — Evidence lines are real, but conclusions fail. (1) The lib.rs:390 scalarization is transient: build_implicit_rhs_compute_block preserves tensor derivative/residual nodes in the emitted implicit_rhs (only uncovered algebraic tail rows become scalar), and the projection plan stores indices, not programs — no scalar value shadow in SolveProblem. (2) SPEC_0007 places JVP scalar blocks in SolveArtifacts materialized when a runtime boundary asks; artifacts are gated by needs_solve_artifacts() (Runtime only) and SolveRuntime::new consumes both unconditionally; RuntimeValueOnly/GpuPreparation profiles already skip them. (3) Runtime re-scalarization lives in rumoca-eval-solve, where the spec assigns scalarization.
- **[runtime] Per-name clone + re-preparation of the whole visible-rows block on the sample-recording path** — Evidence quote is real (runtime_events.rs:133-137), but the claimed per-sample hot path is not. record_sample_if_new (diffsol lib.rs:945) early-returns via runtime.record_visible_sample_if_new whenever recorder.runtime is Some — and every non-test SampleRecorder site passes Some (lib.rs:229/427/474/888, runtime.rs:384/451/524, init_projection.rs:292/332); that path uses the plan or the PreparedScalarProgramBlock cached once at SolveRuntime construction (runtime.rs:355, 1528-1554). The lib.rs:960 fallback fires only with runtime: None, which exists solely in tests/runtime_value_tests.rs. The other caller (runtime.rs:1552) only runs when rows.len()!=names.len(), so the clone guard at runtime_events.rs:133 is false there — no clone happens.
- **[solvers] Value-based boolean/signed root discrimination can fire spurious events** — Code reads as described, but the impact claim fails. The cited example (h=x-1, ~0->~1.0) is a legitimate zero crossing: signed_root_crossed(0,1,tol) also returns true (0.signum()!=1.signum()), so both branches agree — not spurious. The boolean branch fires where signed wouldn't only when old grazes at +eps AND new lands within atol of exactly 1.0 — a measure-zero coincidence for a real signed surface. The actual runtime path (rk45 root_crossings_with_relation_memory) routes relations with condition memory to signed-only via root_relation_memory_targets, so the value heuristic only touches memory-less/synthetic/clock rows. Worst realistic case is an idempotent redundant event (perf), not wrong results.
- **[tensor] Structural phase is entirely per-scalar; solve must reassemble structure from rows** — Factual mechanism is real (StructuredProgram is per-scalar-row, regrouped in push_structured_programs), but the SPEC_0032 violation framing fails. §4 forbids only rediscovering stencils by scanning ANONYMOUS scalar rows; structured_tensor_at instead REQUIRES structured-family provenance (slots/dae_equation_index) and gates preservation on it — matching §2's authoritative-family / view-fallback model. Corner-based paths (affine_strides_from_corner_rows, regular_family_full_domain, interiors_materialized) already read only corner rows and let flatten skip interiors — the recommended family-native lowering, partly landed. No correctness or major-perf impact; O(N^2/N^3) is guarded by batch-scalarizing declining families. Architecture observation, not high.
- **[project-spec] Connection equality silently caps mismatched array sizes and defaults unknown size to 1** — The claim that a genuine array-size mismatch silently under-generates equations is refuted. validate_dimension_compatibility (CONN-008, mod.rs:696-719) raises a hard FlattenError::incompatible_connectors when dims differ, for both primitive (mod.rs:514) and expanded connector-member (mod.rs:742) connections. It runs before equation generation whenever strict_connection_validation is true, which is the default (lib.rs:144) and the compile-session value (compile_support.rs:141). So size-5-vs-3 errors out before line 88 is reached. The .unwrap_or(1) path applies to subscripted scalar-element refs (e.g. b.u[1]) not stored in flat.variables, where count 1 is correct. The cap only fires on cases that already passed CONN-008 (legitimate subranges). Residual heuristic concern about subrange scalarization is at most low, not the claimed high soundness bug.
- **[project-spec] ir-galec ships a ~10k-line generated parser inside an IR crate** — Facts check out (parser ~10k lines, feature-gated in ir-galec; SPEC_0029 §3 lists only ast/flat/dae; §12 exception is printing; SPEC_0034 is DRAFT). But the core conclusion fails. SPEC_0034 GAL-014 explicitly authorizes a parser in rumoca-ir-galec ("A parser, if added, parses into the GALEC AST only"), and the module layout, D11, and Conformance Ladder all cover parsing — so the parser is directly specified, not unspec'd. §3's pure-data invariant enumerates only 3 crates and does not include ir-galec, which lib.rs+GAL-010 document as a "language module," not a pipeline IR stage; so no invariant is blurred. Only the trivial true residue is SPEC_0034 being DRAFT — a process-status note, not an architecture/spec defect. Concern already handled by on-point spec text.
- **[tests] Balance KPI closes continuous-equation deficits with initial equations and ignores initial algorithms** — Code quotes are accurate, but the claimed harm is unreachable. Balance pipeline compiles strict (balance_pipeline_core.rs:1035 allow_unbalanced=false); ToDae rejects non-partial models where balance()!=0 (phase-dae/lib.rs:467). The report's detail.equations_unknowns() and balance() are the SAME function (balance.rs:61/162), refuting the finding's 'different counting' hedge. Thus counted (non-partial) models always have deficit_before=0, so closure_used=0 and the initial-equation fold-in AND hardcoded initial_algorithm_scalars=0 have zero effect on the balanced verdict. Baseline initial_unbalanced_models=0 confirms the init-check is a no-op. Partial classes aren't simulated. Test-metric only, no simulation-result impact.
- **[tests] Current dev triage snapshot trails promoted baseline on every sim metric without declaring scope** — Core premise ("committed triage snapshot, commit 8cdc741") is false: dev/ is gitignored (.gitignore:19:dev/) and dev/msl-triage-current.md is untracked (git ls-files --error-unmatch fails; git status clean). It is a local scratch report, not committed, not in any PR, not evaluated by CI, so it cannot cause baseline drift or violate SPEC_0032 §5, whose rules govern the tracked baseline json and promotion — not scratch reports. The regression numbers are just work-in-progress dev-run data. Metadata that "should be declared" already exists in the machine-readable msl_quality_current.json (run_scope:"full"). Secondary code claim (triage.rs get_f64 without fallback) is literally true but trivial/cosmetic and the current quality json already carries sim_success_rate. Not a spec violation, not a sim-correctness issue.
- **[tests] Pinned assertion-corpus gate simulates with --stop-time 0.0 (assertions never exercised in time)** — Code evidence (ci.yml:1408-1413, --stop-time 0.0) and spec text (SPEC_0025:212) are accurate, but the finding's premise is false. I read both smoke models (modelica_models/Tests/LieGroupTests/SO2.mo, SE2.mo): they are purely static algebraic models — no der(), no states, no time-varying quantities, no experiment StopTime. All variables are computed once from constants and the assert()s check time-invariant identities. So there are NO trajectories; simulating to any StopTime yields identical values and checks the same assertions. --stop-time 0.0 is the correct, efficient choice. The recommendation to run to an "experiment StopTime" is moot (none exists). The ModelicaTest parity gate (MuxDemux, --sim-set full) does integrate over full trajectories. Defect as described does not exist.
- **[gap:Runtime nonlinear-loop and event fixed-p] Causal refresh secant step uses a hardcoded unit probe and takes a single un-verified step** — Traced eval_refresh_row_value->solve_refresh_residual_row: secant reached only for non-direct rows. Certification (refresh_plan.rs complete_/dependency_causal) requires Direct assignment for all rows; certifies_direct_target_assignment=>assignment_shape Direct=>eval returns Some(value), taking fast path, not secant. refresh_slots_with_plan accepts causal result without projection only if causal_solution_certified (runtime.rs:494); otherwise runs project_algebraics_with_plan which verifies residual to tolerance (projection.rs:364,392) and errors+restores if unmet. Thus secant output is always either unused (certified) or re-solved/verified (non-certified). Claim of accepted un-verified wrong results is refuted.

### B.2 Uncertain (could not be decided statically)
- **[resolve] Inherited members absent from scope tree: enclosing names shadow inherited ones** (crates/rumoca-phase-resolve/src/contents.rs) — Verified in code: resolve_component_reference lacks the inherited-member check that sibling resolvers have, and ScopeTree omits inherited members (separate lookup_inherited_member exists). So the resolve-phase def_id CAN mis-bind in a collision. But static reading cannot establish this yields wrong simulation results: def_id is documented preliminary, inherited members are merged and re-resolved by name+source_scope_id downstream, and the common case leaves def_id None (handled). The INST-013 'unchecked' sub-claim is refuted by an enforced contract test. Mechanism confirmed, impact and a sub-claim not established.
  - **Resolution (2026-07-24):** Resolved. After cycle-checked extends
    resolution, the resolver now materializes non-import effective inherited
    members into each class scope. Ordinary lookup consults that view before
    moving to an enclosing scope. Differing declarations from multiple bases
    are represented as ambiguous and stop lookup rather than binding either
    base or an enclosing declaration. Focused tests cover both the collision
    and ambiguity cases.

### B.3 Reported but never verified (over per-area verification cap)
- **[resolve][low] For-loop iterator DefIds clobber global name_to_def entries** (`crates/rumoca-phase-resolve/src/contents.rs`) — bind_loop_index_name allocates iterator DefIds via alloc_def_id(None, index_name), which inserts the bare iterator name ('i', 'k', ...) into the resolver-global name_to_def map, overwriting any existing top-level class o

# Appendix C — Research fact base (web-sourced, mid-2026)


# RESEARCH omc-new-backend
## summary
OpenModelica's "new backend" (NB, enabled with `--newBackend`, which also forces the new frontend) is a ground-up rewrite of the symbolic/structural backend whose central design goal is to stay array-preserving end-to-end instead of scalarizing every array equation as the old backend (OB) does. Its architecture is a modular pipeline (Lowering, Bindings, Function Alias/Inline, Simplify, Events, DetectStates, Alias, Clocked Partitioning, Causalize, Categorize, Initialization, DAE-Mode, Tearing, Solve, Jacobian, SimCode); per the February 2026 workshop status talk by Karim Abdelhak and Bernhard Bachmann (HSBI Bielefeld), most modules are "Finished" or "Core Finished" for array handling, with Tearing/Solve/Jacobian/SimCode areas still work-in-progress. The key algorithm is "Pseudo Array Causalization" (PAC, Abdelhak, Casella, Bachmann, Modelica Conference 2023): array variables/equations and for-equation iterators are mapped to unique scalar indices, standard scalar matching/sorting (causalization) runs on this internally-scalarized bipartite graph, and array equations plus for-loops are then reconstructed ("causalize modes") — so arrays are never symbolically scalarized and generated C code keeps compact for-loops. Published PAC results on ScalableTestSuite models (CascadedFirstOrder, HarmonicOscillator, OneDHeatTransferTT_FD, CounterCurrentHeatExchangerEquations) show build time reduced by one to two orders of magnitude and simulation runtime by a factor of 2–3 versus scalarized causalization, unlocking models beyond one million equations that the old backend cannot build at all (timeout/memory overflow); build time with PAC is near-constant below ~1,000 equations and grows only linearly after.

On array preservation specifics: the new frontend already does array-preserving (non-scalarized) flattening; the NB keeps for-equations intact through matching/sorting, has initial/static index reduction support (dynamic index reduction and index reduction touching only some array elements are still open issues #15064/#15050), and adds "resizable" arrays/parameters — `--resizableArrays` "assumes all arrays are resizable, only works with the new backend", so array sizes can be changed without retranslation; runtime "target code resizable support" and array-based sparse Jacobian generation were listed as current development in Feb 2026. Recent NB additions per the 2026 status talk: implicit-algorithm handling via a tearing formulation that reduces iteration variables dramatically (their 1000-element example: 1 iteration variable and a 1x1 Jacobian versus 1001 variables and a 1001x1001 Jacobian for the naive function-wrapping approach), static index reduction, better DAE-mode support, and basic clocked/synchronous support.

Maturity: the NB is explicitly experimental and NOT default as of the 1.26.0 release (Dec 18, 2025) and mid-2026 nightly builds (v1.28.0-dev). Casella's Feb 2026 workshop keynote states NB success on the OSMC library testsuite is "currently around 30%", while models up to 3.2M equations compile and run in a few minutes. Official nightly coverage reports (libraries.openmodelica.org, 2026-07-21, v1.28.0-dev.203) quantify the MSL gap precisely: on MSL 4.0.0 (514 models) the NB gets frontend 512, backend 304, C compilation 280, simulation 219 (42.6%), verification 150 (29.2%) — versus the old backend's 514 compiled, 503 simulated (97.9%), 479 verified (93.2%). There are 80 open GitHub issues under label COMP/OMC/New Backend (July 2026), including a notable July 2026 issue admitting "The NB is less efficient than the OB on large models without arrays" (#16059), sparsity-pattern regressions (#16058), missing dedicated linear-system handling (#15757/#15500), Jacobian coloring/generation failures on ScalableTestGrids and PowerGrids (#14874/#14873), clocked-model failures, and invalid generated C code for some models. The 2026 OSMC plan of operations funds NB work heavily via RTE (four-year contract to June 2027) targeting fast compilation of large-scale power-system models, event-handling support, and efficiency parity with the OB; planned releases are 1.27.0 (April/May 2026) and 1.28.0 (Nov/Dec 2026).

The new frontend (NF) is a separate, finished story: it became the default in OpenModelica 1.16.0 (October 2020) with an average 10–20x flattening speedup over the old frontend (with a per-model fallback flag at the time). As of Feb 2026 it flattens 100% of models (excluding illegal Modelica and a few expandable-connector cases) across ~45 open-source libraries including MSL 3.2.3/4.0.0, Buildings, IBPSA, ClaRa, Dynawo, OpenIPSL, PowerGrids, ThermoPower, ThermoSysPro, ScalableTestSuite and ScalableTestGrids; it does array-preserving flattening, powers instance-based graphical editing in OMEdit, and emits Base Modelica flat output (`--BaseModelica`). Remaining NF gaps: state-machine support is still missing and expandable-connector handling was still being improved (targeted Q1 2026). Scalarization in the NF is controlled by the `nfScalarize` debug flag (on by default for the old backend path); with `--newBackend` the non-scalarized path is used, and `--simCodeScalarize` (default true) still scalarizes variables at the SimCode phase.

Competitive read for a rival compiler: OpenModelica's array story is real and published (PAC gives order-of-magnitude build-time wins on regular, array-structured models and 3.2M-equation capability), but as of mid-2026 the NB remains experimental, covers only ~30% of the library testsuite and simulates only ~43% of MSL 4.0.0 (verifying 29%), is admitted to be slower than the old backend on large non-array models, and still lacks mature tearing/Jacobian/linear-system/event handling. The window where a rival can claim both array-preservation AND full-library robustness is still open, but OSMC has dedicated RTE funding through June 2027 aimed squarely at closing it.
## key facts
- The new backend is enabled by the `--newBackend` OMC flag, documented as: 'Activates experimental new backend for better array handling. This also activates the new frontend. [WIP]' (default false) — OpenModelica User's Guide compiler flags, v1.28.0-dev, mid-2026.
- `--resizableArrays` flag: 'Assumes all arrays are resizable. Only works with the new backend --newBackend' (default false); `--simCodeScalarize` (default true) scalarizes variables at the SimCode phase; `nfScalarize` debug flag controls scalarization in the new frontend.
- Pseudo Array Causalization (PAC) paper — Abdelhak, Casella, Bachmann, Proceedings of the 15th International Modelica Conference, Aachen, Oct 9-11 2023, DOI 10.3384/ecp204177: maps array variables/equations and for-equation iterators to unique scalar indices, runs standard scalar matching/sorting on the flattened bipartite graph without symbolic scalarization, then reconstructs array equations and for-loops.
- PAC measured results (ScalableTestSuite: CascadedFirstOrder, HarmonicOscillator, OneDHeatTransferTT_FD, CounterCurrentHeatExchangerEquations): build time one/two orders of magnitude smaller than scalarized causalization, simulation runtime lower by a constant factor of about 2-3; largest tests could not be run at all with the scalarized (SCC) method due to timeout or memory overflow; PAC build time nearly constant below 1,000 equations, then linear; enables building models exceeding one million equations.
- Feb 2, 2026 'Status of the New Backend' (Abdelhak & Bachmann, OpenModelica Workshop 2026): backend module pipeline = Lowering, Bindings, FunctionAlias/Inline, Simplify, Events, DetectStates, Alias, ClockedPartitioning, Causalize, Categorize, Initialization, DAE-Mode, Tearing, Solve, Jacobian, SimCode; most marked Finished/Core-Finished for array handling, Tearing/Solve/Jacobian/SimCode work-in-progress.
- Same Feb 2026 talk — recent development: implicit algorithms, static index reduction, better DAE-mode support, basic clocked support; current development: Jacobian and sparsity updates, target-code resizable support.
- Implicit algorithm tearing formulation (Feb 2026 talk): algorithms matched to unexpected variables are torn into inner equations plus residuals 0 = oU - ~oU; in their 1000-element example the smart method needs 1 iteration variable and a 1x1 Jacobian vs 1001 iteration variables and a 1001x1001 Jacobian for the naive wrap-in-function approach.
- Casella keynote, OpenModelica Workshop Feb 2026: New Backend success ratio on the library testsuite 'currently around 30%'; 'currently testing models up to 3.2 M equations, a few minutes compile & run time'; initial support for index reduction; ongoing work on resizable array support and array-based sparse Jacobian generation.
- Official nightly coverage report (libraries.openmodelica.org, newInst-newBackend branch, 2026-07-21, OMCompiler v1.28.0-dev.203): MSL 4.0.0, 514 models — frontend 512, backend 304, simcode 304, templates 301, C compilation 280, simulation 219 (42.6%), verification 150 (29.2%).
- Same-day master (old backend) report (OMCompiler v1.28.0-dev.202): MSL 4.0.0, 514 models — all 514 through frontend/backend/compilation, 503 simulate (97.9%), 479 verify (93.2%) — the old backend remains far more complete on MSL.
- 80 open GitHub issues under label 'COMP/OMC/New Backend' (OpenModelica/OpenModelica, July 2026); examples: #16059 'The NB is less efficient than the OB on large models without arrays' (2026-07-16), #16058 sparsity-pattern regression, #15757/#15500 linear systems not handled as such, #15064 NB incorrectly claims it can't do dynamic index reduction, #15050 index reduction with partial array involvement unsupported, #14874/#14873 Jacobian coloring/generation failures on ScalableTestGrids and PowerGrids, #14829 invalid C code for SOFC model, several clocked-model failures (#14682-#14695).
- OpenModelica 1.26.0 (released Dec 18, 2025): new backend still experimental via --newBackend, 31 NB issues fixed vs 12 old-backend issues and 25 new-frontend issues; release notes say NB 'is a lot more efficient in particular when handling arrays'. Planned: 1.27.0 April/May 2026, 1.28.0 Nov/Dec 2026.
- New frontend (NF) became default in OpenModelica 1.16.0 (2020), giving 'on the average a factor of 10-20 speed improvement in the flattening phase', with a user flag to fall back to the old frontend (release notes, User's Guide).
- NF status Feb 2026 (Casella keynote): flattens 100% of models (excluding illegal code and a few expandable-connector cases) in ~45 OS libraries incl. Modelica 3.2.3 & 4.0.0, ModelicaTest, Buildings, IBPSA, ClaRa, Dynawo, OpenIPSL, PowerGrids, ThermoPower, ThermoSysPro, ScalableTestSuite, ScalableTestGrids; array-preserving flattening; Base Modelica flat output via --BaseModelica; 'very stable and fast'; state machines still missing; expandable connector improvements targeted Q1 2026.
- Buildings/IBPSA (with current default pipeline): 100% build success, 97% simulation success on Buildings and 99% on IBPSA (Casella, Feb 2026).
- Funding/roadmap: RTE four-year contract (Jul 2023 - Jun 2027) funds 'efficient generation of simulation code for large models with the new backend' for the Dynawo power-system tool; OSMC 2026 plan: improve NB library coverage from ~30%, fast array-preserving code generation, efficient runtime handling of resizable arrays and sparse Jacobians, event-handling support, and multi-rate integration via selective RHS evaluation.
- Related prior art acknowledged in the PAC paper: Otter & Elmqvist 2017 (array index reduction to index-1 without scalarizing), Neumayr & Otter 2023 (array sizes changeable after code generation — the inspiration for NB resizable for-equations of variable size), Zimmermann/Fernandez/Kofman 2020 (set-based graph matching that splits arrays only when unavoidable); Otter & Elmqvist's separate 'Resizable Arrays in Object-Oriented Modeling' paper (Modiator tool) demonstrates resizing after translation and during simulation with ~tens-of-ms translation times but imposes model restrictions.
- Nightly test infrastructure runs dedicated NB configurations: branches 'newInst-newBackend' and 'newBackend-daeMode' at libraries.openmodelica.org/branches/, updated daily (last seen 2026-07-21), covering MSL 3.1-4.1.0, ModelicaTest, and many third-party libraries.

# RESEARCH competitive-landscape
## summary
The incumbent to beat is OpenModelica (OSMC, GPL/OSMC-PL), which now has very strong MSL coverage: the live coverage infrastructure at libraries.openmodelica.org tests every library nightly, and the current master report (OMCompiler v1.28.0-dev, run 2026-07-21) shows MSL 4.0.0 at 514/514 models passing frontend/backend/simcode, 503/514 compiling and 479/514 (93%) simulating. Its acknowledged weak spots are (a) compile-time and scalability on large models — the frontend historically consumed ~half of total translation time, GitHub issue #10722 documents instantiation of the same component degrading from ~1 s to ~14 s as a workspace grows, and forum threads ("Speed of Translate/compile", "How to speedup timeBackend") plus trac tickets #3625/#4453 record long-standing complaints about backend time on models with large arrays; and (b) the still-experimental "new backend" meant to fix array scalability, which as of the 1.27.0 release notes only simulated 41% of MSL and 48% of the Buildings library. FMI 3.0 export is likewise still labeled experimental (FMI 1.0/2.0 are stable). This is the opening for a tensor-native compiler: OpenModelica's own roadmap concedes that scalable array handling is the unfinished part of the pipeline.

Dymola (Dassault Systèmes) retains paying industrial users despite costing thousands-to-tens-of-thousands per year (annual or perpetual + ~20% maintenance) because of qualities beyond raw language coverage: mature symbolic processing and robust initialization, deep diagnostics (nonlinear-system breakdowns with iteration-variable listings, "plot dependencies" debugging), vendor-quality proprietary libraries and Media/MultiBody performance, script/automation features that only work in Dymola (creating de facto lock-in), production-grade FMI 2/3 export (FMI 3.0 arrays supported; event-mode co-sim in beta as of Dymola 2026x R1), and commercial support. Independent write-ups (Claytex, TLK-Energy) emphasize that Dymola's debugging of nonlinear/initialization systems is a primary reason engineers stay; academic comparisons note both tools apply structural simplification to initialization, but practitioners report some models simply fail in OpenModelica while running in Dymola.

MARCO (Politecnico di Milano, Casella/Agosta et al.) is the closest research analog to a performance-first compiler: MLIR/LLVM-based, using array-aware (roughly O(1) in array size) equation/variable matching so compile time is independent of array extents, with a 2023 Modelica Conference paper demonstrating models over 10 million equations and build-time reductions of one to two orders of magnitude versus scalarizing compilers. But it remains an explicitly experimental prototype: v0.0.1 released February 2025, C++/MLIR, depends on the OpenModelica frontend (OMC) for parsing/flattening, no package/library ecosystem support yet ("packages are not yet available"), and no claim of MSL coverage. It validates the tensor-native thesis while leaving the "usable product with MSL coverage" space open.

The Julia ecosystem is the most commercially active challenger, but it has pivoted away from Modelica-the-language. ModelingToolkit.jl gained Pantelides index reduction, tearing and alias elimination in 2021; JuliaSimCompiler (2023) added loop "regeneration" (re-rolling) and LLVM/C codegen for large-scale and embedded targets, with 2024 work on multibody optimization. MTK's array story is a known pain point — symbolic array friction and default scalarization are documented in Symbolics.jl docs and MTK issues. BaseModelica import landed in 2024, but full Modelica import is not offered; instead JuliaHub launched Dyad at JuliaCon 2025 (rebranding JuliaSim), a new source-available declarative language that lowers to ModelingToolkit and pitches "LLM-based translation tools" (in development) as the Modelica migration path. Niche efforts: Modia.jl (Elmqvist/Otter, MIT-licensed) is still maintained (v0.12.3, Feb 2026) but is a Julia-embedded DSL, not a Modelica compiler; Pymoca is a Python Modelica-to-CAS translator (CasADi/SymPy targets) used mostly for optimization workflows — notably adjacent to rumoca's CasADi/JAX positioning.

On export targets: FMI is the de facto industry exchange standard with 170+ supporting tools; FMI 3.0 (2022) added arrays, clocks and binary types enabling vECU/digital-twin use cases, and commercial credibility increasingly requires solid FMI 2.0 + 3.0 export (Dymola ships it; OpenModelica's is experimental). eFMI (physics models → embedded production code) has a mostly-commercial tool ecosystem and had a dedicated tutorial at the 16th Modelica & FMI Conference (Lucerne, Sept 2025) — a plausible differentiator for a Rust compiler targeting embedded/real-time (which aligns with rumoca's stated SIL/real-time backend in the arXiv paper 2606.14998).

Switching drivers that emerge from the evidence: (1) compile-time scalability on array-heavy/large models (OpenModelica's most persistent complaint; MARCO's raison d'être; JuliaSimCompiler's selling point); (2) MSL and commercial-library coverage — the table stakes that keep users on OpenModelica/Dymola and that MARCO/MTK lack; (3) diagnostics for initialization and nonlinear-system failures — the single most cited reason to pay for Dymola; (4) licensing/cost — Dymola's per-seat cost and tool-locked automation push users toward open tools, while Dyad's source-available license is drawing criticism from open-source-minded users; (5) integration with modern toolchains (Python/JAX/CasADi/Julia, ML workflows) — the gap Dyad and rumoca both explicitly target, Dyad by abandoning Modelica syntax, rumoca by keeping Modelica as the frontend. A credible rumoca pitch versus OpenModelica is therefore: Modelica-source-compatible, tensor-native (no scalarization cliff), Rust-native single binary with modern diagnostics, and algebraic export to CasADi/JAX/Julia plus FMI — competing on the axes where OpenModelica is weakest without asking users to leave the language, unlike Dyad, and without MARCO's prototype status.
## key facts
- OpenModelica nightly coverage report for MSL 4.0.0 (OMCompiler v1.28.0-dev.202, run 2026-07-21, master branch): 514 total models; 514 pass frontend/backend/simcode/templates; 503 compile; 479 simulate (~93%). Source: libraries.openmodelica.org/branches/master/Modelica_4.0.0/Modelica_4.0.0.html
- OpenModelica publishes per-branch, per-library coverage reports (v1.12 through v1.27 plus master, with cvode/gbode/ida/cpp/fmi variants) at libraries.openmodelica.org/branches/ — a transparency practice rumoca could mirror.
- OpenModelica 1.27.0 release notes: the experimental new backend (--newBackend), built to fix array scalability, only simulates 41% of MSL and 48% of the Buildings library — the array-native rewrite is far from parity with the legacy backend.
- OpenModelica GitHub issue #10722 (open, v1.22.0-dev): instantiating the same component grows from ~1 s to ~14 s after loading a larger model into the workspace — user-visible compile-time pain on large libraries (Rexroth BRSL).
- OpenModelica forum threads 'Speed of Translate/compile' and 'How to speedup timeBackend' plus trac tickets #3625 ('Extremely long compilation time') and #4453 ('Compilation Time of a model with large size arrays') document persistent complaints about translation time; the frontend phase historically took ~half of total compile time before the New Frontend brought a 10-20x flattening speedup.
- OpenModelica FMI support: FMI 1.0 (deprecated) and FMI 2.0 stable for ME/CS; FMI 3.0 export is experimental (User's Guide, latest). Dymola 2026x Refresh 1 supports FMI 2 and 3 including FMI 3.0 arrays, with event-mode co-simulation and terminals+icons as beta features (CATIA-Systems/dymola-fmi-compatibility).
- Dymola retention drivers: nonlinear-system diagnostics (per-system iteration-variable breakdown in the simulation log), 'plot dependencies' debugging, initialization-failure debugging introduced in Dymola 2019, vendor libraries, and Dymola-only automation/scripting functions that create lock-in (Claytex and TLK-Energy technical blogs; ALSETLab notes some models only fully execute in Dymola).
- Dymola pricing: no public list prices; entry-level runs several thousand pounds/year, enterprise tens of thousands annually; perpetual licenses become cost-effective past ~2 years with maintenance ~20% of purchase price/year (Claytex licensing blog, reseller pages).
- MARCO (marco-compiler/marco, Politecnico di Milano): MLIR/LLVM-based experimental Modelica compiler; 2023 Modelica Conference paper (doi 10.3384/ecp20413) demonstrates models with over 10 million equations and build-time reductions of one to two orders of magnitude via array-aware equation-variable matching; status v0.0.1 (Feb 2025), ~2,450 commits, uses OpenModelica's OMC as frontend, package/library support 'not yet available', dual LGPL-3.0/GPL-3.0.
- Casella's earlier 'Towards a High-Performance Modelica Compiler' work reports that simulation code generated by OpenModelica and Dymola for the ThermalChip benchmark is ~100x slower than hand-optimized C++ — the founding motivation for MARCO.
- ModelingToolkit.jl added acausal support (Pantelides index reduction, tearing, alias elimination) in 2021; JuliaSimCompiler (2023) added loop-regeneration optimizations and direct LLVM/C compilation for embedded devices; 2024 focused on multibody optimizations; 2025 roadmap promises 'major JuliaSimCompiler performance improvements' (SciML State of SciML, June 2025).
- MTK/Symbolics array weakness is documented first-party: Symbolics.jl docs describe scalarize() converting array expressions to fully scalarized form, and MTK issue #605 plus community posts note 'the friction associated with working with arrays of parameters and variables is very large.'
- Julia's Modelica import story: BaseModelica import added 2024 (SciML); full Modelica import is not offered. JuliaHub launched Dyad at JuliaCon 2025 (rebrand of JuliaSim) — a new source-available declarative language lowering to ModelingToolkit, with 'LLM based translation tools in development' as the Modelica migration path and no import guarantees (JuliaHub 'Why Dyad? A Perspective for Modelica Users').
- Dyad's critique of Modelica (per JuliaHub): proprietary graphics/annotation metadata vs SVG/JSON, weak imperative semantics/FFI, HTML annotations vs Markdown, no package manager (monolithic MSL), weaker state machines/enums — a checklist of ecosystem gaps any new compiler is judged against.
- Modia.jl (Elmqvist/Otter, MIT license) is still alive — v0.12.3 released Feb 16, 2026, 847 commits — but is a Julia-embedded acausal DSL (symbolic processing + Sundials IDA/KLU), not a Modelica-language compiler; ~335 GitHub stars indicates niche adoption.
- Pymoca (pymoca/pymoca) is a Python Modelica-to-computer-algebra translator that flattens Modelica classes and emits CasADi, SymPy, or ModelicaXML; most development and usage has been with the CasADi target (optimization workflows) — the closest prior art to rumoca's 'Modelica as algebraic frontend' pitch.
- FMI landscape: 170+ tools officially support FMI; FMI 3.0 (released May 2022) targets vECUs, digital twins, AI and advanced co-simulation; eFMI (physics models to embedded production code, layered on FMI CS) has mostly commercial third-party tooling and had a tutorial at the 16th International Modelica & FMI Conference, Lucerne, Sept 8, 2025.
- Rumoca's own arXiv paper (arXiv:2606.14998, submitted June 12, 2026; Condie, Woodbury, Meschede, Andersson, Goppert) positions it as 'Modelica as a Universal Algebraic Frontend': Rust-native, explicit phase boundaries parse-to-DAE, template-driven codegen to CasADi/JAX/Julia, native real-time/SIL simulation backend, VS Code extension, runs in-browser via WebAssembly — and is already being surfaced by search engines alongside OpenModelica/MARCO/Dyad results.
- Switching-driver synthesis: evidence points to five levers — compile-time scalability on arrays/large models (OpenModelica's top complaint, MARCO's and JuliaSimCompiler's pitch), MSL + commercial library coverage (table stakes), initialization/nonlinear diagnostics (why industry pays for Dymola), licensing cost and openness (Dymola cost, Dyad source-available backlash risk), and integration with Python/Julia/ML toolchains (Dyad's and rumoca's shared target).
